# Rendering math and sampling specification

Version 0.2 | 2026-09-04 | Proposed baseline

## Reference representation

The correctness reference buffer is RGBA float32 in linear light with premultiplied RGB. Alpha is a linear coverage/opacity scalar in 0..1. Intermediate math may exceed 0..1 where an effect requires it; final integer output conversion clamps only at the declared encoding step.

Transparent black is `(0,0,0,0)`. Whenever straight color must be recovered from a premultiplied pixel with alpha zero, define straight RGB as zero to avoid NaN/Inf propagation.

## Coordinate system

Composition and layer coordinates use origin at the top-left, +x to the right and +y downward. Pixel `(i,j)` has center `(i+0.5, j+0.5)`. Geometry is expressed in continuous pixel coordinates.

For a layer point p, the 2D transform is:

`p_comp = T(position) * R(rotation) * S(scale/100) * T(-anchor) * p_layer`

Position denotes where the anchor lands in composition coordinates. Positive rotation is clockwise in the screen-coordinate system. Renderer sampling uses the inverse transform from destination pixel center to source space.

## Parenting

Accepted by D-57 on 2026-09-15. Call the transform above `M(L)`. A layer with a parent has the parent's whole transform applied after its own:

`M_world(L) = M_world(parent(L)) * M(L)`, and `M_world(L) = M(L)` for a layer with no parent.

A child's position is therefore a point in its parent's layer space, the space the parent's anchor is measured in. Step 4 of the layer render order below uses `M_world` in place of `M`, and so do the matte layer's own transform and every viewer overlay drawn on a layer. Opacity, masks, effects, mattes, blend mode and exposure are not inherited.

Keeping place, when a parent is set at frame `f`: with `W` the parent's `M_world` at `f`, the child's new position is `W^-1` applied to its position, its new rotation is its rotation less the sum of the rotations along the parent's chain at `f`, and its new scale is its scale divided, component by component, by the product of the scales along that chain at `f`. The anchor is unchanged. Every keyframe value of position, rotation and scale is converted the same way with `W` taken at `f`, and a spatial handle, being an offset, by the linear part of `W^-1` alone. Clearing a parent applies the reverse. This is exact, every point of the layer landing where it was, when every layer in the parent's chain has equal x and y scale, or when neither the child nor any layer in the chain is rotated. Otherwise the exact result is a skew this transform cannot hold: the anchor lands exactly where it was, the rest may shift, and the command reports it. A chain with a zero scale component at `f` has no inverse, and setting the parent is refused.

## Resampling and outside bounds

G1 final-quality transform sampling is bilinear in premultiplied linear RGBA. Samples outside the source extent are transparent black. A future higher-order filter may be added only with independent edge fixtures and explicit bounds rules.

Bilinear weights are computed from source pixel-center coordinates. Sampling must not mix straight RGB across zero-alpha boundaries.

## PNG interpretation

For G1 PNG input, alpha is interpreted as straight/unassociated unless the decoder specification states otherwise. RGB tagged sRGB is converted to linear light before premultiplication. An untagged PNG defaults to sRGB for G1, with an explicit user override reserved for later UI. Embedded profiles/gamma metadata require decoder-specific tests before being claimed as supported.

PNG output converts the linear working RGB to the declared output encoding, then writes straight alpha unless the chosen encoder/format contract explicitly differs. Display transforms are never baked into export unless selected as the output transform.

## EXR interpretation

D-62, accepted on 2026-09-17. EXR input is linear light and premultiplied, the working buffer's own convention, so samples are used without conversion. The drawing is the display window; data outside it is cut off, and display pixels the data window does not cover are transparent black. Colour below 0 and above 1 is kept. A non-finite sample becomes 0, then alpha is clamped to 0..1. Which channels are used, and what is reported, are D-62's.

EXR output writes the working buffer unchanged and premultiplied. Float output is exact. Half output clamps each sample to -65504..65504 and rounds it to the nearest half, ties to even; that is its declared encoding step. No tolerance applies in either direction: the `exr` crate was measured against OpenEXR's own library and matched to the bit (D-62).

## Layer render order

For each raster layer, the G1 order is:

1. Decode the selected source drawing into tagged linear premultiplied RGBA.
2. Apply the layer's masks in layer/source space.
3. Evaluate ordered layer effects in layer space.
4. Transform the resulting image into composition space, and project it to the screen through the composition camera (see Camera and depth below, D-58).
5. Apply the referenced alpha matte in composition space.
6. Multiply by animated layer opacity.
7. Composite with the accumulated background using the layer blend mode.

The matte layer is evaluated through its own source, mask, effects and transform at the same frame. When marked matte-only for a dependent layer, it contributes its alpha to that dependency but is not separately composited into the final stack unless another explicit layer instance also displays it.

## Camera and depth

Specified by D-58, accepted by the owner on 2026-09-15 and built by B-13c. Every layer sits on a plane parallel to the
screen at a depth `d` - a scalar property read at the frame being drawn, absent meaning 0 - and a
parented layer's depth adds to its parent's up the
chain: `world_depth(L) = depth(L) + world_depth(parent(L))`.

A composition is seen through a camera with a position, a depth of its own and a zoom, all three
animatable and all three read at the frame being drawn. A composition whose file carries no
camera has the default one: position at the centre of the frame, with depth and zoom both
`width * 50 / 36`, a 50 mm lens on a 36 mm film back (D-58).

A camera with a parent (D-171, proposed) reads its position as a point in the parent's layer space: `camera_position = M_world(parent) * position` and `camera_depth = depth + world_depth(parent)`, with the parent's chain at the frame being drawn. The zoom is the camera's own, and the formulas below are unchanged: a parent's rotation and scale move the camera and never turn or scale the view. A parent that is missing is ignored, with `PARENT_REFERENCE_MISSING`.

For a point already carried into composition space by the transform above:

`s = zoom / (world_depth(L) - camera_depth)`

`p_screen = centre + (p_comp - camera_position) * s`

where `centre` is `(width/2, height/2)`. A plane at the camera's true-size distance has `s = 1`;
one twice as far has `s = 1/2`. The map is a uniform scale about a point followed by a shift, so
it composes with `M_world` into the one matrix step 4 samples through, and adds no second
resampling. **A projection that is the identity must be left out rather than applied**, so that a
composition with the default camera and no depths reproduces earlier results exactly rather than
within a tolerance.

`world_depth(L) - camera_depth` at or below zero means the layer is level with the camera or
behind it: it is not drawn and `CAMERA_PLANE_BEHIND` is reported. A `zoom` at or below zero is
invalid rather than clamped.

Layers are drawn from far to near, and layers at equal depth keep composition order. Depth
therefore overrides the layer stack wherever two layers differ in depth. The order is settled at
every frame rather than once, because a depth can be animated and two planes can cross. A matte layer is
projected at its own depth before its alpha is sampled, so a matte on another plane slides
against the layer it shapes.

## Motion blur

Accepted by the owner with ADR-019 and D-188 on 2026-09-28, and built on the processor by B-124b the same day. For a layer that document 20 blurs, steps 1 to 3 run once, at the frame. Step 4 is taken once for each of document 20's `N` moments, each with the one matrix of `M_world` and the camera read at that moment, resampling the same picture by the rule above into a picture of the frame. The layer's picture is the `N` results summed in the order of `k` in the working numbers and divided by `N` once, in linear premultiplied RGBA; steps 5 to 7 then run once on it. **If the `N` matrices are all bitwise the same, step 4 is taken once with that matrix**, so a layer that does not move gives exactly the frame it gives with its switch off (FX-MB-010). A moment at which the layer is level with the camera or behind it adds transparent black to the sum and reports `CAMERA_PLANE_BEHIND`. A matte layer is placed by its own switch, and its alpha, blurred or not, is what step 5 multiplies by (FX-MB-024, 025). Null, adjustment and audio layers have no switch; a composition layer's inner composition is drawn once, at the layer's local frame, and moved as one picture (FX-MB-028). Draft preview uses the same moments. The graphics card does not draw it yet: the viewer draws such a frame on the processor and reports `GPU_PREVIEW_ON_CPU` (D-101).

## Time stretch and frame blending

**Accepted by the owner with ADR-020 and D-216 on 2026-09-29; built in B-150b.** For a raster or composition layer, step 1 above is document 20's `P(f)` at the layer's source time. When document 20 mixes, step 1 is `P(f) + w * (P(f + 1) - P(f))`, taken on each of the four linear premultiplied working numbers of each pixel, `P(f)` and `P(f + 1)` each decoded, or rendered at the inner composition's own size, as step 1 would at that frame. Written this way, two equal pictures mix to that picture bit for bit, and `w = 0` is `P(f)` exactly (FX-FBLEND-012). The drawing dissolve is the same sum with its own share, taken on the two decoded drawings before any mix (FX-FBLEND-038). Steps 2 to 7 run once, on the result, at composition frame `n`, with the layer's keys read at document 20's key time `u`, so they stretch with it (FX-FBLEND-023, 025 to 028), and motion blur moves that one picture. Draft preview uses the same rule at the draft divisor. The graphics card does not draw it yet: until a card unit, the viewer draws a frame with a mixed or dissolved layer on the processor and reports `GPU_PREVIEW_ON_CPU` (D-101). FX-FBLEND-010 to 039 in document 25 are the cases.

## Adjustment layers

D-66, accepted on 2026-09-17 and built in B-17b. An adjustment layer has no drawing. Its shape is an opaque rectangle the size of the composition in its own layer space, taken through steps 2, 4 and 5 above - mask, transform with parent and camera, matte - so its coverage at a pixel is that rectangle's alpha there. Where it comes in the draw order, the frame drawn so far is one picture `B` the size of the frame. Its enabled effects run on `B` in order, as defined under G1 effects below, with samples outside the frame transparent black and anything grown past the frame cut off, giving `E(B)`. Then, with `c` the coverage times the layer's opacity:

`out = B + c*(E(B) - B)`, for all four premultiplied channels.

D-297 (2026-10-04, from P-26) replaces D-66's "normal only": with `M` the layer's blend mode laying `E(B)` over `B` at full cover (the Blending section's formula, `src = E(B)`, `dst = B`), `out = B + c*(M - B)`. For normal `M = E(B)` and the rule above is unchanged, bit for bit. The graphics card leaves an adjustment layer in another mode to the CPU. A layer with no enabled effects, outside its in and out frames or switched off leaves the frame exactly as it was, bit for bit. The draw order is unchanged: far to near, the stack breaking ties. The effect stack runs on the whole frame; the mix is per pixel. At draft preview size the blur's sigma is divided by the draft divisor. FX-ADJ-001 to 013 in document 25 are the cases.

## Solid layers

D-74, accepted by the owner on 2026-09-19 ("accept D-74, proceed with B-23b") and built in B-23b. A solid layer's step 1 is not a decode: its source is `width` by `height` pixels, every one premultiplied `(r, g, b, 1)` for its colour, in its own layer space with (0, 0) at its top left. Steps 2 to 7 then run on it exactly as on a decoded drawing, so a solid and an opaque drawing of the same colour and size give the same frame. The colour is in the working space and is not converted. FX-SOL-001 to 008 in document 25 are the cases.

D-78, accepted by the owner on 2026-09-20 ("accept D-78, proceed with B-25b") and built in B-25b. A shape layer's step 1 is not a decode either. Its picture is the composition's width by its height, starting transparent black - a shape layer has no size of its own, so a shape's coordinates and the frame's are the same numbers - and each shape in the list is drawn into it, first to last, so a later shape covers an earlier one: its fill, then its stroke over its own fill. A path with keys is resolved to the frame first, by document 20's rules, exactly as a mask's path and an effect's settings are. Coverage is ADR-016's rule and nothing else - the same 4 by 4 ordered grid, the same sixteenths, the same tie-break - so a shape's edge is the quantum a mask's edge has always been. A sample is inside the fill by the even-odd rule, an open path being closed for the fill by a straight line from its last point to its first; it is inside the stroke when its distance to the flattened path is `width_px / 2` or less, which is why joins and caps are round. Each coverage, multiplied by its own opacity, composites its colour over what the layer holds so far by the normal blend in premultiplied linear RGBA. Steps 2 to 7 then run on the finished layer exactly as on a decoded drawing, so a shape layer is masked, transformed, parented, matted and blended like any other - and a shape is not a mask. A shape that cannot be drawn is left out and reported per frame as `SHAPE_INVALID_OUTLINE`. FX-SHP-001 to 017 in document 25 are the cases.

D-168, proposed on 2026-09-27 as B-108a. A fill or stroke with a `gradient` lays down a colour that differs from pixel to pixel; its coverage is D-78's unchanged. For a pixel centre p (x + 0.5, y + 0.5) in the layer's space, start s and end e, both resolved to the frame by document 20, and d = e - s: a linear gradient has t = ((p - s) . d) / |d|^2 and a radial one t = |p - s| / |d|; t = 1 where |d| is 0, and t is then held within 0 and 1. The stops are put in the order of their offsets by a stable sort. Below the first offset the first stop is used; otherwise i is the last stop whose offset is at most t, and if it is the last stop it is used; otherwise f = (t - offset_i) / (offset_i+1 - offset_i), each colour channel is decode(encode(c_i) + (encode(c_i+1) - encode(c_i)) f) with document 21's sRGB curves, and the opacity is o_i + (o_i+1 - o_i) f. The paint's alpha is coverage times the fill's or stroke's opacity times that opacity, composited by the normal blend as before. FX-SHP-040 to 069 in document 25 are the cases.

D-169, proposed on 2026-09-27 as B-109a. A shape with a `trim` draws its stroke along only a stretch of its path; its fill is drawn as before. On D-77's flattened outline, a closed one with its closing side from the last point back to the first as its last piece, L is the sum of the pieces' straight lengths. With the three numbers resolved to the frame by document 20, s and e are start and end over 100, taken low then high. If e - s is 1, or L is 0, the stroke is drawn whole, exactly as with no trim; if e = s it is not drawn. Otherwise a = s + offset / 360 less floor of itself, so 0 <= a < 1, and b = a + (e - s). The stretch is the path from a L to b L if b <= 1, and otherwise the two stretches a L to L and 0 to (b - 1) L. A place d along the path is on the first piece of non-zero length whose far end is at least d along, at the share (d - where that piece starts) / its length. Each stretch is the open line from its first place through every outline point strictly inside it to its last place, and the stroke's coverage is D-78's over those lines: a sample counts if it is within half the width of any of them. FX-SHP-070 to 095 in document 25 are the cases.

D-170, proposed on 2026-09-27 as B-110a. A fill's colour and opacity and a stroke's colour, opacity and width are each resolved to the frame by document 20, a colour channel by channel on shared keys, before anything below. A stroke with `join` and `cap` both round, as when neither is written, is D-78's coverage unchanged. Otherwise, with r half the width and each line the whole flattened outline, or each of D-169's stretches as an open line (a closed path's stretch that runs through its first point being one line through it), a sample counts if it lies in any of these, edges included. The body of each piece of non-zero length from a to b: with p the sample less a and d = b - a, 0 <= p.d <= |d|^2 and |p x d| <= r |d|. A cap at each end of an open line: round, the disc of radius r round the end; square, the first and last pieces' bodies carried on r |d| further in p.d past the end; butt, nothing. A join at each place two consecutive pieces of non-zero length meet, and on a closed line where its last piece meets its first: round, the disc of radius r round the point v; otherwise, with u1 and u2 the two pieces' unit directions, t = u1 x u2 and d = u1.u2, nothing if t = 0; else, with s = -1 if t > 0 and 1 if not, n the unit left normal (-u.y, u.x), p1 = v + s r n1 and p2 = v + s r n2, the triangle v, p1, p2 for bevel, and for miter, if 2 <= limit^2 (1 + d), the four-sided v, p1, m, p2 with m = v + s r (n1 + n2) / (1 + d), and the bevel's triangle if not. A line with no piece of non-zero length is its cap at its one place c: the disc of radius r for round, |x - c.x| <= r and |y - c.y| <= r for square, nothing for butt. FX-SHP-100 to 127 in document 25 are the cases.

## Precompositions

D-67, accepted on 2026-09-18 and built in B-18b. A composition layer's step 1 is not a decode: its source is the composition it names, rendered by this same order at the layer's local frame (document 20), at that composition's own width and height, through that composition's own camera, in linear premultiplied; transparent black outside that composition's own start and length. Steps 2 to 7 then run on that picture exactly as on a decoded drawing, the mask and effects in the inner composition's pixel space, so a blur on the layer blurs the inner picture as one (FX-PRE-002). Nesting is recursion: the inner composition's own composition layers render first, to any depth, and a cycle is `COMPOSITION_CYCLE` before any pixel is drawn. An adjustment layer inside the inner composition sees only what is drawn beneath it there (FX-PRE-009). The same composition rendered for two layers gives the same picture twice (FX-PRE-013). At draft preview size the inner composition is rendered at the draft divisor too. FX-PRE-001 to 015 in document 25 are the cases.

## Layer settings

D-189, accepted on 2026-09-28. An effect's layer setting gives the effect on a layer, the holder, a map: the picture of the layer it names, at the holder's composition frame n. It is made in four steps.

1. **The named layer's picture.** Steps 1 to 3 of the layer render order above for that layer, at n by its own timing (document 20), cut back to its step-1 rectangle, in linear premultiplied. Its transform, parent, depth, the camera, its matte, opacity, blend mode, its switch, a matte-only role and motion blur are not read. If it is the holder itself, steps 1 and 2 only. An adjustment layer gives a white rectangle the size of the composition through its masks, its effects not run. A null, a layer outside its in and out, a composition layer at a frame outside its inner composition, a composition layer whose composition is missing (`COMPOSITION_REFERENCE_MISSING`) and a drawing whose file is missing (`MEDIA_MISSING`) give an empty picture, 0 by 0.
2. **Fit.** The picture, w by h, is fitted to the holder's step-1 picture, W by H. `center`: dx = floor((W - w) / 2) and dy = floor((H - h) / 2), and pixel (x, y) is the picture's (x - dx, y - dy), transparent outside it. `tile`: the picture's ((x - dx) mod w, (y - dy) mod h), the remainder never negative. `stretch`: u = (x + 0.5) w / W - 0.5 held within 0 and w - 1, v likewise, read bilinearly between floor(u) and min(floor(u) + 1, w - 1), and the same down, in premultiplied. An empty picture fits to transparent.
3. **Draft.** The holder's draft divisor is d where its own effects already run at d: a drawing with an effect switched on (D-99), a composition layer (D-67) and an adjustment layer (D-66). It is 1 for a solid or a shape layer, and 1 at Full. At d the named layer's step-1 picture is taken down by the bilinear read at ((x + 0.5) d, (y + 0.5) d), a composition layer's inner frame being drawn at d; its mask points and a blur's sigma are divided by d; and W and H are the holder's own, divided by d and rounded up.
4. **The name.** An empty name gives no map and says nothing. A name that is no layer of the holder's composition gives no map and `EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING`, and the effect is skipped. Layer settings that lead back round, an effect naming a layer whose effects name, at any depth, the first layer, are `EFFECT_LAYER_CYCLE` before any pixel is drawn; an effect naming its own layer is not one, and a chain ends at an adjustment layer, whose effects are not run.

FX-LMAP-001 to 042 in document 25 are the cases.

## Mask and matte math

Mask coverage `m` in 0..1 multiplies both premultiplied RGB and alpha. G1 polygon edges use the same deterministic rasterization rule in CPU reference and production backend; multisample details must be fixture-tested before claiming subpixel equivalence.

D-77, accepted on 2026-09-19 and built in B-24b, says how a list of masks becomes that one `m`, without changing the rasterization rule ADR-016 fixes. A curved segment is a cubic Bezier through its two points and their handles, flattened into `n` straight pieces at equal steps of t before the sampler sees it, `n` the control polygon's length in pixels halved and rounded up, held between 16 and 512 - so a segment whose handles are both zero is the straight line it always was and draws the same pixels. One mask's own coverage is worked out in this order: expansion, a sample counting as inside when its distance to the outline, positive within, plus `expansion_px` is zero or more; then feather, this document's Gaussian on the coverage alone with `sigma = feather_px / 2`, separable, normalised, radius `ceil(3 * sigma)`, computed over a field extended by that radius so no coverage is invented at the frame's edge; then invert, which is `1 - m`; then opacity, which multiplies. The masks then combine first to last from an accumulator of nothing, except that a first mask in Subtract or Intersect starts from the whole layer: Add is `a + m - a*m`, Subtract is `a * (1 - m)`, Intersect is `a * m`, Difference is `a + m - 2*a*m`, each held within 0 and 1. A mask in mode `none` takes no part, and a layer with no mask that takes part is whole. At a draft preview size the points, both handles, the feather and the expansion are divided by the draft divisor with everything else (D-67). D-298: a keyed `opacity`, `feather_px` or `expansion_px` is first its value at the layer's key time for the frame (document 20), then used here as a plain number.

Alpha matte coverage is the matte layer's post-transform alpha sampled at the destination pixel. Apply `C'=C*m` and `A'=A*m`. D-293: with mode `luma`, `m` is the picture luma of the matte layer's post-transform premultiplied pixel `p` sampled there, `linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 p.r + 0.7152 p.g + 0.0722 p.b, 0, 1))`; with `alpha_inverted` or `luma_inverted`, `m` is one minus the alpha or the luma, so a pixel where the matte layer is transparent, or outside it, has `m = 1`. The matte is drawn through its transform into a picture the composition's size and read at the destination pixel's centre, at Full and at Draft alike.

## Normal composite

For premultiplied source `(Cs, As)` over destination `(Cd, Ad)`:

`Co = Cs + Cd*(1-As)`

`Ao = As + Ad*(1-As)`

This is the authoritative B-02 reference formula.

## Blend modes

For multiply, screen and add, first recover straight colors `cs` and `cd` where alpha is nonzero. Define the blend function B component-wise:

- multiply: `B = cs * cd`
- screen: `B = cs + cd - cs*cd`
- add: `B = min(1, cs + cd)` for the bounded G1 display blend

Then use the premultiplied source-over blend equation:

`Co = (1-As)*Cd + (1-Ad)*Cs + As*Ad*B(cs,cd)`

`Ao = As + Ad - As*Ad`

Zero-alpha straight colors are zero. Independent fixtures in 25 must verify each mode before the GPU implementation is accepted.

D-301 (P-26, 2026-10-04) adds four of After Effects' modes. Overlay and soft light use the same equation, with `B` worked on the encoded colours held to 0 to 1, as After Effects blends them, and decoded again: `B = to_linear(f(to_srgb(cd), to_srgb(cs)))`, where `f` is the W3C's overlay, `2b*c` for `b <= 0.5` and `1 - 2(1-b)(1-c)` above, or its soft light, the same function D-185's Paraffin uses; so a 50% encoded grey leaves the backdrop exactly as it is. Stencil alpha and stencil luma are not drawn: every pixel beneath, all four channels, is multiplied by `k`, the source's alpha `As` for stencil alpha, or for stencil luma the encoded luma of its premultiplied colour, `to_srgb(clamp(0.2126*Cs.r + 0.7152*Cs.g + 0.0722*Cs.b))`, the source over black as a Luma matte reads it. A stencil reaches the whole frame, so beneath it where it is empty, or where it has collapsed to nothing, is cleared. The GPU draws none of the four; a frame with one is drawn on the CPU and says so (`GPU_PREVIEW_ON_CPU`).

D-319 (P-26, 2026-10-04), the Float working depth. A composition with `float_depth` true draws in Float; every other one, and every file before D-319, in Display, by the rules above. A composition drawn inside another takes the depth of the outermost composition being drawn, not its own. In Float three rules change and nothing else does:

- add: `B = cs + cd`, not held to 1.
- screen: `B = cs + cd - cs*cd` where `cs <= 1` or `cd <= 1`, else `B = max(cs, cd)` (Nuke's rule; the plain formula turns back down once both pass white). Fractal Noise's own screen blend takes the same rule.
- Fractal Noise's value is `v = max(0, 0.5 + 0.5 F contrast / 100 + brightness / 100)`, held at black only.

These stay held to 0 to 1 in both depths: Overlay and Soft Light (worked on encoded colours); Solid Composite; Echo's add; Glow's screen, on the CPU and the card; Light Wrap's screen; the colour effects that hold their result (Levels, Curves, Hue/Saturation and the others of `grade.rs`); and the final 8- and 16-bit encode of the viewer, a PNG, an MP4 and a GIF. An EXR keeps the floats. The GPU does not draw Float: such a frame is drawn on the CPU and says `GPU_PREVIEW_ON_CPU`. `tools/float_depth_reference.py` is the reference; FX-BLEND-ADDF-001 to 003, FX-BLEND-SCRF-001 to 003 and FX-FNOISE-HDR-001 to 003 pin it.

D-330 (P-26, 2026-10-05), the 8 bpc (After Effects) working depth. A composition with `eight_bpc` true draws in 8 bpc; it cannot also have `float_depth` true, and such a file is refused. As with Float, a composition drawn inside another takes the outermost one's depth. In 8 bpc everything is drawn as in Display except a layer's effects: (1) after each effect, each pixel `p` is held to what 8 bits keep, `a8 = round(clamp(p.a) * 255) / 255`, and where `a8 > 0`, `c8 = round(to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / p.a)) * 255) / 255` channel by channel and `p = (to_linear(c8) * a8, a8)`; where `a8 = 0` the pixel is clear. (2) Gaussian Blur, Fast Box Blur, Directional Blur and Radial Blur average the display values, `(to_srgb(p.rgb / p.a) * p.a, p.a)`, as After Effects does with no linear working space, and their result is taken back to linear light before (1). Every other effect works as in Display; Exposure in linear light (After Effects CS3 manual, p.402). A layer with no effects is not rounded. The graphics card takes no effect in 8 bpc: every effect runs on the CPU, and the card only lays the layers together, as in Display. Known limit: an adjustment layer's effects are not rounded. `tools/eight_bpc_reference.py` is the reference; FX-8BPC-001 to 005 and FX-8BPC-LOAD-001 and 002 pin it.

D-333 (P-26, 2026-10-06), the 32 bpc (After Effects) working depth. A composition with `ae_32bpc` true draws in it; it must also have `float_depth` true, and a file with `ae_32bpc` true and `float_depth` not true is refused. As with Float, a composition drawn inside another takes the outermost one's depth. Everything is drawn as in Float except two of a layer's effects: (1) Gaussian Blur, Fast Box Blur, Directional Blur and Radial Blur average the display values, `(to_srgb(max(p.rgb / p.a, 0)) * p.a, p.a)`, held at 0 below and not above, as After Effects does with no linear working space, and their result is taken back with `to_linear(max(e.rgb / e.a, 0)) * e.a`; a pixel with no alpha is clear. (2) Exposure `e` works on the display value through a 2.2 curve: where `p.a > 0`, `p.rgb = to_linear(to_srgb(max(p.rgb / p.a, 0)) * (2^e)^(1/2.2)) * p.a`, which is going to linear with `d^2.2`, multiplying by `2^e` and coming back with `^(1/2.2)`; a pixel with no alpha is multiplied by `2^e` as in Float. After Effects' CS3 manual (p.402) says Exposure works in a linear colour space; Adobe's community says its non-linear blending uses a 2.2 gamma. Which curve After Effects' Exposure uses is not written anywhere found, and the 2.2 curve is this program's best reading. Nothing is rounded and nothing is held at white. The graphics card takes no effect in this depth: every effect runs on the CPU, and effects' results are not kept between frames. Known limit: an adjustment layer's effects work as in Float. `tools/ae_32bpc_reference.py` is the reference; FX-AE32-001 to 006 and FX-AE32-LOAD-001 and 002 pin it.

D-337 (P-26, 2026-10-07), Glow and Solid Composite on display values in After Effects' depths. In a composition at 8 bpc (D-330) or 32 bpc (After Effects) (D-333), and in no other, Solid Composite and Glow also work on display values, `top` being 1 at 8 bpc and none at 32 bpc: the layer is taken to `(to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / p.a, 0, top)) * p.a, p.a)` before the effect and back with `to_linear(clamp(e.rgb / e.a, 0, top)) * e.a` after it, a pixel with no alpha clear. Solid Composite's colour is then used as written, without `to_linear`. Glow tests what glows on the pixel itself, as before; its light is the pixel's display value, or the tint as written times the pixel's alpha; the spread is as before. In units `after_effects` the blurred light's colour is multiplied by `GI * c`, `c = t + 16 (1 - t)`, and its covering is left as blurred: D-322's rule, as Creative COW measured it in an After Effects working in display values; D-331's covering divided by `c` stays for the linear depths. In units `classic` both are multiplied by the intensity. Add and Screen lay the light on as before. At 8 bpc the result is then held to 8 bits (D-330). Tutorial 2 puts its glow layer on black and glows it: in linear light the black lifted the faint blue into a bright cyan pool where the tutorial shows a dim gold one. `tools/glow_display_reference.py` is the reference; FX-GLDISP-001 to 008 pin it, and FX-AE32-001 and 002 changed with it.

## G1 effects

Exposure: parameter is stops `e`, from -40 to 40 (D-90 set -20 to 20; D-318 widened it to After Effects' range on 2026-10-04); linear premultiplied RGB is multiplied by `2^e`; alpha is unchanged.

Exposure's Offset, Gamma Correction and Bypass Linear Light Conversion (D-335, P-26, 2026-10-07): parameters `offset`, -0.5 to 0.5 (absent 0), and `gamma`, 0.01 to 9.99 (absent 1), both keyable; and `bypass`, `off` or `on` (absent `off`, any other word EFFECT_PARAMETER_INVALID); each written only when not its default or the file had it. On a pixel with alpha, the straight value `v` is `max(p.rgb / p.a, 0)` in Float and at 8 bpc, and `to_srgb(max(p.rgb / p.a, 0))^2.2` at 32 bpc (After Effects) (D-333); with `bypass` `on` at 8 bpc or 32 bpc (After Effects) it is the display value `to_srgb(max(p.rgb / p.a, 0))`. Then `u = v * 2^e + offset`, `u = sign(u) * |u|^(1 / gamma)`, and `u` is taken back the way `v` came (32 bpc: `to_linear(sign(u) * |u|^(1/2.2))`; bypass: `to_linear(u)`), times `p.a`. In Float `bypass` changes nothing. A pixel with no alpha is multiplied by `2^e` alone. With `offset` 0 and `gamma` 1 the rule above for each depth is exactly the one before. At 8 bpc the result is held to 8 bits (D-330). After Effects' help gives the order and that bypass works on the original pixel values; how a value below 0 takes a gamma is this program's own rule. Tutorial 2's ground texture uses Exposure 2.47, Gamma Correction 1.69. `tools/exposure_ae_reference.py` is the reference; FX-EXPAE-001 to 016 pin it.

Solid-color tint: parameter color is linear RGB and amount `t` in 0..1. Recover straight source RGB where alpha > 0, compute `mix(source_rgb, tint_rgb, t)`, then premultiply by original alpha. Alpha is unchanged.

Tint with After Effects' settings (D-401, 2026-10-09). `tools/tint_reference.py` is the reference. A `core.tint` whose parameters hold `color` or `amount` is the older solid-colour tint above, unchanged. Otherwise its parameters are `map_black_to` and `map_white_to`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive, written in small letters (#000000 and #ffffff when added), and `amount_to_tint` in 0..100 (100), keyable, held inside its range at each frame. It is Gradient map's rule (below) with `shadow_color` the Map Black To, `highlight_color` the Map White To, `midtone_color` halfway between the two in encoded values (each channel's 8-bit values over 255, averaged), `midpoint` 50 and `amount` the Amount to Tint; so at a pixel with `a > 0`, `t = linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 b.r + 0.7152 b.g + 0.0722 b.b, 0, 1))`, `E = black + t (white - black)` per channel, `M = srgb_to_linear(E)`, and the output is `((b + amount / 100 (M - b)) a, a)`. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is; amount 0 is the input; as added it is a grey picture. After Effects' Swap Colors is a button in the panel that trades the two colours, not a setting. Drawn on the card by Gradient map's pass. FX-TINT-001 to 016 pin it.

Tritone (D-402, 2026-10-09). `tools/tritone_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `highlights`, `midtones` and `shadows`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive, written in small letters (#ffffff, #8c7355 and #000000 when added), not keyable; `blend_with_original` in 0..100 (0), keyable, held inside its range at each frame. It is Gradient map's rule (below) with `shadow_color` the Shadows, `midtone_color` the Midtones, `highlight_color` the Highlights, `midpoint` 50 and `amount` 100 less the blend; so at a pixel with `a > 0`, `t = linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 b.r + 0.7152 b.g + 0.0722 b.b, 0, 1))`, `E = shadows + 2 t (midtones - shadows)` when `t <= 1/2`, else `midtones + (2 t - 1) (highlights - midtones)`, per channel on each colour's 8-bit values over 255, `M = srgb_to_linear(E)`, and the output is `((b + (1 - blend / 100) (M - b)) a, a)`. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is; blend 100 is the input. Drawn on the card by Gradient map's pass. FX-TRITONE-001 to 015 pin it.

Aerial Haze (D-403, 2026-10-09). `tools/aerial_haze_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `haze_color`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive, written in small letters (#b4c8dc when added), not keyable; `amount` in 0..100 (30), keyable, held inside its range at each frame; `layer`, D-189's layer setting ("" when added, for none); `fit`, `stretch` (when added), `center` or `tile`. `H` is the haze colour, each channel's 8-bit value over 255 taken to linear. With no layer named `v = 1`; with one, `v` is the picture luma (the sRGB encoding of `clamp(0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b, 0, 1)`) of D-189's map fitted to the drawing by `fit`, read at the pixel less the drawing's own top-left `(ox, oy)`, transparent (so 0) outside the map. With `k = amount / 100 * v`, the output of a premultiplied pixel `O` is `(O.rgb + k (H O.a - O.rgb), O.a)`: the covering never changes and a clear pixel stays clear. A layer named that is not in the composition leaves the input with D-189's EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING. Drawn on the card by its own pass, the matte through the map texture. FX-HAZE-001 to 025 pin it.

Transform (D-408, 2026-10-09). `tools/transform_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `anchor_point` and `position`, [x, y] in -1000..1000 per cent of the drawing's own width and height ([50, 50]); `uniform_scale` "on" (when added) or "off"; `scale_height` and `scale_width` in -10000..10000 per cent (100); `skew` in -85..85 degrees; `skew_axis` and `rotation` in -3600..3600 degrees (0); `opacity` in 0..100 (100); `use_composition_shutter_angle` "on" or "off"; `shutter_angle` in 0..360 (0); `sampling` "bilinear" or "bicubic". Numbers keyable, words not. With the drawing W by H at (ox, oy) in the input, A = (ox + a_x W / 100, oy + a_y H / 100) and P likewise; `sy = scale_height / 100`, `sx = sy` when uniform, else `scale_width / 100`. M = T(P) R(rotation) K S(sx, sy) T(-A), R(r) = [[cos r, -sin r], [sin r, cos r]] (clockwise, y down), K = R(axis) [[1, -tan skew], [0, 1]] R(-axis). The output is the input's size; at a pixel centre q it is the input sampled at M^-1 q (bilinear, or Keys' cubic with a = -0.5 over 4 by 4 pixel centres, each number then at least 0 and the covering at most 1; transparent outside the input), clear when the determinant is 0 or not finite, times opacity / 100. Motion blur only with the layer's switch on and the composition's blur enabled: the moments are the composition's shutter, or with Use Composition's Shutter Angle off the effect's own angle with phase minus half of it and the composition's samples (angle 0: none); the settings are read at each moment and the transformed pictures summed in order and divided once by their number. Drawn on the card by the warp pass's mode 22 in f64; through the shutter, on the processor (D-188). FX-XFORM-001 to 032 pin it.

Lens Chromatic Aberration (D-409, 2026-10-10): the new form of `core.chromatic_aberration`, read when its parameters hold `mode`; without it the effect is D-120's form above, unchanged. `tools/lens_chromatic_aberration_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `mode` "radial" or "offset" (not keyable); `amount` in 0..100, a distance (3); `center`, two numbers in -1000..1000 (radial, [50, 50]); `angle` in -3600..3600 degrees (offset, 90); `falloff` in 0..100 (radial, 0); `red_scale`, `green_scale`, `blue_scale` in -200..200 per cent (100, 0, -100); `fringe_blur` in 0..100 (0). With `c`, `R` and `d = p - c` as in D-120's rule, `s` a channel's scale / 100 and `b = fringe_blur / 100`: the channel takes `n = 1` sample when `b s = 0`, else `n = ceil(amount |s| b) + 1`, at `t_j = j / (n - 1) - 1/2` (`t = 0` when `n = 1`). Radial: `rho = |d| / R`, `g = rho^(2 falloff / 100)` (1 when the power is 0, 0 at rho 0), `k = amount / R`, the sample `bilinear(input, c + d (1 - s k g + t_j b |s| k g))`. Offset: `u = (sin angle, -cos angle)`, `m = amount (t_j b |s| - s)`, the sample `bilinear(input, p + u m)`. Each channel is the mean of its samples' own channel (premultiplied), and the covering the largest of the three channels' sample-mean coverings. The output has the input's size. For a draft `amount` is scaled as a distance. With `amount = 0` or every scale 0 the output is the input. FX-LENSCA-001 to 026 pin it.

Gaussian blur: parameter `sigma_px` from 0 to 500 (D-90). Use separable normalized Gaussian weights with kernel radius `ceil(3*sigma_px)`. Sigma zero is identity. Samples outside the image are transparent black, unless `edges` is `repeat` (D-109, below). Blur operates on premultiplied RGB and alpha together to avoid dark/bright fringe artifacts. Bounds expand by the kernel radius. D-303 adds `dimensions`, exactly `both`, `horizontal` or `vertical`, `both` when the file leaves it out: `horizontal` uses the Gaussian weights across and, down, weights of 1 at the centre and 0 elsewhere, so each pixel spreads along its row only; `vertical` the other way round. The bounds expansion is the kernel radius on every side for all three. Any other word is outside the contract (D-46). D-321 (P-26, 2026-10-04) adds `units`, exactly `sigma` or `blurriness`, `sigma` when the file leaves it out, which is the rule above exactly. With `blurriness` the number is After Effects' Blurriness `B`: `sigma = 0.3 * B`, the factor two players of After Effects' files use (lottie-web's `SVGGaussianBlurEffect.js`, "Empirical value, matching AE's blur appearance", and Skia's Skottie `SkottiePriv.h`, "Close-enough to AE"), and the kernel radius is `ceil(6.5 * sigma)`, the weights normalised after that cut, so an Exposure lifted far after the blur finds no straight edge three sigmas out. Everything else, `edges`, `dimensions` and the bounds expansion by the kernel radius, is as above with that radius. A Gaussian Blur added from now on writes `blurriness`. Any other word is outside the contract (D-46). `tools/blurriness_reference.py` is the reference; FX-BLURRY-001 to 009 pin it.

Reduce Interlace Flicker (D-549, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Reduce Interlace Flicker (Stylize, Obsolete), a second name over Gaussian Blur's one-way blur (D-303, as D-383 and D-394); Adobe publishes neither its kernel nor what Softness measures, so the rule is ours; `tools/reduce_interlace_flicker_reference.py` is the reference. Setting: `softness` (0..500, 1 when added, keyable). It is drawn as Gaussian Blur with `sigma_px` the softness, `units` sigma, `dimensions` vertical and `edges` repeat: each column alone is blurred by the normalised Gaussian of radius ceil(3 softness) on the premultiplied linear pixels, the rows past the top and bottom reading the edge row, so the edges do not fade and the bounds do not grow. Softness 0 is identity. It is drawn on the processor, as every one-way Gaussian is. FX-RIF-001 to 009 pin it.

D-68, accepted on 2026-09-18: each parameter above is its value at the composition frame (document 20), so the arithmetic is unchanged and the blur's radius and bounds are that frame's. FX-FXK-001 to 009 pin it.

Line smoothing (D-86, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `softness` in 0..100 and `threshold` in 0..255, each held inside its range after its value at the frame is worked out. It is Reshetov's morphological antialiasing as OpenToonz's `common/trop/tantialias.cpp` does it (commit 6571328019e3a6a99c13f408ee6f4755c9cddf73, BSD-3), with D-86's three changes, and `tools/smooth_reference.py` is the reference. (1) **Encoded values.** Each pixel `p` of the drawing, linear premultiplied, is converted to `e = (srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a) * p.a, p.a)`, and `e = 0` where alpha is 0. Everything below compares and mixes `e`. (2) **Same colour.** Two pixels are the same when `max over the four channels of |e1 - e2| <= threshold / 255`. (3) **Softness 0** is identity. Otherwise `slope = 50 / softness`. (4) **The two passes.** First every pair of neighbouring rows, top to bottom, then every pair of neighbouring columns, left to right; both read the original `e` and mix into one output that starts as a copy of it. Along a pair, a **run** is a stretch where each of the two lines keeps its own colour and the two differ; its length is `L`. At each end of a run, when the colour of one line continues past the end in the other line, the pixels of the run on the side that does not continue take the other line's colour by the area a slope leaves them: the slope starts at height 0.5 at the end and falls by `slope / L` a pixel, or `slope / (2L)` for the first and the last run of the pair, which reach the edge of the drawing. A whole pixel between heights `h0` and `h1` has area `(h0 + h1) / 2`, the pixel where the slope reaches 0 has the triangle's area, and each mix is `out = out * (1 - area) + other * area`. When both colours continue past the end, OpenToonz's count of the eight neighbours chooses. A run of length 1 is mixed in the row pass only, and only when the colours beyond it do not carry the same edge on (OpenToonz's `checkLength`). The ends are taken in OpenToonz's order along each pair, the first run's right end, then each middle run's left and right, then the last run's left, because a pixel mixed twice keeps both. (5) **Corner guard.** An end is left alone when `L >= 4` and the edge that crosses the end, counted from this pair outward both ways while both of its sides keep their colours, is 4 or longer. (6) **Back to working values.** A pixel no end mixed keeps its value exactly. A mixed one is `(srgb_decode(e.rgb / e.a) * e.a, e.a)`. It runs on the drawing's own pixels before its transform (ADR-017), reads nothing outside them, and its bounds expansion is 0. FX-SMOOTH-001 to 022 pin it.

Selective colour blur (D-87, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `blur`, held inside 0..200 after its value at the frame is worked out and then rounded to a whole number of pixels `r`, a half rounding up, and `colors`, 0 to 8 colours `#rrggbb`. It is F's Plugins SelectiveColorBlur (`SelectiveColorBlur/SelectiveColorBlurSub.cpp`, commit db6dad3959522cfcd593dfe12a9fa4ad022d31e3, MIT), with D-87's two changes, and `tools/selblur_reference.py` is the reference. (1) **Chosen pixels.** A pixel `p` of the drawing, linear premultiplied, is chosen when `p.a > 0` and `round_half_up(srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a) * 255)`, channel by channel, equals one of `colors`. (2) **Identity.** When `r = 0` or no pixel is chosen, the drawing is unchanged. (3) **Encoded colours.** Each chosen pixel's straight colour `c = srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a)`; alpha takes no part. (4) **Weights.** `w[k] = exp(-k^2 / (2 (r/3)^2))` for `k = 0..r`, from the full `r` in every pass. (5) **One pass** along rows (across) or columns (down), with a reach `n`: each chosen pixel becomes `sum(w[k] c) / sum(w[k])` over itself (`k = 0`) and the pixels `k = 1..n` away on each side, each side stopping before the first pixel that is not chosen or at the edge of the drawing. A pass reads the colours the pass before it wrote and writes new ones; pixels that are not chosen are never read or written. (6) **The passes**, in order: across with `n = r`, down with `r`, across with `floor(r/4)`, down with `floor(r/16)`, across with `floor(r/64)`, a pass with `n = 0` left out. (7) **Back to working values.** A chosen pixel becomes `(srgb_decode(c) * p.a, p.a)`, its alpha unchanged; any other pixel keeps its value exactly. It runs on the drawing's own pixels before its transform (ADR-017), reads nothing outside them, and its bounds expansion is 0. FX-SELBLUR-001 to 024 pin it. D-88 (accepted on 2026-09-25) adds `tolerance`, held inside 0..255 at the frame and not rounded, and (1) becomes: a pixel is chosen when `p.a > 0` and, for one of `colors` `t`, `|round_half_up(srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a) * 255) - t| <= tolerance` in each of the three channels; a tolerance of 0 is D-87's exact match. FX-SELBLUR-025 to 033 pin it.

Glow (D-89, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `based_on`, `"bright"` or `"colors"`; `threshold` in 0..100; `colors`, 0 to 8 colours `#rrggbb`, with `tolerance` in 0..255; `radius` in 0..500; `intensity` in 0..10; `operation`, `"add"` or `"screen"`; and `tint`, `""` or a colour `#rrggbb`. Each number is held inside its range after its value at the frame is worked out, and none is rounded. `tools/glow_reference.py` is the reference. (1) **What glows.** A pixel `p` of the input, linear premultiplied, lights the glow when `p.a > 0` and, with `q = round_half_up(srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a) * 255)` channel by channel: for `bright`, `100 * max(q) >= 255 * threshold`; for `colors`, every channel of `q` is within `tolerance` of the same chosen colour's (D-88). (2) **The source** `S`: a pixel that lights the glow gives `p`, or with a tint `t`, `(srgb_decode(t) * p.a, p.a)`; any other gives 0. (3) **Spread.** `G` is `S` through the Gaussian blur above at `sigma_px = radius / 3`, so the kernel radius is `ceil(radius)` and the bounds grow by it; at `radius = 0`, `G = S`. (4) **Strength.** `G = intensity * G`, all four channels. (5) **On top**, with `O` the input, transparent black outside its bounds. `add`: `rgb = O.rgb + G.rgb`, not clamped, and `a = min(1, O.a + G.a)`. `screen`: each channel of `G` clamped to 0..1, then `O + G - O * G` on all four channels. Its bounds expansion is blur's at `radius / 3`. On a drawing it runs on its own pixels before its transform (ADR-017); a draft preview scales `radius` as it scales blur's sigma. FX-GLOW-001 to 033 pin it. D-322 (P-26, 2026-10-04) adds `units`, exactly `classic` or `after_effects`, `classic` when the file leaves it out, which is the rule above exactly. With `after_effects` the numbers are After Effects' own, from the Creative COW thread "Glow Effect and transparent background mechanics", where Glow was measured on white shapes ("Glow Radius pretty much creates layer's Gaussian blur and adds it to the layer. This is the only parameter which changes alpha of surrounding pixels"; brightness `GI*(GT/100) + GI*16*(1-GT/100)`): (1) and (2) as above; (3) `G` is `S` through Gaussian Blur with `units` `blurriness` at `B = radius` (`sigma = 0.3 * radius`, kernel radius `ceil(6.5 * sigma)`), the bounds growing by that radius; (4) `G.rgb = intensity * G.rgb` and `G.a = G.a / c` with `c = t + 16 * (1 - t)`, `t = threshold / 100`, so that read straight the glow is `intensity * c` times the light, the thread's measurement (D-331, 2026-10-05; D-322 multiplied the colour by `intensity * c` and left `G.a`, which lit P-26's tutorials 2 and 3 about fifteen times too bright); (5) as above. Intensity 0 changes nothing. A Glow added from now on writes `after_effects`, with After Effects' defaults threshold 60, radius 10, intensity 1, `add`. Any other word is outside the contract (D-46). `tools/glow_ae_reference.py` is the reference; FX-GLOW-AE-001 to 010 pin it.

Line recolour (D-91, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `colors`, 0 to 8 colours `#rrggbb`, `tolerance` in 0..255, held inside its range at each frame and not rounded, and `new_color`, a colour `#rrggbb`. `tools/recolor_reference.py` is the reference. A pixel `p` of the input, linear premultiplied, is chosen when `p.a > 0` and, with `q = round_half_up(srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a) * 255)` channel by channel, every channel of `q` is within `tolerance` of the same chosen colour's (D-88). A chosen pixel becomes `(srgb_decode(new_color) * p.a, p.a)`; every other pixel is unchanged. Its bounds expansion is 0. FX-RECOLOR-001 to 018 pin it.

Directional blur (D-92, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `direction` in -3600..3600 degrees and `length` in 0..500 pixels, each held inside its range at each frame and not rounded. `tools/directional_blur_reference.py` is the reference. With `u = (sin direction, -cos direction)`, exactly `(0, -1)`, `(1, 0)`, `(0, 1)` or `(-1, 0)` when the direction is a whole multiple of 90 degrees, the output is D-98's reading by lines, below, on premultiplied RGBA alike. D-92's first rule, the plain average of `ceil(length) + 1` bilinear samples along the line through each pixel, is retired by D-98. Its bounds expansion is `ceil(length / 2)`, or none when `edges` is `repeat` (D-109). FX-DIRBLUR-001 to 015 pin it.

Select colour (D-93, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `colors`, `tolerance` in 0..255 held inside its range at each frame, and `keep`, exactly `chosen` or `others`. `tools/select_color_reference.py` is the reference. With no colour the output is the input. Otherwise a pixel is chosen by D-88's test, and the output is the input pixel when `chosen(p)` equals `keep == chosen`, and transparent `(0, 0, 0, 0)` when not. FX-SELECT-001 to 016 pin it.

Line width (D-94, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `width` in -20..20 and `tolerance` in 0..255, each held inside its range at each frame and not rounded, `based_on`, exactly `shape` or `colors`, and `colors`. `tools/line_width_reference.py` is the reference. The disc is every offset `(dx, dy)` with `dx^2 + dy^2 <= width^2`, ordered by `dx^2 + dy^2`, then `dy`, then `dx`, and input outside the layer is transparent. With width 0 the output is the input. Shape, width above 0: the output is the input pixel of greatest alpha in the disc, the first in order of equals. Shape, width below 0: with `m` the least alpha in the disc, the output is `p * m / p.a` for a pixel `p` that shows and transparent for one that does not. Colours, with none chosen: the output is the input. Colours, width above 0: a pixel not chosen by D-88's test takes the first chosen pixel in the disc, if there is one. Colours, width below 0: a chosen pixel takes the first pixel in the disc that is not chosen, if there is one. Every other pixel is its own. Its bounds expansion is `ceil(width)` for a width above 0 and none otherwise. FX-WIDTH-001 to 019 pin it.

Radial blur (D-95, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `type`, exactly `spin` or `zoom`, `amount` in 0..100, and `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000, amount and centre held inside their ranges at each frame and not rounded. `tools/radial_blur_reference.py` is the reference. With `w, h` the drawing's own size (the input's size less any growth of effects before it), `o` that growth, the centre `c = o + (center_x / 100 * w, center_y / 100 * h)`, `p` the centre of the output pixel, `d = p - c` and `r = |d|`, the path is `r * amount * pi / 180` for spin and `r * amount / 100` for zoom, and `n = min(ceil(path) + 1, 256)`. With `n = 1` the output is `bilinear(input, p)`. Otherwise, for `k = 0..n-1`, spin samples at `c + R(-amount / 2 + k * amount / (n - 1) degrees) d` and zoom at `c + s_k * d` with `s_k = 1 - amount / 100 + k * (amount / 100) / (n - 1)`, from the pixel back toward the centre, so a zoom smears the picture outward only (D-110, proposed on 2026-09-26; D-95's zoom took `1 - amount / 200` to `1 + amount / 200`, half each way), and the output is `(1 / n) * sum_k bilinear(input, sample_k)`, transparent outside the input (unless `edges` is `repeat`, D-109). Premultiplied channels are averaged alike. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-RADIAL-001 to 018 pin it, and FX-RADIAL-002, 005 and 007 the zoom's direction since D-110.

Spin & zoom blur (D-361, 2026-10-08), after CycoreFX's CC Radial Blur, whose exact sampling Cycore does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/spin_zoom_blur_reference.py` is the reference. `core.spin_zoom_blur`: parameters `type`, exactly `straight_zoom`, `fading_zoom`, `centered_zoom`, `rotate`, `scratch` or `rotate_fading`, `amount` A in -360..360, `quality` q in 1..100 and `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000, the numbers keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; a file without `quality` reads 50, and it is written only when set otherwise, keyed or already in the file. With c, p, d and r as Radial blur's (D-95), the path is `r |A| pi / 180` for the turns (`rotate`, `scratch`, `rotate_fading`) and `r |A| / 100` for the zooms, and `n = min(ceil(path q / 50) + 1, 256)`; with `n = 1` the output is `bilinear(input, p)`. Otherwise, with `u_k = k / (n - 1)` for `k = 0..n-1`, `straight_zoom` and `fading_zoom` sample at `c + (1 - A u_k / 100) d`, `centered_zoom` at `c + (1 + A / 200 - A u_k / 100) d`, `rotate` and `rotate_fading` at c plus d turned by `-A u_k` degrees, and `scratch` at c plus d turned by `-A / 2 + A u_k` degrees. `fading_zoom` and `rotate_fading` weigh sample k by `n - k`, the others evenly, and the output is the weighted mean of the bilinear samples, premultiplied channels alike, transparent outside the input. A zoom past 100 reads through the centre. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, no edge choice, and nothing is scaled for a draft. Drawn on the card in Radial blur's pass, which takes the sample's start, the weights and the density. FX-SPINZOOM-001 to 022 pin it.

Fast zoom blur (D-362, 2026-10-08), after CycoreFX's CC Radial Fast Blur, whose exact weights Cycore does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/fast_zoom_blur_reference.py` is the reference. `core.fast_zoom_blur`: parameters `amount` A in 0..100 and `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000, both keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame, and `zoom`, exactly `standard`, `brightest` or `darkest`; a file without `zoom` reads standard, and it is written only when set otherwise or already in the file. With c, p, d and r as Radial blur's (D-95), the path is `r A / 100` and `n = min(ceil(path) + 1, 256)`; with `n = 1` the output is `bilinear(input, p)`. Otherwise `v_k = bilinear(input, c + (1 - A k / (100 (n - 1))) d)` for `k = 0..n-1`, transparent outside the input, and `f_k = (n - k) / n`. `standard` is the mean of the `v_k` weighted by `f_k`; `brightest` is, channel by channel, premultiplied red, green, blue and alpha alike, the largest of `v_0 + f_k (v_k - v_0)`, and `darkest` the smallest, so brightest never darkens and darkest never lightens. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. Drawn on the card in Radial blur's pass. FX-FASTZOOM-001 to 014 pin it.

Bloom (D-96, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `threshold` in 0..100, `radius` in 0..500, `intensity` in 0..10, `streaks`, exactly `none`, `cross` or `star`, `length` in 0..500 and `angle` in -3600..3600, the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame and not rounded. `tools/bloom_reference.py` is the reference. (1) The light L is the input pixel where its covering is above 0 and `100 * max(q) >= 255 * threshold`, `q` its 8-bit encoded straight colour, exactly D-89's bright test, and transparent elsewhere. (2) The halo `Hl = (1/4) * sum over s in {1, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8} of blur(L, radius / 3 * s)`, each this document's Gaussian blur with its own reach `ceil(3 * sigma)`. (3) With streaks on, the lines are the directions `angle + j * 180 / n` for `j = 0..n-1`, `n = 2` for cross and 4 for star, each a unit step `u` as D-92's (exact at whole quarter turns). One line's value is D-98's streak line, below; D-96's first streak, a tent of `2 * ceil(length) + 1` bilinear samples along the line through each pixel, is retired by D-98. T is the average over the lines, and nothing with streaks `none`. (4) The output is `O + intensity * (Hl + T)` in red, green and blue and `min(1, O_a + intensity * (Hl_a + T_a))` in covering, O the input, transparent outside it. The bounds expand by `max(ceil(3 * (radius / 3)), ceil(length) if streaks are on), the widest blur's reach`; for a draft the radius and the length are scaled. FX-BLOOM-001 to 028 pin it.

Directional blur and bloom's streaks by lines (D-98, accepted on 2026-09-25). `tools/directional_blur_reference.py` and `tools/bloom_reference.py` are the reference. With `u` as D-92's, the blur is **across** when `|u_x| >= |u_y|` and **down** otherwise; down is across with x and y exchanged throughout. Across, `s = u_y / u_x`. The lines are `y = k + 0.5 + X * s` at column `X`, for every whole `k`. A line's sample in column `X` is this document's bilinear sample at `(X + 0.5, k + 0.5 + X * s)`, transparent outside the input. A line's value at column `X` is `sum_j w_j * sample(X + j)`, with weights summing to 1. A pixel `(x, y)` has `beta = y + 0.5 - x * s`, `k = floor(beta - 0.5)` and `f = beta - 0.5 - k`, and it is `(1 - f) * line_k(x) + f * line_(k+1)(x)`. **Directional blur:** with `d = length / ceil(length)` and `H = |u_x| * (length + d) / 2`, `w_j = (1 / 2H) * integral from -H to H of tent(j - t) dt`, `tent(v) = max(0, 1 - |v|)`. Length 0 is the input untouched. **Bloom's streak line:** with `h = |u_x| * length`, `w_j = max(0, h - |j|)` divided by the sum over j. Length 0 is the light untouched. The lines are averaged and the rest is D-96 as written. The bounds are D-92's and D-96's. FX-DIRBLUR-001 to 015, FX-RADIAL-012 and FX-BLOOM-001 to 028 pin it.

Repeat edge pixels (D-109, proposed on 2026-09-26): Gaussian blur, directional blur and radial blur each take one more parameter, `edges`, exactly `transparent` or `repeat`. A file without it means `transparent`, which is each rule above exactly as written, and `transparent` is not written back, so a file from before D-109 saves the same. `tools/edges_reference.py` is the reference. With `repeat`, every point where the blur samples its input is first held inside the rectangle of the input's pixel centres: for an input `w` by `h`, `x` is held inside `0.5..w - 0.5` and `y` inside `0.5..h - 0.5` before the bilinear sample. Directional blur's line samples (D-98) and radial blur's samples are held so. Gaussian blur's taps fall on whole pixels, so each tap's column is held inside `0..w - 1` and its row inside `0..h - 1`, in both passes. The input is the effect's own: the drawing, or what the effects before it made of it, grown pixels included. Nothing is then read outside it, so the pixels at the edge carry on past it, and the output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. Any other word is outside the contract and the effect is left out with `EFFECT_PARAMETER_INVALID` (D-46). A draft preview changes nothing about it. FX-EDGES-001 to 012 pin it.

Colour key (D-97, accepted on 2026-09-25): parameters `colors`, up to eight `#rrggbb`; `tolerance` and `softness`, each in 0..255 and held inside it at each frame; and `match`, exactly `rgb` or `hue`. `tools/color_key_reference.py` is the reference. With no colour the output is the input. Otherwise, for a pixel that shows, `q` is its straight colour through the sRGB curve rounded to 8 bits, as D-88's; for each colour `t`, `d = max_i |q_i - t_i|` with `rgb`, and with `hue` `d = 255 * min(|h(q) - h(t)|, 360 - |h(q) - h(t)|) / 180`, or 255 when either is grey (largest channel equal to smallest), where `h` is the hue in degrees: with `M` the largest channel and `C = M - min`, `60 * ((g - b) / C mod 6)` when `M` is red, else `60 * ((b - r) / C + 2)` when `M` is green, else `60 * ((r - g) / C + 4)`. `d` is the least over the colours. The pixel's premultiplied working value is multiplied by `f = 0` when `d <= tolerance`, else `1` when softness is 0, else `min(1, (d - tolerance) / softness)`. A pixel that does not show stays as it is. FX-KEY-001 to 023 pin it.

Curves (D-111, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `master`, `red`, `green` and `blue`, each a list of 2 to 16 points `[in, out]` with both in 0..255 and `in` strictly increasing, not keyable. `tools/curves_reference.py` is the reference. When all four are exactly `[[0, 0], [255, 255]]` the output is the input. Otherwise a pixel with `a == 0` stays as it is; for any other, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` per channel and `x = 255 e_c`; `y = clamp(M(clamp(C_c(x), 0, 255)), 0, 255)`, where `C_c` is the channel's curve and `M` the master; the pixel becomes `srgb_to_linear(y / 255) * a` per channel, `a` unchanged. A curve through `(x_i, y_i)`, `i = 0..n-1`, is `y_0` for `x <= x_0`, `y_{n-1}` for `x >= x_{n-1}`, and between them the natural cubic spline: with `h_i = x_{i+1} - x_i`, `M_0 = M_{n-1} = 0` and for `i = 1..n-2`, `h_{i-1} M_{i-1} + 2 (h_{i-1} + h_i) M_i + h_i M_{i+1} = 6 ((y_{i+1} - y_i) / h_i - (y_i - y_{i-1}) / h_{i-1})`; on `[x_i, x_{i+1}]` with `t = x - x_i`, `y = y_i + t ((y_{i+1} - y_i) / h_i - h_i (2 M_i + M_{i+1}) / 6) + t^2 M_i / 2 + t^3 (M_{i+1} - M_i) / (6 h_i)`. FX-CURVES-001 to 020 pin it. D-302 adds `alpha`, the same kind of curve, `[[0, 0], [255, 255]]` when the file leaves it out: when it is not straight, after the colour step each pixel's `a` becomes `a' = clamp(A(255 a), 0, 255) / 255`, where `A` is the alpha curve, and its colour `p.rgb * a' / a`, or 0 where `a == 0`.

Levels (D-112, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `input_black`, `input_white`, `output_black` and `output_white`, each in 0..255, and `gamma` in 0.1..10, each held inside its range at each frame. `tools/levels_reference.py` is the reference. A pixel with `a == 0` stays as it is; for any other, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` per channel and `x = 255 e_c`; `v = 1` when `x >= input_black` and `0` otherwise if `input_white == input_black`, else `v = clamp((x - input_black) / (input_white - input_black), 0, 1)`; `v = v^(1 / gamma)`; `e'_c = (output_black + v (output_white - output_black)) / 255`; the pixel becomes `srgb_to_linear(e'_c) * a` per channel, `a` unchanged. A white below its black inverts and is allowed. FX-LEVELS-001 to 024 pin it.

Levels with a set for each channel (D-383, accepted on 2026-10-09 by the owner's choice, "Both names, one engine"). `tools/levels_individual_reference.py` is the reference. `core.levels` gains `channel`, "rgb", "red", "green", "blue" or "alpha" (the word exact), which picks the set the panel shows and never changes the picture; `core.levels_individual`, Levels (Individual Controls), is the second name over the same rule, with no `channel`. Five sets of five settings: the RGB set is D-112's `input_black`, `input_white`, `gamma`, `output_black` and `output_white`; each other set is the same five names after `red_`, `green_`, `blue_` or `alpha_`. Inputs and outputs in 0..255, gamma in 0.1..10, starts 0, 255, 1, 0, 255 (a set at its start changes nothing); all 25 keyable, each held inside its range at each frame. One set on a value x on the 0..255 scale is D-112's step. At a pixel with `a > 0`, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1: each of red, green and blue on 255 e goes through its own set and then through the RGB set (the order is ours, the same as Curves', D-111), a set at its start skipped; the result over 255, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a. A pixel with `a == 0` keeps its colour. Then, when the alpha set is not at its start, the covering 255 a goes through the alpha set, over 255, and the straight colour is kept at the new covering, as Curves' alpha (D-302): a pixel that did not show and now does is black. Every set at its start leaves the layer exactly as it is. A file's `core.levels` with D-112's five settings and no `channel` is D-112's Levels, unchanged; one with a `channel` reads each set not written at its start. Drawn on the card in the colour pass (mode 1, the RGB, red, green and blue sets, gamma sent as 1 / gamma, fused in a colour run); an alpha set not at its start, or any set whose input white equals its input black, is drawn on the processor. FX-LVLIC-001 to 020 pin it; B-262 pins the build.

Photo Filter (D-384, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Photo Filter, whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/photo_filter_reference.py` is the reference. `core.photo_filter`: parameters `filter`, one of "warming_85", "warming_81", "cooling_80", "cooling_82", "sepia", "underwater" or "custom" ("warming_85"; the word exact); `color`, `#rrggbb` ("#ec8a00"), used only when the filter is "custom" but always checked; `density` in 0..100 (25), keyable, held inside its range; `preserve_luminosity`, "on" or "off" ("on"). The presets' colours F: warming_85 #ec8a00, warming_81 #ebb113, cooling_80 #006dff, cooling_82 #00b5ff, sepia #ac7a33, underwater #00c2b1 (fmwconcepts' colorfilter, after Photoshop's Photo Filter). At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1 and d the density over 100: f = e (1 - d + d F) per channel. With Preserve Luminosity on and L(f) > 0, where L(c) = 0.2126 c_r + 0.7152 c_g + 0.0722 c_b on the encoded values, f becomes f L(e) / L(f); when L(f) is 0, f (black) is kept. The result held inside 0..1 (the effect works on 8-bit values, as After Effects' does), back to linear at covering a. The covering is never changed; a pixel with covering 0 stays as it is; density 0 leaves the layer exactly as it is. Drawn on the card in the colour pass (mode 32), fused in a colour run. FX-PFILT-001 to 020 pin it; B-263 pins the build.

Color Grade (D-397, 2026-10-09), after Premiere Pro's and After Effects' Lumetri Color, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so every rule here is ours, built where it can be from rules this program already has. `tools/color_grade_reference.py` is the reference. `core.color_grade`, its first unit (Basic Correction, Creative, Vignette; Curves, Color Wheels and HSL Secondary are D-398's): parameters `temperature`, `tint`, `contrast`, `highlights`, `shadows`, `whites`, `blacks`, `vibrance` and `tint_balance`, each in -100..100 (0); `exposure`, stops in -5..5 (0); `saturation`, `look_intensity` and `creative_saturation`, each in 0..200 (100); `faded_film`, `shadow_tint_amount` and `highlight_tint_amount`, each in 0..100 (0); `shadow_tint_hue` and `highlight_tint_hue`, degrees in 0..360 (0); `vignette_amount` in -5..5 (0); `vignette_midpoint` (50), `vignette_roundness` (0) and `vignette_feather` (50), each in 0..100; every number keyable and held inside its range; `look`, a word, not keyable: the id of an asset of kind `lut`, or empty (empty when added). At a pixel with covering a > 0, e is its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1, L(c) = 0.2126 c_r + 0.7152 c_g + 0.0722 c_b on encoded values. The steps run in Lumetri's order, each one that changes anything a step of its own: its result held inside 0..1 and back to linear at covering a before the next step reads it. (1) Basic tone, when any of temperature, tint, exposure, contrast, highlights, shadows, whites or blacks is not 0: with t and n the temperature and tint over 100, the gains (2^(t/2), 2^(-n/2), 2^(-t/2)), each divided by their L so white's brightness stays, times 2^exposure; when a gain is not 1 each channel is taken to linear light, multiplied by its gain, held inside 0..1 and encoded again. Then on each channel v, with c, h, s, w, b the contrast, highlights, shadows, whites and blacks over 100, in turn: v += c v (1 - v)(2v - 1); v += h v² (1 - v); v += s v (1 - v)²; v += w v² / 4; v += b (1 - v)² / 4. (2) Saturation, when not 100: Vibrance's rule (D-140) with vibrance 0 and saturation `saturation` - 100. (3) The look, when `look` names a lut asset whose file reads and the intensity is not 0: Color Lookup's reading (D-182) gives l, and the step gives e + I (l - e), I the intensity over 100. (4) Faded film, when not 0: v + f (0.25 (1 - v)² - 0.1 v²) on each channel, f over 100. (5) Vibrance and creative saturation, when either changes anything: Vibrance's rule with `vibrance` and `creative_saturation` - 100. (6) Tints, when either amount is not 0: Color balance's rule (D-130), luminosity not kept, the midtones untouched, the shadows' setting A_s (1 - B)(C_s - L(C_s)) and the highlights' A_h (1 + B)(C_h - L(C_h)), A the amount, B the balance over 100 and C the hue's pure colour (HSL saturation 1, lightness 1/2). (7) Vignette, when the amount is not 0: Vignette's rule (D-126) with amount |vignette_amount| × 20, the colour black below 0 and white above, size 50 + midpoint, the roundness, softness the feather, centred on the layer, which a draft scales. A pixel with covering 0 stays as it is; the covering is never changed; with every setting as added the layer is left exactly as it is. A `look` naming nothing that is a lut asset of the project draws the rest of the grade with `EFFECT_PARAMETER_INVALID`, on opening and at each frame, as Color Lookup warns. On the card the steps are one chain of their own (tone modes 40, 7 and 41, grade modes 6 and 8), not fused into a colour run, so a Mix (B-221) is the whole grade's. FX-GRADE-001 to 034 pin it; B-276 pins the build.

Color Grade's second unit (D-398, 2026-10-09): Lumetri's Curves, Color Wheels and HSL Secondary, run between the tints and the vignette, every rule ours as D-397's. `tools/color_grade_2_reference.py` is the reference. New parameters: `curve_master`, `curve_red`, `curve_green` and `curve_blue`, each Curves' list of points (D-111, 2 to 16 points [in, out], 0..255, in rising), straight when added; `hue_saturation`, 0 to 16 points [hue, saturation], hue in 0..<360 rising, saturation 0..200, none when added; `shadow_wheel_hue`, `midtone_wheel_hue`, `highlight_wheel_hue` and `secondary_wheel_hue`, degrees in 0..360 (0); `shadow_wheel_amount`, `midtone_wheel_amount`, `highlight_wheel_amount` and `secondary_wheel_amount`, 0..100 (0); `shadow_lightness`, `midtone_lightness`, `highlight_lightness`, `secondary_temperature`, `secondary_tint`, `secondary_contrast` and `secondary_lightness`, -100..100 (0); `secondary_saturation`, 0..200 (100); `key_hue`, degrees in 0..360 (0); `key_hue_range`, 0..180 (180) and `key_hue_softness`, 0..180 (0), degrees; `key_saturation_low`, `key_saturation_high`, `key_lightness_low` and `key_lightness_high`, per cent in 0..100 (0, 100, 0, 100); `key_softness`, 0..100 (0); every number keyable and held inside its range; `key_invert`, "off" or "on" ("off"), and `key_view`, "off" or "mask" ("off"), words, not keyable. A file without them (D-397's) reads each as added. After D-397's step 6: (8) RGB curves, when any of the four is not straight: Curves' rule (D-111), each channel through its own curve, then the master. (9) Hue versus saturation, when a point's saturation is not 100: with H the HSL hue of e in degrees (0 for a grey) and S(H) the curve, Vibrance's rule (D-140) with vibrance 0 and saturation S(H) - 100. One point is its saturation at every hue; with more, from each point to the next round the circle (the last to the first through 360), s_i + (s_{i+1} - s_i) u² (3 - 2u), u the share of the way. (10) Color wheels, when any amount or lightness is not 0: Color Balance's rule (D-130), luminosity not kept, with each range's setting A (C - L(C)) + K on every channel, A the wheel's amount, C its hue's pure colour (HSL saturation 1, lightness 1/2), K its lightness. (11) HSL Secondary, when the correction changes anything or `key_view` is "mask". The key m from e's HSL hue H (degrees), saturation S and lightness Lh (per cent; S held at 100 at most, since the arithmetic can carry a pure colour's saturation a rounding step past 1, which would otherwise leave it outside a band that ends at 100): the hue's weight is 1 when `key_hue_range` is 180; otherwise 0 for a grey, and for a colour 1 within the range of `key_hue` round the circle, 1 - t² (3 - 2t) over `key_hue_softness` beyond it (t the share of the softness passed), 0 past that; the saturation's weight 1 from its low to its high and falling the same way over `key_softness` outside them, the lightness's likewise; m their product, 1 - m with `key_invert` "on". With `key_view` "mask" the step gives the grey m and the vignette is not drawn. Otherwise the correction c from e: step 1's gains of `secondary_temperature` and `secondary_tint` (exposure 0) in linear light, held inside 0..1, when either is not 0; then v += k v (1 - v)(2v - 1), k the contrast over 100; then L(c) + (c - L(c)) s, s the saturation over 100; then c + (A (C - L(C)) + K) / 200 for the wheel and lightness as step 10's; and the step gives e + m (c - e). On the card the curves are Curves' grade pass (mode 0), the wheels the grade pass's Color Balance (mode 6), the hue curve tone mode 42 and the secondary tone mode 43, each a stage of the grade's own chain. FX-GRADE2-001 to 040 pin it; B-277 pins the build.

Color Grade's third unit (D-571, 2026-10-12): the rest of Lumetri's Curves and HSL Secondary, every rule ours as D-397's, as Adobe publishes none. `tools/color_grade_3_reference.py` is the reference. New parameters: `hue_hue`, 0 to 16 points [hue, shift], hue in 0..<360 rising, shift -180..180 degrees; `hue_luma`, 0 to 16 points [hue, luma], hue as before, luma -100..100; `luma_saturation` and `saturation_saturation`, 0 to 16 points [x, saturation], x in 0..100 rising, saturation 0..200; each none when added; `secondary_wheels`, "one" or "three" ("one"), a word, not keyable; `secondary_shadow_wheel_hue` and `secondary_highlight_wheel_hue`, degrees in 0..360 (0); `secondary_shadow_wheel_amount` and `secondary_highlight_wheel_amount`, 0..100 (0); `secondary_shadow_lightness` and `secondary_highlight_lightness`, -100..100 (0); `key_denoise` and `key_blur`, 0..100 (0); every number keyable and held inside its range. A file without them (D-398's) reads each as added and draws D-398's picture. After step 9, in this order, each run only when one of its points' second numbers is not its flat value (0 for the two hue curves, 100 for the two saturation curves): (9a) Hue versus hue: with H the HSL hue of e, the colour of hue (H + T(H)) mod 360, its HSL lightness and saturation kept; a grey (no hue) is left as it is. (9b) Hue versus luma: e + T(H) (max(e) - min(e)) / 200 on every channel, H 0 for a grey (whose chroma is 0, so it is unchanged). T is step 9's curve round the hue circle, through these points. (9c) Luma versus saturation and (9d) saturation versus saturation: L(e) + (e - L(e)) V(x) / 100, L D-397's luma (0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722), x = 100 L(e) for 9c and 100 times e's HSL saturation, held at 1 at most, for 9d; V is one point's saturation at every x, and otherwise from each point to the next s_i + (s_{i+1} - s_i) u^2 (3 - 2u), u the share of the way, and held at the first point's below it and the last point's above it (not round a circle). Step 11, the HSL Secondary, changes in two ways. (a) Three wheels: with `secondary_wheels` "three" the push P = (A (C - L(C)) + K) / 200 of step 11 becomes ws Ps + (1 - ws - wh) Pm + wh Ph, ws = clamp(1 - 2 L(e), 0, 1), wh = clamp(2 L(e) - 1, 0, 1), as Color Balance's ranges (D-130), Pm the old wheel and `secondary_lightness` (now the midtones'), Ps and Ph the same from the shadow and highlight wheel hue, amount and lightness; the step also runs when any of those four amounts and lightnesses is not 0. With "one" the shadow and highlight wheels are not used. (b) Refine: when `key_denoise` or `key_blur` is above 0, the key m (after `key_invert`) is worked out for every pixel of the layer, 0 where the pixel is uncovered, then run through Median's disc (D-203) of radius `key_denoise` / 10 pixels, which changes nothing below 1, and then Gaussian Blur's normalised kernel of sigma `key_blur` / 10 pixels, radius ceil(3 sigma), the layer's edge pixels repeated as Shadow/Highlight's (D-400); that m is used in the correction and in the mask view. The bounds do not grow. A Draft preview at half size halves both, distances. Highlight recovery past white is not built: the grade works on encoded colours held inside 0..1 (D-397), so there is no value past white to recover, and any rule would be invented. On the card the four curves are tone modes 120 to 123, each a stage of the grade's chain, and the three wheels ride with tone mode 43; a grade with a refined key is drawn on the processor, with no refusal. FX-GRADE3-001 to 034 pin it; B-450 pins the build.

Color Grade's fourth unit (D-637, 2026-10-13): Lumetri's Creative Sharpen and a squarer vignette, the rules ours. `tools/color_grade_4_reference.py` is the reference. New parameter `sharpen`, -100..100 (0), keyable and held inside its range; `vignette_roundness` now -100..100. A file without `sharpen` (D-571's) reads it as 0. (4b) Sharpen, after step 4 and before step 5, when not 0: the Sharpen effect's rule (D-147) with amount `sharpen` (so the colour moves `sharpen` / 100 of its distance from its blur, toward the blur below 0), radius 1 pixel, threshold 0, on the layer's premultiplied linear pixels; the radius is not scaled by a draft. Step 7, below 0 roundness: the half-axes are those of roundness 0, and with dx, dy the offsets over them, q = (1 + roundness / 100) / 2, M = max(|dx|, |dy|): d = M when q = 0 or M = 0, otherwise d = M ((|dx| / M)^(1/q) + (|dy| / M)^(1/q))^q / 2^q, which is D-126's d at q = 1/2.
