A path draws nothing when its from is not less than its to. **Dabs.** Round dabs `brush_size` across at every k step along each path from its first point, step = `spacing` / 100 `brush_size`, those from the first place drawn to the last, both ends included, so they stay put as Start moves; `spacing` 0 lays the brush all along. d is the distance from a pixel's centre to the nearest dab (with `spacing` 0, the nearest drawn point). With r = `brush_size` / 2, w = max(r (1 - `brush_hardness` / 100), 1) and t = clamp((r + 0.5 - d) / w, 0, 1), the covering is c = `opacity` / 100 t^2 (3 - 2 t). Dabs do not build up: the nearest counts. `brush_size` 0 draws nothing. **Paint style**, with C the colour in linear light and O the layer, premultiplied: `on_original`, O.rgb (1 - c) + C c and O.a (1 - c) + c; `on_transparent`, C c and c; `reveal`, O c. Bounds never grow: the stroke is drawn inside the layer, its drawing's top-left at (ox, oy) however far earlier effects grew it. A draft scales `brush_size` as a distance.

Compound Blur (D-191, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `layer`, D-189's layer setting; `fit`, `center`, `stretch` or `tile`; `max_blur` in 0..500, a distance, keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `invert`, `off` or `on`; `edges`, `transparent` or `repeat`. `tools/compound_blur_reference.py` is the reference. With no map (no layer named, a missing one) or `max_blur` 0, the output is the input. Bounds never grow. With `O` the input, linear and premultiplied, its drawing's own top-left pixel at `(ox, oy)` however far earlier effects grew it, and `M` D-189's map fitted to the drawing's own `W` by `H` (the composition's for an adjustment layer), pixel `(x, y)` reads `M(x - ox, y - oy)`, transparent outside it, and `v` is its picture luma, the sRGB encoding of `clamp(0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b, 0, 1)`, as Find Edges reads a picture; with `invert` `on`, `v` is `1 - v`. With `S = max_blur / 3`, the levels are `s_0 = 0` and `s_j = S 2^(j - 5)` for `j` = 1 to 5. `B_0` is `O`, and `B_j` is `O` through Gaussian Blur's normalised kernel at sigma `s_j`, radius `ceil(3 s_j)`, read at `O`'s own pixels: with `edges` `transparent` nothing outside `O`, with `repeat` its outer pixels held. At each pixel `s = v S`; with `j` the level where `s_j <= s <= s_(j + 1)` and `t = (s - s_j) / (s_(j + 1) - s_j)`, the output is `B_j + t (B_(j + 1) - B_j)`, all four numbers. For a draft, `max_blur` is scaled as a distance and the map is made at the holder's divisor (D-189, step 3). FX-CBLUR-001 to 026 pin it.

Displacement Map (D-193, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `layer`, D-189's layer setting; `fit`, `center`, `stretch` or `tile`; `horizontal` and `vertical`, each `red`, `green`, `blue`, `alpha`, `luminance`, `hue`, `lightness`, `saturation`, `full` or `off`; `max_horizontal` and `max_vertical` in -1000..1000, distances, keyable and held inside their range at each frame; `wrap`, `off` or `on`. `tools/displacement_map_reference.py` is the reference. With no map (no layer named, a missing one) the output is the input. Bounds never grow, unless D-315's `expand` is `on`. With `O` the input, linear and premultiplied, its drawing's own top-left pixel at `(ox, oy)` however far earlier effects grew it, and `M` D-189's map fitted to the drawing's own `W` by `H` (the composition's for an adjustment layer), pixel `(x, y)` reads `m = M(x - ox, y - oy)`, transparent outside it, with covering `a`. Each direction's word gives `v`: `full` 1, `off` 0.5, `alpha` `a`; every other word 0.5 where `a` is 0, and otherwise `0.5 + a (k - 0.5)`, the map laid over mid grey, where with `c = linear_to_srgb(clamp(m.rgb / a, 0, 1))` `k` is `c`'s red, green or blue; for `luminance`, `linear_to_srgb(0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b)` of the straight linear colour `clamp(m.rgb / a, 0, 1)`; for `hue`, `saturation` and `lightness`, Hue/Saturation's HSL of `c` (D-113), the hue in degrees divided by 360. Then `dx = (2 v_h - 1) max_horizontal` and `dy = (2 v_v - 1) max_vertical`, and the output pixel is `O` read by this document's bilinear resampling at `(x + 0.5 + dx, y + 0.5 + dy)`, transparent outside `O`; with `wrap` `on`, the four pixels it reads are taken modulo `O`'s width and height. For a draft both maxima are scaled as distances and the map is made at the holder's divisor (D-189, step 3). FX-DMAP-001 to 031 pin it. D-315 (2026-10-04) adds `expand`, `off` or `on`: `off` when a file does not say, and written only when `on` or already in the file. With `expand` `on` and `wrap` `off`, and a map, `O` first grows clear on every side by `g = ceil(max(|max_horizontal|, |max_vertical|))`, so the drawing's corner is at `(ox + g, oy + g)`, and a pixel outside `M` reads `M`'s nearest edge pixel instead of nothing, so a push near the edge carries on past it. With `wrap` `on`, or `g` 0, `expand` changes nothing. B-196 pins it.

Displacement Map's colour amounts (D-412, Map Chromatic Displacement, 2026-10-10): parameters `red_amount`, `green_amount` and `blue_amount` in -1000..1000 per cent and `spectrum` in 3..32, all keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; absent 100, 100, 100 and 3. `tools/map_chromatic_reference.py` is the reference. With `N` the whole part of the spectrum and the amounts `r, g, b` as fractions (per cent / 100), sample `i` of `0..N-1` sits at `t = i / (N - 1)`, from red (0) through green (1/2) to blue (1), with the share `r + 2t (g - r)` for `t <= 1/2`, else `g + (2t - 1)(b - g)`, and the weights `w_r = max(0, 1 - 2t)`, `w_g = 1 - |2t - 1|`, `w_b = max(0, 2t - 1)`. Sample `i` is the layer read as D-193 reads it (bilinear, wrapped with `wrap`) with the displacement times its share. Each colour channel is the sum of its weight times the sample's same channel over the sum of its weights, so at `N = 3` red, green and blue are the three samples' own; the covering is the largest sample's covering, so no colour exceeds it. When the three shares are equal, the pixel is D-193's read with the displacement times that share, and at 100 D-193's rule exactly. Expand Output grows by the larger maximum times the largest share. The amounts are shares, not distances: a draft leaves them. Each is written only when changed, keyed, or in the file already. FX-MAPCHROMA-001 to 019 pin it.

Gradient Wipe (D-194, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `completion` and `softness` in 0..100, keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; `layer`, D-189's layer setting; `fit`, `center`, `stretch` or `tile`; `invert`, `off` or `on`. `tools/gradient_wipe_reference.py` is the reference. With no map (no layer named, a missing one) or at completion 0 the output is the input exactly; at completion 100 every pixel is `(0, 0, 0, 0)`. Bounds never grow. Otherwise, with `O` the input, linear and premultiplied, its drawing's own top-left pixel at `(ox, oy)` however far earlier effects grew it, and `M` D-189's map fitted to the drawing's own `W` by `H` (the composition's for an adjustment layer), pixel `(x, y)` reads `M(x - ox, y - oy)`, transparent outside it, and `v` is its picture luma, the sRGB encoding of `clamp(0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b, 0, 1)`, as Compound Blur reads a map; with `invert` `on`, `v` is `1 - v`. With `c = completion / 100`, `s = softness / 100` and `E = -s / 2 + c (1 + s)`: `k = clamp((v - E) / s + 0.5, 0, 1)` when `s > 0`, else `k = 1` when `v >= E` and 0 otherwise; the output is `p k`, all four premultiplied channels. The softness-0 comparison is a cliff, and the fixtures hold no decided pixel within `1e-5` of it. For a draft the map is made at the holder's divisor (D-189, step 3); no setting is a distance. FX-GWIPE-001 to 028 pin it.

Echo (D-195, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `echo_time` in -120..120 frames, `echoes` in 0..30, `intensity` and `decay` in 0..1, all keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame, with `t = floor(echo_time)` and `N = floor(echoes)`; `operator`, exactly `add`, `maximum`, `minimum`, `screen`, `composite_in_back`, `composite_in_front` or `blend`. `tools/echo_reference.py` is the reference. On an adjustment layer the output is the input. Otherwise, at the holder's composition frame `n`, with its drawing's own top-left pixel at `(ox, oy)` in the input however far earlier effects grew it and the drawing `W` by `H` at `n`: for `k = 0..N`, `P_k` is the holder's picture at composition frame `n + k t` by steps 1 and 2 of the layer render order alone, its effects not run (D-189's picture of a layer read by itself), empty outside the layer's in and out points, laid on the drawing corner to corner and cut to `W` by `H`, transparent outside it. `Q_k = I D^k P_k`, all four premultiplied numbers, `I = intensity`, `D = decay`, `D^0 = 1`. `R` is, number by number: `add` `min(1, sum Q_k)`; `maximum` and `minimum` the largest and smallest `Q_k`; `screen` `1 - prod (1 - clamp(Q_k, 0, 1))`; `composite_in_back` `Q_0 over Q_1 over ... over Q_N`; `composite_in_front` `Q_N over ... over Q_0`; `blend` `sum Q_k / (N + 1)`; where `a over b = a + (1 - a.alpha) b`. The output is `R` with its top-left pixel at `(ox, oy)` and `(0, 0, 0, 0)` elsewhere: what effects before it made is replaced. Bounds never grow. For a draft each `P_k` is made at the holder's divisor, as D-189's map is (step 3); no setting is a distance. FX-ECHO-001 to 030 pin it.

Posterize Time (D-196, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameter `frame_rate` in 0.1..99 frames a second, keyable and held inside its range at each frame. `tools/posterize_time_reference.py` is the reference. On an adjustment layer it changes nothing. Otherwise, at composition frame `n`, in a composition of `F` frames a second (numerator over denominator) starting at frame `S`, on a layer whose in point is `I`: `h = n`, and for each switched-on, valid Posterize Time of the layer's stack in order, with `r` its `frame_rate` at frame `h`: if `r < F`, `s = floor((h - S) r / F + 1e-9)` and `h = S + ceil(s F / r - 1e-9)`; then `h = max(h, I)`. Whether the layer is active is decided at `n`. Steps 1 to 3 of the layer render order (the source, by the layer's own timing, its masks, and its whole effect stack, every setting, layer map and Echo copy included) are taken at `h`; step 4 on (transform, opacity, motion blur, depth) at `n`. The effect's own step in the stack is the identity. What resolving at `h` reports belongs to frame `n`. Holding twice is holding once. FX-PTIME-001 to 020 pin it.

Change to Color (D-197, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `from` and `to`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive; `change`, exactly `hue`, `hue_lightness`, `hue_saturation` or `hue_lightness_saturation`; `change_by`, exactly `setting` or `transforming`; `hue_tolerance`, `lightness_tolerance`, `saturation_tolerance` and `softness` in 0..100, keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; `view_matte`, `off` or `on`. `tools/change_to_color_reference.py` is the reference. The HLS of an encoded colour `e`: with `mx`, `mn` its largest and smallest channel, lightness `l = (mx + mn) / 2`; saturation `s = 0` when `mx == mn`, else `(mx - mn) / (1 - |2 l - 1|)`; hue `h` Leave Color's HSV hue in degrees, none when `mx == mn`. `(h_f, l_f, s_f)` and `(h_t, l_t, s_t)` are those of `from` and `to`, each channel its 8-bit value over 255. At a pixel with `a > 0`, with `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` and `(h, l, s)` its HLS: `d_h = min(|h - h_f|, 360 - |h - h_f|) / 180`, or 1 when either hue is none; `d_l = |l - l_f|`; `d_s = |s - s_f|`. With `t_x` the part's tolerance / 100 and `w = softness / 100`, the part keeps `k_x = 1` when `d_x <= t_x`, else `0` when `w = 0`, `t_x = 0` or `d_x >= t_x (1 + w)`, else `1 - (d_x - t_x) / (t_x w)`; `k = min(k_h, k_l, k_s)`. With `view_matte` `on` the output is `(srgb_to_linear(k) a, srgb_to_linear(k) a, srgb_to_linear(k) a, a)`. Otherwise a pixel with `k = 0` is its input exactly; else, reading a hue of none as 0, `(h', l', s')` starts as `(h, l, s)`; with `setting`, `h' = h_t`, and `l' = l_t` when `change` names lightness, `s' = s_t` when it names saturation; with `transforming`, `h' = (h + h_t - h_f)` taken into [0, 360), and as named `l' = clamp(l + l_t - l_f, 0, 1)`, `s' = clamp(s + s_t - s_f, 0, 1)`. Its colour `c`: `C = (1 - |2 l' - 1|) s'`, `H = h' / 60`, `X = C (1 - |H mod 2 - 1|)`, sector `i = min(floor(H), 5)` giving `(C, X, 0)`, `(X, C, 0)`, `(0, C, X)`, `(0, X, C)`, `(X, 0, C)` or `(C, 0, X)`, each plus `l' - C / 2`. The output is `(srgb_to_linear(clamp(e + k (c - e), 0, 1)) a, a)`. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. Where a part has no band (`w = 0` or `t_x = 0`) its tolerance is a cliff; the fixtures keep every distance more than 1e-5 from it, but for a hue of exactly 0 at a hue tolerance of 0. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-CTC-001 to 027 pin it.

Corner Pin (D-198, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `upper_left`, `upper_right`, `lower_left` and `lower_right`, each two numbers `[x, y]` in -400..500, keyable and each number held inside that range at each frame. `tools/corner_pin_reference.py` is the reference. `W` and `H` are the drawing's own size (the input's size less any growth of effects before it) and `o` that growth; the corners in the drawing's own space are `A = upper_left`, `B = upper_right`, `C = lower_right` and `D = lower_left`, each `(x / 100 * W, y / 100 * H)`, worked in that order. With all four at exactly [0, 0], [100, 0], [0, 100] and [100, 100] the output is the input and nothing grows. Otherwise, with `z_i` the cross product `(P_i - P_(i-1)) x (P_(i+1) - P_i)` at each corner of the ring `A, B, C, D`, unless all four are above 0 or all four below 0 the output is the input's size and clear. Otherwise, Heckbert's square-to-quad map: `Sx = A.x - B.x + C.x - D.x`, `Sy` likewise; `dx1 = B.x - C.x`, `dx2 = D.x - C.x`, `dy1 = B.y - C.y`, `dy2 = D.y - C.y`; `den = dx1 dy2 - dx2 dy1`; `g = (Sx dy2 - dx2 Sy) / den`, `h = (dx1 Sy - Sx dy1) / den`; `a = B.x - A.x + g B.x`, `b = D.x - A.x + h D.x`, `c = A.x`, `d = B.y - A.y + g B.y`, `e = D.y - A.y + h D.y`, `f = A.y`; `M = [[a, b, c], [d, e, f], [g, h, 1]]`, which carries `(u, v)` of the unit square to `((a u + b v + c) / (g u + h v + 1), (d u + e v + f) / (g u + h v + 1))`. The layer grows by `gx = max(0, ceil(max(-o.x - min x, max x - (W + o.x))))` on the left and on the right, over the four corners' `x`, and by `gy` likewise over their `y` at the top and bottom. At an output pixel whose centre is `P` in the drawing's own space, `(U, V, T) = adj(M) (P.x, P.y, 1)`, `adj(M)` the adjugate, so that `adj(M) M = det(M) I`: the pixel is clear unless `T det(M) > 0`, the side of the horizon the drawing lies on; otherwise it is the bilinear sample of the whole input buffer, an earlier effect's growth and all, at `(U / T * W, V / T * H) + o`, transparent outside it. Nothing is scaled for a draft: the corners are in per cent. FX-PIN-001 to 018 pin it.

Light Sweep (D-199, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `center`, two numbers `[x, y]` in -1000..1000; `direction` in -3600..3600; `width` in 0..10000; `sweep_intensity` and `edge_intensity` in 0..100; `edge_thickness` in 1..50, all keyable and each number held inside its range at each frame; `shape`, `linear`, `smooth` or `sharp`; `light_color`, `#rrggbb`; and `light_reception`, `add`, `composite` or `cutout`. `tools/light_sweep_reference.py` is the reference. `W` and `H` are the drawing's own size (the input's size less any growth of effects before it) and `o` that growth; the output is the input's size. The band's centre line passes through `C = (center.x / 100 * W, center.y / 100 * H) + o` along the unit vector `direction` degrees clockwise from up, and `n` is the unit vector `direction + 90` degrees clockwise from up, both exact at quarter turns. At a pixel whose centre is `P`, `s = |n . (P - C)|` and, with `r = width / 2`, the band's strength `p` is `max(0, 1 - s / r)` (`linear`), `1 - t^2 (3 - 2 t)` with `t = s / r` when `t < 1` and else 0 (`smooth`), or `clamp(r + 0.5 - s, 0, 1)` (`sharp`); `p` is 0 everywhere when `r` is 0. With `a` the pixel's covering and `k = floor(edge_thickness)`, the edge `e` is `a` less the least covering in the `(2k + 1)` by `(2k + 1)` square centred on the pixel, the outside of the input counting as uncovered. At a pixel with `a > 0` the light is `L = p (sweep_intensity / 100 + edge_intensity / 100 * e / a)`, and with `K` the light colour's linear value, `O` the premultiplied pixel and `m = min(L, 1)`: `add` gives `(O.rgb + L a K, a)`, `composite` gives `(O.rgb + m (a K - O.rgb), a)`, and `cutout` gives `(m a K, m a)`. A pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is; a pixel whose `L` is 0 is left exactly as it is, except under `cutout`, where it is cleared. A draft scales `width` and `edge_thickness` by its factor, the thickness held at 1 or more. FX-SWEEP-001 to 026 pin it.

Radio Waves (D-200, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `producer_point`, two numbers `[x, y]` in -1000..1000; `sides` in 3..64, floored; `interval` and `lifespan` in 1..1000; `expansion`, `velocity`, `fade_in_time`, `fade_out_time`, `start_width` and `end_width` in 0..1000; `orientation` and `direction` in -3600..3600; `spin` in -360..360; and `opacity` in 0..100, all keyable and each number held inside its range at each frame; `profile`, `square`, `triangle` or `sine`; and `color`, `#rrggbb`. `tools/radio_waves_reference.py` is the reference. `W` and `H` are the drawing's own size (the input's size less any growth of effects before it) and `o` that growth; the output is the input's size. The producer is `P = (producer_point.x / 100 * W, producer_point.y / 100 * H) + o`. With `f` the composition frame, a wave is born at each frame `k * interval`, `k = 0, 1, 2, ...`, and is alive at `f` when its age `t = f - k * interval` satisfies `0 <= t < lifespan`. A wave of age `t` has its centre at `C = P + velocity * t * u`, `u` the unit vector `direction` degrees clockwise from up, exact at quarter turns; its corners at `R = expansion * t` from `C`, the first at `theta = orientation + spin * t` degrees clockwise from up and the rest every `a = 360 / n` degrees, `n = floor(sides)`; its width `w = start_width + (end_width - start_width) * t / lifespan`; and its strength `g = opacity / 100 * min(1, t / fade_in_time) * min(1, (lifespan - t) / fade_out_time)`, a fade's factor 1 when its time is 0. At a pixel whose centre is `X`, with `q = X - C`, `phi = atan2(q.x, -q.y)` in degrees and `delta = phi - theta - a * (floor((phi - theta) / a) + 0.5)`, the pixel's distance from the wave's outline is `s = | |q| cos(delta) - R cos(180 / n degrees) |`, its distance from the edge it faces, so corners are mitred. With `h = w / 2`, the profile is `clamp(min(s + 0.5, h) - max(s - 0.5, -h), 0, 1)` (`square`), `max(0, 1 - s / h)` (`triangle`), or `cos(90 s / h degrees)` when `s < h` and else 0 (`sine`), and 0 everywhere when `w` is 0. With `T` the product over the waves alive of `1 - g * profile`, `K` the colour's linear value and `O` the premultiplied pixel, the output is `T O + (1 - T) (K, 1)`; a pixel with `T = 1` is left exactly as it is. Waves are clipped to the input's rectangle. A draft scales `expansion`, `velocity`, `start_width` and `end_width` by its factor. FX-RWAVE-001 to 027 pin it.

Polar Coordinates (D-201, accepted on 2026-09-28): parameters `interpolation` in 0..100, keyable and held inside that range at each frame, and `conversion`, `rect_to_polar` or `polar_to_rect`. `tools/polar_coordinates_reference.py` is the reference. `W` and `H` are the drawing's own size (the input's size less any growth of effects before it) and `o` that growth; the output is the input's size. At an output pixel whose centre is `P` in the drawing's own space, `rect_to_polar` reads the drawing at `S = (phi / 360 * W, r * H)`, where `n = ((P.x - W / 2) / (W / 2), (P.y - H / 2) / (H / 2))`, `r = |n|` and `phi = atan2(n.x, -n.y)` in degrees, plus 360 when below 0; and `polar_to_rect` reads it at `S = (W / 2 * (1 + v sin a), H / 2 * (1 - v cos a))`, where `v = P.y / H` and `a = 360 * P.x / W` degrees, the inverse. With `k = interpolation / 100` and `Q = P + k (S - P)`, the output is the bilinear sample of the whole input buffer, an earlier effect's growth and all, at `Q + o`, transparent outside it; except that in `rect_to_polar` each of the sample's four taps whose column in the drawing's own space is `c` reads column `c mod W`, the remainder from 0 to `W - 1`, its row as it is, so that the drawing's left and right edges join straight up from the middle. With `k = 0` the output is the input exactly. Nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-POLAR-001 to 015 pin it.

D-320 (P-26, 2026-10-04) adds `shape`, `ellipse` (absent in a file) or `circle`. With half-axes `(rx, ry) = (W / 2, H / 2)` for the ellipse and `(min(W, H) / 2, min(W, H) / 2)` for the circle, `rect_to_polar` takes `n = ((P.x - W / 2) / rx, (P.y - H / 2) / ry)` and `polar_to_rect` reads `S = (W / 2 + rx v sin a, H / 2 - ry v cos a)`; everything else is as above, so the ellipse is D-201's rule exactly. A circle on a wide drawing leaves its ends, beyond the circle, transparent in `rect_to_polar`. `tools/polar_circle_reference.py` is the reference; FX-POLAR-016 to 022 pin it.

Mix, on every effect (D-202, accepted on 2026-09-28): each effect instance's `mix`, 0..100, 100 when absent, keyable and held inside that range at each frame. `tools/effect_mix_reference.py` is the reference. With `m = mix / 100`, where a switched-on, valid effect in a stack takes the buffer `before` and gives `after`, the next effect takes `out = before + m (after - before)` at every sample of `after`, all four premultiplied channels, where `before` is placed where it was: when the effect grew the buffer by `(dx, dy)` on each side, `before`'s pixel `(x, y)` is at `after`'s `(x + dx, y + dy)`, and `before` is transparent outside its own rectangle. At `m = 1` the output is `after` exactly and nothing is kept for the mix; at `m = 0` it is `before`, exactly, placed. This holds wherever a stack runs: a layer's own, an adjustment layer's on the frame beneath (D-66), and an effect's other layer or an Echo's copies. A Light Wrap (D-132) is mixed the same way where it runs, `before` the placed layer and `after` the same with the light laid on, before the layer's opacity and blend mode. Posterize Time's step changes nothing and it has no mix: one other than 100 bypasses it. Nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-MIX-001 to 017 pin it.

Median and Smart Blur (D-203, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/median_smart_blur_reference.py` is the reference. Both use the disc of offsets `(dx, dy)` with `dx^2 + dy^2 <= radius^2`, the pixel's own among them; a tap outside the input buffer is transparent; when the disc holds only `(0, 0)`, radius below 1, the output is the input exactly. A tap shows when its covering is above 0. The output is the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft scales `radius` by its factor. Median (`core.median`): parameters `radius` in 0..10, keyable and held inside that range at each frame, and `operate_on_alpha`, `off` or `on`. The median of `n` values sorted is the middle one when `n` is odd and the mean of the middle two when even. An output pixel's covering `a` is its own under `off` and the median of every tap's covering, transparent taps too, under `on`; with `a = 0` the output is `(0, 0, 0, 0)`, and otherwise each colour channel is `a` times the median of that straight linear channel (premultiplied value over covering) over the taps that show. Smart Blur (`core.smart_blur`): parameters `radius` in 0..10 and `threshold` in 0..255, both keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. A pixel that shows has four levels, D-88's: each straight channel through the sRGB curve, and its covering, each clamped to 0..1 and rounded to 8 bits as `floor(v * 255 + 0.5)`. A pixel that does not show stays `(0, 0, 0, 0)`; a pixel that shows becomes the mean of the premultiplied taps inside the buffer that show and whose four levels each differ from its own by at most `threshold`, itself always among them. FX-MEDIAN-001 to 011 and FX-SMART-001 to 012 pin them.

Bilateral Blur (D-358, 2026-10-08), after After Effects' Bilateral Blur, whose exact weights Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/bilateral_blur_reference.py` is the reference. `core.bilateral_blur`: parameters `radius` in 0..50 and `threshold` in 0..255, both keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame, and `colorize`, `on` or `off`, kept as written so a wrong word is reported. The taps are Median's disc of `radius`; a tap outside the input buffer, or one whose covering is 0, is left out. A pixel whose covering is 0 stays `(0, 0, 0, 0)`; every other pixel keeps its own covering `a`, and the output is the input's size: no bounds expansion. A pixel's straight linear colour is its premultiplied value over `a`; a value's level is `E(v) = 255 * to_srgb(v)` with the sRGB curve unclamped. With s = `radius` / 2 and T = `threshold`, a tap at `(dx, dy)` with covering `a_q` weighs `exp(-(dx^2 + dy^2) / (2 s^2)) * a_q * exp(-(E(v_q) - E(v_p))^2 / (2 T^2))`, v the value worked. With `colorize` on each of red, green and blue is worked alone, its own weights from its own levels, and becomes the weighted mean of the taps' straight values of it. With `colorize` off the value worked is the luminance `Y = 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B` of the straight linear colour, and red, green and blue each become the weighted mean of the taps' Y: grey. The result is premultiplied by `a`. When the disc holds only `(0, 0)` (radius below 1) or `threshold` is 0, each pixel counts only itself: under `on` the output is the input exactly, under `off` its luminance, grey. A draft scales `radius` by its factor and never `threshold`. FX-BILAT-001 to 017 pin it.

Snowfall (D-204, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/snowfall_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `color`, `#rrggbb`, either case; `density` in 0..100; `spacing` in 2..1000; `size` in 0..100; `depth` in 0..100; `speed` in 0..1000; `wind` in -1000..1000; `wiggle` in 0..100; `period` in 1..1000; `seed` in 0..100000, floored after it is held; `opacity` in 0..100; all but `color` keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; and a hidden `frame`, the composition frame `f`. As Rain's, the field lies on the drawing's own coordinates, `(X, Y) = (x - o_x + 0.5, y - o_y + 0.5)`, and `U(seed, i, j, l, ch)` is Noise's hash (D-119) with the plane `l` in the frame's place. For planes `l = 0, 1, 2`, the scale `z = 1 - depth / 100 * l / 3`, `s = spacing z`, `rmax = size z / 2`, `wz = wiggle z`, and the plane's field point is `(A, B) = (X - wind z f, Y - speed z f)`. Cell `(i, j)` holds a flake when `(U(seed, i, j, l, 0) + 1) / 2 < density / 100`; with `u_k = (U(seed, i, j, l, k) + 1) / 2`, its centre is `(s (i + u_1) + wz sin(2 pi (f / period + u_4)), s (j + u_2))` and its radius `r = rmax (0.5 + 0.5 u_3)`. With `d` the distance from `(A, B)` to the centre, the flake's light is `clamp(r + 0.5 - d, 0, 1) min(1, 2 r)`; `q` is the largest light of any flake of any plane, 0 where none reaches (only cells `i` in `floor((A - rmax - 0.5 - wz) / s) ..= floor((A + rmax + 0.5 + wz) / s)` and `j` in `floor((B - rmax - 0.5) / s) ..= floor((B + rmax + 0.5) / s)` can). A pixel with covering 0 stays as it is. With `b` its straight linear colour, `a` its covering, `G` the colour's linear value and `op = q opacity / 100`, the output is `((b + op (G - b)) a, a)`. The output is the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft scales `spacing`, `size`, `speed`, `wind` and `wiggle` by its factor. FX-SNOW-001 to 029 pin it; the reference checks no reached cell is within 1e-5 of the density's cliff.

Kaleidoscope (D-205, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/kaleidoscope_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `segments` in 2..32, floored after it is held; `mode`, `mirror` or `repeat`; `rotation` in -3600..3600 degrees; `size` in 10..1000; `center`, two numbers in -1000..1000; all but `mode` keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. `W` and `H` are the drawing's own size and `o` its corner in the input buffer, which an earlier effect may have grown; the output is the input's size. The centre is `C = (center_x / 100 W, center_y / 100 H)`, `n = floor(segments)` and `w = 2 pi / n`. At an output pixel whose centre, in the drawing's own space, is `P`, `d = P - C`, `r = |d|` and `theta = atan2(d_x, -d_y)`, clockwise from straight up; `theta' = theta - rotation` in radians, `k = floor(theta' / w)` and `t = theta' - k w`, and under `mirror` `t = w - t` when `k` is odd. With `phi = rotation + t` and `S = C + r 100 / size (sin phi, -cos phi) + o`, each coordinate of `S` is folded into the input buffer by mirroring at its edges (across a side `m` long, `u mod 2m`, or `2m` less that when it is past `m`) and held inside the buffer's pixel centres, `[0.5, m - 0.5]`; the output is the bilinear sample there. No bounds expansion; nothing is scaled in a draft. FX-KALEIDO-001 to 020 pin it; the reference checks no pixel of a Repeat case is within 1e-6 of a wedge's edge.

Roughen Edges (D-206, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/roughen_edges_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `edge_type`, `roughen` or `roughen_color`; `edge_color`, `#rrggbb`; `border` in 0..500; `size` in 1..1000; `complexity` in 1..10 and `seed` in 0..100000, floored after they are held; `evolution` in -100000..100000; `speed` in -360..360; all but the two words keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. The output is the input's size. At an input pixel whose centre is `P` in the drawing's own space (the input buffer's pixel centre less its corner `o`), `F` is D-128's field, channel 0, `floor(complexity)` octaves and the seed floored, at `(P / size, (evolution + speed frame) / 360)`, and the depth is `e = border clamp(0.5 + F, 0, 1)`. With `a(Q)` the covering of the bilinear sample of the input at `Q`, transparent outside the input, `m = min(a(P), a(P +- (e, 0)), a(P +- (0, e)))`. A pixel whose covering is 0 is left as it is. Under `roughen` the output is the input pixel times `m / a(P)`; under `roughen_color`, with `m2` the same least covering at `2e`, held to at most `m`, and `t = 1 - m2 / m` (0 when `m` is 0), the output colour is `(1 - t)` times the input's colour times `m / a(P)` plus `t` times the edge colour's linear value times `m`, and its covering `m`. No bounds expansion; `border` and `size` are scaled in a draft, `size` held at 1. FX-ROUGH-001 to 024 pin it.

Beam (D-207, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/beam_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `start` and `end`, each two numbers in -1000..1000; `length`, `time` and `softness` in 0..100; `start_thickness` and `end_thickness` in 0..500; `inside_color` and `outside_color`, `#rrggbb`; `composite`, `on` or `off`; all but the three words keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. The output is the input's size. `S` and `E` are the two points in per cent of the drawing's width and height, in the drawing's own space, its top-left corner (0, 0). With `l = length / 100` and `a = time / 100 (1 - l)`, the lit stretch runs from `A = S + a (E - S)` to `B = S + (a + l) (E - S)`. At each output pixel's centre `X` in the drawing's own space, `t = clamp((X - A) . (B - A) / |B - A|^2, 0, 1)` (0 when `A = B`), `d = |X - A - t (B - A)|`, `u = a + t l`, and `r = (start_thickness + u (end_thickness - start_thickness)) / 2`. With `w = max(1, 2 r softness / 100)`, the covering is `c = clamp((min(d + w / 2, r) - max(d - w / 2, -r)) / w, 0, 1)`, and the colour is `L = (1 - q) inside + q outside`, both linear, with `q = clamp(d / r, 0, 1)` (1 when `r = 0`). Under `on` the output is the input times `1 - c` plus `(L c, c)`; under `off` it is `(L c, c)`. No bounds expansion; the two thicknesses are scaled in a draft. FX-BEAM-001 to 029 pin it.

4-Color Gradient (D-208, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/four_color_gradient_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `point_1` to `point_4`, each two numbers in -1000..1000; `color_1` to `color_4`, `#rrggbb`; `blend` in 1..1000; `opacity` in 0..100; `blending_mode`, `normal`, `multiply`, `screen` or `add`; the points, blend and opacity keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. The output is the input's size. `P_k` are the points in per cent of the drawing's width and height, in the drawing's own space, its top-left corner (0, 0), and `c_k` the colours, each channel its 8-bit value / 255. Opacity 0 leaves the input exactly as it is. Otherwise, at each pixel with covering `a > 0`, `X` its centre in the drawing's own space and `d_k = |X - P_k|`: when some `d_k` is 0, `w_k` is 1 where `d_k` is 0 and 0 elsewhere; otherwise, with `m` the least `d_k` and `e = 200 / blend`, `w_k = (m / d_k)^e`. `G = srgb_to_linear(sum(w_k c_k) / sum(w_k))`, each channel. With `o = opacity / 100`, `b` the pixel's straight linear colour and `f = G`, `b G`, `1 - (1 - b)(1 - G)` or `b + G` by the mode, the output is `((b + o (f - b)) a, a)`; a pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is. No bounds expansion; nothing is scaled in a draft. FX-4CG-001 to 026 pin it.

Cell Pattern (D-209, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/cell_pattern_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `pattern`, `bubbles`, `crystals`, `plates` or `static_plates`; `invert`, `off` or `on`; `contrast` in 0..1000; `disperse` in 0..1.5; `size` in 1..1000; `evolution` in -100000..100000 degrees; `seed` in 0..100000, its whole part counted; `dark_color` and `light_color`, `#rrggbb`; `opacity` in 0..100; `blend`, `normal`, `multiply`, `screen` or `add`; the six numbers keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. The output is the input's size. Opacity 0 leaves the input exactly as it is. Pixel `(X, Y)` of the drawing's own space, its top-left corner (0, 0), is at `p = ((X + 0.5) / size, (Y + 0.5) / size)`. Cell `(m, n)` holds one point: with `U_c = U(seed, m, n, 0, c)` Noise's number (D-119), `r = disperse / 2 * (U_0 + 1) / 2`, `phi = pi U_1`, `s = 1` when `U_2 >= 0` and -1 otherwise, `t` the evolution in radians, the point is `(m + 0.5 + r cos(phi + s t), n + 0.5 + r sin(phi + s t))` and the cell's grey `g = (U_3 + 1) / 2`. The nearest and second nearest points to `p`, at `F1` and `F2`, are sought among the cells `m` in `floor(p.x) - 2 .. floor(p.x) + 2` and `n` likewise, `n` outer and `m` inner, each ascending, by squared distance, a point taking the lead only when strictly nearer; `g` is the nearest's. `k = 2 F1 / (F1 + F2)`, or 0 when `F1 + F2 = 0`. `v` is `sqrt(1 - k^2)` (bubbles), `1 - k` (crystals), `min(1, 4 (1 - k))` (plates) or `g` (static_plates); `1 - v` when invert is on; then `v = clamp(0.5 + (v - 0.5) contrast / 100, 0, 1)`. At each pixel with covering `a > 0`, `G = srgb_to_linear(dark + v (light - dark))` per channel, the colours' 8-bit values / 255, and with `op = opacity / 100`, `b` the straight linear colour and `f = G`, `b G`, `1 - (1 - b)(1 - G)` or `b + G` by the blend, the output is `((b + op (f - b)) a, a)`; a pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is. No bounds expansion. A draft scales `size` by the draft's scale, held at 1. FX-CELL-001 to 033 pin it.

Optics Compensation (D-210, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/optics_compensation_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `field_of_view` in 0..180 degrees; `reverse`, `off` or `on`; `orientation`, `horizontal`, `vertical` or `diagonal`; `center`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each in -1000..1000; `field_of_view` and `center` keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. The output is the input's size. Field of view 0 leaves the input exactly as it is. With `theta` half the field of view in radians, `R` half the drawing's own width, height or diagonal `hypot(width, height)` by the orientation, and `c` the centre in the drawing's own space as Bulge's (D-152), a pixel whose centre `P` is at `d = |P - c|` takes, when `d = 0`, the sample at `P`; otherwise, with `a = d / R * theta`: when reverse is `off`, it is empty (0, 0, 0, 0) if `a >= pi / 2` and otherwise `s = R tan(a) / theta`; when reverse is `on`, `s = R atan(a) / theta`; and the pixel takes document 21's bilinear sample of the input at `c + (s / d)(P - c)`, transparent outside it. No bounds expansion. A draft changes no setting. FX-OPTICS-001 to 020 pin it.

Bend It (D-377, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Bend It, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/bend_it_reference.py` is the reference. `core.bend_it`: parameters `bend` in -360..360 degrees (45); `start` and `end`, points in per cent of the drawing's own width and height as Radial Blur's centre, each -1000..1000 ((50, 100) and (50, 0): the bar standing up the drawing's full height); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `render_prestart`, exactly `none`, `static`, `bend` or `mirror` (none); `distort`, exactly `legal` or `extended` (legal); all written. With S and E the points, `L = |E - S|`, `t = (E - S) / L` and `n` t turned 90 degrees clockwise on the screen (y down), a point of the drawing is (u, v), u along t from S and v along n. Each output pixel's centre P gathers every place of the drawing the bend lays on it and lays them one over another, the place with the largest u on top, each this document's bilinear sample of the drawing, transparent outside it. With `theta = bend pi / 180`, `R = L / theta` (signed) and the circle's centre `C = S + R n`, the bar's points 0 <= u <= L go to `C + (v - R) n(phi)`, `phi = u theta / L`, where `t(phi) = t cos phi + n sin phi` and `n(phi) = n cos phi - t sin phi`; worked back from P, with `q = P - C` and `phi+ = atan2(-q.t, q.n)`, the places on P are `phi = phi+ + j pi` for every whole j, `v = R + (-1)^j |q|`, `u = phi L / theta`. Before S (u < 0): `none` draws nothing; `static` draws the drawing unbent, P itself, wherever `(P - S).t < 0`; `bend` carries the same bend on back for L more and lays what lies past -L straight along the tangent there (`P = C(-L) + a t(-theta) + b n(-theta)` with a < 0 reads (-L + a, b)); `mirror` bends the bar's own stretch back from -L to 0, reading (-u, v), and draws nothing past -L. Past E (u > L): `legal` draws nothing; `extended` lays it straight along the tangent at E (`P = C(L) + a t(theta) + b n(theta)` with a > 0 reads (L + a, b)). Bend 0 is the straight bar, Render Prestart and Distort still cutting; S = E is no bar and leaves the drawing exactly as it is. The layer does not grow, so what the bend carries past its edges is cut. A draft changes no setting. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the shape of the bend, the order of overlapping sheets and the meaning of each Render Prestart choice are this program's reading of its sentences. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 12). FX-BENDIT-001 to 023 pin it.

Bender (D-378, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Bender, whose manual says what each style does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/bender_reference.py` is the reference. `core.bender`: parameters `amount` in -1000..1000 (20); `top` and `base`, points in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((50, 0) and (50, 100): the axis standing up the drawing's full height, the base below); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `style`, exactly `bend`, `marilyn`, `sharp` or `boxer` (bend); `adjust_to_distance`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); all written. With Bs and T the points, `L = |T - Bs|`, `t = (T - Bs) / L` and `n` t turned 90 degrees clockwise on the screen (y down), a pixel's centre P is `s = (P - Bs).t / L` along the axis and takes this document's bilinear sample of the drawing at `P - d(s) n`, transparent outside it. A is `amount` pixels, or with `adjust_to_distance` on `amount` per cent of L. `bend`: d is 0 for s < 0, `A s^2` for 0 <= s <= 1, `A (2 s - 1)` past the Top; `marilyn`: `A sin^2(pi s)` for 0 <= s <= 1, else 0; `sharp`: `A (1 - |2 s - 1|)` for 0 <= s <= 1, else 0; `boxer`: 0 for s < 0, `A (3 s^2 - 2 s^3)` for 0 <= s <= 1, A past the Top. Every d meets 0 or its straight part without a step. Amount 0, and Top = Base, which is no axis, leave the drawing exactly as it is. The layer does not grow: what is pushed past its edges is cut. A draft scales `amount` unless `adjust_to_distance` is on. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the four shapes are this program's reading of its sentences; its Blend w. Original is the Mix every effect has (D-202). Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 13). FX-BENDER-001 to 023 pin it.

Blobbylize (D-379, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Blobbylize, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/blobbylize_reference.py` is the reference. `core.blobbylize`: parameters `layer` and `fit`, D-189's layer setting (the Blob Layer, `""` the holder itself; `stretch`, `center` or `tile`, stretch); `property`, exactly `red`, `green`, `blue`, `alpha`, `luminance` or `lightness` (alpha); `softness` 0..100 pixels (10); `cut_away` 0..100 (0); `light_intensity` 0..400 (100); `light_color`, `#rrggbb` (#ffffff); `light_type`, exactly `distant` or `point` (distant); `light_height` -1000..1000 (100); `light_position`, per cent of the drawing's width and height, each -1000..1000 (30, 30); `light_direction` -3600..3600 degrees (-45); `ambient` (25), `diffuse` (75), `specular` (50) and `metal` (100), each 0..100; `roughness` 0.001..1 (0.05); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all written. At the holder's frame, with its input O linear and premultiplied: the map is D-189's, fitted to the drawing's own W by H; with the name `""`, or a name that is no layer of the composition (with `EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING`, as CC Vector Blur and Moment Map take it), it is O itself. F, the blob's height, is the map's `property` as CC Vector Blur reads it, times the covering, or the covering itself for `alpha`. Bv is F through this document's Gaussian at sigma `softness / 2`, nothing outside the drawing, worked out past its edges as far as the kernel reaches (F itself at softness 0); Ob is O through the same Gaussian, all four channels. The colour: `K = O + (1 - O.a) Ob` and `C = K.rgb / K.a`, straight (0 where K.a is 0). The covering: with `c = cut_away / 100`, `a = clamp((Bv - c) / (1 - c), 0, 1)`, 0 at c = 1. The surface normal N is `(-k gx, -k gy, 1)` made one long, g Bv's central differences and `k = 1.25 max(softness, 1)`, as CC Glass slopes its bump at height 100. The light Lv, made one long, is `(100 ux, 100 uy, light_height)` for `distant`, (ux, uy) this document's step at `light_direction` degrees clockwise from up, or for `point` `(Lx - px, Ly - py, light_height)` from the pixel's centre to the light at `light_position`; none at no length. With `nl = N.Lv`, the reflection `R = 2 nl N - Lv`, the highlight `Rz^(1 / roughness)` where nl and Rz are above 0 (else 0), `I = light_intensity / 100` and Lc the light colour in linear light, each channel is `C (ambient + diffuse I Lc max(nl, 0)) + specular I (Lc + (C - Lc) metal) highlight`, ambient, diffuse, specular and metal as shares of 100; the output is that times a, with covering a. The layer does not grow. A draft scales `softness` and a point light's height. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the blob, the cut and the shading are this program's; only the effect's own distant or point light, not After Effects' light layers; Blend w. Original is the Mix every effect has (D-202). Drawn on the card: the height, two blurs, the colour and the lit surface, each its own pass. FX-BLOB-001 to 027 pin it.

Flow Motion (D-385, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Flo Motion, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/flow_motion_reference.py` is the reference. `core.flow_motion`: parameters `knot_1` and `knot_2`, points in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((25, 50) and (75, 50)); `amount_1` and `amount_2`, each -1000..1000 (10 and -10); `falloff` 0..10 (5); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `tile_edges`, exactly `off` or `on` (on); `finer_controls`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); `antialiasing`, exactly `low`, `medium` or `high` (low); all written. With w0 by h0 the drawing's own size, the knots' reach is `sigma = hypot(w0, h0) 0.01 2^(0.7 falloff)` pixels, and each knot K has the strength `a = amount / 10`, or `amount / 200` with `finer_controls` on. At a point P, a knot's weight is `g = sigma^2 / (sigma^2 + |P - K|^2)` and its scale `m = 1 + a g` for a >= 0, `1 / (1 - a g)` below, so m is above 0 for every amount; P reads the drawing at `S = P + sum (P - K)(m - 1)` over the knots with a non-zero strength, this document's bilinear sample there: with `tile_edges` on, of the buffer repeated round itself with every other copy turned over (column i reads column j = i mod 2W, or 2W - 1 - j where j >= W, and the same for rows), transparent outside it with `tile_edges` off. A positive amount reads from farther out and so draws the picture in towards its knot; a negative one reads from nearer and blows it out like a lens. A pixel is the mean of n by n such points spread evenly across it, n = 1, 2 or 4 for `low`, `medium` and `high`. Both amounts 0 leave the drawing exactly as it is. The layer does not grow. A draft changes nothing: the reach and the knots are shares of the drawing. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the pull, the reach Falloff gives and the amounts' scale are this program's; Tile Edges mirrors the whole buffer the effect is given. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 14). FX-FLOW-001 to 024 pin it.

Griddler (D-386, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Griddler, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/griddler_reference.py` is the reference. `core.griddler`: parameters `horizontal_scale` and `vertical_scale`, each -1000..1000 per cent (80 and 80); `tile_size` 0.1..100 per cent of the drawing's own width (10); `rotation` -3600..3600 degrees, clockwise on the screen (0); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `cut_tiles`, exactly `off` or `on` (on); all written. With T = `tile_size` per cent of the drawing's width w0 in pixels, the tiles are T squares measured from the drawing's top-left corner. A pixel's centre P, in the drawing's own frame, lies in the tile whose centre is `C = (floor(P / T) + 1/2) T` on each axis; with `q = P - C` turned back by `rotation` (`ux = qx cos + qy sin`, `uy = qy cos - qx sin`) and divided by the scales over 100, `u = (ux / sx, uy / sy)`, the pixel takes this document's bilinear sample of the drawing at `C + u`, transparent outside it; with `cut_tiles` on it is transparent wherever either part of u is over T / 2 in size, so each tile shows its own piece, scaled and turned, with clear gaps. Scales 100 and 100 with rotation 0 leave the drawing exactly as it is; a scale of 0 draws nothing; a negative scale turns each tile's piece over. The layer does not grow. A draft changes nothing: the tile is a share of the drawing. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the rule is this program's; with Cut Tiles off each tile is filled with the view round its own centre rather than repeated pieces; the edges are hard, not smoothed. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 15). FX-GRIDDLER-001 to 022 pin it.

Fisheye (D-387, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Lens, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/fisheye_reference.py` is the reference. `core.fisheye`: parameters `center`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `size` 0..1000 per cent of half the drawing's diagonal (50); `convergence` -100..100 (50); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all written. With R = `size` per cent of `hypot(w0, h0) / 2` pixels, a pixel's centre P lies `r = |P - center|` from the centre and is covered by `cover = clamp(R - r + 0.5, 0, 1)`; where cover is 0 it is transparent. Otherwise, with `rho = min(r / R, 1)` and `c = convergence / 100`, it reads `f(rho) = rho + c (2 asin(rho) / pi - rho)` for c >= 0, `rho - c (sin(pi rho / 2) - rho)` below, of the way out: this document's bilinear sample of the drawing at `center + (P - center) f(rho) / rho` (the centre itself at r = 0), transparent outside it, times cover. A positive convergence swells the middle out like a fish-eye, a negative one draws it in; f keeps the lens's middle and its rim in place. A size of 0 draws nothing; size 100 with convergence 0 reaches every corner and leaves the drawing exactly as it is. The layer does not grow. A draft changes nothing: the lens is a share of the drawing. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the curve is this program's; outside the lens the layer is transparent, its rim softened over one pixel. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 16). FX-FISHEYE-001 to 020 pin it.

Page Turn (D-388, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Page Turn, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/page_turn_reference.py` is the reference. `core.page_turn`: parameters `controls`, exactly `classic`, `top_left`, `top_right`, `bottom_left` or `bottom_right` (bottom_right); `fold_position`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((75, 75)); `fold_direction` and `light_direction`, -3600..3600 degrees clockwise from up (-60 and -45); `fold_radius` 0..1000 pixels (30); `back_opacity` 0..100 (100); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `render`, exactly `full`, `front` or `back` (full); `back_page`, D-189's layer setting fitted as `stretch` fits it, "" for none (""); `paper_color`, `#rrggbb` (#f0f0f0); all written. In the drawing's own space, y down: with `classic` the fold line passes through F = `fold_position` and n, the unit step across it towards the part that lifts, points opposite `fold_direction`, so a point X lies `d = (X - F) . n` past the line; with a corner, the chosen corner K is turned over so that it lies at P = `fold_position`: with `D = |K - P|` (0 leaves the layer as it is), `n = (K - P) / D` and `d = (X - P) . n - (D - pi R) / 2`. The page rolls round a cylinder of radius R = `fold_radius` lying on the line: a point s past the line on the flat page is carried, for s from 0 to pi R, to `d = R sin(s / R)`, its front up while s < pi R / 2 and its back after, and past pi R lies flat again, back up, at `d = pi R - s`. So at an output point d past the line: d > R is transparent; 0 <= d <= R shows the back at `s = R (pi - asin(d / R))` over the front at `s = R asin(d / R)`; d < 0 shows the back at `s = pi R - d` over the page itself at X. At R = 0 every d >= 0 is transparent and the back is the page mirrored in the line. The page at s is this document's bilinear sample f of the input at `X + (s - d) n`, transparent outside; its back, with m the back page at that same point (or the opaque paper colour when `back_page` is "" or names no layer, with `EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING`) and `o = back_opacity / 100`, is rgb `f.a o m.rgb + (1 - o m.a) f.rgb` covering f.a: the back page laid over the page's own picture and cut to its shape. Where the back curls (0 <= d <= R) its rgb is times `clamp(l (d / R) + sqrt(1 - (d / R)^2), 0, 1)`, l = L . n with L the unit step `light_direction` points; the flat back is unshaded. `render` `full` lays the back over the front (premultiplied over), `front` keeps only the front and `back` only the back. The layer does not grow. A draft halves the radius. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the cylinder and the shading are this program's; the back page is fixed to the paper, so a turned page shows it mirrored, as paper seen through; with a corner the turned corner lands exactly on Fold Position only once the cylinder is unrolled (D >= pi R), and before that the whole page can roll out of the frame, as FX-PAGETURN-001 does on a drawing 16 pixels wide. Drawn on the card in its own pass, `pageturn`, the back page as a second texture. FX-PAGETURN-001 to 027 pin it.

Power Pin (D-389, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Power Pin, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours, built on Corner Pin's (D-198). `tools/power_pin_reference.py` is the reference. `core.power_pin`: parameters `top_left`, `top_right`, `bottom_left` and `bottom_right`, points in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -400..500 ((0, 0), (100, 0), (0, 100), (100, 100)); `perspective` 0..100 (100); `expansion_top`, `expansion_left`, `expansion_right` and `expansion_bottom`, each -40..100 (0); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `unstretch`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); all written. With the pins where they start and every expansion 0 the output is the input and nothing grows. Unless the ring A = top left, B = top right, C = bottom right, D = bottom left is convex (Corner Pin's test) the output is clear. With p = `perspective` / 100, M Heckbert's square-to-quad map onto the ring (Corner Pin's) and Bl the bilinear one, `Bl(u, v) = A + u (B - A) + v (D - A) + u v (A - B + C - D)`: the expanded square's corners (-l, -t), (1 + r, -t), (1 + r, 1 + b), (-l, 1 + b), with l, t, r, b the expansions over 100, are each carried to `p M(u, v) + (1 - p) Bl(u, v)`, the target ring Q; a corner not expanded is its pin exactly. When p > 0 a corner M carries to or past its horizon (M's third row at most 0) leaves the output clear, and so does a Q that is not convex. Unstretch `off`: the layer grows as Corner Pin's does, so Q's corners are inside it; each output pixel centre X is taken back to the square by the inverse of Q's square-to-quad map, `(U, V, T) = adj(M_Q) X`, which needs `T det(M_Q) > 0`, and by the inverse of Q's bilinear map, which needs a real root; at p = 1 only the first is used, at p = 0 only the second, between them p times the first plus 1 - p times the second; missing one, the pixel is transparent, otherwise this document's bilinear sample of the input at (u w0, v h0). Unstretch `on`: nothing grows; each pixel centre is the square's (u, v) = (X_x / w0, X_y / h0) carried to `p M_Q(u, v) + (1 - p) Bl_Q(u, v)` (M_Q's third row above 0 when p > 0) and sampled there, Q's shape stretched out to fill the drawing. A draft changes nothing: every setting is a share of the drawing. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the blend between perspective and the even squeeze and the reading of the expansions (each side moved out by its share of the pinned square, carried by the same map) are this program's; there are no pin handles on the viewer yet, the pins are set in Effect Controls. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 17). FX-POWERPIN-001 to 025 pin it.

Ripple Pulse (D-390, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Ripple Pulse, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/ripple_pulse_reference.py` is the reference. `core.ripple_pulse`: parameters `center`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `pulse_level` -1000..1000 (0); `time_span` 0..10 seconds (1); `amplitude` 0..1000 (10); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `render_bump_map`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); all written. At composition frame f, with n = `floor(time_span fps + 0.5)` frames of history, L_j (j = 0 to n) is `pulse_level` as it is at frame f - j, read through the layer's time stretch as its keys are, its expression run and held to its range (before the first key, the first key's value). The change `g_j = L_j - L_(j+1)` made j frames ago has run out to `u = j + 1/2`, where `u = n r / R` measures the distance r from the centre C in frames of travel and R is half the drawing's diagonal, so a ring reaches the corners after the time span; D(u) is g joined by straight lines between those places, g_0 inside u = 1/2 and 0 from u = n + 1/2 out (g_n = 0); with n = 0 there are no rings. A pixel's centre P, r = |P - C| > 0, takes this document's bilinear sample of the drawing at `P - s (P - C) / r`, `s = amplitude / 10 D(u)`, transparent outside: a rise in the level pushes the picture outward, a fall draws it in; P = C reads itself. The layer does not grow. `render_bump_map` `on` draws the rings' heights instead, opaque grey everywhere, `clamp(1/2 + amplitude (z(u) - L_n) / 2000, 0, 1)` in every channel, z(u) the level L joined by straight lines with L_j at u = j, held to L_0 inside and L_n outside. A draft halves the amplitude, so the bump map's slopes are halved with it. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the rings' shape and the amplitude's scale are this program's; the level's history is read frame by frame, so a ring keyed between frames moves in whole-frame steps. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 18), the levels read on the processor. FX-RPULSE-001 to 019 pin it.

Slant (D-391, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Slant, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/slant_reference.py` is the reference. `core.slant`: parameters `slant`, degrees -80..80 (0); `height`, per cent 0..1000 (100); `floor`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((50, 100)), of which only the height counts; every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `stretching` and `set_color`, exactly `off` or `on` (off, off); `color`, `#rrggbb` (#000000), read in lower case; all written. With F the floor's height in the buffer, theta the slant and s = `height` / 100, times cos theta when `stretching` is `off` (tipped over, so the leant edges keep their length): each output pixel centre (x, y), with v = y - F, is this document's bilinear sample of the input at (x + v tan theta, F + v / s), transparent outside it; plus leans the top to the right. s = 0 leaves the output clear. With `set_color` `on`, the sample's colour is replaced by the colour in linear light times the sample's alpha, its alpha kept. Slant 0 at height 100 without a set colour is the input. Nothing grows: what leans past the drawing's edge is cut off. A draft changes nothing: every setting is an angle or a share of the drawing. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the tipping by cos theta without Stretching and the level floor line are this program's reading; there is no floor handle on the viewer yet, the floor is set in Effect Controls. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 19). FX-SLANT-001 to 024 pin it.

Smear (D-392, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Smear, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/smear_reference.py` is the reference. `core.smear`: parameters `from` and `to`, points in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((40, 50), (60, 50)); `reach`, per cent -1000..1000 (100); `radius`, pixels 0..1000 (70); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all written. With F and T the points in the buffer and the drag V = (T - F) `reach` / 100: each output pixel centre P has s = clamp((P - F) . V / |V|^2, 0, 1), how far along the drag its nearest point lies, and rho = |P - F - s V|, how far it is from that point; inside the radius, w = (1 - (rho / R)^2)^2, and P is this document's bilinear sample of the input at P - w s V, transparent outside it; at or past the radius it is the input's own pixel. Radius 0, Reach 0 or From on To is the input. Nothing grows. A draft scales the radius with the drawing; the points are shares. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the drag that grows along the line from From to To and the (1 - (rho / R)^2)^2 fall-off are this program's; there are no From and To handles on the viewer yet. Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 20). FX-SMEAR-001 to 020 pin it.

Split (D-393, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Split, whose manual says what each control does in a sentence and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/split_reference.py` is the reference. `core.split`: parameters `point_a` and `point_b`, points in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each -1000..1000 ((25, 50), (75, 50)); `split`, pixels 0..1000 (50); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all written. With A and B the points in the buffer, L = |B - A| and u the unit step from A to B: each output pixel centre P, t = (P - A) . u / L of the way along and d = the cross product of u with P - A to the side, has the gap's half width g = `split` / 2 sin(pi t) for t in 0..1 and 0 past the points, and D = g + L / 2. With |d| at least D it reads the input at P; with g <= |d| < D it reads the input at A + t L u + sign(d) (|d| - g) D / (D - g) n, n the side direction, so each side is squeezed outward and meets the input again at D; inside the gap it reads the line itself. The sample, this document's bilinear one, is then multiplied by the share of the pixel's width across the line outside the gap, 1 - max(0, min(|d| + 0.5, g) - max(|d| - 0.5, -g)), so the gap's edges are soft. Split 0 or A on B is the input. Nothing grows. A draft scales the split with the drawing; the points are shares. **Deviations:** CycoreFX's manual gives no formula, so the sine-shaped gap and the squeeze reaching half the line's length to each side are this program's; there are no point handles on the viewer yet. CC Split 2 (each side's split set apart) is Split 2 below, a second name over this rule (D-394). Drawn on the card in the warp pass (mode 21). FX-SPLIT-001 to 017 pin it.

Split 2 (D-394, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Split 2, the owner's choice "Second name, one engine (Recommended)": Split's rule with each side's own amount. `tools/split2_reference.py` is the reference. `core.split_2`: parameters `point_a` and `point_b` as Split's ((25, 50), (75, 50)); `split_1` and `split_2`, pixels 0..1000 (50 each); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all written. A, B, L, u, t and d are Split's. With w = sin(pi t) for t in 0..1 and 0 past the points, g1 = `split_1` / 2 w opens the side d < 0, the one on the left as you walk from A to B on the screen, and g2 = `split_2` / 2 w the side d >= 0, on the right. A pixel uses g = g2 when d >= 0 and g1 otherwise, then Split's D = g + L / 2, its squeeze with |d| and its sample; the coverage is 1 - max(0, min(d + 0.5, g2) - max(d - 0.5, -g1)). Equal amounts are Split's rule exactly, and Split is drawn by this rule with its one amount on both sides, so its pictures are unchanged bit for bit. Both amounts 0 or A on B is the input. Nothing grows. A draft scales both amounts. **Deviations:** CycoreFX names the sides only as Split 1 and Split 2, so which side is which (left of the walk from A to B for Split 1) is this program's; there are no point handles on the viewer yet. Drawn on the card in Split's warp pass (mode 21), the left amount in its own slot. FX-SPLIT2-001 to 019 pin it.

Tiles (D-404, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Tiler, whose manual says what each control does and gives no formula, so this rule is ours: Motion Tile's sized tile (D-304) with the output kept at the layer's size. `tools/tiles_reference.py` is the reference. `core.tiles`: `scale`, per cent 1..100 (50); `center`, two numbers, per cent of the width and height of the buffer as it reaches the effect, -1000..1000 (50, 50); `blend`, per cent 0..100 (0); every number keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all written. With the buffer n_x by n_y and s = `scale` / 100, the tiles are t_x = n_x s by t_y = n_y s, one starting at c_x / 100 n_x - t_x / 2 across and c_y / 100 n_y - t_y / 2 down. Place p of a row reads u = p - start, k = floor(u / t), f = u - k t, then f / s held to 0.5..n - 0.5. Each pixel (x, y), counted from the buffer's corner, is the mean of m by m bilinear samples at (x + (a + 0.5) / m, y + (b + 0.5) / m), m = ceil(1 / s) held to 1..16, summed in single precision. Then out = T (1 - B) + O B with B = `blend` / 100, the premultiplied tiled and original pixels. Blend 100, or scale 100 with the centre at 50, 50, is the input. Nothing grows; a draft changes nothing, the settings being shares of the buffer. Blend 0 is Motion Tile with `tile_width` and `tile_height` both `scale`, `tile_center` the centre and the output 100 per cent, pixel for pixel. **Deviations:** CC Tiler's Scale runs down to 0 per cent; below 1 is outside this contract; the starting scale of 50 is ours; Blend w. Original is a linear mix of premultiplied pixels. Drawn on the card in the warp pass, mode 25 (Motion Tile's own sized tile stays on the processor). FX-TILES-001 to 021 pin it.

Magnify (D-405, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Magnify, whose page says what each control does and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/magnify_reference.py` is the reference. `core.magnify`: `shape`, "circle" or "square" (circle); `center`, two numbers, per cent of the width and height of the buffer as it reaches the effect, -1000..1000 (50, 50); `magnification`, per cent 100..1000 (200); `link`, "none", "size" or "size_feather" (none); `size`, pixels 0..1000 (100), the radius; `feather`, pixels 0..1000 (0); `opacity`, per cent 0..100 (100); `scaling`, "standard", "soft" or "scatter" (standard); `blending_mode`, "none", "normal", "add", "multiply", "screen", "overlay" or "soft_light" (normal); `resize_layer`, "off" or "on" (off). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable; all written. With the buffer w by h, c = (center / 100 w, center / 100 h), m = magnification / 100: R = size, or m size when linked; F = feather, or m feather when link is size_feather. With resize_layer on and link none the buffer grows by g = ceil(max(0, R - c_x, c_x + R - w, R - c_y, c_y + R - h)) on every side, else g = 0. Each output pixel's centre p (buffer coordinates), v = p - c, d = |v| for a circle (inside when v_x^2 + v_y^2 <= R^2) or max(|v_x|, |v_y|) for a square; k = 1 inside and 0 outside when F = 0, else clamp((R - d) / F, 0, 1). Where k > 0 the buffer is read at q = c + v / m: standard the pixel holding q, soft document 21's bilinear sample, scatter the pixel holding q + (1 - 1/m)(u_x, u_y), u Noise's hash `grade::unit(mix(0), i, j, 0, 0 or 1)` at the pixel's whole-number place in the ungrown buffer; clear outside the buffer. A = S k opacity / 100 (premultiplied); O the buffer's own pixel, clear outside. Then none: A; normal: A over O; the rest document 21's layer blend of A over O (out = (1 - a_A) O + (1 - a_O) A + a_A a_O B(c_A, c_O) on straight colours, Add held to 1, Overlay and Soft Light on the encoded colours). Opacity 0 with any mode but none is the input. A draft scales Size and Feather; the centre is a share and Magnification is not scaled. **Deviations:** Adobe's page gives no formula, so the rule is ours; Soft is document 21's bilinear sample where Adobe names a spline; Scatter nudges the read by Noise's hash (by up to 1 - 1/m pixels) where Adobe's noise is unknown; the feather lies inside the edge; Magnification runs 100 to 1000 and Size and Feather 0 to 1000 (Adobe's ranges were not read); Blending Mode offers None and six of AE's layer modes (Normal, Add, Multiply, Screen, Overlay, Soft Light), any other refused by name; the starting values other than Normal (circle, centre 50, 50, 200 per cent, size 100, feather 0, opacity 100, standard) are ours; the centre is a share of the picture as it reaches the effect; Resize Layer grows the layer to hold the square round the lens and does nothing while Size is linked, as Adobe's page says. A centre far off the layer with Resize Layer on can grow the layer to about ten widths; the card has no texture-size guard, the same ceiling Motion Tile already has. Drawn on the card in the warp pass, mode 23. FX-MAGNIFY-001 to 034 pin it.

Checkerboard (D-413, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Checkerboard (Generate), whose manual page says what each control does and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/checkerboard_reference.py` is the reference. `core.checkerboard`: `anchor` and `corner`, two numbers each, per cent of the drawing's own width and height, -1000..1000 (50, 50 and 60, 60); `size_from`, "corner_point", "width_slider" or "width_and_height_sliders" (width_slider); `width` and `height`, pixels 1..10000 (64); `feather_width` and `feather_height`, pixels 0..10000 (0); `color`, `#rrggbb` (white), read in small letters; `opacity`, per cent 0..100 (100); `blending_mode`, "none", "normal", "add", "multiply", "screen", "overlay", "soft_light" or "stencil_alpha" (none). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. With X = (x, y) a pixel's centre in the drawing's own pixels (its top-left corner (0, 0), however far an effect above grew the buffer), A the anchor and K the corner in those pixels: the cell is w by h, Corner Point w = max(|K_x - A_x|, 1) and h = max(|K_y - A_y|, 1), Width Slider w = h = width, Width & Height Sliders w = width and h = height. Along x, u = (x - A_x) / w, i = floor(u), d = w min(u - i, i + 1 - u), s = +1 for even i and -1 for odd, f_x = s min(d / (r_x / 2), 1) with r_x = max(feather_width, 1); f_y the same down with h and feather_height; the covering c = (1 + f_x f_y) / 2. The pattern is S = (C c o, c o), premultiplied, C the colour in linear light, o = opacity / 100. The layer's pixel D becomes: none, S; normal, S + D (1 - S_a); stencil_alpha, D S_a; the others document 21's layer blend of S onto D as Magnify's (D-405). After Effects' Hard Light, Color Dodge, Color Burn, Darken, Lighten, Difference, Exclusion, Hue, Saturation, Color, Luminosity and Silhouette Alpha are not offered. Width, height and the feathers are distances: a draft divides them by its scale, width and height held at least 1. The layer never grows. FX-CHECK-001 to 033 pin it.

Circle (D-414, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Circle (Generate), whose manual page says what each edge does and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/circle_reference.py` is the reference. `core.circle`: `center`, two numbers, per cent of the drawing's own width and height, -1000..1000 (50, 50); `radius`, pixels 0..10000 (75); `edge`, "none", "edge_radius", "thickness", "thickness_radius" or "thickness_feather_radius" (none); `edge_thickness`, 0..10000 (10); `feather_outer` and `feather_inner`, 0..10000 (0); `invert`, "off" or "on" (off); `color`, `#rrggbb` (white), read in small letters; `opacity`, per cent 0..100 (100); `blending_mode`, D-413's eight words (none). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. With R the radius, T the edge thickness, F_o and F_i the feathers: for thickness_radius T becomes T R / 100, and for thickness_feather_radius T, F_o and F_i become T R / 100, F_o R / 100 and F_i R / 100 (per cent of the radius, since a product of two distances is not a distance). The ring is R_o to R_i: none, R and 0; edge_radius, max(R, T) and min(R, T); the three thickness edges, R and max(R - T, 0), the ring inside the radius. With d the distance from a pixel's centre to the centre (in the drawing's own pixels, however far an effect above grew the buffer), the covering is c = clamp((R_o - d) / max(F_o, 1) + 1/2, 0, 1), times clamp((d - R_i) / max(F_i, 1) + 1/2, 0, 1) when R_i > 0; Invert Circle makes it 1 - c. The shape is laid as Checkerboard's (D-413): S = (C c o, c o), the layer's place for none, else document 21's layer blend. Radius is a distance, and so are the edge thickness and the feathers unless the edge reads them as per cent of the radius: a draft divides those by its scale. The layer never grows. FX-CIRCLE-001 to 040 pin it.

Ellipse (D-415, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Ellipse (Generate), whose manual page says only that it draws an ellipse, so this rule is ours. `tools/ellipse_reference.py` is the reference. `core.ellipse`: `center`, two numbers, per cent of the drawing's own width and height, -1000..1000 (50, 50); `width` and `height`, pixels 1..10000 (200, 200); `thickness`, pixels 0..10000 (8); `softness`, per cent 0..100 (50); `inside_color` and `outside_color`, `#rrggbb` (white, #3c8cff), read in small letters; `composite`, "on" or "off" (on). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. With a = width / 2, b = height / 2 and (u, v) a pixel's centre less the centre (in the drawing's own pixels, however far an effect above grew the buffer): k = sqrt(u^2 / a^2 + v^2 / b^2), g = sqrt(u^2 / a^4 + v^2 / b^4), and the distance to the outline d = |k - 1| k / g, or min(a, b) where g = 0. Then Beam's line (D-207): r = thickness / 2, s = max(2 r softness / 100, 1), covering c = clamp((min(d + s/2, r) - max(d - s/2, -r)) / s, 0, 1), outside share q = min(d / r, 1) (1 when r = 0), colour C = (1 - q) inside + q outside in linear light; the result is P (1 - c) + C c with alpha P_a (1 - c) + c when composite is on, and C c with alpha c alone when off. Width, height and thickness are distances: a draft divides them by its scale, keeping width and height at least a pixel. The layer never grows. FX-ELLIPSE-001 to 027 pin it.

Fractal (D-416, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Fractal (Generate), whose help page gives the escape rectangle (-2, -2, 2, 2) and the palettes in words but no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/fractal_reference.py` is the reference. `core.fractal`: `set_choice` `mandelbrot` (when added), `mandelbrot_inverse`, `mandelbrot_over_julia`, `mandelbrot_inverse_over_julia`, `julia` or `julia_inverse`; `equation` `z2` (when added) to `z6`; `mandelbrot_center` and `julia_center`, two numbers -10..10 ((-0.75, 0) and (0, 0)); `mandelbrot_magnification` and `julia_magnification` -10..40 (0); `mandelbrot_escape_limit` and `julia_escape_limit` 1..10000 (100); `overlay`, `transparency`, `edge_highlight` "off" or "on" (off); `palette` `lightness_gradient` (when added), `hue_wheel`, `black_and_white` or `solid_color`; `hue` degrees -36000..36000 (0); `cycle_steps` 1..1000 (10); `cycle_offset` 0..1000 (0); `oversample_method` `edge_detect` (when added) or `brute_force`; `oversample_factor` 1..8 (2). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; counts (escape limits, cycle steps and offset, factor) take their whole part. At a point X of the drawing's own pixels (w by h at the frame's scale), a view (c, m) makes one pixel u = 3 / (h 2^m) units and X the complex number p = (cx + (x - w/2) u, cy - (y - h/2) u). z^n + c is worked by repeated multiplication. Mandelbrot takes z0 = 0 (the Julia centre for Over Julia) and c = p in the Mandelbrot view; Julia takes z0 = p in the Julia view and c the Mandelbrot centre; the inverse ones use 1 / p (1 / 0 escapes at once). The escape count is the first k from 1 to the limit after which |z.re| > 2 or |z.im| > 2; a point that never escapes is inside. Bands: inside -1; Lightness Gradient (k + offset) mod 8 steps, gradient g = n div steps at hue H + 45 g, lightness (j + 1) / (steps + 1) for step j; Hue Wheel (k + offset) mod steps, hue H + 360 n / steps, lightness 0.5; Black And White (k + offset) mod 2, black then white; Solid Color the hue at lightness 0.5 inside and clear outside (the other way round with Transparency). Colours are HSL at saturation 1, encoded, then decoded through the sRGB curve and premultiplied; inside is opaque black, or clear with Transparency. Oversampling, at Full only and factor f above 1: Brute Force averages the f by f points ((a + 0.5) / f, (b + 0.5) / f) of every pixel, linear and premultiplied; Edge Detect only at pixels whose band differs from one of its four neighbours'. Edge Highlight, with nothing oversampled: a pixel whose band differs from its left or upper neighbour's becomes opaque white. Overlay: where the opposite set (never inverted) is inside, the pixel goes half way to opaque white, then a cross at (floor(w/2), floor(h/2)), arms max(2, floor(h/20)), its black shadow one pixel right and down, the cross white. The layer is replaced and never grows; a draft halves w and h, so the view is the same, and draws at factor 1. Drawn on the card in the warp pass, mode 30, with the processor's rounding; past 1e10 steps of work it is drawn on the processor (D-416's guard). FX-FRACTALSET-001 to 042 under "Fractal (D-416)" pin it.

Grid (D-417, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Grid (Generate), whose help page describes the grid in words only, so this rule is ours. `tools/grid_reference.py` is the reference. `core.grid`: `anchor` and `corner`, two numbers each, per cent of the drawing's own width and height, -1000..1000 ((50, 50) and (60, 60)); `size_from` `corner_point` (when added), `width_slider` or `width_and_height_sliders`; `width` and `height`, pixels 1..10000 (64); `border`, pixels 0..10000 (2); `feather_width` and `feather_height`, pixels 0..10000 (0); `invert` "off" or "on" (off); `color` `#rrggbb` (white), read in small letters; `opacity` 0..100 (100); `blending_mode` Checkerboard's eight words (`none`). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. With A the anchor and K the corner in the drawing's own pixels (however far an effect above grew the buffer) and X a pixel's centre, the cell w by h is Checkerboard's (D-413): Corner Point |K - A| per axis, each at least 1; Width Slider width by width; Width & Height Sliders width by height. Along x: u = (x - A.x) / w, t = u - floor(u), d1 = w t, d2 = w (1 - t); with B = border and r = max(feather_width, 1), line(d) = clamp((min(d + r/2, B/2) - max(d - r/2, -B/2)) / r, 0, 1) and g_x = min(line(d1) + line(d2), 1); g_y the same down with h and feather_height. The covering is c = g_x + g_y - g_x g_y, or 1 - c with Invert. The pattern S = (C c o, c o), C the colour in linear light and o = opacity / 100, is laid as Checkerboard's (`lay_pattern`): `none` the pattern alone in place of the layer, `normal`, `add`, `multiply`, `screen`, `overlay`, `soft_light`, or `stencil_alpha` (the layer kept only under the grid). Width, height, border and the feathers are distances: a draft divides them by its scale, keeping width and height at least a pixel. The layer never grows; opacity 0 leaves the layer as it was, except under `none`, where it leaves it clear. Drawn on the card in the warp pass, mode 31. FX-GRID-001 to 038 pin it.

Fill (D-418, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Fill (Generate), whose help page names the controls and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/fill_reference.py` is the reference. `core.fill`: `mask`, 0..1000 (0), its floor taken; `all_masks` "off" or "on" (off); `color` `#rrggbb` (red), read in small letters; `invert` "off" or "on" (off); `horizontal_feather` and `vertical_feather`, pixels 0..1000 (0); `opacity` 0..100 (100). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. The paths are Path Stroke's (D-356): the layer's masks at the frame, flattened as document 19 flattens them, in the drawing's own space; with `all_masks` on every mask switched on with two points or more, whatever its mode, inversion, feather, expansion or opacity; with it off the mask numbered floor(`mask`) when it is such a mask; `mask` 0 none, and m = 1 everywhere. Asked for a mask and finding none, the layer is left as it is and EFFECT_PATH_MISSING is said each frame.
