Each path's covering is ADR-016's 4 by 4 even-odd samples; several join as m = 1 - (1 - m1)(1 - m2)...; the covering is blurred across by document 21's Gaussian with sigma `horizontal_feather` / 2 and down with sigma `vertical_feather` / 2, each cut at ceil(3 sigma) and normalised, on the plane (worked past the layer's edge, never faded at it); Invert makes it 1 - m. With k = m `opacity` / 100 and C the colour in linear light, each premultiplied pixel P becomes P (1 - k) + C P.a k: the alpha is kept. The feathers are distances: a draft divides them by its scale. The layer never grows; opacity 0, or the whole layer inverted, leaves it as it was. Drawn on the card by `fillfx` (the covering made on the processor). FX-FILL-001 to 044 pin it.

Eyedropper Fill (D-419, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Eyedropper Fill (Generate), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/eyedropper_fill_reference.py` is the reference. `core.eyedropper_fill`: `sample_point` two numbers -1000..1000 per cent ((50, 50)); `sample_radius` pixels 0..10000 (0); `average_pixel_colors` `skip_empty` (when added), `all`, `all_premultiplied` or `including_alpha`; `maintain_original_alpha` "off" or "on" (off); `blend_with_original` 0..100 (0). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. The point is per cent of the drawing's own width and height from its top-left corner, however far an effect above grew the buffer, as Radial Blur's centre: (px, py). The area is every pixel whose centre lies within r of the point, row by row: for row y, from ceil(py - 0.5 - r) to floor(py - 0.5 + r), dy = y + 0.5 - py, hw = sqrt(max(0, r r - dy dy)), the columns ceil(px - 0.5 - hw) to floor(px - 0.5 + hw). When it holds no pixel, the area is the one pixel (floor px, floor py). n counts the area's pixels wherever they lie; those outside the buffer count as clear. Summed in double precision over the buffer as it reaches the effect (premultiplied, linear light): P, the pixels' sum, and over the m pixels with alpha above 0 their straight colours summed, S. The colour c and covering A: Skip Empty c = S / m (black when m is 0), A = 1; All c = S / n, A = 1; All Premultiplied c = P.rgb / n, A = 1; Including Alpha c = P.rgb / P.a (black when P.a is 0), A = P.a / n. Every pixel becomes E = (c A, A), times its own alpha a with Maintain Original Alpha on; then with b = `blend_with_original` / 100, E (1 - b) + P b. The radius is a distance: a draft divides it by its scale. The layer never grows; blend 100 leaves it as it was. Drawn on the card by `eyerow`, `eyesum` and `eyefill` (the rows listed on the processor), adding in the processor's order. FX-EYEFILL-001 to 032 pin it.

Paint Bucket (D-440, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Paint Bucket (Generate), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/paint_bucket_reference.py` is the reference. `core.paint_bucket`: `fill_point` two numbers -1000..1000 per cent ((50, 50)); `fill_selector` `color_and_alpha` (when added), `straight_color`, `transparency`, `opacity` or `alpha_channel`; `tolerance` 0..100 (10); `view_threshold` and `invert_fill` "off" or "on" (off); `stroke` `antialias` (when added), `feather`, `spread`, `choke` or `stroke`; `spread_radius`, `stroke_width` and `feather_softness` pixels 0..10000 (3, 3, 10); `color` #rrggbb (red); `opacity` 0..100 (100); `blending_mode` `normal` (when added), `add`, `multiply`, `screen`, `overlay`, `soft_light` or `fill_only`. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. The point is per cent of the drawing's own width and height from its top-left corner, however far an effect above grew the buffer, as Radial Blur's centre: (px, py); the pixel holding it is (floor px, floor py), and a point past the buffer holds none, so nothing matches. Each pixel's alpha a and straight colour encoded e (sRGB, each held to 0..1, 0 where a is 0); with t = tolerance / 100 and the point pixel's (E, A) a pixel matches when: color_and_alpha, the largest of |e a - E A| over the three colours and |a - A| is at most t; straight_color, the largest |e - E| is at most t; transparency, a <= t; opacity, a >= 1 - t; alpha_channel, |a - A| <= t. The area: for alpha_channel every match; else the matches joined to the point pixel by matches up, down, left and right (none when the point pixel does not match itself). Invert Fill turns it over within the buffer. View Threshold on: every pixel opaque, white where it matches (before Invert Fill), black elsewhere, and nothing more. With distances between pixel centres and every place past the buffer outside the area: spread, the area and every pixel within Spread Radius of it; choke, the area's pixels farther than Spread Radius from every pixel outside it; stroke, the area's pixels within Stroke Width of a pixel outside it; antialias and feather, the area as it is. The covering m is that area (1 in, 0 out) blurred across then down, edges held: for feather the Gaussian above with sigma Feather Softness / 2 (0 leaves it hard); else a box three pixels wide, each tap a third. k = m opacity / 100, times the pixel's own alpha for straight_color. The fill (C k, k), C the colour in linear light, is laid on the pixel as Checkerboard (D-413) lays its pattern by the mode; fill_only is the fill alone. The three distances are distances: a draft divides them by its scale. The layer never grows; opacity 0 leaves it as it was unless fill_only or View Threshold. The matching, flood fill and distances are made on the processor on either path; on the card the held Gaussian pass blurs and `bucket` lays. FX-PAINTBUCKET-001 to 045 pin it.

Write-on (D-441, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Write-on (Generate), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/writeon_reference.py` is the reference. `core.write_on`: `brush_position` two numbers -1000..1000 per cent ((50, 50)); `color` #rrggbb (#ffffff); `brush_size` 0..200 (6); `brush_hardness` 0..100 (75); `brush_opacity` 0..100 (100); `stroke_length` 0..3600 seconds (0); `brush_spacing` 0.001..10 seconds (0.01); `paint_time_properties` `none` (when added), `color`, `opacity` or `color_and_opacity`; `brush_time_properties` `none` (when added), `size`, `hardness` or `size_and_hardness`; `paint_style` `on_original` (when added), `on_transparent` or `reveal`. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read; the words are not keyable. At composition frame f the layer's key time is u = in + (f - in) 100 / stretch (document 20), fps the composition's. With s = `brush_spacing` and L = `stroke_length` read at u, mark k = 0, 1, ... n, n = floor((u - in) / (s fps) + 1e-9), was laid at u_k = in + k s fps; with L > 0 it is kept only while u - u_k < L fps - 1e-9. Its centre is `brush_position` at u_k, per cent of the drawing's own width and height as Radial Blur's centre; its size and hardness are each read at u_k when Brush Time Properties names them, else at u; its opacity at u_k when Paint Time Properties names opacity, else at u. The colour is always read at u (colours are not keyable here, so `color` reads as `none`). Path Stroke's brush (D-356): r_k = size_k / 2, w_k = max(r_k (1 - hardness_k / 100), 1), t_k = clamp((r_k + 0.5 - d_k) / w_k, 0, 1) with d_k the distance from the pixel's centre, c_k = opacity_k / 100 t_k^2 (3 - 2 t_k), a mark of size 0 covering nothing; c is the largest c_k (marks do not build up). Laid as Path Stroke lays: on_original, O.rgb (1 - c) + C c and O.a (1 - c) + c, C the colour in linear light; on_transparent, C c and c; reveal, O c. The layer never grows; a draft divides every mark's size by its scale. Every size 0 or opacity 0 on the original leaves the layer as it was. The marks are listed on the processor on either path; on the card the `writeon` pass draws them. FX-WRITEON-001 to 041 pin it.

Scribble (D-442, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Scribble (Generate), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/scribble_reference.py` is the reference and its opening comment states the rule step by step; in short: `core.scribble`: `scribble` `single_mask` (when added), `all_masks` or `all_masks_using_modes`; `mask` 1..1000 (1, its floor taken); `fill_type` `inside` (when added), `centered_edge`, `inside_edge`, `outside_edge`, `left_edge` or `right_edge`; `edge_width` 0..1000 (10); `color` #rrggbb (#ffffff); `opacity` 0..100 (100); `angle` -3600..3600 degrees (45); `stroke_width` 0..1000 (5); `curviness` and `curviness_variation` 0..100 (5, 1); `spacing` 1..1000 (5); `spacing_variation` 0..1000 (0.5); `path_overlap` -1000..1000 (0); `path_overlap_variation` 0..1000 (2); `start` and `end` 0..100 (0, 100); `fill_paths_sequentially` `off` or `on` (on); `wiggle_type` `static`, `jumpy` or `smooth` (when added); `wiggles_per_second` 0..100 (5); `random_seed` 0..100000 (1); `composite` `on_original` (when added), `on_transparent` or `reveal`. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read. The masks are those switched on with two points or more, as Path Stroke's (D-356): Single Mask takes mask floor(`mask`), All Masks each in turn, Using Modes all of them as one region combined by D-77 (starting at the first mask whose mode is not None, Subtract or Intersect first starting from the whole buffer, inversion used; None left out). Each region is the even-odd inside of its outline, or a band: Centered Edge within `edge_width` / 2 of the outline, Inside Edge within `edge_width` inside, Outside Edge within `edge_width` outside, Left and Right Edge the inside or outside band as the outline turns (clockwise on the screen: Right is inside). In a frame turned by `angle`, lines run across at spacing s_i = `spacing` + `spacing_variation` r, r a hash in [-1, 1] of the seed, the slot and the line; each line is cut to the region's crossings, run `path_overlap` + `path_overlap_variation` r past each end, every other line reversed, and joined end to end by a cubic turn bulging `curviness` (+ variation) per cent of the gap beyond the edge; where a line crosses the region more than once the pen lifts between the pieces. The slot is floor(t `wiggles_per_second`) for Jumpy, t the layer's time from its in point in seconds; Static always slot 0; Smooth blends the scribbles of the slots either side by the fraction. The scribble is trimmed from `start` to `end` per cent of its length (all regions as one length when sequential and more than one mask, else each its own) and drawn with Path Stroke's brush of `stroke_width` across, hardness 100, `opacity`, laid on_original, on_transparent or reveal as Path Stroke lays. No mask found: the layer is left as it is and EFFECT_PATH_MISSING is said each frame. The layer never grows; a draft divides the six distances by its scale. The lines are found on the processor on either path; on the card Path Stroke's `stroke` pass draws them. FX-SCRIBBLE-001 to 071 pin it.

Add Grain (D-443, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Add Grain (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/addgrain_reference.py` is the reference and its opening comment states the rule step by step; in short: `core.add_grain`: `intensity` 0..10 (1); `size` 0.1..100 pixels (1); `softness` 0..1 (0); `aspect_ratio` 0.25..4 (1); `red_intensity`, `green_intensity`, `blue_intensity` 0..10 (1); `monochromatic` `off` (when added) or `on`; `saturation` 0..1 (1); `blending_mode` `film` (when added), `add` or `overlay`; `shadows`, `midtones`, `highlights` 0..10 (1); `midpoint` 0.01..0.99 (0.5); `animation_speed` 0..10 (1); `animate_smoothly` `on` (when added) or `off`; `random_seed` 0..100000 (0, its whole part). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read. At the drawing's own pixel (x, y) (its top-left (0, 0) however far an effect above grew the buffer), with e the straight colour through the sRGB curve: X = (x + 0.5) / (size aspect_ratio), Y = (y + 0.5) / size, z = frame animation_speed (floored when not smooth). For each channel c (only channel 0, copied to all three, when monochromatic) the value noise of P0-19 (`grade::value`, D-127) for the seed and c at (X, Y, z) is worked blocky (one number a cell, z still smooth) and smooth and mixed (1 - softness) block + softness smooth; unless monochromatic each n_c becomes m + saturation (n_c - m), m the three's mean; then times the channel's intensity. L = 0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b; the weight is shadows + (midtones - shadows) L / midpoint up to the midpoint, else midtones + (highlights - midtones)(L - midpoint)/(1 - midpoint); A = 0.1 intensity weight. Film: e + A n 4 e (1 - e); Add: e + A n; Overlay: B = clamp(0.5 + 0.5 A n), 2 e B for e at most a half, else 1 - 2 (1 - e)(1 - B). Held in 0 and 1, back through the curve, times the covering; a pixel that does not show is left. Intensity 0 changes nothing. A draft halves the size. On the card: the grade pass, mode 10. FX-ADDGRAIN-001 to 041 pin it.

Vegas (D-444, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Vegas (Generate), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours, nothing ported; `tools/vegas_reference.py` is the reference. By the owner's decision ("Masks only for now") `stroke` "image_contours" is read, kept and refused with a sentence, the effect left out of every frame. The paths: `stroke` "masks", the layer's masks at the frame, closed, as document 19 flattens them; "shapes", the shape layer's paths, open or closed as each says; `mask` (its floor) picks one, or `all_masks` "on" takes every one, exactly Path Stroke's choice; with none nothing is drawn and EFFECT_PATH_MISSING is said. The dashes: each path c, of length L from its first point, N = floor(`segments`), l = `length` L / N, o = (`rotation` / 360 + phase) L, phase 0 or, with `random_phase` "on", (mix(mix(floor(`random_seed`)) xor c) >> 11) / 2^53 (D-119's `mix`); dash i begins at a = o + i L / N ("even") or o + i l ("bunched"), taken modulo L, and covers the path from a to a + l, running on from the path's start past its end; each straight piece it meets gives a straight bit from P to Q, its ends at s_P, s_Q = (u - a) / l along the dash. The brush: at a pixel's centre x, for each bit, t = clamp(((x - P) . (Q - P)) / |Q - P|^2, 0, 1), d = |x - P - t (Q - P)|, s = s_P + t (s_Q - s_P); r = `width` / 2, w = max(r (1 - `hardness`), 1), v = clamp((r + 0.5 - d) / w, 0, 1); the bit gives v^2 (3 - 2 v) times the fade at s, from S = `start_opacity` at 0 to M = clamp(S + m (E - S) + `mid_point_opacity`, 0, 1) at m = `mid_point_position`, then to E = `end_opacity` at 1, in straight lines; the covering c is the largest any bit gives. With O the layer (linear, premultiplied) and C the colour (linear): "over" O.rgb (1 - c) + C c, O.a (1 - c) + c; "under" O.rgb + (1 - O.a) C c, O.a + (1 - O.a) c; "transparent" C c, c; "stencil" O c. `length` 0 or `width` 0 draws nothing; the layer never grows; a draft scales `width`. Ranges: `mask` 1..1000 (1), `segments` 1..1000 (32), `length` 0..1 (1), `rotation` -360000..360000 degrees (0), `random_seed` 0..100000 (1), `width` 0..200 (2), `hardness` 0..1 (0), `start_opacity` 0..1 (1), `mid_point_opacity` -1..1 (0), `mid_point_position` 0..1 (0.5), `end_opacity` 0..1 (0); words as named, "masks", "off", "bunched", "off", "over"; `color` #rrggbb (white). Drawn on the card by its own pass, the bits worked out on the processor. FX-VEGAS-001 to 060 pin it.

Noise Alpha (D-450, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Noise Alpha (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/noisealpha_reference.py` is the reference and its opening comment states the rule step by step; in short: `core.noise_alpha`: `noise` `uniform_random` (when added), `squared_random`, `uniform_animation` or `squared_animation`; `amount` 0..100 (20); `original_alpha` `clamp` (when added), `add`, `scale` or `edges`; `overflow` `clip` (when added), `wrap_back` or `wrap`; `random_seed` 0..100000 (0, its whole part); `noise_phase` -100000..100000 degrees (0); `cycle_noise` `off` (when added) or `on`; `cycle` 1..1000 (1, its whole part). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read. At the drawing's own pixel (x, y) (its top-left (0, 0) however far an effect above grew the buffer), n is P0-19's value noise (`grade::value`, D-127) on channel 0 read blocky (one number a pixel): for the Random kinds under the seed at depth 0; for the Animation kinds under seed 0 at depth noise_phase / 360, smooth along the depth, the depth wrapped every cycle when cycle_noise is on. The Squared kinds make n copysign(1 - (1 - |n|)^2, n). d = amount / 100 n. With a the pixel's covering: Clamp works only where a = 1, Edges only where 0 < a < 1, Add everywhere, each t = a + d; Scale works where a > 0, t = a + d a. Overflow: Clip holds t in 0..1; Wrap Back turns t > 1 into 2 - t and t < 0 into -t; Wrap turns t > 1 into t - 1 and t < 0 into t + 1; then held in 0..1. A pixel whose t is a is left; else it becomes its straight colour (black where a was 0) times t, with covering t. Amount 0 changes nothing. On the card: the grade pass, mode 11. FX-NOISEALPHA-001 to 031 pin it.

Curl Noise (D-446, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Curl Noise (Noise & Grain, new in 26.3), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours, nothing ported, built on P0-19's value noise; `tools/curl_noise_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, at a point p of the drawing's own pixels (its top-left corner (0, 0), however far an effect above grew the buffer): 1. The field's size is s = size 2^(-density / 50); it drifts speed / 10 pixels a frame towards Direction (0 up, 90 right), so p' = p - offset - drift frame. Its depth is z = (evolution + turbulence_speed frame / 10) / 360. 2. The noise N: four octaves of P0-19's smooth value noise for seed 0, octave o at (p' / s 2^o, z 2^o) on channel 8 o, weighted 1, r, r^2, r^3 with r = 0.7 - 0.4 smoothness / 100, over the sum of the weights; and its slope, worked exactly from the smoothing curve's own slope 30 t^2 (1 - t)^2, over all eight corners of each cell. 3. The flow: the slope turned by -90 degrees + swirl N degrees (at Swirl 0 exactly the curl, which never gathers or spreads), then its across part times min(1, 2 (1 - b)) and its down part times min(1, 2 b), b = vertical_bias / 100, and made one long (left nought where it is nought). 4. The seed: P0-19's value noise on channel 7 at (p' / (s / 50), z), blocks and smooth mixed by edge_definition / 100 (1 all blocks). 5. These four numbers, the flow's two, N and the seed, are worked for every pixel of the buffer and M = ceil(sample_radius) + 2 round it, kept in single precision, as the card keeps them. 6. Final Render: from the pixel's middle the flow is followed both ways, n = floor(sample_count) steps of sample_radius / n each, reading the four numbers between pixels as `render::sample_bilinear` does, until the flow is nought; the seed is averaged with weight 1 at the middle and 1 - i / (n + 1) at step i, and scaled by the weights' sum over the root of their squares' sum, L. With f = flow_softness / 100, the lines are N + (1 - f) min(1, sample_radius / 8) (L - N), then times 1 - flow_falloff / 100 (0.5 - 0.5 N), and the grey is 0.5 + 0.5 times that. Input Noise: the grey is 0.5 + 0.5 N. Curl Generation: red 0.5 + 0.5 the flow across, green 0.5 + 0.5 the flow down, blue 0.5 + 0.5 N. 7. Each channel o becomes 0.5 + (o - 0.5) contrast / 100 + brightness / 100, held at 0, and at 1 too unless Clip HDR Results is off in a composition that works in Float. 8. Channel RGB: the colour, through the sRGB curve, replaces the pixel's at its covering; Red, Green or Blue: only that channel is replaced; Alpha: the covering is multiplied by the three's mean, held in 0 and 1, and the colour kept. A pixel that does not show is left. Settings: `source` `internal` (when added; `this_layer` and `other_layer` refused); `speed` 0 to 100, 10 when added; `direction` -100000 to 100000 degrees, 0; `size` 1 to 1000 pixels, 100; `offset` a point, -100000 to 100000, [0, 0]; `evolution` -100000 to 100000 degrees, 0; `turbulence_speed` 0 to 200, 20; `swirl` -360 to 360, 45; `density` -100 to 100, 0; `smoothness` 0 to 100, 50; `vertical_bias` 0 to 100, 50; `sample_count` 3 to 24, 12, its whole part; `sample_radius` 0 to 200 pixels, 30; `flow_softness` 0 to 100, 20; `edge_definition` 0 to 100, 50; `flow_falloff` 0 to 100, 0; `view` `final_render` (when added), `input_noise` or `curl_generation`; `contrast` 0 to 1000, 100; `brightness` -1000 to 1000, 0; `clip_hdr_results` `on` (when added) or `off`; `channel` `rgb` (when added), `red`, `green`, `blue` or `alpha`. Every number can be keyed. Drawn on the card by two passes of its own, `curlfield` and `curlflow`; a Float composition by the processor. FX-CURL-001 to 044 pin it.

Turbulent Noise (D-445, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Turbulent Noise (Noise & Grain), a second name over Fractal Noise's rule (D-383, D-394, as Tritone over Gradient Map, D-402): Fractal Noise with no speed, dark #000000, light #ffffff and cycle 0, nothing ported; `tools/turbulent_noise_reference.py` is the reference. The field. V(seed, ch, x, y, z, block) is D-128's smooth value noise in -1..1 (from `tools/fractal_noise_reference.py`): with i, j, k = floor(x), floor(y), floor(z) and sx, sy, sz = fade of the parts past them, the sum over the eight corners of their weights times U(seed, i + di, j + dj, k + dk, ch), U Noise's hash (D-119); with `block` sx = sy = 0, each cell one number across and down while the depth still mixes (D-299). F is n octaves: octave o is V(seed, 8 o, x 2^o, y 2^o, z 2^o, block) at strength 0.5^o; basic gives the sum over the sum of the strengths, turbulent gives 2 (the sum of the strengths times |V|) over the sum, less 1. Pixel (X, Y) of the drawing's own space samples ((X + 0.5 - offset x) / (size scale_width / 100), (Y + 0.5 - offset y) / (size scale_height / 100), evolution / 360). The rule. At a pixel with a > 0: n = F, turned over (-n) when inverted; v = clamp(0.5 + 0.5 n contrast / 100 + brightness / 100, 0, 1); G = srgb_to_linear(v) in each channel; with b the straight linear colour, f = G (normal), b G (multiply), 1 - (1 - b)(1 - G) (screen) or b + G (add), not clamped, and the output is ((b + op (f - b)) a, a) with op = opacity / 100. Settings: `fractal_type` "basic" (when added) or another of D-567's seventeen; `noise_type` "smooth" (when added) or another of D-567's five; `invert` "off" (when added) or "on"; `contrast` 0 to 1000, 100; `brightness` -1000 to 1000, 0; `size` 1 to 1000 pixels, 100; `scale_width` and `scale_height` 1 to 10000 per cent, 100; `offset` x then y, -100000 to 100000 pixels, [0, 0]; `complexity` 1 to 20, 6, its whole part; `evolution` -100000 to 100000 degrees, 0; `seed` 0 to 100000, 0, its whole part; `opacity` 0 to 100, 100; `blend` "normal" (when added), "multiply", "screen" or "add". In a composition that works in Float v is not held at 1, as Fractal Noise's (D-319). Drawn on the card by Fractal Noise's pass (grade mode 9). FX-TURBNOISE-001 to 032 pin it.

Fractal and noise types (D-567, 2026-10-12), Turbulent Noise's and Fractal Noise's, one engine; Adobe publishes no formulas, so every type past basic, turbulent, smooth and block is ours, after its name. `tools/turbulent_noise_types_reference.py` is the reference. `fractal_type` is one of "basic", "turbulent", "turbulent_smooth", "turbulent_sharp", "dynamic", "dynamic_progressive", "dynamic_twist", "max", "smeary", "swirly", "rocky", "cloudy", "terrain", "subscale", "small_bumps", "strings", "threads" and `noise_type` one of "smooth", "block", "linear", "soft_linear", "spline"; each written only when not basic or smooth (D-304), so every older file draws as before. **The noise** V(seed, ch, x, y, z, noise) in -1..1 from U Noise's hash (D-119) at whole points, with t the parts past floor: smooth and block as D-128 and D-299; linear mixes the eight corners by t itself; soft_linear by 3t^2 - 2t^3; spline mixes four points across and four down by the Catmull-Rom curve, the depth by smooth's curve, held to -1..1. **The fractal.** Octave o of n is V(seed, 8 o, ...) at strength a = 0.5^o (0.35^o for cloudy) and fineness 2^o (4 2^o for small_bumps), at (x, y, z) times the fineness and moved as the type says; s, "so far", is the sum of the weighted octaves before over the sum of their strengths (0 first) and p the previous octave's v (0 first). Moves, before the fineness: dynamic x + 4 s, y + 4 p; dynamic_progressive x + 2 o s, y + 2 o p; dynamic_twist (o > 0) x + 2 cos(2 pi s), y + 2 sin(2 pi s); smeary (o > 0) x / 4 + 2 s. Each octave's g: v, except turbulent |v|, turbulent_smooth v^2, turbulent_sharp sqrt|v|, terrain (1 - |v|)^2 times the previous octave's (1 - |v|)^2 (1 first), threads 2 max(0, 1 - 6|v|) - 1, subscale (o > 0) g (p + 1) / 2. B = the sum of a g over the sum of a. max instead starts r at the first v and lifts it by a max(0, v - r) each octave after. The result: 2 B - 1 for turbulent, turbulent_smooth, turbulent_sharp and terrain; r for max; sin(3 pi B) for swirly; clamp(floor(2 B + 0.5) / 2, -1, 1) for rocky; (B + 1)^2 / 2 - 1 for cloudy; 2 max(0, 1 - 6|B|) - 1 for strings; B otherwise. Then Turbulent Noise's or Fractal Noise's rule as before, with Fractal Noise's cycle wrapping every type's cells. basic and turbulent on smooth or block are D-445's numbers to the bit. On the card: grade mode 9 as before for those four, grade mode 111 for every other, in double precision. FX-TURBTYPE-001 to 025 pin it.

Mesh and brush warping's shared part (D-601, 2026-10-12), P0-18, for Bezier Warp, Mesh Warp, Warp, Liquify, Reshape and Smear; this program's own rule, `tools/mesh_reference.py` the reference. **A forward mesh.** The drawing (W by H) is cut into `cols` by `rows` equal cells; the effect says where each of the (cols + 1) (rows + 1) grid corners lands, point (i, j) row by row being where the drawing's (i / cols W, j / rows H) goes. Each cell is two triangles, (i, j) (i + 1, j) (i + 1, j + 1) then (i, j) (i + 1, j + 1) (i, j + 1), numbered in that order, cell by cell, row by row. An output pixel centre X lies in a triangle A B C when, with d = (B - A) x (C - A) not 0, l1 = ((X - A) x (C - A)) / d and l2 = ((B - A) x (X - A)) / d are each at least 0 and l1 + l2 at most 1. Where X lies in several (a fold) the last numbered wins; in none, the pixel is clear. In the winning triangle X came from the grid place A + l1 (B - A) + l2 (C - A), so from the drawing's (u / cols W, v / rows H), and the pixel is this document's bilinear sample there. A mesh at the grid's own places draws the drawing back. On the card: warp mode 150, the triangles tested per pixel in the same order. **A backward field.** A grid of `gx` by `gy` cells holds at each corner (i, j), at the drawing's (i / gx W, j / gy H), a step (dx, dy); the step at any place is the bilinear blend of the four corners round it, the place held inside the grid first. The output at X is the drawing's bilinear sample at X + s D(X - o), `s` a share and `o` an offset (Liquify's Distortion Percentage and Distortion Mesh Offset; 1 and 0 elsewhere). On the card: warp mode 151. Both work on the layer's own drawing, before its transform.

Bezier Warp (D-602, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Bezier Warp (Distort), `core.bezier_warp`; Adobe publishes no formula, so the rule is ours, `tools/bezier_warp_reference.py` the reference. Twelve points in per cent of the drawing (W by H), as Corner Pin's pins: `top_left_vertex` (0, 0), `top_left_tangent` (100/3, 0), `top_right_tangent` (200/3, 0), `right_top_vertex` (100, 0), `right_top_tangent` (100, 100/3), `right_bottom_tangent` (100, 200/3), `bottom_right_vertex` (100, 100), `bottom_right_tangent` (200/3, 100), `bottom_left_tangent` (100/3, 100), `left_bottom_vertex` (0, 100), `left_bottom_tangent` (0, 200/3), `left_top_tangent` (0, 100/3) when added, each -400 to 500 each way; `quality` 1 to 10, 8. All keyable. Each edge is the cubic Bezier P(t) = s^3 P0 + 3 s^2 t P1 + 3 s t^2 P2 + t^3 P3, s = 1 - t: top T(u) from the top left vertex through the top tangents to the right top vertex; right R(v) from there through the right tangents to the bottom right vertex; bottom Bm(u) from the left bottom vertex through the bottom tangents to the bottom right vertex; left L(v) from the top left vertex through the left tangents to the left bottom vertex. The surface is their Coons patch, S(u, v) = (1 - v) T(u) + v Bm(u) + (1 - u) L(v) + u R(v) - ((1 - u)(1 - v) TL + u (1 - v) TR + (1 - u) v BL + u v BR). With q = floor(`quality` + 0.5) and n = 4 q, the drawing's n by n grid goes to S(i / n, j / n) and is drawn as D-601's forward mesh: outside the outline clear, a fold drawn later on top, nothing past the drawing's edges. Every point where it starts draws the drawing. Everything is a share of the drawing, so a draft needs no change. FX-BEZIER-001 to 017 pin it.

Mesh Warp (D-603, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Mesh Warp (Distort), `core.mesh_warp`; Adobe publishes no formula, so the rule is ours, `tools/mesh_warp_reference.py` the reference. `rows` and `columns` 1 to 31, 7; `quality` 1 to 10, 8; `distortion_mesh` empty (the mesh at rest) or 2 (R + 1) (C + 1) numbers, x then y for each vertex row by row from the top left, each per cent of the drawing, -400 to 500; a list of any other length is refused. All keyable; a keyed mesh keeps its length. R = floor(`rows` + 0.5), C = floor(`columns` + 0.5); at rest vertex (i, j) is at (i / C W, j / R H). Past the edges each vertex row and column is carried on straight, V(i, -1) = 2 V(i, 0) - V(i, 1) and V(i, R + 1) = 2 V(i, R) - V(i, R - 1), then across the same way. The surface is the Catmull-Rom spline both ways, CR(P0, P1, P2, P3, s) = 0.5 (2 P1 + (P2 - P0) s + (2 P0 - 5 P1 + 4 P2 - P3) s^2 + (3 P1 - P0 - 3 P2 + P3) s^3), across at s on the four vertex rows round the cell, then down at t. With q = floor(`quality` + 0.5) each cell is cut q by q and the drawing's C q by R q grid drawn as D-601's forward mesh. At rest, the drawing. **Gap:** After Effects gives each vertex Bezier handles; this effect has none, so a cell's edges bend only as the vertices round it pull. FX-MESH-001 to 018 pin it.

Warp (D-604, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Warp (Distort), `core.warp`; After Effects names fifteen styles and publishes no formula, so the shapes are ours, `tools/warp_reference.py` the reference. The drawing (W by H) is laid on u and v, each -1/2 to 1/2 (x = (u + 1/2) W, y = (v + 1/2) H); b = `bend` / 100, h = `horizontal_distortion` / 100, d = `vertical_distortion` / 100, e = 1 - 4 u^2, f = 1 - 4 v^2. With `warp_axis` `vertical`, u and v change places before the style and back after it. `arc`: T = b pi / 2, R = 1 / T, a = u T, r = R - v, u' = r sin a, v' = R - r cos a (b 0 unmoved). `arc_lower`: v' = v + b e (v + 1/2) / 2. `arc_upper`: v' = v - b e (1/2 - v) / 2. `arch`: v' = v - b e / 2. `bulge`: v' = v (1 + b e / 2). `shell_lower`: as `arc_lower` and u' = u (1 - b (v + 1/2) / 2). `shell_upper`: as `arc_upper` and u' = u (1 - b (1/2 - v) / 2). `flag`: v' = v + b sin(2 pi u) / 4. `wave`: v' = v + b sin(2 pi u + pi (v + 1/2)) / 4. `fish`: v' = v (1 + b sin(2 pi u) / 2). `rise`: v' = v - b sin(pi u) / 2. `fisheye`: s = 1 + b (1 - 2 (u^2 + v^2)) / 2, u' = u s, v' = v s. `inflate`: u' = u (1 + b f / 2), v' = v (1 + b e / 2). `squeeze`: u' = u (1 - b f / 2), v' = v (1 + b e / 2). `twist`: p = b pi (1 - 2 (u^2 + v^2)) / 2, (u', v') = (u cos p - v sin p, u sin p + v cos p). A coordinate a style leaves out stays. Then u'' = u' (1 + d v'), v'' = v' (1 + h u'). The drawing's 64 by 64 grid, point (a, c) at u = a / 64 - 1/2, v = c / 64 - 1/2, goes to ((u'' + 1/2) W, (v'' + 1/2) H) and is drawn as D-601's forward mesh: outside clear, a fold's later part on top, nothing past the drawing's edges. `warp_style` one of the fifteen, `arc`; `warp_axis` `horizontal` or `vertical`, `horizontal`; `bend` -100 to 100, 50; both distortions -100 to 100, 0; the numbers keyable. Bend 0 with both distortions 0 draws the drawing. Everything is a share of the drawing, so a draft needs no change. FX-WARP-001 to 030 pin it.

Liquify (D-605, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Liquify (Distort), `core.liquify`; After Effects names its brushes and publishes no formula, so the rule is ours, `tools/liquify_reference.py` the reference. `tool` one of `warp`, `twirl_clockwise`, `twirl_counterclockwise`, `pucker`, `bloat`, `shift_pixels`, `warp`; `brush_size` 1 to 1000, 64; `brush_pressure` 1 to 100, 50; `stroke` empty or x then y for each point, per cent of the drawing, -100 to 200, empty; `distortion_percentage` 0 to 200, 100; `distortion_mesh_offset` x then y in pixels, -10000 to 10000, (0, 0); all keyable, the stroke as one list. The stroke's points go to pixels (X / 100 W, Y / 100 H). R = `brush_size` / 2, s = max(R / 4, 1/2), L the path's length, n = max(1, ceil(L / s)) dabs, dab k (1 to n) at k L / n along the path, each moving d, L / n along its piece of path (one point, or no length: one dab at the first point, d = (0, 0)). A dab at c reaches p with f = P (1 - t^2)^2, t = |p - c| / R below 1, else 0, P = `brush_pressure` / 100, and adds a step: `warp` -f d; `twirl_clockwise` the turn of (p - c) by -a about c less (p - c), a = 0.2 f radians, `twirl_counterclockwise` by +a; `pucker` 0.2 f (p - c); `bloat` -0.2 f (p - c); `shift_pixels` -f (d_y, -d_x). The steps are summed over every dab at each corner of a gx by gy grid, gx = min(256, ceil(W / 2)), gy = min(256, ceil(H / 2)), and drawn as D-601's backward field: the output at X is the drawing at X plus `distortion_percentage` / 100 of the step at X less the offset, bilinear, clear outside. No stroke, or `distortion_percentage` 0, draws the drawing. **Gap:** After Effects' Turbulence, Reflection, Clone and Reconstruction brushes, its freeze and thaw areas and its many strokes are not here; one stroke with one brush. FX-LIQUIFY-001 to 021 pin it.

Reshape (D-606, 2026-10-13), after After Effects' Reshape (Distort), `core.reshape`; After Effects names its controls and publishes no formula, so the rule is ours, `tools/reshape_reference.py` the reference. `source_mask`, `destination_mask` and `boundary_mask` 0 to 1000, the mask numbered so (floor taken), 0 for none, 0 each when added; `percent` 0 to 100, 100; `elasticity` one of `stiff`, `less_stiff`, `below_normal`, `normal` (when added), `above_normal`, `super_fluid`, `liquid`; all keyable. The masks are chosen as Fill chooses them (D-418): on, two points or more, flattened as document 19 says, in the drawing's own space, whatever their mode. A source or destination of 0 draws the drawing and says nothing; a boundary of 0 is the drawing's own rectangle; a mask named and not found draws the drawing and says EFFECT_PATH_MISSING every frame. Each outline is resampled to K = 64 points equally spaced along it from its first point; where source and destination wind opposite ways (shoelace sums of opposite signs) the destination is turned round keeping its first point; point k answers point k. With t = `percent` / 100, T_k = S_k + t (D_k - S_k) carries the step S_k - T_k, and the boundary's points B_k a step of 0. The step at p is 0 outside the boundary (even-odd at p) and inside it the inverse-distance blend of every T_k's and B_k's step, w = (d^2)^(-e / 2), a place on a point taking that point's step (the first such), e 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4 or 5 from `stiff` to `liquid`. The steps are worked at the corners of a gx by gy grid, gx = min(256, ceil(W / 2)), gy = min(256, ceil(H / 2)), and drawn as D-601's backward field, share 1, no offset. `percent` 0 draws the drawing. **Gap:** After Effects' Correspondence Points (hand-placed matching points between the two outlines) and its Interpolation choice are not here; the outlines are matched by length from their first points. FX-RESHAPE-001 to 022 pin it.

Mask Smear (D-607, 2026-10-13), after After Effects' Smear (Distort), `core.mask_smear` (`core.smear` is D-392's, a different effect); After Effects names its controls and publishes no formula, so the rule is ours, `tools/mask_smear_reference.py` the reference. `source_mask` and `boundary_mask` 0 to 1000, 0 each when added; `mask_offset` x then y in pixels, -10000 to 10000, (0, 0); `mask_rotation` degrees clockwise, -3600 to 3600, 0; `mask_scale` per cent, 0 to 1000, 100; `percent` 0 to 100, 100; `elasticity` one of Reshape's seven words, `normal`; all keyable. The masks are chosen as Reshape's are (D-606): a source of 0 draws the drawing and says nothing; a boundary of 0 is the drawing's own rectangle; a mask named and not found draws the drawing and says EFFECT_PATH_MISSING every frame. The source outline is resampled to Reshape's 64 points S_k, c their mean; with s = `mask_scale` / 100, a = `mask_rotation` in radians and (dx, dy) = s (S_k - c), the destination point is D_k = c + `mask_offset` + (dx cos a - dy sin a, dx sin a + dy cos a), clockwise on the screen, y down. From S_k and D_k the drawing is drawn as Reshape draws it: pulled `percent` / 100 of the way, the boundary held, at the elasticity's power, through D-601's backward field. The offset is a distance, so a draft scales it with the drawing. `percent` 0 draws the drawing. **Gap:** the moved mask is worked from Smear's offset, rotation and scale about the outline's mean (After Effects does not say where it turns the mask), and the masks are chosen by number. FX-SMEAR-001 to 022 pin it.

Noise HLS (D-451, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Noise HLS (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/noisehls_reference.py` is the reference and its opening comment states the rule step by step; in short: `core.noise_hls`: `noise` `uniform` (when added), `squared` or `grain`; `hue`, `lightness` and `saturation` 0..100 (0, 10, 0); `grain_size` 0.5..100 pixels (1); `noise_phase` -100000..100000 degrees (0). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read. At the drawing's own pixel (x, y) (its top-left (0, 0) however far an effect above grew the buffer), z = noise_phase / 360 and n_k, k = 0, 1, 2, is P0-19's value noise (`grade::value`, D-127) under seed 0 on channel k: Uniform blocky (one number a pixel) at (x, y, z); Squared that number made copysign(1 - (1 - |n|)^2, n); Grain smooth at ((x + 0.5) / grain_size, (y + 0.5) / grain_size, z). The straight colour through the sRGB curve, as D-113's HSL (h, s, l): h + 180 n_0 hue / 100 wrapped round 360, s + n_2 saturation / 100 and l + n_1 lightness / 100 each held in 0..1; back to a colour, held, back through the curve, times the covering. A pixel with no covering is left. Hue, lightness and saturation all 0 change nothing. A draft scales grain_size with the picture, held at 0.5. On the card: the grade pass, mode 12. FX-NOISEHLS-001 to 026 pin it.

Noise HLS Auto (D-452, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Noise HLS Auto (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/noisehlsauto_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.noise_hls_auto`: `noise`, `hue`, `lightness`, `saturation` and `grain_size` as Noise HLS's (D-451) and `animation_speed` 0..10 (1) in place of `noise_phase`. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read. The rule is Noise HLS's with z = frame animation_speed, the frame being the composition's and the speed read at that frame. On the card: the grade pass, mode 12. FX-NOISEHLSAUTO-001 to 024 pin it.

Dust & Scratches (D-453, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Dust & Scratches (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/dust_scratches_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.dust_scratches`: `radius` 0..10 (1), `threshold` 0..255 (0), keyable and held inside their ranges at each time read, and `operate_on_alpha` off/on (off). The disc and the medians are Median's (D-203): the taps with dx^2 + dy^2 <= r^2, a tap outside the layer transparent. With Operate on Alpha on, the covering is the median of every tap's covering m_a, kept as the pixel's own when |q(m_a) - q(a)| <= threshold, q(v) = quantise_u8(v); off, the pixel's own. Each straight colour channel takes the median of the taps that show, m_c, unless the pixel shows and |q(srgb(c)) - q(srgb(m_c))| <= threshold, when it keeps its own; srgb is D-88's curve. Radius below 1 or threshold 255 is the layer unchanged; Median is this rule with no channel kept. On the card: the median pass, mode 1. FX-DUST-001 to 018 pin it.

Brush Strokes (D-447, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Brush Strokes (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours, nothing ported; `tools/brush_strokes_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, for a pixel whose middle is at q in the drawing's own pixels (its top-left corner (0, 0), however far an effect above grew the buffer): 1. The stroke direction d = (sin angle, -cos angle) (0 up, 90 right) and n = (-d_y, d_x) across it; u = q . d along the strokes and v = q . n across them. 2. With r the brush size, L the stroke length, D the density and R the randomness, m = min(R, 1): the strokes sit on cells s_u = max(1, max(L, 2 r) / D) long and s_v = max(1, 2 r / D) across. Cell (i, j) holds one stroke. Its six numbers h_0 to h_5 are 0.5 + 0.5 times P0-19's hash (`grade::unit`, D-119) of the seed's whole part, i, j, the frame (0 when `animate` is off) and the channel 0 to 5. 3. Its start c = ((i + 0.5 + (h_0 - 0.5) m) s_u, (j + 0.5 + (h_1 - 0.5) m) s_v) in (u, v); its slope t = (2 h_2 - 1) R tan 10 degrees, so it runs along e = (1, t) / sqrt(1 + t^2); its length l = L (1 - 0.5 m h_3); its radius rho = r (1 + 0.25 m (2 h_4 - 1)); and its place in the pile h_5, the highest on top. 4. Its colour is the buffer's pixel (premultiplied, linear) under c, that is at floor(c_u d + c_v n + the drawing's corner), held inside the buffer. 5. It covers the pixel by clamp(rho + 0.5 - the distance from (u, v) to the segment from c to c + l e, 0, 1). 6. Of the strokes covering the pixel (only cells i from floor((u - L - r') / s_u) - 1 to floor((u + r') / s_u) and j from floor((v - r' - L s) / s_v) - 1 to floor((v + r' + L s) / s_v) can, r' = 1.25 r + 0.5 and s = R tan 10 / sqrt(1 + (R tan 10)^2)), the two highest in the pile are laid on the paint surface, the lower first, each as colour times covering over what is below times 1 - covering times the colour's covering. 7. The surface: the pixel itself (original), nothing (transparent), white or black, each opaque. 8. The result is mixed back towards the pixel itself by Blend With Original / 100. The layer never grows: strokes past its edge are cut off. Settings: `stroke_angle` -100000 to 100000 degrees, 135 when added; `brush_size` 0.5 to 20 pixels, 2; `stroke_length` 0 to 100 pixels, 8; `stroke_density` 0.1 to 4, 1; `stroke_randomness` 0 to 2, 1; `paint_surface` `original` (when added), `transparent`, `white` or `black`; `blend_with_original` 0 to 100, 0; `random_seed` 0 to 100000, 0, its whole part; `animate` `on` (when added) or `off`. Every number can be keyed. Drawn on the card by one pass of its own, `brush`. FX-BRUSH-001 to 029 pin it.

Cartoon (D-448, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Cartoon (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours, nothing ported; `tools/cartoon_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, for a layer: 1. Smoothing. The layer goes through D-358's Bilateral Blur with Radius = Detail Radius, Threshold = Detail Threshold and Colorize on (`tools/bilateral_blur_reference.py`); call the result S. Every pixel keeps its covering. 2. Edges. Each pixel of S has its picture luma, its colour over black, encoded (D-146's, as Find Edges). Sobel's sums over the 3 by 3 round it, the border repeated, give gx and gy, and the edge measure g = 10 sqrt(gx^2 + gy^2) / 2 (a hard step from black to white gives 20). The pixel's edge E = clamp((g - Edge Threshold) / (10.01 - 10 Edge Contrast), 0, 1). 3. The line. With W = Edge Width and h = 0.5 + Edge Softness / 100 * W, a pixel's ink is Edge Opacity / 100 times the largest, over the pixels of S (itself among them) at distance d from it, of E there times clamp((W + 0.5 - d) / (2 h) + 0.5, 0, 1). Width 0 with Softness 0 is the edge pixel alone. 4. The fill, for a pixel of S that shows, its straight colour c: e = c through the sRGB curve for each of red, green and blue, and l = the luminance 0.2126 c_r + 0.7152 c_g + 0.0722 c_b through the curve (both held inside 0 to 1). With n = Shading Steps (its whole part) - 1 and s = Shading Smoothness / 100: q = l n, k = floor(q), t = q - k; the ramp is 1 if t >= 0.5 and 0 if not when s is 0, else clamp((t - 0.5) / s + 0.5, 0, 1); l' = (k + ramp) / n. The fill is clamp(e + l' - l, 0, 1). So Shading Steps sets how many shades there are, and Smoothness 100 leaves the shading as it was. 5. Edge Enhancement, x = Edge Enhancement / 100, when not 0: the fill f moves to clamp(f + x E (f - m), 0, 1), m the mean of the fills of the 3 by 3 round the pixel that show (the border repeated). Above 0 an edge's two sides are pushed apart, sharper; below 0 they are drawn together, softer. 6. The result, encoded, b = Edge Black Level: Fill & Edges, f (1 - ink) + b ink; Fill, f; Edges, (1 - b)(1 - ink) + b ink, the line on paper (black on white at 0, white on black at 1). Back to linear, premultiplied by the pixel's own covering. A pixel that does not show stays empty; the layer never grows. Settings: `render` `fill`, `edges` or `fill_and_edges` (when added); `detail_radius` 0 to 50 pixels, 8 when added; `detail_threshold` 0 to 255, 10; `shading_steps` 2 to 64, 8, its whole part; `shading_smoothness` 0 to 100, 40; `edge_threshold` 0 to 10, 1.6; `edge_width` 0 to 10, 1.5; `edge_softness` 0 to 100, 60; `edge_opacity` 0 to 100, 100; `edge_enhancement` -100 to 100, 0; `edge_black_level` 0 to 1, 0; `edge_contrast` 0 to 1, 0.5. Every number can be keyed. Drawn on the card by Bilateral Blur's two passes, then `cartoonedge` and `cartoon`. FX-CARTOON-001 to 032 pin it.

Block Load (D-449, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' CC Block Load (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the sources show, nothing ported; `tools/block_load_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, for a layer w by h pixels as it reaches the effect, the drawing's own corner at o in that buffer (o = 0 unless an earlier effect grew the layer): 1. n = Scans' whole part. The pictures are P_k for k = 0 to n: the layer cut into square blocks 2^(n - k) pixels wide (D-144's Mosaic: laid from the drawing's corner, cut short at the far edge, each block the mean of its premultiplied pixels), so P_0 is the roughest and P_n, with blocks one pixel wide, is the layer as it is. 2. The passes. Start Cleared on: the passes are k = 0 to n, T = n + 1 of them, and before the first the layer is empty. Off: the passes are k = 1 to n, T = n, and before the first the layer is P_0. 3. q = Completion / 100 * T; j = floor(q) passes are done and the one under way is j's, f = q - j of it. When j = T every pass is done and the result is P_n. Otherwise the pass under way paints P_k (k the j-th pass's) over what was there, row of blocks by row of blocks from the top: of the m rows of P_k's blocks (as Mosaic cuts them, the first and last perhaps cut short), the first floor(f m) are P_k; the rows below them keep the picture before this pass (P_(k-1), or empty before Start Cleared's first pass). 4. Bilinear, when on, replaces each flat block by a smooth blend: a pixel at (x + 0.5, y + 0.5) takes, across and then down, the straight-line blend of the means of the two blocks whose centres (the middle of each block as cut) are either side of it, or the edge block's mean alone beyond the first or last centre. Blocks one pixel wide are left as they are. The layer never grows. Settings: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `scans` 1 to 16, 4, its whole part; `start_cleared` `on` (when added) or `off`; `bilinear` `off` (when added) or `on`. Completion and Scans can be keyed. A draft scales the blocks with the picture. Which row a pass has reached is a choice made from a number, so `check` asserts that no case puts f m within 1e-6 of a whole number unless exactly on one. Drawn on the card by Mosaic's `tiles` pass (its mode 1 the bilinear blend), then `blockload`. FX-BLOCKLOAD-001 to 024 pin it.

Burn Film (D-460, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' CC Burn Film (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the sources show, nothing ported; `tools/burn_film_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, for a layer w by h pixels as it reaches the effect (its own buffer, grown or not by the effects above), D = sqrt(w^2 + h^2) its diagonal: 1. The burn points. The first is the centre, c = (Center / 100 w, Center / 100 h), starting at once (delay 0). Then six more, i = 1 to 6, each from D-119's number u(seed, i, 0, -1, ch) in -1..1 (seed = Random Seed's whole part): at ((u0 + 1) / 2 w, (u1 + 1) / 2 h), with delay 0.25 + 0.125 (u2 + 1), between 0.25 and 0.5. 2. Each pixel's burn time, at its centre p: the least, over the points q, of |p - q| / D plus the point's delay, plus a ragged edge 0.12 (n + 1) / 2, where n is D-127's fractal value noise in -1..1 (six octaves, the seed's, channel 0) at (p / (D / 10), depth 0). The time is never below 0. 3. M, the latest any pixel can burn: the farthest of the buffer's four corners from c, over D, plus 0.12. The burn front s = Burn / 100 (M + 0.08) - 0.06, so at Burn 0 it sits 0.06 before any pixel and at Burn 100 0.02 past the last. 4. g = the pixel's time - s. g >= 0.06: the pixel as it is. 0 <= g < 0.06, scorched: its colour times g / 0.06, darkening to black at the front, its covering unchanged. -0.02 < g < 0, the char: black, its covering times (1 + g / 0.02), fading out. g <= -0.02: burned away, empty. Every step meets the next without a jump. The layer never grows. Settings: `burn` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `center` two numbers, per cent of the picture, -1000 to 1000 each, (50, 50); `seed` 0 to 100000, 0, its whole part. All three can be keyed. Everything is measured against the picture's size, so a draft burns the same shapes. Drawn on the card by `burnfilm`, the burn points worked on the processor. FX-BURN-001 to 020 pin it.

Hex Tile (D-461, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' CC HexTile (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the sources show, nothing ported; `tools/hex_tile_reference.py` is the reference. The rule. W and H are the drawing's own size and o its place in the input buffer, which an earlier effect may have grown. The output is the input's size; nothing grows. 1. The centre C = (center_x / 100 W, center_y / 100 H). The honeycomb's origin O is C, or, with Lock Center Tile on, the drawing's middle (W / 2, H / 2). With a = rotate in radians, c = cos a and s = sin a. 2. At an output pixel whose centre is P, in the drawing's own space, d = P - O, turned into the honeycomb's frame: u = c d_x + s d_y, v = c d_y - s d_x. 3. The tiles are hexagons with a flat top and bottom, corners at R = radius from their middles, the middles at (3/2 R q, sqrt(3) R (r + q / 2)) for whole q and r. The pixel's tile is the nearest middle: q' = u (2 / (3 R)), r' = u (-1 / (3 R)) + v (sqrt(3) / (3 R)), and with x = q', z = r', y = -x - z, each rounded to the nearest whole number (half up, floor(t + 0.5)), the one rounded farthest is put back as minus the other two (x first, then y, then z; a tie keeps the earlier). Its middle is (m_u, m_v) = (3/2 R) q and sqrt(3) R (r + q / 2), and the pixel's place in it l = (u - m_u, v - m_v). 4. Render. Tile: l as it is, every tile the same. Fold Aligned and Fold Seamlessly cut each tile into six triangles from its middle to its corners and fill every one from the first, the one from 0 to 60 degrees (the angle from the u axis towards v). The triangle k is found with no angle worked: e0 = l_v, e1 = l_v / 2 - h l_u and e2 = -l_v / 2 - h l_u, h = sqrt(3) / 2; with e0 >= 0, k is 0 if e1 < 0, else 1 if e2 < 0, else 2; with e0 < 0, k is 3 if e1 > 0, else 4 if e2 > 0, else 5. Then l is turned back by k 60 degrees: (C_k l_u + S_k l_v, C_k l_v - S_k l_u), C_k and S_k the cosine and sine of k 60 degrees. Fold Aligned stops there: six turned copies, which meet at a seam. Fold Seamlessly then mirrors every odd triangle across the first one's middle line: (l_u / 2 + h l_v, h l_u - l_v / 2). Neighbouring triangles, and neighbouring tiles, then meet as mirror images, with no seam. 5. The picture is read at S = C + (c l_u - s l_v, s l_u + c l_v) + m (c m_u - s m_v, s m_u + c m_v), m = smearing / 100: at Smearing 0 every tile shows the picture round the centre, and at 100 with Lock Center Tile off, under Tile, the picture as it is. S plus o is folded back into the input by mirroring at its edges, each coordinate across a side n long becoming t mod 2n, or 2n - t past n, then held inside the pixel centres, as Kaleidoscope's (D-205) is, so the honeycomb never goes clear; and document 21's bilinear sample of the input there is the output. Tile and Fold Aligned make a yes-or-no choice from a pixel's place (its tile, and under Fold Aligned its triangle): `check` asserts that no pixel of those cases is within 1e-6 of a tile's edge or a triangle's. Settings: `render` "tile", "fold_aligned" or "fold_seamless", "tile" when added; `radius` 1 to 4000 pixels, 50, scaled with a draft as the other distances are; `center` two numbers, per cent of the drawing, -1000 to 1000 each, (50, 50); `lock_center` "on" or "off", "off"; `rotate` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `smearing` 0 to 100 per cent, 0. Words are kept as written, so a wrong one is reported. The numbers can be keyed. Drawn on the card by the warp pass's mode 100, the turn worked on the processor. FX-HEX-001 to 022 pin it.

Mr. Smoothie (D-462, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' CC Mr. Smoothie (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the sources show, nothing ported; `tools/mr_smoothie_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, at the holder's frame, with its input O, linear and premultiplied, W by H, its corner at o in the buffer an earlier effect may have grown: 1. The map is D-189's layer setting, fitted to the drawing's own W by H and lying on it, clear outside it; with the name "" (or a layer not in the composition, with the warning) it is O itself. F is at each pixel the map's `property` as CC Vector Blur reads it: red, green, blue, luminance, lightness, hue (over 360) or saturation of the straight encoded colour, times the covering; or the covering itself for `alpha`. 2. Bv is F through document 21's Gaussian at sigma `smoothness / 2`, nothing outside the drawing; at smoothness 0, Bv is F. 3. v = Bv held to 0..1, and t = v + `phase` / 360. Only a t below 0 or above 1 is wrapped, t - floor(t), so at Phase 0 the channel's 0 and 1 are the gradient's two ends. 4. The place along the gradient, s: AB, t; BA, 1 - t; ABA, 1 - |2 t - 1|; BAB, |2 t - 1|. So AB runs from A to B, BA back, and ABA and BAB go there and back, with no jump where t wraps. 5. A and B are `sample_a` and `sample_b`, per cent of the drawing's width and height, plus o. The colour is document 21's bilinear sample of O at P = A + s (B - A), P first held inside the buffer's pixel centres, made straight (its colour over its covering; black where it has none). 6. The output is that colour times O's own covering at the pixel: the layer keeps its shape and only its colours change. The layer does not grow. For a draft the smoothness is a distance. AB and BA jump from one end to the other where t wraps: `check` asserts that no pixel of those cases has a wrapped t within 1e-6 of a whole number. `layer` D-189's word, ""; `fit` `stretch`, `center` or `tile`, `stretch`; `property` one of the eight words above, `luminance`; `smoothness` 0 to 100 pixels, 5; `sample_a` and `sample_b` -1000 to 1000 per cent each way, 25, 50 and 75, 50; `phase` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `color_loop` `ab`, `ba`, `aba` or `bab`, `aba`. Every number keyable. Words are kept as written, so a wrong one is reported. Apart from Property, the values when added are chosen here; the manual gives none. After Effects' Blend w. Original is not in the manual and is not a setting; the effect's own mix (D-137) does it. Drawn on the card by Vector Blur's channel read, the card's Gaussian and the `smoothie` pass. FX-SMOOTHIE-001 to 026 pin it.

Plastic (D-463, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' CC Plastic (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the manual shows, nothing ported; `tools/plastic_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, at the holder's frame, with its input O, linear and premultiplied: 1. The map is D-189's layer setting, fitted to the drawing's own W by H and lying on it, clear outside it; with the name "" (or a layer not in the composition, with the warning) it is O itself. F is at each pixel the map's `property` as CC Vector Blur reads it: red, green, blue, luminance or lightness of the straight encoded colour, times the covering; or the covering itself for `alpha`. 2. Bv is F through document 21's Gaussian at sigma `softness / 2`, nothing outside the drawing, worked out past its edges as far as the kernel reaches; at softness 0, Bv is F and 0 outside. 3. The cut: with lo = `cut_min / 100` and hi = `cut_max / 100`, b = (Bv - lo) / (hi - lo) held to 0..1, so the values below lo lie flat at 0 and those above hi flat at 1; with lo above hi the bumps are turned inside out; with lo = hi, b is 1 where Bv is at least lo and 0 below. 4. The surface: N is (-k gx, -k gy, 1) made one long, g the central differences of b, `(b(x+1, y) - b(x-1, y)) / 2` and down the same, k = `height / 100` 1.25 max(softness, 1), as CC Glass slopes its bump. A negative height sinks the bright parts. 5. The light, one of the effect's own (After Effects' light layers are not offered): Lv, toward it, made one long, is (100 ux, 100 uy, `light_height`) for `distant`, (ux, uy) document 21's step at `light_direction` degrees clockwise from up; or for `point`, the light at `light_position` (per cent of the drawing's width and height) and `light_height` pixels above, Lv = (Lx - px, Ly - py, light_height) from the pixel's centre; at no length, none. 6. The shading: nl = N.Lv; diffuse max(nl, 0); dust, Lommel and Seeliger's law for a dusty surface seen from straight in front, nl / (nl + Nz) where nl is above 0, else 0; the reflection R = 2 nl N - Lv and the highlight Rz^(1 / roughness) where nl and Rz are above 0, else 0. With C the layer's straight colour (0 where it is clear), I = `light_intensity / 100`, Lc the light colour and Ac the ambient light colour in linear light, and ambient, diffuse, dust, specular and metal as shares of 100: each channel out = C (ambient Ac + diffuse I Lc diffuse-term + dust I Lc dust-term) + specular I (Lc + (C - Lc) metal) highlight. 7. The output is out times O's own covering, with that covering: the layer keeps its shape and does not grow. For a draft the softness is a distance, and so is the height of a point light. `layer` D-189's word, ""; `fit` `stretch`, `center` or `tile`, `stretch`; `property` red, green, blue, alpha, luminance or lightness, `alpha`; `softness` 0 to 100, 10; `height` -100 to 100, 50; `cut_min` and `cut_max` 0 to 100, 0 and 100; `light_intensity` 0 to 400, 100; `light_color` a colour, white; `light_type` `distant` or `point`, `distant`; `light_height` -1000 to 1000, 100; `light_position` -1000 to 1000 per cent each way, 30, 30; `light_direction` -3600 to 3600, -45; `ambient_light_color` a colour, white; `ambient` 25, `diffuse` 75, `dust` 0, `specular` 50 and `metal` 0, each 0 to 100; `roughness` 0.001 to 1, 0.05. Every number keyable. The values when added are chosen here (the light and shading Blobbylize's but Metal, 0 for plastic); the manual gives none. Dust's law is this program's choice: the manual names no formula. Drawn on the card by Vector Blur's channel read, the card's Gaussian and the `plastic` pass. FX-PLASTIC-001 to 031 pin it.

Repeat Tile (D-464, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' CC RepeTile (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the manual shows, nothing ported; `tools/repeat_tile_reference.py` is the reference. The rule. The input as it reaches the effect is w by h pixels, the pixels an earlier effect grew included, as Motion Tile's (D-154) is; that whole input is one tile. 1. Each expansion is made a whole number of pixels by rounding up: l, r, u and d. The output is w + 2 gx by h + 2 gy, gx = max(l, r) and gy = max(u, d), its origin moved by (gx, gy) so the picture stays where it was; the layer grows the same both ways, as Power Pin's (D-389) does. 2. Output pixel (X, Y) is at x = X - gx, y = Y - gy from the input's corner. Outside -l <= x < w + r and -u <= y < h + d it is clear. 3. Slide: the input pixel at (x, y) held inside it, clamp(x, 0, w - 1) and clamp(y, 0, h - 1). None: the input pixel at (x, y) inside the input, clear outside it. Every other: the tile i = floor(x / w) across and j = floor(y / h) down, Brick first moving every odd row of tiles half a tile right (x - floor(w / 2) when j is odd), the place in it lx = x - i w, ly = y - j h, and the tile's way o, a number 0 to 7 whose 1 bit flips across, 2 bit flips down and 4 bit swaps across and down: (u, v) = (lx, ly), or with 4 set (q(ly, h, w), q(lx, w, h)), q(t, n, m) = floor((2 t + 1) m / (2 n)), the place scaled from one side to the other; then u = w - 1 - u with 1 set and v = h - 1 - v with 2 set. So 0 is the picture, 1 flipped across, 2 flipped down, 3 turned half round, 4 flipped on its diagonal, 5 turned a quarter anticlockwise, 6 a quarter clockwise and 7 flipped on the other diagonal. The way for each Tiling, with p = (i + j) mod 2 and m the floor-based remainder: Repeat and Brick 0; Unfold (i mod 2) + 2 (j mod 2); Checker Flip H, Checker Flip V, Checker 180, Checker Flip 45, Checker 90 CW and Checker 90 CCW 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 5 when p is 1, else 0; Rosette 0, 6, 3 and 5 for (i mod 2, j mod 2) (0, 0), (1, 0), (1, 1) and (0, 1); Turn CW, Turn CCW and Twist a number of quarter turns clockwise, (i + j), -(i + j) and (i - j) mod 4, 0 to 3 being ways 0, 6, 3 and 5; Random the hash t of i and j, each as 32 unsigned bits, t = i 0x9E3779B1 xor j 0x85EBCA77, t = t xor (t >> 15), t = t 0x2C1B3C6D, t = t xor (t >> 12), all kept to 32 bits, and the way t mod 8. 4. Blend Borders (b = blend_borders / 100), except under Unfold, None and Slide: with (u, v) the input pixel step 3 chose, the pixel half a tile on, u' = (u + floor(w / 2)) mod w and v' = (v + floor(h / 2)) mod h, is mixed in towards the tile's edges: wx = clamp(1 - min(u + 1/2, w - u - 1/2) / (b w / 2), 0, 1), wy likewise from v and h, each 0 when b is 0, and the output is (1 - wy) ((1 - wx) P(u, v) + wx P(u', v)) + wy ((1 - wx) P(u, v') + wx P(u', v')), all four channels. Each side of a seam is then mostly the picture's middle, which runs on across it. 5. Without blending the output is the input pixel at (u, v), exactly. Every choice is made from whole numbers, so nothing sits on a cliff. All four expansions 0, and Blend Borders 0 or a Tiling that ignores it: the output is the input. Settings: `expand_right`, `expand_left`, `expand_down` and `expand_up` 0 to 4000 pixels, 0 when added, scaled with a draft as the other distances are; `tiling` one of the sixteen words below, "unfold" when added; `blend_borders` 0 to 100 per cent, 0. Words are kept as written, so a wrong one is reported. The numbers can be keyed. Drawn on the card by the warp pass's mode 101. FX-REPEAT-001 to 030 pin it.

Scatter (D-465, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Scatter (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so this rule is ours beyond what the Qt ports show, nothing ported; `tools/scatter_reference.py` is the reference. The rule. The input as it reaches the effect is w by h pixels, the pixels an earlier effect grew included; the output is the same size and sits where the input did. 1. The reach is n = floor(a + 1/2) whole pixels, a the Scatter Amount (draft-scaled). With n 0 the output is the input. 2. The frame number used is f, the composition frame when Randomize Every Frame is on, else 0. 3. For output pixel (x, y), counted from the input's corner, the hash of (x, y, f, k), k 0 for across and 1 for down, each as 32 unsigned bits: t = x 0x9E3779B1 xor y 0x85EBCA77 xor f 0xC2B2AE3D xor k 0x27D4EB2F, t = t xor (t >> 15), t = t 0x2C1B3C6D, t = t xor (t >> 12), t = t 0x297A2D39, t = t xor (t >> 15), all kept to 32 bits. 4. The move across is dx = (t(k = 0) mod (2 n + 1)) - n, 0 when Grain is Vertical; the move down is dy = (t(k = 1) mod (2 n + 1)) - n, 0 when Grain is Horizontal. Each is a whole number from -n to n. 5. The output pixel is the input pixel at (x + dx, y + dy), exactly, or clear where that lies outside the input. Every choice is made from whole numbers, so nothing sits on a cliff, and no pixel is mixed: the layer's edge frays inwards and nothing grows past it. Settings: `scatter_amount` 0 to 127 pixels, 10 when added, scaled with a draft as the other distances are; `grain` "both", "horizontal" or "vertical", "both"; `randomize` "on" or "off", "off". Words are kept as written, so a wrong one is reported. The amount can be keyed. Drawn on the card by the warp pass's mode 102. FX-SCATTER-001 to 019 pin it.

Strobe Light (D-466, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Strobe Light (Stylize), for which only the controls were published (Premiere 6.0's help), so the operators past Copy and the random draw are ours, nothing ported; `tools/strobe_light_reference.py` is the reference. The rule. The input as it reaches the effect is w by h pixels, premultiplied, in the working (linear) space. 1. The time is t, the layer's key time from its in point in seconds, and the count is k = floor of the same in frames. The strobe is on when the period P is at most the duration D, or when t - P floor(t / P) < D; or when the random draw for k is below the probability: the hash of (k, s), s the seed's floor, each as 32 unsigned bits, h = k 0x9E3779B1 xor s 0x85EBCA77, h = h xor (h >> 15), h = h 0x2C1B3C6D, h = h xor (h >> 12), h = h 0x297A2D39, h = h xor (h >> 15), all kept to 32 bits, is on when h / 2^32 < probability / 100. 2. When it is off, the output is the input. 3. When it is on, with b = blend_with_original / 100: Makes Layer Transparent scales every channel of every pixel by b, so the layer fades to clear. Operates On Color Only takes each pixel's straight colour c (its colour over its alpha; a clear pixel stays clear) and the strobe colour s, in the working space, works r per channel, holds it in 0 to 1, and gives (1 - b) r + b c times the pixel's own alpha, the alpha kept: Copy s; Add c + s; Subtract c - s; Multiply c s; Difference |c - s|; And, Or and Xor the bitwise operation on floor(255 c + 1/2) and floor(255 s + 1/2), c and s held in 0 to 1 first, over 255; Minimum and Maximum the smaller and the larger, channel by channel; Darken and Lighten the whole colour, c or s, whose brightness 0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b is lower (Darken) or higher (Lighten), c on a tie, then held in 0 to 1 too. Settings: `strobe_color` a colour, white; `blend_with_original` 0 to 100, 0; `strobe_duration` 0 to 100 seconds, 0.1; `strobe_period` 0 to 100 seconds, 1; `random_strobe_probability` 0 to 100 per cent, 0; `strobe` "color_only" or "transparent", "color_only"; `strobe_operator` one of the twelve words below, "copy"; `random_seed` 0 to 100000, its whole part counted, 0. Words are kept as written, so a wrong one is reported. The numbers can be keyed. Drawn on the card by the warp pass's mode 103. FX-STROBE-001 to 030 pin it.

Texturize (D-467, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Texturize (Stylize), for which no formula was published, so the relief, Emboss's read from the texture, is ours, nothing ported; `tools/texturize_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, at the holder's frame, with its input O, linear and premultiplied: 1. The texture is D-189's layer setting, fitted to the drawing's own W by H by `fit` (`tile`, `center` or `stretch`, After Effects' three placements) and lying on it. With the name "" (no texture, as the effect is added) or a layer not in the composition (with the warning), nothing happens; nor with Texture Contrast 0. 2. t is at each pixel the texture's luminance as CC Vector Blur reads it: the encoded luminance of its straight colour, times its covering; 0 where the texture is clear or not there. 3. d is one pixel along u(`light_direction`), u(θ) = (sin θ, -cos θ), degrees clockwise from up, exact at whole quarter turns. At a pixel with covering a > 0 and centre P, each sample of t document 21's bilinear sample with the point first held inside the pixel centres (Emboss's held sample): v = `texture_contrast` (t(P + d) - t(P - d)) / 2, so the side of each bright part facing along d turns lighter and the far side darker. 4. Each encoded straight channel e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(O.rgb / a, 0, 1)) becomes e + v, and the output is (srgb_to_linear(clamp(e + v, 0, 1)) a, a): the layer keeps its colours, its covering and its size, and a pixel with a = 0 stays as it is. `layer` D-189's word, ""; `fit` `tile`, `center` or `stretch`, `tile`; `light_direction` -3600 to 3600, 135; `texture_contrast` 0 to 2, 1. Every number keyable. Adobe's help gives no values; the ones when added and the contrast's range are chosen here, as is the half in step 3. Drawn on the card by CC Vector Blur's height pass and the texturize pass. FX-TEXTURIZE-001 to 020 pin it.

Channel Threshold (D-468, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Threshold (Stylize), a second name over Threshold's rule (D-138, as D-383 and D-394); CycoreFX's manual says what each channel does, and how luminance, saturation and the blend are read is ours; `tools/channel_threshold_reference.py` is the reference. The rule. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its encoded straight colour (Threshold's), b its straight linear colour, and o = 1 - blend / 100: decide(v) = 1 if 255 v + 1e-4 >= threshold else 0, flipped by Invert luminance M = (d, d, d), d = decide(Y(e)), Y Threshold's encoded luma rgb M = (decide(e.r), decide(e.g), decide(e.b)) saturation M = (d, d, d), d = decide(S(e)), S the HSL saturation (D-113's to_hsl) out = ((b + o (M - b)) a, a) alpha t = decide(a), a' = a + o (t - a); out = (b a', a') A pixel that does not show stays as it is, so the layer never grows. Luminance with Invert off and Blend 0 is exactly Threshold at the same level; `check` asserts it against tools/threshold_reference.py's own numbers. Drawn on the card by the tone pass's mode 100. FX-CHTHRESH-001 to 023 pin it.

Threshold RGB (D-469, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Threshold RGB (Stylize), a second name over Threshold's rule beside Channel Threshold (D-468, as D-383 and D-394); CycoreFX's manual says what each channel does, and the blend is ours; `tools/threshold_rgb_reference.py` is the reference. decide_c = 1 if 255 e_c + 1e-4 >= threshold_c else 0, flipped by that channel's Invert out = ((b + o (M - b)) a, a), M = (decide_r, decide_g, decide_b), o = 1 - blend / 100 at a pixel with covering a > 0, e its encoded straight colour and b its straight linear colour. A pixel that does not show stays as it is; the covering never changes. Drawn on the card by the tone pass's mode 100. FX-THRESHRGB-001 to 014 pin it.

Contour Paint (D-470, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Contour Paint (Stylize, beta), an effect of its own, the rule ours as Adobe publishes none (a coherence-enhancing smear, Kyprianidis and Kang 2011, with Line Blur's tensor, D-183); `tools/contour_paint_reference.py` is the reference. The rule, at the holder's frame, with its input O, linear and premultiplied, w by h, and px the size of a pixel (1 at Full, D-259's fraction at Draft): 1. The flow is read from a picture F: the layer's own drawing as it is at each pass, or, with `edge_map_layer` naming a layer (D-189's layer setting, centred on the drawing and lying on it), that layer once for all passes. A layer not in the composition is said, and the layer's own drawing is used. 2. l is at each pixel F's luminance as CC Vector Blur reads it: the encoded luminance of its straight colour, times its covering. Its slopes gx, gy are Sobel's over 8, each neighbour held inside the picture. The tensor (gx², gx gy, gy²) is blurred by D-109's held Gaussian of sigma (0.5 + 4.5 `cleanliness` / 100) px. 3. With the blurred tensor (a, b, c), r = sqrt((a - c)² + 4 b²) and the flow t is Line Blur's: (b, -(a - c + r) / 2) when a >= c, else (-(c - a + r) / 2, b), made one long, or none where its length is 0. Its strength is e = min(1, 4 sqrt(r)). 4. The flow is turned `offset_flow_angles` degrees clockwise, (x cos - y sin, x sin + y cos), exact at whole quarter turns, then, with `quantize_flow_angles` q > 0, snapped to the first of the directions (cos kq, sin kq), k = 0 to ceil(180 / q) - 1, whose |dot| with it is largest. 5. One pass of the smear: sigma s = (0.5 + 7.5 `stylization` / 100) px, n = ceil(2 s) steps. A pixel with a flow walks from its centre both ways, along t and along -t: each step one pixel along the way it faces, the point then held inside the pixel centres, the picture sampled there (document 21's bilinear sample of the premultiplied pixels) at weight exp(-k² / 2 s²) for step k, and the way it faces then becomes the bilinear mix of the flow at the four pixels round the point, each turned to face the same way first (negated where its dot with the way is below 0), made one long; the walk ends early where that mix's length is 1e-6 or less. S is the pixel and its samples, weighted (the pixel at weight 1), and the pass's result is O' = O + e (S - O). Without `apply_to_alpha` the covering is kept: each result pixel is its straight colour times the covering it had before the pass. 6. `smear_iterations` (floored) passes, each from the last one's result. 7. With `draw_color_fills` not `none`: the palette is made from O, sampled every g pixels across and down from the corner, g = max(1, floor(sqrt(w h / 4096))), at every sample with a covering, each the encoded straight colour. `median` is Heckbert's median cut to `posterization_level` (floored) boxes: while there are fewer, the box whose largest channel range is widest (the first on a tie, none when every range is 0) is sorted, stably, on that channel (the lowest on a tie) and cut in two at half its length rounded down, the upper half put after it; the palette is each box's mean. `k_means` starts from that palette and moves each colour to the mean of the samples nearest it, 8 times, a colour with none staying. Each pixel of the smeared picture with a covering takes the palette colour nearest its encoded straight colour (the first on a tie), at its covering, where the flow strength of the smeared picture (steps 1 to 3 read from it, or the edge-map layer) times 100 lies from `low_strength` to `high_strength`. `over_smeared_image` keeps the smeared pixel elsewhere; `only` leaves elsewhere clear. 8. View `edge_map` instead shows steps 1 to 4 read once from O (or the edge-map layer): each pixel opaque grey, its encoded value e, and over it in cells 12 px square from the corner a cyan (0, 1, 1) line through the cell's middle along the flow at the pixel nearest the middle (held in the picture), 0.4 of the cell long each way, laid on at that pixel's e times clamp(1 - d / px, 0, 1), d the distance from the pixel's centre to the line. Nothing happens with View `final_result`, Smear Iterations 0 and Draw Color Fills `none`. `view` `final_result` or `edge_map`, `final_result`; `stylization` 0 to 100, 50; `cleanliness` 0 to 100, 50; `smear_iterations` 0 to 100, 3; `apply_to_alpha` `off` or `on`, `off`; `draw_color_fills` `none`, `over_smeared_image` or `only`, `none`; `reduction_method` `k_means` or `median`, `k_means`; `posterization_level` 2 to 32, 8; `low_strength` and `high_strength` 0 to 100, 0 and 100; `offset_flow_angles` -3600 to 3600, 0; `quantize_flow_angles` 0 to 90, 0; `edge_map_layer` D-189's word, "". Every number keyable. Every value, range and constant here is chosen by this program; Match to Nearest Custom Color, the custom colour set, Freehand Flow Adjustments, the arrows' heads and the External Edge Map's placement are not built (gaps logged). Drawn on the card in five passes of its own and the shared Gaussian, except with Quantize Flow Angles above 0, a step, which stays on the processor (D-122's reason). FX-CONTOUR-001 to 032 pin it.

Fast Blur (Legacy) (D-471, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Fast Blur (Legacy) (Obsolete), a second name over Fast Box Blur's rule (D-327, as D-383 and D-394); the 0.37 factor is Maschwitz's visual match, not Adobe's; `tools/fast_blur_legacy_reference.py` is the reference. So `core.fast_blur_legacy`: blurriness 0 to 1000, keyable, 0 when added (After Effects' start); radius = 0.37 x it dimensions "both", "horizontal" or "vertical", as Gaussian Blur's (D-303) edges "transparent" or "repeat" (Repeat Edge Pixels), as Gaussian Blur's (D-109) drawn as Fast Box Blur at radius 0.37 x Blurriness and 3 iterations, with the same dimensions and edges. The factor 0.37 is Maschwitz's visual match, not Adobe's; the range's top, 1000 (radius 370, inside Fast Box Blur's 500), is ours. Drawn on the card by Fast Box Blur's passes. FX-FASTLEG-001 to 013 pin it.

Gaussian Blur (Legacy) (D-472, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Gaussian Blur (Legacy) (Obsolete), a second name over Gaussian Blur's rule in Blurriness (D-321, as D-383 and D-394); the old effect's own sigma is not published; `tools/gaussian_blur_legacy_reference.py` is the reference. So `core.gaussian_blur_legacy`: blurriness 0 to 500, keyable, 0 when added; Gaussian Blur's Blurriness: sigma 0.3 x it, the kernel reaching ceil(6.5 sigma) pixels (D-321) dimensions "both", "horizontal" or "vertical", as Gaussian Blur's (D-303) drawn as Gaussian Blur in Blurriness with those dimensions and its edges always transparent (the layer grows by the reach). The old effect's own sigma is not published: Gaussian Blur's Blurriness rule is taken, as Adobe's upgrade script takes it; the range's top, 500, is Gaussian Blur's. Drawn on the card by Gaussian Blur's passes, both ways only (D-303). FX-GAUSSLEG-001 to 011 pin it.

Basic Text (D-473, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Basic Text (Obsolete), Numbers' words rule (D-526) with the words typed and proportional spacing on; `tools/basic_text_reference.py` is the reference. The settings, each in the file under its own name: - text: the words, up to 1000 characters, "Text" when added; a line break starts a new line below at the font's own line height (D-263) - position: two numbers, per cent of the drawing's width and height, -1000 to 1000, 50, 50 when added: where the first line's baseline meets its aligned end; keyable - display: "fill_only" (when added), "stroke_only", "fill_over_stroke" or "stroke_over_fill" - fill_color, "#ffffff" when added; stroke_color, "#ff3300" when added; encoded sRGB, "#rrggbb" - stroke_width: 0 to 500 pixels, 3 when added; keyable - size: 1 to 2000 pixels (the words' em), 64 when added; keyable - tracking: thousandths of the size, -1000 to 1000, added after each character, 0 when added; keyable - composite_on_original: "on" (when added) or "off" - font: a font file's name as text layers name one (D-263), the bundled "MPLUSRounded1c-Regular.ttf" when added - alignment: "left", "center" (when added) or "right" The values when added are Numbers' (D-526), so the two agree; After Effects' own are not published here. Not built, and said so in EFFECTS.md: Style (a font file names its style, as a text layer's does), Direction (Vertical and Rotate: our text engine lays words across only) and Line Spacing (After Effects' unit is not published; the font's own line height is used).
