An empty text draws nothing: the layer as it was with composite on original "on", nothing with it "off". A font this computer does not have is said each frame as `TEXT_FONT_MISSING` and nothing is drawn, the layer left as it was (D-263). On the card the words are drawn on the processor and laid on by the card's over pass, as Numbers'. FX-BTEXT-001 to 025 pin it.

Contour Paint, Match to Nearest Custom Color (D-584, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Contour Paint (Stylize, beta) Reduction Method of that name, a Reduction Method of Contour Paint (D-470); `tools/contour_paint_reference.py` is the reference. D-584: `match_to_nearest_custom_color` (After Effects' Match to Nearest Custom Color) takes the palette from `colors` instead, each `#rrggbb` its encoded colour, and nearest is the smallest CIE76 colour difference: the straight Euclidean distance in CIE L*a*b* (D-400's: the sRGB matrix of IEC 61966-2-1 and its own white, L* a* b* unscaled) between the custom colour taken to linear and the pixel's encoded straight colour taken back to linear, the first on a tie. Posterization Level is not used. With no colours nothing is filled. On the card the fill pass takes the L*a*b* key by a flag; the palette's keys are worked on the processor. FX-CONTOUR-033 to 041 pin it.

Curl Noise, Source This Layer and Other Layer (D-583, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Curl Noise (Noise & Grain, 26.3) Source menu, Curl Noise's (D-446) Source This Layer and Other Layer; `tools/curl_noise_reference.py` is the reference. D-583, Source This Layer or Other Layer: steps 2's noise and slope come from a picture instead. S1. The picture: This Layer, the layer's pixels as they reach the effect (the whole buffer, so what an effect above grew is in it); Other Layer, D-189's map of the layer `source_layer` names, fitted to the drawing by Source Mapping, `fill` stretched over it (D-189's stretch) or `center` at its own size in the middle (D-189's center). Other Layer naming no layer is refused; a name not in the composition draws the frame without the effect, with D-189's EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING. S2. Its lightness plane: each pixel's luminance (`grade::phase_of`: Rec. 709 weights on the straight linear colour, through the sRGB curve) times its covering, so an empty pixel is 0, kept in single precision. With Softness above 0 the plane is blurred by D-109's Gaussian with the edges held, sigma softness / 10 pixels, its sums in single precision. S3. At the point p, after the slide of step 1 (p' = p - offset - drift frame), the plane is read at p' on the picture (This Layer: p' plus the buffer corner's distance from the drawing's), pixel centres at halves, between pixels as document 21's bilinear does; outside the plane 0, or with Mirror at Edge on, the pixel mirrored back in (... 1 0 | 0 1 ... w-1 | w-1 ...). The picture is read at its own size: Size and Density set only the seed's blocks (step 4). S4. N = 2 L(p') - 1, and the slope is L one pixel right less L one pixel left, across, and the same down; a slope under 1e-5 long is nought. Smoothness and the depth do not touch N; Evolution and Turbulence Speed still move the seed. Steps 3 to 8 are unchanged. On the card the plane is made by the lightness pass, Softness by `curlsoft` summing exactly as the processor does, and the walk reads the field by `curl_sample`, the same exact sums. FX-CURL-045 to 060 pin it.

Curl Noise, Transform (D-646, 2026-10-13), after After Effects' Curl Noise Rotation, Scale and Perspective Offset; `tools/curl_transform_reference.py` is the reference. With c = (drawing width / 2, drawing height / 2) in the drawing's own pixels (a Motion Tile that grows the buffer does not move it), m = 100 / Scale, theta = Rotation in degrees and shift = Offset + drift x frame as in D-446, octave o (0 to 3) of the made noise is read at p_o = p - w_o shift, w_o = 0.5^o with Perspective Offset on, else 1, then q_o = c + m R(-theta) (p_o - c), R(-theta) (x, y) = (x cos theta + y sin theta, -x sin theta + y cos theta), in place of D-446's slid point. The slope summed in q (divided by the weights' total and Size as before) is (gx, gy) -> m (gx cos theta - gy sin theta, gx sin theta + gy cos theta) before Swirl and Vertical Bias. The seed is read at q_0. With Rotation 0 and Scale 100 q_o = p_o exactly. With Source This Layer or Other Layer the picture is read at p - shift (D-583), unturned; only the seed uses q_0.

Contour Paint, Arrowheads (D-585, 2026-10-12), after the cyan arrows After Effects' Contour Paint (Stylize, beta) Edge Map view draws, the Arrowheads of Contour Paint's (D-470) Edge Map view; `tools/contour_paint_reference.py` is the reference. D-585: with `arrowheads` `on` each line has a head at its end along the flow t (the line's middle plus 0.4 of the cell times t): two strokes from that end, 0.2 of the cell long, along -t turned 30 degrees each way (cos 30 = 0.8660254037844386, sin 30 = 0.5); d is then the distance to the nearest of the line and the two strokes. So Offset Flow Angles 180 turns the heads to the other ends. On the card the Edge Map pass draws the heads by a flag, in double precision. FX-CONTOUR-042 to 047 pin it.

Match Grain (D-454, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Match Grain (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/match_grain_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.match_grain`: `layer`, "" (none, when added) or the id of a layer of the composition, and every setting of Add Grain (D-443) with its range and value when added. The noise source is read whole, as Color Link's (D-375): its picture as drawn with its own effects, at the frame. In it, each pixel with covering a > 0 gives e_c = srgb(clamp(c / a, 0, 1)) for red, green and blue (D-88's curve). For every 3 by 3 window whose nine pixels all have a > 0, f_c = sum of M * e_c with M = [[1,-2,1],[-2,4,-2],[1,-2,1]]; over the N such windows, sigma_c = sqrt(pi / 2) / (6 N) * sum |f_c| (Immerkaer 1996), and sigma_c = 0 when N = 0. The layer then takes Add Grain's rule with red, green and blue intensity each multiplied by sigma_c * 10 * sqrt(3). No noise source, intensity 0 or every channel 0 is the layer unchanged; a noise source that is no layer is the layer unchanged with EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING. On the card: the measure on the processor, then Add Grain's pass. FX-MATCHGRAIN-001 to 023 pin it.

Remove Grain (D-455, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Remove Grain (Noise & Grain), for which no formula was found, so this rule is ours. `tools/remove_grain_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.remove_grain`: `noise_reduction` n 0 to 3 (1), `passes` 1 to 4 (1, its whole part P), `mode` "multichannel" (when added) or "single_channel", `unsharp_amount` 0 to 500 (0), `unsharp_radius` 0 to 100 (1), `unsharp_threshold` 0 to 255 (0). The layer as it reaches the effect is measured as Match Grain measures its source (D-454): sigma_c for red, green and blue. h_c = 2 n sigma_c; Multichannel uses h = 2 n sqrt((sigma_r^2 + sigma_g^2 + sigma_b^2) / 3) for all three. Each pixel's straight colour goes through D-88's sRGB curve, held in 0 to 1: e. Pass k = 1 to P, on the last pass's e: each pixel with covering a > 0 becomes sum_j w_j e_j / sum_j w_j over the taps j with dx^2 + dy^2 <= 4 k^2 that lie on the layer and have a_j > 0, w_j = a_j exp(-(dx^2 + dy^2) / (2 k^2)) times exp(-D^2 / (2 h^2)) with D^2 the mean over the channels of (e_j - e)^2 (Multichannel), or per channel exp(-(e_j,c - e_c)^2 / (2 h_c^2)), a channel with h_c = 0 keeping its value (Single Channel). The result goes back through the curve, times a, which is kept. n = 0 or every h 0 skips this, the pixels untouched. Then D-147's sharpen with D-317's threshold (Amount, Radius, Threshold). On the card: the measure on the processor before the frame, then the degrain pass (mode 0 encode, mode 1 one pass, mode 2 decode) and Sharpen's pass; a threshold above 0 with an amount above 0 stays on the processor. FX-REMOVEGRAIN-001 to 021 pin it.

Deband (D-456, 2026-10-10), our own effect after CelFX's Deband, whose page names the controls and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/deband_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.deband`: `view` "normal" (when added), "edge_only" or "selected_edge"; `edge_threshold` T 0 to 255 (4); `edge_softness` S 0 to 255 (2); `blur_amount` 0 to 100 (8); `edge_blur_reduction` 0 to 100 (100); `diffusion_amount` D 0 to 20 (1); `edge_diffusion_reduction` 0 to 100 (100); `diffusion_reseed_interval` 0 to 100 (1, its whole part); `noise_amount` 0 to 32 (1); `edge_noise_reduction` 0 to 100 (100); `noise_reseed_interval` 0 to 100 (1, its whole part). Each pixel that shows has its straight colour through D-88's sRGB curve, held in 0 to 1: e. Its step g, in levels, is the largest |e_n - e| over the channels and the four neighbours that lie on the layer and show; E = 0 for g <= T, 1 for g >= T + S, (g - T) / S between (S 0: 1 only where g > T). The blur, across then down, with document 21's Gaussian taps k (sigma blur / 3): each tap t lends a_t (1 - E_t) k times the product of (1 - E) over the pixels strictly between; blurred = sum of e times weight over sum of weight (e where that is 0); m = e + (blurred - e)(1 - E reduction / 100). The diffusion reads m at round(D s u_x), round(D s u_y) pixels away (half away from 0), s = 1 - E reduction / 100, u D-119's hash (seed 0, channels 0 and 1, depth the diffusion's step); off the layer or empty: its own m; else d = m_tap (1 - E_tap) + m E_tap. The noise: out = d + (u_2 + u_3) / 2 x noise / 255 x (1 - E reduction / 100), held in 0 to 1, depth the noise's step. A step is floor(frame / n) with a reseed interval's whole part n >= 1, else 0. Back through the curve, times the covering, which is kept. Edge Only shows E as grey; Selected Edge out (1 - E) + (1, 0, 0) E. A draft scales blur and D. On the card: the deband pass, mode 0 (e and 1 - E), modes 2 and 3 (the blur across into a single-precision buffer, then down, in single precision since D-550), mode 5 (diffusion, noise, views, E again in double precision). FX-DEBAND-001 to 024 pin it.

Advanced Spill Suppressor (D-457, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Advanced Spill Suppressor, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/advanced_spill_suppressor_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.advanced_spill_suppressor`: `method` "standard" (when added) or "ultra"; `suppression` 0 to 100 (100), S its share; read only for Ultra: `key_color` `#rrggbb` (#00ff00), `tolerance` T 0 to 100 (50), `desaturate` 0 to 100 (25), `spill_range` 0 to 100 (50), `spill_color_correction` 0 to 100 (50), `luma_correction` 0 to 100 (50); D, R, C and U their shares. Each pixel that shows has its straight colour through D-88's sRGB curve: e. Standard: for green and for blue, with p and q the other two, where e_s - max(e_p, e_q) > 0 the channel becomes e_s - S (e_s - max(e_p, e_q)). Ultra: s is the key's largest channel (green before blue before red on a tie), p and q the other two; h_k and h the HSV hues of the key and of e; if either is a grey the pixel is kept; else d = |((h - h_k + 180) mod 360) - 180|, w = max(0, 1 - d / (1.8 T)); with hi and lo the larger and smaller of e_p and e_q, L = hi - R (hi - lo); x = S w max(0, e_s - L). Then o_s = e_s - x, o_p and o_q each + C x / 2; f = x / e_s (0 when e_s is 0); o moves toward its luma Y (0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722) by D f; each channel + U (Y(e) - Y(o)). Held in 0 to 1, back through the curve, times the covering, which is kept. Suppression 0, or Ultra at Tolerance 0, leaves the layer exactly as it is. On the card: the tone pass, mode 61, its settings `grade::spill_settings`. FX-SPILL-001 to 028 pin it.

CC Simple Wire Removal (D-458, 2026-10-10), after CycoreFX's CC Simple Wire Removal, whose maths Cycore does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/simple_wire_removal_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.simple_wire_removal`: `point_a`, `point_b` in per cent of the layer's own width and height, -1000 to 1000 ((25, 50), (75, 50)); `removal_style` "fade", "frame_offset", "displace" (when added) or "displace_horizontal"; `thickness` T 0 to 100 pixels (5, scaled with the picture in a draft); `slope` 0 to 100 (50); `mirror_blend` 0 to 100 (25), m its share; `frame_offset` -1000 to 1000 (-1), taken down to a whole frame. A and B in pixels, L = |B - A|, u the unit step from A to B, n = (-u_y, u_x); for a pixel's centre P, t = (P - A) . u / L and d = (P - A) . n. With h = T / 2, r = T (1 - slope / 100) and E = h + r: w = 1 for |d| <= h, 1 - (|d| - h) / r out to E, 0 past E or for t outside 0 to 1. Each pixel becomes c (1 - w) + R w, premultiplied: Fade R = 0; Frame Offset R = the layer's own pixel at the frame plus the offset, with the effects before this one (the picture as it was where the layer does not show there, or on an adjustment layer, which says TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED); Displace, with F = A + t L u, R = (1 - m) I + m M, I the samples at F - E n and F + E n mixed by (d + E) / (2 E), M the sample at F + s (2 E - |d|) n, s the sign of d (1 at 0); Displace Horizontal the same with each sample taken along P's own row at the same d, and R = c when the wire lies flat. Samples bilinear, the point held inside the pixel centres (D-109). The layer does not grow. Thickness 0, or A on B, leaves the layer exactly as it is. On the card: the `wire` pass of the effect shader. FX-WIRE-001 to 030 pin it.

Color Difference Key (D-459, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Color Difference Key, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours, after Stefan Ihringer's reverse engineering (bildfehler.de/fx/cdk.html, 2007). `tools/color_difference_key_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.color_difference_key`: `view` "final" (when added), "source", "partial_a_uncorrected", "partial_a", "partial_b_uncorrected", "partial_b", "matte_uncorrected" or "matte"; `key_color` #rrggbb (#0000ff); for partial A and B Levels' five numbers `partial_a_in_black`, `_in_white`, `_gamma`, `_out_black`, `_out_white` (and `partial_b_`), 0 to 255 but the gamma 0.1 to 10, plain when added; `matte_in_black`, `matte_in_white`, `matte_gamma` (0, 255, 1). At a pixel with covering a > 0, e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a)); H its hue (0 for a grey), L = (max + min) / 2, S = (max - min) / (1 - |2L - 1|) (0 for a grey); K the key's hue (0 for a grey key). w(c) = clamp((d - 60) / 60, 0, 1), d the distance round the wheel from H to c; t_B = S w(K) + (1 - S) / 2, t_A = S w(K - 60) + (1 - S) / 2. Below L = 1/2, A = 2 t_A L and B = 1 - 2 (1 - t_B) L; from there A = 1 - 2 (1 - t_A)(1 - L) and B = 2 t_B (1 - L). Each corrected partial is its partial through D-112's levels on 255 t, over 255; the uncorrected matte is 1 - (1 - A)(1 - B); the corrected matte M is the screen of the corrected partials through the matte's levels (output 0 to 255). "final": every premultiplied channel times M; "source": the layer exactly; any other view m: rgb = srgb_to_linear(m) a, a kept. A pixel with a = 0 is left as it is. The gammas' powers in single precision on the card, as Levels'. On the card: the tone pass, mode 70. FX-CDK-001 to 026 pin it.

Color Range (D-500, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Color Range, whose maths and starting values Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/color_range_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.color_range`: `color_space` "lab" (when added), "yuv" or "rgb"; `fuzziness`, `min_1`, `max_1`, `min_2`, `max_2`, `min_3`, `max_3`, each 0 to 255, 0 when added (a min above its max is allowed). At a pixel with covering a > 0, e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1)). Its parts v1, v2, v3: "rgb" 255 e; "yuv" Y = 255 (0.299 r + 0.587 g + 0.114 b), U = 128 + 255 (-0.168736 r - 0.331264 g + 0.5 b), V = 128 + 255 (0.5 r - 0.418688 g - 0.081312 b); "lab" from Shadow/Highlight's Lab (l, p, q) of the linear colour srgb_to_linear(e): L = 255 l, a = 128 + 128 p, b = 128 + 128 q, not clamped. d = max over the three of max(min_i - v_i, v_i - max_i, 0). The share kept m = min(1, d / fuzziness); with fuzziness 0, m = 0 when d = 0 and 1 otherwise. Every premultiplied channel times m. A pixel with a = 0 is left as it is. On the card: the tone pass, mode 71. FX-CRANGE-001 to 018 pin it.

Difference Matte (D-501, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Difference Matte, whose maths and starting values Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/difference_matte_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.difference_matte`: `view` "final" (when added) or "matte"; `layer`, D-189's layer setting, "" when added; `fit` "center" (when added) or "stretch"; `matching_tolerance` 0 to 100, 15 when added; `matching_softness` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `blur_before_difference`, a Gaussian sigma in pixels, 0 to 100, 0 when added. With no layer named, or one named that is not in the composition (D-189's warning), the layer is left as it is. Otherwise P is the layer's map, fitted to the drawing by `fit`. With the blur above 0, the input O and P are each blurred by this document's Gaussian at that sigma (transparent outside, the kernel cut at ceil(3 sigma)), only for the comparison. At pixel (x, y), S = O'(x, y) and M = P'(x - ox, y - oy), transparent outside; each taken to straight encoded colour, c = linear_to_srgb(clamp(rgb / a, 0, 1)) when a > 0, else 0, with the covering min(a, 1). d = 100 max(|dr|, |dg|, |db|, |da|). The share kept m = clamp((d - tolerance) / softness, 0, 1); with softness 0, m = 0 where d <= tolerance and 1 elsewhere. "final": every premultiplied channel of O times m; "matte": the colour srgb_to_linear(m) O.a in every channel and the covering O.a. On the card: the `dmatte` pass. FX-DMATTE-001 to 026 pin it.

Inner/Outer Key (D-502, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Inner/Outer Key, whose maths and starting values Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/inner_outer_key_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.inner_outer_key`: `foreground` and `background` 0 to 1000, 0 when added, the layer's masks numbered so (the floor taken; switched on with two points or more; in the drawing's own space; whatever their mode), 0 for none; `single_mask_highlight_radius` 0 to 1000 pixels, 10 when added; `edge_thin` -100 to 100 pixels; `edge_feather` 0 to 1000 pixels; `edge_threshold` 0 to 100; `blend_with_original` 0 to 100; `invert_extraction` "off" (when added) or "on"; the numbers 0 when added but the radius. Foreground 0 leaves the layer as it is; a mask named and not found leaves it as it is with EFFECT_PATH_MISSING every frame. Regions: cin and cout are the foreground's and background's ADR-016 coverings. Two masks: kept K where cin >= 0.5, gone G where not kept and cout < 0.5, unknown U otherwise. One mask: d the distance from the pixel's centre to the closed outline, r the radius: U where d <= r, else K inside (cin >= 0.5), G outside. Invert swaps K and G. For S in K, G: D_S the Chebyshev distance to S; E_S the sum of the premultiplied colours over the sum of the alphas of the S pixels in the square of half-side D_S (0 when that sum is 0). F = E_K, B = E_G (0 when the set is empty). a: K 1, G 0; U 0 with K empty, else 1 with G empty, else clamp((C - B) . v / |v|^2, 0, 1), v = F - B, C the straight colour, or D_G / (D_K + D_G) when |v|^2 <= 1e-12. The colour Fc: K C; U clamp((C - (1 - a) B) / a) when a > 0, else F; G F. The matte m = a, then Edge Thin t (above 0 the least within the disc dx^2 + dy^2 <= t^2, below 0 the greatest, pixels inside the buffer only), Edge Feather f (this document's Gaussian at sigma f / 2, edges held), Edge Threshold q = h / 100 (m = clamp((m - q) / (1 - q)); at q = 1, 1 where m is 1, else 0). The pixel P (alpha A) becomes A m Fc with alpha A m, then P' (1 - b) + P b, b the blend / 100. The radius, thin and feather halve at Draft. The layer never grows. On the processor only. FX-IOK-001 to 039 pin it.

Key Cleaner (D-503, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Key Cleaner, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/key_cleaner_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.key_cleaner`: `additional_edge_radius` 0 to 100 pixels, 10 when added (Adobe's tutorial); `alpha_contrast` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `strength` 0 to 100, 100 when added. R the radius and k the contrast: the layer P becomes P' = Refine Soft Matte (D-352, above) with edge radius R, feather 0, contrast k, shift edge 0 and decontaminate off; that is, over the edge region of whole radius floor(R), the covering is the guided filter of the alpha by the straight sRGB colour, then ramped by k, and pixels with no covering take the colour of the box of 2R about them. Then P + s (P' - P), premultiplied, s the strength / 100. Radius 0 or strength 0 leaves the layer as it is. The radius halves at Draft. The layer never grows. Reduce Chatter is not built. On the processor only. FX-KCLEAN-001 to 018 pin it.

Linear Color Key (D-504, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Linear Color Key, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/linear_color_key_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.linear_color_key`: `view` "final" (when added), "source" or "matte"; `key_color` "#rrggbb", "#0000ff" when added; `match_colors` "rgb" (when added), "hue" or "chroma"; `key_operation` "key" (when added, and when a file has none) or "keep" (D-541); `matching_tolerance` and `matching_softness` 0 to 100, 10 and 10 when added, keyable. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0 to 1 and k the key's 8-bit values over 255, the distance d is 100 max|e_i - k_i| (rgb); 100 times the HSV hue angle between them over 180, the shorter way round, and 100 when either is grey (hue); 100 max|c_i - ck_i| with c = e / (eR + eG + eB), a black's (1/3, 1/3, 1/3) (chroma). The share kept m = clamp((d - tolerance) / softness, 0, 1); at softness 0, m is 0 where d <= tolerance and 1 elsewhere; at tolerance 0, m is 1 everywhere, whatever the softness (D-541, Adobe: "the entire image opaque"). "final": every channel, colour and covering, times m; "source": the layer as it is; "matte": the grey m, back to linear, times a, covering a. A pixel with a = 0 is left as it is. Keep Colors (D-541, the rule ours): the run above a "keep" instance is the Linear Color Keys directly above it, one after another, switched-off, refused and unsupported instances passed over and any other effect ending it; P is the pixels before the run's first instance (the keep instance's own input when the run is empty). Where P's covering a0 > 0, kk = 1 - m, m worked as above on P's straight colour by the keep instance's settings; Q the keep instance's input, covering aq. "final": P kk where a0 kk > aq, else Q; "matte": the grey max(aq, a0 kk) / a0, back to linear, times a0, covering a0; "source": Q. Where a0 = 0, Q. P is kept only when the stack holds a valid keep instance. On the card as the tone pass's mode 72; a keep instance on the processor, with the keys above it. FX-LCKEY-001 to 024 pin it.

Unmult (D-505, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Unmult, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/unmult_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.unmult`: `background_color` "black" (when added) or "white"; `black_level` 0 to 100, 0 when added, used with black; `white_level` 0 to 100, 100 when added, used with white; `softness` 0 to 100, 0 when added; the three keyable; `remove_color_matting` "on" (when added) or "off"; `clip_hdr_results` "off" (when added) or "on". At a pixel with premultiplied linear colour c and covering a > 0: over black m = clamp(max c, 0, a) and L = black_level / 100; over white m = clamp(a - min c, 0, a) and L = 1 - white_level / 100. The share s = clamp((m - L) / (softness / 100), 0, 1), and at softness 0, s is 1 where m > L and 0 elsewhere; the new covering is m s, and a covering of 0 gives 0, 0, 0, 0. With remove color matting on, the straight colour u = c / m (black) or (c - a + m) / m (white), held inside 0 to 1 when clip HDR results is on; off, u = c / a. The pixel becomes u times the new covering, with that covering. A pixel with a = 0 is left as it is. As added it is Soft Physical Glow's unmult (D-353). On the card as the tone pass's mode 73. FX-UNMULT-001 to 019 pin it.

3D Glasses (D-506, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' 3D Glasses, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/three_d_glasses_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.three_d_glasses`: `left_view` and `right_view` D-189's layer words, "" (the layer itself) when added; `scene_convergence` and `vertical_alignment` -1000 to 1000, 0 when added; `units` "pixels" (when added) or "percent" (of the drawing's own width for the convergence, own height for the alignment); `swap_left_right` "off" (when added) or "on"; `three_d_view` one of "side_by_side", "over_under", "interlace", "red_green", "red_blue", "balanced_red_green", "balanced_red_blue" and "balanced_colored_red_blue" (when added); `balance` 0 to 10, 0.75 when added; the two distances and the balance keyable. Each view is read in the drawing's own W by H space (the input's corner (ox, oy) taken off): the layer itself at (u + ox, v + oy), or D-189's map of the layer, centred, at (u, v), by the bilinear above, transparent outside; a view naming a layer not in the composition leaves the layer as it is (D-189's warning). Swap exchanges the two. At own pixel (x, y): the anaglyphs and interlace read l = L(x + 1/2 - c/2, y + 1/2 + v/2) and r = R(x + 1/2 + c/2, y + 1/2 - v/2); side by side reads L(2x + 1, y + 1/2) where 2x < W, else R(2x + 1 - W, y + 1/2); over under likewise down; interlace takes l on even rows, r on odd. The balanced views first set m = (l + r) / 2, l = m + b (l - m), r = m + b (r - m) on all four channels. With Y = 0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b (linear, premultiplied): red green (Y(r), Y(l), 0), red blue (Y(r), Y(l), Y(l)), balanced colored red blue (r.r, l.g, l.b); covering clamp(max(l.a, r.a), 0, 1), colour held at 0 or above. The layer never grows. Draft halves the distances in pixels only. On the card as the `glasses` pass. FX-GLASSES-001 to 037 pin it.

CC Spotlight (D-507, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Spotlight, whose maths CycoreFX does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/cc_spotlight_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.cc_spotlight`: `from` and `to`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's own width and height, [25, 25] and [50, 50] when added; `height` 0 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's own width, 50 when added; `cone_angle` 0 to 180 degrees, 40 when added; `edge_softness` 0 to 100, 20 when added; `intensity` 0 to 1000 per cent, 100 when added; the six keyable; `color` `#rrggbb`, "#ffffff" when added; `render` "light_only", "light_add", "light_add_plus" or "light_shadow" (when added), exact; the four Gel renders and `gel_layer` are D-519's (below). In the drawing's own W by H space (the input's corner (ox, oy) taken off), at the pixel's centre P = (x + 1/2, y + 1/2, 0): the lamp F = (from_x W, from_y H, -height W) / 100, the target T = (to_x W, to_y H, 0) / 100, A = T - F, D = P - F, theta = atan2(|A x D|, A . D). With o = cone_angle / 2 and n = o (1 - edge_softness / 100), the share f is 1 where theta <= n, 0 where theta >= o, else t^2 (3 - 2t) with t = (o - theta) / (o - n); f is 0 everywhere when A is 0 or o is 0. With i = intensity / 100, k = f i and C the colour's linear value, on the premultiplied linear pixel (c, a): light_only (C k, min(k, 1)); light_add (c + C k a, a); light_add_plus (c + C k, a + (1 - a) min(k, 1)); light_shadow (c ((1 - f)(1 - min(i, 1)) + f C), a). No fall-off with distance. The layer never grows; Draft changes nothing, every distance being a share of the drawing. On the card as the `spotlight` pass. FX-SPOT-001 to 027 pin it.

CC Wide Time (D-520, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Wide Time, whose maths CycoreFX does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/cc_wide_time_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.cc_wide_time`: `forward_steps` and `backward_steps` 0 to 100 frames, 3 when added, keyable, each taken whole (floor) at the frame; `native_motion_blur` "off" (when added) or "on", exact. On an adjustment layer the output is the input. Otherwise at composition frame n, with F and B the whole steps, P_m for m = n - B to n + F is the holder's own picture at frame m by steps 1 and 2 alone (its drawing, solid, shapes or composition, and its masks, by the layer's own timing; its effects not run, the ones before CC Wide Time included), empty outside the layer's in and out points, laid on the drawing corner to corner and cut to the drawing at n. R = (sum of P_m) / (B + F + 1), all four premultiplied linear numbers; the output is R at the drawing's corner, replacing what effects before made. The bounds never grow; no setting is a distance, so Draft changes nothing but the size each P_m is made at. With `native_motion_blur` "off", every composition other than the holder's own is drawn with its motion blur disabled; "on" draws them as they are. Processor only. FX-WIDE-001 to 019 pin it.

Energy Stroke (D-508, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of Video Copilot's Saber, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/energy_stroke_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.energy_stroke`: `source` "points" (when added), "masks", "shapes" or "text"; `core_start` and `core_end`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing, [25, 50] and [75, 50] when added; `evolution` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `start_offset` and `end_offset` 0 to 100, 0 and 100; `start_size` and `end_size` 0 to 100, 100; `core_size` 0 to 200 pixels, 8; `core_softness` 0 to 100, 30; `glow_color` `#rrggbb`, "#3080ff"; `glow_intensity` 0 to 1000 per cent, 100; `glow_spread` 0 to 1000 pixels, 40; `flicker_amount` 0 to 100, 0; `flicker_speed` 0 to 100, 20; `distortion_amount` 0 to 500 pixels, 0; `distortion_scale` 1 to 1000 pixels, 60; `distortion_speed` 0 to 100, 20; `random_seed` 0 to 10000, its floor, 0; `composite` "add" (when added), "transparent" or "black". Every number keyable. The paths: Points, one open path between the two points; otherwise every mask on with two points (closed), every shape on with two points (open or closed), or every text outline (closed), as Path Stroke finds them (P0-22); none says EFFECT_PATH_MISSING every frame and the layer is drawn without the effect. Along each path of length L, with s and e the smaller and larger offset over 100, the stretch u from s L to e L; on a closed path begun frac(evolution / 360) L along it. The stretch is cut at its ends, at every corner, and with distortion every D = clamp(distortion_scale / 8, 0.5, 2) along it; each cut's width w is start_size mixed to end_size by its share of the stretch, over 100. Distortion moves each cut by A (2 n1 - 1) across and A (2 n2 - 1) down, n1 and n2 value noise (smoothstep-eased, Scribble's hash) at the path's place over distortion_scale (repeating round a closed path) and frame distortion_speed / 10. At the pixel's centre, for each bit: d the distance, w the width there, r = core_size w / 2, soft = max(r core_softness / 100, 1); core c = smoothstep((r + 1/2 - d) / soft) when r > 0; glow g = w (1/4) sum over k = 0 to 3 of exp(-d^2 / (2 sigma_k^2)), sigma_k = max(glow_spread / 3 / 2^k, 1/4), 0 when glow_spread is 0; c and g the largest over every bit. Flicker F = 1 - flicker_amount / 100 n3, n3 the noise's channel 3 at frame flicker_speed / 10. The light L = F (c + (1 - c) g G i) per channel, G the glow colour's linear value, i = glow_intensity / 100, its covering a = min(1, max(L)); on the premultiplied pixel: add (rgb + L, alpha + (1 - alpha) a); transparent (L, a); black (L, 1). The layer never grows. A draft scales core_size, glow_spread, distortion_amount and distortion_scale. On the card as the `energy` pass, which looks only within each bit's reach (its core, and six times sigma_0 for the glow: past it the glow is below 2e-8). FX-ENERGY-001 to 053 pin it.

Energy Stroke's extras (D-565, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of Saber's Start and End Roundness, Lock Noise and Alpha Mode; the rule is ours. `tools/energy_stroke_extras_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `start_roundness` and `end_roundness` in 0..100, keyable, 100 when added; `noise_along_path` "on" (when added) or "off"; `alpha_mode` "off" (when added), "masks", "core" or "glow"; `invert_masks` "off" (when added) or "on"; each written only if moved, keyed or already in the file, and a file without them reads those, which draw exactly as D-508. **Ends.** Each stretch of a path has two ends, its first and its last cut, unless it is a closed path drawn whole (offsets 0 and 100). At an end with q its roundness / 100, E its point (after distortion), w the width share there, r = core_size w / 2, soft = max(r core_softness / 100, 1), and o the unit direction from the nearest later (for the last end, earlier) cut point unlike E to E: when q < 1, r > 0 and o exists, each pixel centre X with along = (X - E) . o > 0 and |X - E| < r + 1/2 has its core covering held to at most cap, where cap = 0 when q = 0 and otherwise cap = smoothstep(clamp((r + 1/2 - d) / soft, 0, 1)) with d = hypot((X - E) x o, along / q), the distance across the axis and the distance along it over q. The glow is not shaped. **Noise on the layer.** With `noise_along_path` off each cut, at p on the drawing before it moves, is moved by A (2 n1 - 1) across and A (2 n2 - 1) down with n_c = noise3(seed, c, p.x / S, p.y / S, tau), S the distortion scale and tau as D-508's: value noise on the whole-number grid, corner (i, j, l) hashed(seed, -1, c, i, j, l) with D-508's hash, each axis eased by smoothstep and mixed in x, then y, then z; the same for every path. **Masks.** With `alpha_mode` not off, the matte m over the drawing is every mask of the layer that is on and drawn (document 19), each in mode Add with its own feather, expansion, inversion and opacity: m = m + v - m v from 0, outside the drawing 0; with `invert_masks` on, 1 - m. The core c is multiplied by m for masks and core, the glow g for masks and glow, after the ends and before the light is worked out; the layer is not cut. With no such mask the beam is drawn uncut and EFFECT_PATH_MISSING is said each frame. Open masks are a gap. On the card the ends and the noise on the layer are drawn as here; Alpha Mode on is drawn on the processor. FX-ENERGYX-001 to 033 pin it.

Glass Melt (D-509, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Glass Wipe, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/glass_melt_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.glass_melt`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `layer_to_reveal` D-189's word, "" when added (nothing); `gradient_layer` D-189's word, "" when added (the layer itself); `softness` 0 to 100, 10; `displacement_amount` -500 to 500, 50. The three numbers keyable. The gradient G: the input itself, or D-189's map of the named layer fitted by `stretch`; the reveal R: nothing, or D-189's map of the named layer fitted by `center`; both lying on the drawing at its corner, transparent outside it. A named layer not in the composition (D-189's warning) leaves the output the input. The bump h0 = P(q) q.a, P Colorama's luminance phase of G's pixel q (CC Glass's bump, D-317); h is h0 blurred by the Gaussian at sigma softness / 2 with the edge pixels repeated, none at softness 0. The bend D = displacement_amount times 1.25 max(softness, 1) times the central slope of h, the neighbours held inside. With c = completion / 100, B = 0.1, E = -B / 2 + c (1 + B): k = clamp((h - E) / B + 0.5, 0, 1). Output k O(x + 1/2 + (1 - k) Dx, y + 1/2 + (1 - k) Dy) + (1 - k) R(x + 1/2 - k Dx, y + 1/2 - k Dy), document 21's bilinear, transparent outside. The layer never grows. A draft divides softness and displacement_amount. On the card as `vheight`, the held Gaussian, then the `gmeltk` and `gmelt` passes. FX-GMELT-001 to 033 pin it.

Time Difference (D-521, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Time Difference, whose maths Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/time_difference_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.time_difference`: `layer` D-189's word, "" when added; `time_offset` -60 to 60 seconds, 0 when added, and `contrast` 0 to 100, 0 when added, both keyable; `absolute_difference` "on" (when added) or "off"; `alpha_channel` one of original (when added), target, blend, max, full_on, lightness_of_result, max_of_result, alpha_difference, alpha_difference_only. At composition frame n the shift is k = floor(time_offset x rate + 1e-6). The target T, the size of the drawing, is with no layer named the holder's own picture at n + k by steps 1 and 2 alone (its effects not run), or with a layer named D-189's centred map of it at n + k; outside the target's in and out points it is clear. On an adjustment layer with no layer named the output is the input; a missing layer warns (D-189) and leaves the input; a circle is refused (D-189). At each pixel S is the input and M = T at the same place on the drawing; each to straight encoded colour, c = sRGB(clamp(rgb / a)) for a > 0, else 0, and alpha min(a, 1); d = S - M, all four numbers; g = 1 + contrast / 10; with absolute on e = clamp(g|d|), off e = clamp(0.5 + g d / 2). The covering A: original S.a; target M.a; blend their mean; max the larger; full_on 1; lightness_of_result (max + min) / 2 of e's colour; max_of_result the largest of e's colour; alpha_difference e.a; alpha_difference_only e.a with e's colour white. The output is linear(e.rgb) A, alpha A. The bounds never grow; no setting is a distance. Processor only. FX-TDIFF-001 to 031 pin it.

Tile Wipe (D-510, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Grid Wipe, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/tile_wipe_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.tile_wipe`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `center` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; `rotation` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `border` 0 to 100 pixels, 0; `tiles` 1 to 200, 10; the five numbers keyable; `shape` "doors", "radial" (when added) or "rectangle"; `reverse` "off" (when added) or "on". In the drawing's own W by H space, C = (center_x W, center_y H) / 100 and the tile size s = W / tiles. At the pixel's centre P, q is P - C turned by -rotation; the tile centre t = floor(q / s + 1/2) s on each axis, and m = max(|q_x - t_x|, |q_y - t_y|). The tile's distance d: radial |t|, rectangle max(|t_x|, |t_y|), doors |t_x|; dmax the same measure of the drawing's four corners turned the same way. u = min(d / dmax, 1) (0 when dmax is 0), 1 - u when reversed. With c = completion / 100: p = clamp((c - 0.75 u) / 0.25, 0, 1); the tile's half size a = (1 - p)(s / 2 + b) - b / 2, b the border; the pixel keeps k = clamp((a - m) / b + 1/2, 0, 1), or with b = 0, 1 where m < a, else 0. Every premultiplied channel is multiplied by k. At completion 0 the output is the input. The layer never grows. A draft divides border. On the card as mode 4 of the `wipe` pass. FX-TILE-001 to 028 pin it.

Screen Key (D-540, 2026-10-11), this program's own green and blue screen keyer in Keylight's place; no Keylight maths is used, so this rule is ours. `tools/screen_key_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.screen_key`: `view` "final" (when added), "screen_matte" or "status"; `screen_color` "#rrggbb", "#00ff00" when added; `screen_gain` 0 to 200, 100; `screen_balance` 0 to 100, 50; `clip_black` and `clip_white` 0 to 100, 0 and 100; `despill_bias` 0 to 100, 50; `despill` 0 to 100, 100; `shrink_grow` -100 to 100 pixels, 0; `softness` 0 to 100 pixels, 0; `edge_refine` 0 to 100 pixels, 0; every number keyable, the three distances halved at Draft. k is the screen colour's 8-bit values over 255; its screen channel s is the largest of the three (on a tie green, then blue, then red), o1 and o2 the other two. weigh(c, t) = max(c_o1, c_o2)(1 - t) + min(c_o1, c_o2) t and D(c) = c_s - weigh(c, balance / 100). At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0 to 1: (1) m = clamp(1 - (gain / 100) D(e) / D(k), 0, 1), and m = 1 when D(k) <= 0; at a = 0, m = 1. (2) With cb = clip_black / 100 and cw = clip_white / 100, m = clamp((m - cb) / (cw - cb), 0, 1); where cw <= cb, m is 0 at or below cb and 1 above. (3) shrink_grow g != 0: m becomes the least (g < 0) or the greatest (g > 0) m over Simple Choker's disc of radius |g|, the picture's edge pixels held beyond it. (4) softness sigma > 0: m through this document's Gaussian at sigma, edges held. (5) The despill: e_s less (despill / 100) max(0, e_s - weigh(e, despill_bias / 100)). (6) "final": the despilled colour back to linear, times a m, covering a m; then, at edge_refine r >= 1, Key Cleaner's rule (D-503) at radius floor(r), alpha contrast 0, strength 100 on the result. "screen_matte": the grey m back to linear, times a, covering a. "status": the grey 1 where m >= 0.999, 0 where m <= 0.001, else the encoded 0.5, back to linear, times a, covering a. A pixel with a = 0 is left transparent except where the edge refine's fit reaches it. On the card as the tone pass's mode 90 for "final" and "screen_matte" without shrink, softness, edge refine or a hard clip; the rest on the processor. FX-SKEY-001 to 024 pin it.

Spill Suppressor (D-548, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Spill Suppressor (Keying, Obsolete), a second name over Screen Key's despill (D-540, as D-383 and D-394); Adobe publishes no rule, so it is ours; `tools/spill_suppressor_reference.py` is the reference. Settings: `color_to_suppress` (#rrggbb in either case, #0000ff when added, not keyable) and `suppression` (0..100, 100, keyable). It is drawn as Screen Key with Screen Gain 0 (so every covering is kept), Balance 50, Clip 0 to 100, Despill Bias 50 and Despill the suppression. With k the colour's 8-bit values over 255, its screen channel s the largest of k's three (on a tie green, then blue, then red) and o1, o2 the other two, at a pixel with covering a > 0 and encoded straight colour e held inside 0 to 1: e_s = e_s - (suppression / 100) max(0, e_s - (e_o1 + e_o2) / 2), out = (srgb_to_linear(e) a, a). The other two channels and the covering never change; a = 0 stays transparent. On the card as Screen Key's tone mode 90. FX-SSUP-001 to 015 pin it.

Teeth Wipe (D-511, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Jaws, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/teeth_wipe_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.teeth_wipe`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `center` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; `direction` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `height` 0 to 1000 pixels, 40; `width` 1 to 1000 pixels, 40; the five numbers keyable; `shape` "spikes" (when added), "trapezoids" or "blocks". In the drawing's own W by H space, C = (center_x W, center_y H) / 100, d = (cos a, sin a) and n = (-sin a, cos a), a the direction. At a point P, v = (P - C) . d and w = (P - C) . n. t = |2 frac(v / width) - 1|; the profile s(t): spikes t, trapezoids clamp(2 t - 1/2, 0, 1), blocks 1 when t > 1/2, else 0; the cut g = height (s - 1/2). With c = completion / 100, o = c (height / 2 + the largest |w| of the drawing's four corners). At the pixel's centre: where w + o < g, document 21's bilinear read of the input at P + o n; else where w - o >= g, the read at P - o n; else transparent. At completion 0 the output is the input. The layer never grows. A draft divides height and width. On the card as mode 5 of the `wipe` pass. FX-TEETH-001 to 027 pin it.

Channel Wipe (D-512, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Image Wipe, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/channel_wipe_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.channel_wipe`: `layer` D-189's word, "" when added (the layer itself); `fit` "stretch" (when added), "center" or "tile"; `completion` 0 to 100, 0; `softness` 0 to 100, 10; `property` "red", "green", "blue", "alpha", "luminance" (when added), "lightness", "hue" or "saturation"; `blur` 0 to 100 pixels, 0; `invert` "off" (when added) or "on"; the three numbers keyable. The gradient G is the input itself, lying on itself, or D-189's map of the named layer fitted by `fit`, transparent outside it; a missing layer leaves the input as it is. At each pixel, q the pixel of G there (linear, premultiplied) and e = the sRGB encoding of clamp(q.rgb, 0, 1): red, green, blue are e's channels; alpha is q's covering; luminance is Gradient Wipe's picture luma of q; hue (degrees / 360), saturation and lightness are Hue/Saturation's HSL of e. With `blur` above 0 the readings are blurred by this document's Gaussian at sigma blur / 2, the outer pixels repeated. Then Gradient Wipe's rule (invert, E, k) unchanged; Gradient Wipe is this engine with luminance, no blur, and nothing wiped when no layer is named. The layer never grows. A draft divides blur. On the card in the `gwipe` pass, the blur through the `gauss` pass. FX-CWIPE-001 to 033 pin it.

Glow Wipe (D-513, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Light Wipe, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/glow_wipe_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.glow_wipe`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `center` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; `intensity` 0 to 1000 per cent, 100; `direction` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; the four numbers keyable; `shape` "doors", "radial" (when added) or "rectangular"; `color_from_source` "off" (when added) or "on"; `color` `#rrggbb`, "#ffffff"; `reverse` "off" (when added) or "on". In the drawing's own W by H space, C = (center_x W, center_y H) / 100, d = (cos a, sin a) and n = (-sin a, cos a), a the direction. At a point P, u = (P - C) . d and v = (P - C) . n; the distance is |u| (doors), sqrt(u^2 + v^2) (radial) or max(|u|, |v|) (rectangular). D is the largest distance of the four corners, b = D / 10, c = completion / 100, the front f = c (D + 2 b) - b, and s the distance, or D minus it reversed. The pixel is kept where s >= f and cleared where s < f. The light's share g = max(0, 1 - |s - f| / b) intensity / 100; with Color from Source off the light is g times the colour's linear value, its covering min(g, 1); on, g times the pixel's own premultiplied colour, its covering min(g a, 1). The kept or cleared pixel q becomes q.rgb + light and q.a + (1 - q.a) covering. The layer never grows; nothing is a distance, so a draft changes nothing. On the card, mode 6 of the `wipe` pass. FX-GLOW-001 to 028 pin it.

Stripe Sweep (D-514, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Line Sweep, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/stripe_sweep_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.stripe_sweep`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `direction` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `thickness` 1 to 1000 pixels, 20; `slant` 0 to 99, 50; the four numbers keyable; `flip` "off" (when added) or "on". In the drawing's own W by H space, C = (W / 2, H / 2), d = (cos a, sin a) and n = (-sin a, cos a), a the direction. At a point P, u = (P - C) . d and v = (P - C) . n; U and V are the largest |u| and |v| of the four corners. N = max(1, ceil(2 V / thickness)) lines; the point's line i = min(floor((v + V) / thickness), N - 1); along it t = (u + U) / (2 U), or 1 - t flipped. With o = slant / 99, s = 1 / (1 + (N - 1)(1 - o)) and c = completion / 100, line i has gone p = clamp((c - i s (1 - o)) / s, 0, 1); the pixel is cleared where t < p and kept where t >= p. The layer never grows. A draft scales the thickness. On the card, mode 7 of the `wipe` pass. FX-STRIPE-001 to 024 pin it.

Push Iris (D-515, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Radial ScaleWipe, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/push_iris_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.push_iris`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `center` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; both keyable; `reverse` "off" (when added) or "on". In the drawing's own W by H space, C the centre, D the largest distance of the four corners from C, c = completion / 100 and R = c D. At a point P, r = |P - C|. Forward: clear where r < R, elsewhere the bilinear read of the input at C + (P - C)(r - R) / r. Reversed: clear where r >= (1 - c) D, elsewhere the read at C + (P - C)(r + R) / r (at r = 0, at C + (R, 0)). At completion 0 the input either way; at 100 every pixel clear. The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing. On the card, mode 8 of the `wipe` pass. FX-PUSH-001 to 017 pin it.

Cryptomatte (D-522, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Cryptomatte, whose settings Adobe does not document, so they and the colours are ours; the file is read by Psyop's Cryptomatte specification 1.2.0. `tools/cryptomatte_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.cryptomatte`: `layer` text, "" when added; `selection` text, "" when added; `output` colors (when added), matte or keyed; none keyable, none a distance. A layer T is a channel-name prefix (before the last dot) ending in `00` after at least one character, holding a part `r`, case ignored; the layers in the file's channel order; "" takes the first, otherwise the first whose T, or whose T's last dotted word, equals `layer`, case ignored. Levels T00, T01, ... while the level has a part `r`: red id with green coverage, and blue id with alpha coverage when both are there. A coverage not finite: not-a-number 0, an infinity the largest single-precision number of its sign; an id not finite 0; any such number says MEDIA_EXR_ADJUSTED for the frame. The selection split at commas, each trimmed, empty ones dropped, each name's id = its UTF-8 bytes by MurmurHash3_x86_32 with seed 0, bit 23 flipped where bits 23 to 30 are all 0 or all 1, read as a single-precision number; ids compare as single-precision numbers. At each pixel of the drawing m = clamp(sum of c_k over the ranks whose id is picked, 0, 1); an id's colour, its bits b, is ((b & 255) / 255, (b >> 8 & 255) / 255, (b >> 16 & 127) / 127), a picked id white; P = sum of c_k x colour_k. colors: (P, 1); matte: (m, m, m, 1); keyed: the input times m, all four numbers. Outside the drawing transparent. The numbers come from the layer's own EXR file at that frame (D-348's route), at the file's size, stretched to a draft's as D-189 stretches a map; a layer with no such file, or a file without the layer named, leaves the input and says EFFECT_CHANNEL_MISSING. FX-CRYPTO-001 to 023 pin it.

Depth of Field (D-523, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Depth of Field, whose rule Adobe does not give, so it is ours. `tools/depth_of_field_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.depth_of_field`: `focal_plane` -1,000,000 to 1,000,000 (0 when added) and `focal_plane_thickness` 0 to 1,000,000 (0), in the depth's own units; `maximum_radius` 0 to 200 pixels (10), a distance a draft scales; `focal_bias` 0 to 100 (50); all keyable. With z the depth at a pixel of the drawing (D-348's route, the layer's own EXR file, stretched to a draft's size; a depth not finite read as D-62 reads one, saying MEDIA_EXR_ADJUSTED): beyond = max(0, |z - focal_plane| - focal_plane_thickness / 2), k = focal_bias / 50, f = 0 where beyond = 0 or k = 0, 1 where k beyond >= |z|, k beyond / |z| otherwise, worked in double precision. The output is D-359's Lens Blur with a blur map: radius `maximum_radius`, iris circle, roundness 0, rotation 0, aspect 1, highlight gain 0, threshold 100, edges repeat, the map the fractions f in all four numbers read by alpha, focal distance 0, invert off. A layer with no such file, or a file with no depth, leaves the input and says EFFECT_CHANNEL_MISSING. FX-DOF-001 to 027 pin it.

Fog 3D (D-524, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Fog 3D, whose rule Adobe does not give, so it is ours. `tools/fog_3d_reference.py` is the reference: At composition frame n, with O the layer's picture (linear, premultiplied) whose drawing's own top-left pixel is at (ox, oy), and z the depth at a pixel of the drawing: 1. t = clamp((z - start) / (end - start), 0, 1), or, with the two depths equal, 1 where z >= start and 0 nearer: how far through the fog the pixel lies. 2. With d = scattering_density / 10, the share of fog is f = t at d = 0, and otherwise f = (1 - e^(-d t)) / (1 - e^(-d)): 0 at the start, 1 at the end, and the higher the density, the sooner it thickens. 3. With no gradient layer named (""), g = 1. With one named, its map M is D-189's (`tools/effect_layer_reference.py`), centred on the drawing's own size; the drawing's pixel reads M under it, transparent outside M, and L is its picture luma (the sRGB encoding of clamp(0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b, 0, 1), as Aerial Haze reads its matte); with c = layer_contribution / 100, g = 1 - c + c L. A layer named that is not in the composition leaves O, with D-189's warning. 4. With o = fog_opacity / 100: the fog on the pixel F = f g o, and the fog behind it B = g o with Foggy Background on, 0 off. 5. C is the fog colour, each channel's 8-bit value over 255 taken to linear by the sRGB curve. The output is (O.rgb + F (C O.a - O.rgb) + (1 - O.a) B C, O.a + (1 - O.a) B): the pixel moves toward the fog colour as Aerial Haze moves it, and what does not cover the pixel lets the fog behind show through. Outside the drawing nothing changes. `fog_color` `#rrggbb`, #ffffff when added, read in small letters, not keyable; `fog_start_depth` and `fog_end_depth` -1,000,000 to 1,000,000, 0 and 10 when added; `fog_opacity` 0 to 100, 100 when added; `scattering_density` 0 to 100, 50 when added; `foggy_background` "on" (when added) or "off"; `gradient_layer` D-189's word, "" when added; `layer_contribution` 0 to 100, 100 when added. The numbers are keyable. The depths are in the depth's own units; nothing is in pixels, so a draft changes nothing but the pixels it draws. A layer with no such file, or a file with no depth, leaves the input and says EFFECT_CHANNEL_MISSING. FX-FOG-001 to 030 pin it.

IDentifier (D-525, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' IDentifier (fnord's ProEXR), whose colours and rule are not published, so they are ours. `tools/identifier_reference.py` is the reference: At composition frame n, with O the layer's picture (linear, premultiplied) whose drawing's own top-left pixel is at (ox, oy), v the id at a pixel of the drawing and `id` the setting at n: - "colors": k = floor(v + 0.5), the nearest whole id. Its hue is h = (k x 0.618033988749895) mod 1 (the golden ratio's turn, so ids next to each other are far apart); the colour is HSV (h, 0.7, 0.9) as Python's `colorsys.hsv_to_rgb` works it, each channel taken to linear by the sRGB curve. Opaque. - "luma_matte": white where |v - id| < 0.5 (ID Key's test), black elsewhere. Opaque. - "alpha_matte": O where |v - id| < 0.5, transparent elsewhere: the drawing with the matte as its alpha, ID Key with no feather. - "raw": grey g = clamp(v / 255, 0, 1) as stored, taken to linear by the sRGB curve, so the 8-bit picture reads the id itself (ids past 255 white, below 0 black). Opaque. Outside the ids (past the drawing) the result is transparent, as Pass Extract's is. `aux_channel` "object_id" (when added) or "material_id"; `id` 0 to 1,000,000, 0 when added, keyable; `output` "colors" (when added), "luma_matte", "alpha_matte" or "raw". Nothing is in pixels, so a draft changes nothing but the pixels it draws. A layer with no such file, or a file with no such ids, leaves the input and says EFFECT_CHANNEL_MISSING. FX-IDENT-001 to 023 pin it.

Numbers (D-526, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Numbers (Text), whose layout and formulas are not published, so they are ours. `tools/numbers_reference.py` is the reference: The rule. At composition frame n the layer's time is t = (n - in point) / fps seconds (through its key time, as Scribble's, D-442), its frame k = n - in point, and V the value at n. 1. Random values. With random_values "on", V becomes r x M: M is V, or 30,000 when V is 0, and r = (u + 1) / 2 with u = `grade::unit(mix(0x4E554D42), 0, 0, k, 0)`, D-119's hash; a new number every frame of the layer. 2. What is shown, a string s: - number: V with `decimal_places` places, correctly rounded from its binary value (ties to even); a minus is dropped when every digit shown is 0. - number_leading_zeros: as number, the whole part padded with zeros to five digits, the minus (when kept) in front. - timecode_N (N 30, 25 or 24, non-drop): T = t + V seconds, or with random values T = V (a random time, not the layer's); f = floor(|T| x N + 1e-6) frames; s = hh:mm:ss:ff, each two digits (the hours more when they pass 99), a minus in front when T < 0 and f > 0. - time: V in hours; m = floor(V x 60 + 1e-6) minutes, wrapped into one day (0 to 1439, negative counting back from midnight); s = HH:MM, a 24-hour clock. - the dates: D = floor(V + 1e-6) days after 1 January 1995 (0 is that day, negative before it); numerical_date "M/D/YYYY", short_date "Mon D, YYYY", long_date "Weekday, Month D, YYYY", English names. - hexadecimal: h = floor(|V| x 65536 + 1e-6), as Adobe's 0x10000 for each 1.0; upper-case digits, a minus in front when V < 0 and h > 0. Current Time/Date (the computer's clock at render time) is not built: a frame must be the same on every render (document 21). 3. Layout, in the drawing's pixels, P = position / 100 x the drawing's size. With proportional spacing on, s is one D-263 text: font, size, `tracking`, alignment, its first baseline meeting its aligned end at P, the font's kerning off. With it off, every character sits in a cell as wide as the widest of the font's digits 0 to 9 (c, from the font's advances), centred in it; with a = tracking / 1000 x size, the line is L = len(s) x c + (len(s) - 1) x a wide and starts at x0 = P.x, P.x - L / 2 or P.x - L (left, center, right); character i is its own left-aligned text at x0 + i x (c + a) + (c - w_i) / 2 on the baseline P.y, w_i its own advance, drawn in order, each over the ones before. 4. Two pictures, each the drawing's size: F, the characters filled in the fill colour (D-263); and, when stroke_width > 0 and display is not fill_only, S, the characters in the stroke colour with D-264's stroke of that colour and width round them (the whole stroked shape). A character's picture is laid over the ones before as the layers of a composition are. 5. The words, W: fill_only F; fill_over_stroke F over S; stroke_only S x (1 - F's alpha), the ring outside the letters; stroke_over_fill (S x (1 - F's alpha)) over F. Without S, W is F for the two that fill and nothing for stroke_only. D-264's stroke lies wholly outside the letters, so stroke_over_fill differs from fill_over_stroke only along the letters' edges. 6. Composite on original "on": W over the layer as it was; "off": W alone. 7. A font this computer does not have is said each frame as `TEXT_FONT_MISSING` and nothing is drawn: the layer is left as it was (D-263: no other font in its place). FX-NUM-001 to 045 pin it.

Timecode (D-527, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Timecode (Text), whose layout and formulas are not published, so they are ours. `tools/timecode_reference.py` is the reference: The rule. At composition frame n, with the composition's frame rate fps (a fraction) and the layer's in point `in`: 1. The count f (a whole number of frames) and the rate R it is labelled at: - layer_source: f = floor(s + 1e-6), s the layer's source time at n in frames (document 20, D-216: (n - in) x 100 / stretch + source offset, or its Time Remap); R = fps rounded to a whole number. A file's own starting timecode (a QuickTime timecode track, a DPX header) is not read: every source counts from its first frame, 0, and a layer with a file says so each frame (PROJECT_FEATURE_UNSUPPORTED, information). A solid, which has no file, counts from its in point; a composition layer from its source offset, through its stretch. - composition: f = n, the composition's own frame number (its start frame names its first frame); R as for layer_source. - custom: t = (u - in) / fps seconds, u the layer's key time at n (through its time stretch, as Numbers', D-526); f = starting_frame + floor(t x U + 1e-6), U the time units; R = U. 2. Drop frame. With the custom source, drop frame is the setting. With the other two it is on exactly when the composition's rate is an NTSC one, a denominator of 1001 with R a multiple of 30 (29.97, 59.94 and so on); 23.976 is counted non-drop at 24. 3. The string s, a minus in front when f < 0, worked on |f|: - smpte, non-drop: hh:mm:ss:ff at R frames a second, each two digits (the hours more when they pass 99). - smpte, drop frame: D = R / 15 frame numbers are left out at the start of every minute but each tenth; the frame f is relabelled f' = f + 9 D d + D floor((m - D) / (60 R - D)) when m > D, else f + 9 D d, with d = f div (600 R - 9 D) and m = f mod (600 R - 9 D); f' is written as non-drop but with a semicolon before the frames, hh:mm:ss;ff. - frames: |f| written with at least five digits, zeros in front. - feet_frames_35mm (16 frames a foot) and feet_frames_16mm (40 frames a foot): the feet with at least four digits, a plus, the frames left over with two, "0002+05". 4. Layout, in the drawing's pixels, P = text_position / 100 x the drawing's size. Every character sits in a cell as wide as the widest of the bundled font's digits (c, from its advances), centred in it, as Numbers' with proportional spacing off: the line is L = len(s) x c wide, starts at x0 = P.x - L / 2, and character i is its own left-aligned D-263 text in the bundled font, text_size and the text colour, at x0 + i x c + (c - w_i) / 2 on the baseline P.y. 5. The box, with show_box "on": the rectangle from x0 - p to x0 + L + p across and from P.y - k - p to P.y + p down, p = text_size / 5 and k the top of the font's "0" above its baseline at text_size (its outline's highest point, from the font's glyph table), each edge taken to the nearest whole pixel (a half up), filled with the box colour at the opacity, the words over it. 6. Composite on original "on": the box and the words over the layer as it was; "off": alone. FX-TC-001 to 027 and 040 to 046 pin it.

Stretch Wipe (D-516, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Scale Wipe, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/stretch_wipe_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.stretch_wipe`: `scale` 100 to 100000 per cent, 100 when added; `center` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; `direction` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; all keyable. In the drawing's own W by H space, C the centre, d = (cos a, sin a) and n = (-sin a, cos a), a the direction, S = scale / 100. At a point P, u = (P - C) . d and v = (P - C) . n. Where u <= 0 the point is the input's own; where u > 0 it is the bilinear read of the input at C + (u / S) d + v n. The two sides meet without a seam. The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing. On the card, mode 9 of the `wipe` pass. FX-STRETCH-001 to 017 pin it.

Twister (D-517, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC Twister, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/twister_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.twister`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `backside` D-189's layer word, "" (nothing) when added, the named layer's map fitted to the drawing by `center` and lying at its corner; `shading` "on" (when added) or "off"; `center` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; `axis` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; completion, center and axis keyable. In the drawing's own W by H space, C the centre, d = (cos a, sin a) and n = (-sin a, cos a), a the axis. At a pixel centre P, u = (P - C) . d and v = (P - C) . n; U is the largest |u| of the four corners and t = (u + U) / (2 U). The strip is turned by theta = pi clamp(2 completion / 100 - t, 0, 1) and s = |cos theta|. The point read is Q = C + u d + (v / s) n: the input's bilinear read where cos theta > 0, the back's where cos theta < 0 (clear with none), clear where s = 0. Shading on multiplies the colour by s. A back naming a layer not in the composition leaves the input as it is, with D-189's warning. The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing. On the card, the `twist` pass. FX-TWIST-001 to 030 pin it.

Warp Blend (D-518, 2026-10-11), after the purpose of CycoreFX's CC WarpoMatic, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/warp_blend_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.warp_blend`: `completion` 0 to 100, 0 when added; `layer_to_reveal` D-189's layer word, "" (nothing) when added, the named layer's map fitted to the drawing, centred, lying at its corner; `reactor` "brightness" (when added), "contrast_differences", "brightness_differences" or "local_differences"; `smoothness` 0 to 100, 10; `warp_amount` -500 to 500, 50; `warp_direction` "joint" (when added), "opposing" or "twisting"; `blend_span` 0 to 100, 50; completion, smoothness, warp amount and blend span keyable. With q a pixel's luminance phase times its covering and q_c its channel phases times its covering, from the layer's pixel a and the reveal's b: brightness (q(a) + q(b)) / 2, brightness differences |q(a) - q(b)|, contrast differences ||q(a) - 1/2| - |q(b) - 1/2||, local differences the largest |q_c(a) - q_c(b)|. h is that blurred at sigma smoothness / 2 (none at 0), D = warp_amount times h's central difference times 1.25 max(smoothness, 1), turned to (-Dy, Dx) when twisting. With c = completion / 100 at the pixel centre P: A' = A(P + c D); B' = B(P - (1 - c) D), or B(P + (1 - c) D) when opposing; s = blend_span / 100, m = clamp((c - 1/2 + s/2) / s, 0, 1) (with s 0, 1 where c >= 1/2); the output (1 - m) A' + m B'. A layer to reveal not in the composition leaves the input as it is, with D-189's warning. The layer never grows; a draft divides smoothness and warp amount by its divisor. On the card, the `wreact` and `warpb` passes with Glass Melt's blur and slope. FX-WARPB-001 to 039 pin it.

CC Cylinder (D-530, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Cylinder, whose rule CycoreFX does not give, so it is ours. `tools/cc_cylinder_reference.py` is the reference: The settings, every number keyable. `radius`, 0 to 1000 per cent of half the drawing's width, 50 when added; `position`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50] when added; `position_z`, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width, 0 when added; `rotation_x`, `rotation_y` and `rotation_z`, -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0 when added; `light_intensity`, 0 to 1000 per cent, 100 when added; `light_height`, -100 to 100, 50 when added; `light_direction`, -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0 up and 90 right, -45 when added; `ambient` 40, `diffuse` 60, `specular` 50, `roughness` 10 and `metal` 100 when added, each 0 to 100. `light_color`, `#rrggbb`, "#ffffff" when added; `render`, "full" (when added), "outside" or "inside", exact. The rule, in the drawing's own W by H space, y down, z away from the viewer. A fixed eye sits at E = (W/2, H/2, -Z0), Z0 = W 50/36 (a 50 mm lens on a 36 mm frame), and looks through the pixel's centre P = (x + 1/2, y + 1/2, 0) along d = P - E. The cylinder's centre is C = (px W/100, py H/100, pz W/100), its radius R = radius W/200, its axis the y axis turned by M = Rz Ry Rx (x first), with Rx: (y cos - z sin, y sin + z cos), Ry: (x cos + z sin, -x sin + z cos), Rz: (x cos - y sin, x sin + y cos). In the cylinder's own space the eye is o = M^T (E - C) and the ray's direction is v = M^T d. The ray meets x^2 + z^2 = R^2 at t = (-b -+ sqrt(b^2 - a c)) / a, a = vx^2 + vz^2, b = ox vx + oz vz, c = ox^2 + oz^2 - R^2: the nearer root is the outside, the farther the inside, each only when t > 0 and the hit h = o + t v has |hy| <= H/2 (the ends are open). Nothing is hit when R is 0, a is 0 or b^2 - a c < 0. The picture on the cylinder: phi = atan2(hx, -hz), the drawing at (W/2 + phi W / (2 pi), hy + H/2) by document 21's bilinear, each tap's column taken round the drawing's width and its row outside the drawing transparent; the middle column faces the eye. The light, from the direction L = (cos e sin g, -cos e cos g, -sin e), g the direction and e = height 90/100 degrees (100 is from the eye's side, -100 from behind), turned into the cylinder's own space. The normal n = (hx/R, 0, hz/R), turned round for the inside; V the unit vector back to the eye. With N.L = n . L, the half vector Hv = (L + V) / |L + V| and p = 2 / (roughness/100)^2, the highlight s = max(n . Hv, 0)^p, 0 when roughness is 0, when N.L <= 0 or when L + V is 0. With i = light_intensity/100, Lc the light's linear colour and A, D, S, Mt the shading numbers over 100, on the sampled premultiplied (c, alpha): rgb = c (A + D i Lc max(N.L, 0)) + S i s (Mt c + (1 - Mt) Lc alpha), alpha unchanged. "outside" draws the outside hit, "inside" the inside hit, "full" the outside over the inside. The layer never grows: the cylinder is drawn inside the layer's own picture. A draft changes nothing, every distance being a share of the drawing. One ray a pixel, so the cylinder's edges are not smoothed. FX-CYL-001 to 027 pin it.

CC Spotlight gels (D-519, 2026-10-11), the Gel renders of D-507's CC Spotlight, after CycoreFX's, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/cc_spotlight_gel_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `gel_layer` D-189's layer word, "" (no gel) when added and when a file leaves it out, the named layer's map fitted to the drawing, centred; `render` gains "gel_only", "gel_add", "gel_add_plus" and "gel_shadow". With D-507's light at F aimed along the unit a, e1 the drawing's x direction with its part along a taken off, made a unit (the y direction when that leaves less than 1e-9), e2 = a x e1, and D = P - F at the pixel centre P: g = (D . e1, D . e2) / ((D . a) tan o'), o' the half opening held at most 89 degrees; the gel read bilinearly (document 21's, transparent outside) at (W / 2 + g1 M / 2, H / 2 + g2 M / 2), M the larger of W and H. The light let through per channel T = (1 - ga) + gc from the gel's premultiplied read; each Gel render is its Light render with the colour C replaced by C T (gel_shadow: c ((1 - f)(1 - min(i, 1)) + f C T)); where f is 0 the gel is not read. With "" a Gel render is its Light render. Only a Gel render reads its gel layer, so a Light render naming one is never in a cycle. A gel layer not in the composition leaves the input as it is, with D-189's warning. The layer never grows; a draft changes only D-189's map. On the card, the `spotlight` pass with the gel as a fifth binding. FX-SPOTGEL-001 to 021 pin it.

CC Ball Action (D-551, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Ball Action, whose maths and lighting are not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/cc_ball_action_reference.py` is the reference; in short: `core.cc_ball_action`: `scatter` 0 to 1000 pixels, 0 when added; `rotation_axis` "x" (when added), "y", "z", "xy", "xz", "yz", "xyz", "x15z" or "xy15z"; `rotation` -36000 to 36000 degrees, 0; `twist_property` "x_axis" (when added), "y_axis", "center_x", "center_y", "radius", "random", "red", "green", "blue", "brightness", "diamond", "rectangle" or "fast_top"; `twist_angle` -36000 to 36000 degrees, 0; `grid_spacing` 2 to 200 pixels, 8; `ball_size` 0 to 400 per cent of the spacing, 100; `instability_state` -36000 to 36000 degrees, 0; the six numbers keyable. In the drawing's own W by H space, centre (cx, cy): cols = floor(W / s) by rows = floor(H / s) cells of s = grid_spacing, centred; a cell makes a ball only when the pixel under its centre has alpha exactly 1, the ball that pixel's colour (linear), radius r = s ball_size / 200. From `grade::unit(551, i, j, 0, channel)`: u (channels 0 to 2) the scatter direction, turned about a (3 to 5) by instability_state, Rodrigues' way; u6 the random twist. The twist factor f from the cell centre's xn = (x - cx) / (W / 2), yn = (y - cy) / (H / 2), rho = |(xn, yn)| / sqrt 2: x_axis xn, y_axis yn, center_x |xn|, center_y |yn|, radius and fast_top 1 - rho, random (u6 + 1) / 2, red, green, blue 1 - the channel, brightness 1 - the luminance (each held in 0..1), diamond 1 - (|xn| + |yn|) / 2, rectangle 1 - max(|xn|, |yn|). M is the axis's turn by rotation + twist_angle f (x Rx, y Ry, z Rz, xy Ry Rx, xz Rz Rx, yz Rz Ry, xyz Rz Ry Rx, x15z Rz(15 theta) Rx, xy15z Rz(15 theta) Ry Rx), or for fast_top the axis's turn by rotation times Ry(2 phi) Rx(phi), phi = twist_angle f; p = M (x - cx + scatter u_x, y - cy + scatter u_y, scatter u_z). The eye is CC Cylinder's: Z = W 50 / 36; a ball at d = Z + p_z > Z / 100 is drawn at (cx + p_x Z / d, cy + p_y Z / d), radius R = r Z / d, farthest first (ties by cell). A ball covers a pixel centre by c = clamp(R + 1/2 - e, 0, 1) min(2R, 1); the pixel is c1 (S1, 1) + (1 - c1) c2 (S2, 1) from its nearest and next nearest covering balls, premultiplied, transparent when none. S = C (0.25 + 0.75 max(0, n . L)) + 0.5 max(0, n . Hv)^32 with L = (-1, -1, -2) / sqrt 6, Hv = (L + (0, 0, -1)) normalised, n the sphere's normal (the rim's flat outward normal past it). The layer never grows; a draft scales scatter and grid spacing. On the card, the `balls` and `ballshade` passes, the balls placed and put in order on the processor. FX-BALL-001 to 045 pin it.

Particle Space (D-610, 2026-10-12), the particle engine's first effect, after CycoreFX's CC Particle World, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours. `tools/particle_space_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule; in short: `core.particle_space` has 44 numbers (all keyable; names, ranges and values when added in `PARTICLE_SPACE_SETTINGS`), 7 choices and 8 colours. The world's unit is the layer's width W, its origin the layer's centre, Y down, Z away; sizes are hundredths of W; Birth Rate is hundreds of particles a second. No state is carried from frame to frame (document 20): with u frames since the in point and the settings sampled once a frame, particle k is born where the births' integral reaches k + 1/2, lives Longevity seconds, starts at a random point in the Producer's ellipsoid, is thrown by its Animation's direction at Velocity (1 + Extra (r - 1/2)) plus Inherit Velocity's share of the producer's own, and is stepped on one grid of 4 steps a frame: each animation's push (Twirl, Twirly, Vortex, Fire) and turbulence (the curl of a seeded Perlin field) as a change of velocity, then gravity and wind under linear resistance in closed form, then the floor (30 halvings to the crossing; Glue stops, Ice slides, Bounce reflects by Bounciness with its random part and turns by Bounce Spread). Random draws are `grade::unit(seed, k, stream)` folded to 0 up to 1. Through the composition camera (D-58, when the file has one) a particle at depth z is drawn at a + (x - a) A / (A + z W) in the layer's pixels, a the camera's place and A its distance from the layer's plane, worked out by compose; otherwise through the effect's own camera, turned by Rotation X, Y and Z and Distance away with focal length (W/2) / tan(FOV/2); a particle 0.01 or nearer is not drawn. Lines are antialiased streaks along the frame's motion; Disc, Faded Sphere, Shaded Sphere and Bubble have their own coverings, drawn at least 0.7 pixels across with the opacity scaled by the true size; Shaded Sphere and Bubble are lit by Light Direction and Height (Volume Shade lights Disc and Faded Sphere); Depth Cue Fade and Fog by clamp(1 - depth / Distance); farthest first, equal depths by number, by Composite, Screen or Add, onto clear or, with Composite on Original, onto the input. On the card one pass, `particles`, draws each pixel's 16 by 16 tile's list (placed, ordered and binned on the processor) with the same formulas in double precision. **Gaps:** Black Matte, Hold Particle Release and Grid & Guides (no source says what they do); the composition camera cannot turn (D-58); textured and solid-shape particles (D-611, D-612).

Particle Space's textured particles (D-611, 2026-10-12), after CC Particle World's textured types, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours; `tools/particle_texture_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule. In short: three more Particle Types, `textured_square`, `textured_disc` and `textured_faded_disc`; `rotation_speed` (degrees a second) and `initial_rotation` (degrees), read at the birth b; `texture_layer` (D-189). At a sprite of centre (X, Y) and radius R (the small sizes' rule as Disc's), theta = Initial Rotation r(k, 10) + Rotation Speed age / fps, clockwise; with dx, dy from the centre, a = dx cos theta + dy sin theta, c = -dx sin theta + dy cos theta. Covering: square clamp(R - |a| + 1/2) clamp(R - |c| + 1/2); disc clamp(R - e + 1/2); faded disc max(0, 1 - (e / R)^2)^2, e = sqrt(dx^2 + dy^2). Never lit. With no texture, the Color Map's colour as D-610. With a texture T (linear, premultiplied, Tw by Th): levels each ceil(w / 2) by ceil(h / 2), each pixel the mean of the pixels under it, to 1 by 1; lambda = log2(max(Tw, Th) / 2R) held to 0 up to the top level; the point (a / 2R + 1/2, c / 2R + 1/2) read by this document's bilinear, taps held to the edge, on levels floor(lambda) and the next, mixed by lambda's fraction; the sprite A (fd Tc.rgb + (1 - fd) Fog Tc.a, Tc.a). An empty texture at the frame draws nothing; a missing layer leaves the effect out with EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING (D-189). On the card the `particles` pass reads the levels packed in one picture, widening each part of a read to double precision on its own (D-611's driver note).

Particle Plane (D-612, 2026-10-12), after CC Particle Systems II, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours; `tools/particle_plane_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule. In short: D-610's engine with z held at 0. The engine's settings come from the Plane's: Producer (x, y, 0), Radius (x, y, 0), Direction Axis (sin d, -cos d, 0) for the Direction d, Gravity Vector (0, 1, 0), no wind, turbulence or floor. Born at (Rx rho cos phi, Ry rho sin phi, 0) from the producer, rho = sqrt(r0), phi = 2 pi r2. Thrown, with e1 = (-A_y, A_x, 0), alpha = Extra Angle / 2, a turn t giving cos t A + sin t e1, s = 1 when r4 < 1/2 else -1: Explosive, Viscous, Twirl, Twirly, Vortex, Fractal Omni (cos 2 pi r3, sin 2 pi r3, 0); Direction, Fractal Uni t = alpha (2 r3 - 1); Cone t = s alpha; Fire t = 20 degrees (2 r3 - 1); Jet Sideways s e1. Twirl, Twirly and Vortex swirl about (0, 0, 1); the noise pushes by (dN/dy, -dN/dx, 0). Drawn at (W/2 + x W, H/2 + y W), one unit W pixels, all at one depth, oldest first, over clear. Opacity Max Opacity / 100 times the map at the age fraction f: Constant 1, Fade In f, Fade Out 1 - f, Fade In and Out 1 - |2 f - 1|, Custom the five levels / 100 at 0, 1/4, 1/2, 3/4, 1, straight-line between. Star: ten points, the j-th 36 j degrees clockwise from up at R (even j) or 0.4 R (odd j); covering clamp(1/2 + e) inside by the even-odd rule, clamp(1/2 - e) outside, e the distance to the nearest edge; never lit; small stars as D-610's small sprites. On the card the `particles` pass draws it with the same rule.

Particle Playground (D-613, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Particle Playground, whose maths is not published, so this rule is ours; `tools/particle_playground_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule. In short: on D-610's random numbers and step grid, a particle is (code, a, b): the Cannon's k-th (1, k, 0), born as D-610's births at Particles Per Second, at the barrel (round for a negative radius, square for a positive one), thrown at Direction plus its spread and Velocity plus its spread; the Grid's j-th of whole frame i (2, j, i), still, at the cell centres; the Layer Exploder's square j (3, j, 0), side 2 Radius Of New Particles, cut at the in point where the layer is not clear and thrown every way at up to Velocity Dispersion.
