A step of h seconds moves x + v h + a h^2 / 2 with a the Gravity when it affects the particle; the Wall cuts the move at the first edge it crosses and turns the velocity off it, at most 4 bounces a step. A force affects a particle when the product of its Particles From, its age test (Older/Younger Than, feathered by Age Feather) and its Selection Map's luminance under the particle is above the particle's own random number for that force. The Particle Exploder replaces an affected particle by clamp(floor((R / c)^2 + 1/2), 1, 100) children of radius c, thrown at up to Velocity Dispersion; children never explode. Drawn over clear in the order born: a dot as D-610's disc, a piece as its square of the cut layer, a particle the Layer Map affects as the map's picture centred on it; Auto-Orient turns them the way they move.

Particle Playground's property mappers (D-614, 2026-10-12), whose maths After Effects does not publish, so this rule is ours; `tools/particle_mapper_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule. In short: a mapper with a map named gives an affected particle (D-613's Affects, groups 4 and 5, random streams 24 and 25), for each channel mapped, lo + (hi - lo) e, e the map's channel at (floor x, floor y), sRGB-coded from its premultiplied value held to 0 to 1 (the build holds maps in single precision and puts an e lying within 1e-4 of an 8-bit level k/255 back on k/255; a finer e is kept). Persistent: the values are set at every step point, after the Particle Exploder and before the step, and kept. Ephemeral: the operator (set a = v, add a + v, difference |a - v|, subtract a - v, multiply a v, min, max) on speed and force for each step, the change taken back afterwards, and on colour, opacity, scale, angle, x and y at the frame drawn, for drawing only. Drawn as D-613 with a dot's radius, a piece's side and a Layer Map's picture times the scale, the angle added to the turn, the covering times the opacity held to 0 to 1, and a dot's colour from its red, green and blue when mapped.

Particle Emitter (D-615, 2026-10-12), whose maths Red Giant does not publish, so this rule is ours; `tools/particle_emitter_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule. In short: the settings are worked into D-610's engine once a frame (Birth Rate = Particles Per Second / 100, Producer from the Emitter Position and Emitter Z, Gravity, Wind and Turbulence over the layer's width W, the floor a share of the height, Animation Explosive); particle k is born at D-610's b_k at the Producer plus M d / W, M the emitter's turn Rx Ry Rz and d its place in the point, box, ball or grid; thrown along M (0, -1, 0) by the Direction's rule (Uniform; Directional and Bi-Directional within 180 Direction Spread / 100 degrees; Disc; Outwards and Inwards from the emitter's middle) at Velocity / W (1 + Velocity Random / 100 (2 r5 - 1)), plus Velocity From Motion of the Producer's own; it lives Life fps (1 - Life Random / 100 r11) frames and steps as D-610's, four a frame from its birth. Its size and opacity are their values at birth times five-step curves over its age fraction, its colour the Color, the five-colour gradient at that fraction or at r13, in linear light, times 1 - Color Random / 100 r14. Drawn through the composition camera, or straight on from W 50 / 36 in front; Sphere covers clamp((R - e) / w + 1/2), w = max(1, Sphere Feather / 100 R), and Glow Sphere a core of half the radius plus a glow (1 - e / R)^2 / 2; Star D-612's, Cloudlet D-610's Faded Sphere, Streak D-610's Line back along one frame's movement; none lit; farthest first.

Particle Grid (D-616, 2026-10-12), whose maths Red Giant does not publish, so this rule is ours; `tools/particle_grid_reference.py` is the reference, its opening text the full rule. In short: point j of n_x n_y n_z sits at d0 in a box grid of Form Size X, Y, Z (cell centres) or on shell m of n_z layered spheres, ring l of n_y, turn i of n_x; each map (stretched over the layer) is read with document 21's bilinear sample (the build putting an opaque colour within 1e-4 of an 8-bit level back on it) at d0's spot on the form's front, L its compound-blur luminance; the noise is D-610's at q = (d0 + F) / Fractal Scale + E (0.31, 0.57, 0.77), F and E the Flow settings summed as the area under their frame-by-frame lines from the in point, in seconds. d = d0 + Disperse (2 r - 1) per axis, d_z minus Map Displace L(Displace Map), turned by Ry(Twist d0_y / S_y), plus Displace (N_0, N_1, N_2); x = C + Rx Ry Rz d / W, C from Form Center and Form Center Z. Size = Size (1 + Size Random / 100 (2 r3 - 1)) max(0, 1 + Affect Size / 100 N_3) L(Size Map); opacity = Opacity / 100 (1 - Opacity Random / 100 r4) clamp(1 + Affect Opacity / 100 N_4) alpha(Color Map); colour the Color or the Color Map's sample unpremultiplied. Drawn with Particle Emitter's camera and sprites (D-615), farthest first, equal depths by j.

Pass Extract's alpha and UnMult (D-552, 2026-10-11), after fnord's EXtractoR; fnord publishes the controls but no formula, so the arithmetic is ours. `tools/extractor_alpha_reference.py` is the reference. A named pass may be four names, the fourth the alpha, read as the other three are (0 outside the data window, not-a-number 0, an infinity the largest number of its sign with `MEDIA_EXR_ADJUSTED`); one or three names give alpha 1 everywhere, as D-349. A new word `unmult`, `off` (when added, and left out of the file while off) or `on`. At each pixel with the pass's values (v_r, v_g, v_b) and alpha A: a = clamp(A, 0, 1); where a <= 0 the pixel is clear (0, 0, 0, 0); otherwise each v becomes v / a when unmult is on, then goes through D-348's black and white points, invert and clamp to o, and the pixel is (o_r a, o_g a, o_b a, a), premultiplied. On the card, the `passx` pass does the same in f64. FX-EXA-001 to 021 pin it.

CC Environment (D-531, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Environment, whose rule CycoreFX does not give, so it is ours. `tools/cc_environment_reference.py` is the reference: The settings. `environment`, D-189's layer setting (`tools/effect_layer_reference.py`), "" when added; the layer named is read whole, at its own size, never fitted to the holder. `mapping`, "spherical" (when added), "probe" or "vertical_cross", exact. `horizontal_pan`, `tilt` and `roll`, -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0 when added; `field_of_view`, 1 to 179 degrees across the drawing's width, 40 when added (about a 50 mm lens on a 36 mm frame). `filter_environment`, "on" (when added) or "off". The rule. At composition frame n, with O the holder's input (linear, premultiplied), w by h, whose drawing's own top-left pixel is at (ox, oy), its drawing W = w - 2 ox by H = h - 2 oy, and E the environment's picture, Ew by Eh: 1. With `environment` "", or naming a layer not in the composition (D-189's warning), the output is O. 2. The ray through a point (x, y) of the drawing (y down, z away from the viewer): c = (x - W/2, y - H/2, F), F = (W/2) / tan(field_of_view / 2). Turned by roll r about z: (cx cos r - cy sin r, cx sin r + cy cos r, cz); then by tilt t about x, positive looking up: (x, y cos t - z sin t, y sin t + z cos t); then by pan p about y, positive looking right: (x cos p + z sin p, y, -x sin p + z cos p); d is that, made unit length. 3. The environment's colour along d, read from E by document 21's bilinear (pixel centres at halves) at a point (u, v) found by the mapping: - "spherical": u = Ew (1/2 + atan2(dx, dz) / (2 pi)), v = Eh (1/2 - asin(-dy) / pi), each tap's column taken round the width and its row held within the picture. - "probe": with s = sqrt(dx^2 + dy^2) and q = acos(dz) / (pi s) (q = 0 where s = 0, the middle), u = Ew (1 + dx q) / 2, v = Eh (1 + dy q) / 2, taps held within the picture: the view ahead at the middle, the view behind round the rim. - "vertical_cross": the picture three faces across and four down, each Ew/3 by Eh/4. The face is the axis d leans on most (x first, then y, then z, where they tie), with (a, b) on it, each -1 to 1: ahead (+z) column 1, row 1, (dx/dz, dy/dz); right (+x) column 2, row 1, (-dz/dx, dy/dx); left (-x) column 0, row 1, (dz/|dx|, dy/|dx|); up (-y) column 1, row 0, (dx/|dy|, dz/|dy|); down (+y) column 1, row 2, (dx/dy, -dz/dy); behind (-z) column 1, row 3, (dx/|dz|, -dy/|dz|), upside down as a vertical cross keeps it. u = Ew/3 (column + (a + 1)/2), v = Eh/4 (row + (b + 1)/2), each held within the face's pixel centres (from its first pixel's centre to its last's), then read with taps held within the picture. With `filter_environment` "on" and "spherical", the colour is the mean of four rays at (x +- 1/4, y +- 1/4) from the pixel's centre; otherwise the one ray through (x + 1/2, y + 1/2). An empty picture (the layer not yet begun) reads as transparent. 4. The output at buffer pixel (X, Y), x = X - ox, y = Y - oy: the environment's colour times O's alpha there, so the environment fills the layer's shape and nothing beyond it. The layer never grows. A draft reads a drawing and an environment both smaller, every distance being a share of them, so the same view, coarser. FX-ENV-001 to 030 pin it.

CC Sphere (D-532, 2026-10-11), after CycoreFX's CC Sphere, whose rule CycoreFX does not give, so it is ours. `tools/cc_sphere_reference.py` is the reference: The settings, every number keyable. `radius`, 0 to 1000 per cent of half the drawing's width, 50 when added; `offset`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50] when added; `rotation_x`, `rotation_y` and `rotation_z`, -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0 when added; `light_intensity`, 0 to 1000 per cent, 100 when added; `light_height`, -100 to 100, 50 when added; `light_direction`, -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0 up and 90 right, -45 when added; `ambient` 40, `diffuse` 60, `specular` 50, `roughness` 10 and `metal` 100 when added, each 0 to 100. `light_color`, `#rrggbb`, "#ffffff" when added; `render`, "full" (when added), "outside" or "inside", exact. The rule, in the drawing's own W by H space, y down, z away from the viewer. A fixed eye sits at E = (W/2, H/2, -Z0), Z0 = W 50/36 (a 50 mm lens on a 36 mm frame), and looks through the pixel's centre P = (x + 1/2, y + 1/2, 0) along d = P - E. The sphere's centre is C = (ox W/100, oy H/100, 0), its radius R = radius W/200, turned by M = Rz Ry Rx (x first), with Rx: (y cos - z sin, y sin + z cos), Ry: (x cos + z sin, -x sin + z cos), Rz: (x cos - y sin, x sin + y cos). In the sphere's own space the eye is o = M^T (E - C) and the ray's direction is v = M^T d. The ray meets |h| = R at t = (-b -+ sqrt(b^2 - a c)) / a, a = v . v, b = o . v, c = o . o - R^2: the nearer root is the outside, the farther the inside, each only when t > 0. Nothing is hit when R is 0 or b^2 - a c < 0. The picture on the sphere, the drawing as a map of the world: at the hit h = o + t v, phi = atan2(hx, -hz) and lat = asin(hy / R), the drawing at (W/2 + phi W / (2 pi), H/2 + lat H / pi) by document 21's bilinear, each tap's column taken round the drawing's width and its row held to the drawing's top or bottom row; the middle column faces the eye, the top row is the pole at the top. The light, from the direction L = (cos e sin g, -cos e cos g, -sin e), g the direction and e = height 90/100 degrees (100 is from the eye's side, -100 from behind), turned into the sphere's own space. The normal n = h / R, turned round for the inside; V the unit vector back to the eye. With N.L = n . L, the half vector Hv = (L + V) / |L + V| and p = 2 / (roughness/100)^2, the highlight s = max(n . Hv, 0)^p, 0 when roughness is 0, when N.L <= 0 or when L + V is 0. With i = light_intensity/100, Lc the light's linear colour and A, D, S, Mt the shading numbers over 100, on the sampled premultiplied (c, alpha): rgb = c (A + D i Lc max(N.L, 0)) + S i s (Mt c + (1 - Mt) Lc alpha), alpha unchanged. "outside" draws the outside hit, "inside" the inside hit, "full" the outside over the inside. The layer never grows: the sphere is drawn inside the layer's own picture. A draft changes nothing, every distance being a share of the drawing. One ray a pixel, so the sphere's edge is not smoothed. FX-SPH-001 to 026 pin it.

Card Wipe (D-580, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Card Wipe, whose rule Adobe does not give, so it is ours. `tools/card_wipe_reference.py` is the reference. The settings, every number keyable, the words exact. `transition_completion` and `transition_width`, 0 to 100, 0 and 50 when added; `back_layer` and `gradient_layer`, D-189's layer words, "" when added, each fitted to the drawing as `stretch` fits it; `rows_columns` "independent" (when added) or "columns_follow_rows"; `rows` and `columns`, 1 to 1000, 8, each rounded to the nearest whole number, halves away from zero; `card_scale`, 0 to 10, 1; `flip_axis` "x" (when added), "y" or "random"; `flip_direction` "positive" (when added), "negative" or "random"; `flip_order` "left_to_right" (when added), "right_to_left", "top_to_bottom", "bottom_to_top", "top_left_to_bottom_right", "bottom_left_to_top_right", "top_right_to_bottom_left", "bottom_right_to_top_left" or "gradient"; `timing_randomness`, 0 to 1, 0; `random_seed`, 0 to 100000, 0, floored; `x_rotation`, `y_rotation`, `z_rotation`, -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `xy_position`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the drawing's width and height, [50, 50]; `z_position`, 0.01 to 10 focal lengths, 1; `focal_length`, 1 to 1000 mm on a 36 mm frame, 50; `camera_system` "camera_position" (when added) or "corner_pins" (D-634), with the four pins and `auto_focal_length` of the Corner Pins rule below; `light_type` "distant" (when added) or "point"; `light_intensity`, 0 to 10, 1; `light_color`, `#rrggbb`, "#ffffff"; `light_position`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent, [50, 50]; `light_depth`, 0 to 10 drawing widths towards the viewer, 1; `ambient_light` 0.25, `diffuse_reflection` 0.75 and `specular_reflection` 0, each 0 to 2; `highlight_sharpness`, 1 to 100, 5. The rule, in the drawing's own W by H space, y down, z away from the viewer. (1) The cards: R rows and C columns (C = R for "columns_follow_rows"); card (a, b) is cw = W/C by ch = H/R, centred at ((a + 1/2) cw, (b + 1/2) ch), drawn s = card_scale times its size, half sizes hx = s cw/2, hy = s ch/2. (2) When each flips: with u = (a + 1/2)/C and v = (b + 1/2)/R the order o is u, 1 - u, v, 1 - v, (u + v)/2, (u + 1 - v)/2, (1 - u + v)/2 or (2 - u - v)/2 for the eight directions as listed, or for "gradient" document 21's luma (clamped to 0..1) of the gradient picture's pixel (floor cx, floor cy), 0 with no gradient picture; with g = U(floor seed, a, b, 0, ch) (Noise's hash, D-119) and T the randomness, o' = (1 - T) o + T (g0 + 1)/2; with c = completion/100 and w = width/100 the progress p = clamp((c (1 + w) - o')/w, 0, 1), or for w = 0, 1 where c > 0 and c >= o' and 0 otherwise; the axis is x, y, or for "random" x where g1 < 0; the sign +1, -1, or for "random" -1 where g2 < 0; the card turns by theta = sign p pi about its own axis through its centre, about x (x, y cos - z sin, y sin + z cos), about y (x cos + z sin, y, -x sin + z cos). (3) The camera: F = W focal/36; the eye at E = (xy_x W/100, xy_y H/100, -z F) looks through the pixel's centre along d = (x + 1/2 - W/2, y + 1/2 - H/2, F), turned with the scene about C0 = (W/2, H/2, 0) by M = Rz Ry Rx (x first; Rz (x cos - y sin, x sin + y cos)); in the cards' space o = M^T (E - C0), v = M^T d; as added each pixel sees itself. (4) The hits: for card (a, b), q = (cx - W/2, cy - H/2, 0), o'' = R^T (o - q), v'' = R^T v with R the card's turn; where v''z is not 0, t = -o''z/v''z, a hit when t > 0 and |lx| <= hx, |ly| <= hy for (lx, ly) = o''xy + t v''xy; the least t wins, ties to the first card in rows then columns; Card Scale 0 draws nothing. (5) The colour: where v''z > 0 the front, the drawing at (cx + lx/s, cy + ly/s) by document 21's bilinear, transparent outside; otherwise the back, the back picture at (cx - lx/s, cy + ly/s) about y or (cx + lx/s, cy - ly/s) about x, so a card turned over shows its share upright; with no back picture (none named, a layer not in the composition with D-189's warning, or one not yet begun) the back is clear. (6) The light, in the cards' space: P = M^T ((lp_x W/100, lp_y H/100, -depth W) - C0), h = o + t v, L = unit(P) for "distant" or unit(P - h) for "point" (a zero step stays zero); the front's normal (0, sin theta, -cos theta) about x, (-sin theta, 0, -cos theta) about y, the back's its opposite; V = -v/|v|, Hv = L + V; sp = max(N.Hv/|Hv|, 0)^sharpness where N.L > 0 and |Hv| > 0, else 0 (the build takes this power in single precision); with i the intensity, Lc the light's linear colour and A, D, S the three numbers, on the sampled premultiplied (c, alpha): rgb = c (A + D i Lc max(N.L, 0)) + S i sp Lc alpha, alpha unchanged. (7) Buffer pixel (X, Y) reads x = X - ox, y = Y - oy and takes the nearest hit's colour, or clear. The build tries only the rows and columns a ray can reach inside the slab |z| <= max(hx, hy), with a card's margin each way, which keeps the same nearest hit. The layer never grows. A draft changes nothing, every distance being a share of the drawing. One ray a pixel, so the cards' edges are not smoothed. With "corner_pins" the eye and the ray come from the Corner Pins rule below, the turn the identity, so the light stays on the layer. FX-CW-001 to 069 pin it.

CC Force Motion Blur (D-581, 2026-10-12), after CycoreFX's CC Force Motion Blur, whose rule CycoreFX does not give, so it is ours. `tools/force_motion_blur_reference.py` is the reference. The settings: `motion_blur_samples`, 1 to 64, 8 when added; `override_native_shutter` "off" (when added) or "on"; `shutter_angle`, 0 to 720 degrees, 180; `native_motion_blur` "off" or "on" (when added); the two numbers keyable. At the holder's composition frame n, its settings held inside their ranges at n: (1) only a composition layer is blurred; on any other layer, an adjustment layer included, or a holder with a Time Remap, the layer is left as it is and each frame records EFFECT_UNSUPPORTED. (2) The shutter angle A is `shutter_angle` when the override is on; otherwise the composition's own shutter angle when its motion blur and the holder's switch are both on; otherwise 0. (3) N = 1 when A is 0, else floor(`motion_blur_samples`); the shutter opens on the frame (the phase taken as 0, as CycoreFX ignores it), the moments n + (A/360) k/N for k = 0 .. N-1. (4) At moment k the composition inside is drawn at its whole source frame with every key inside it (transforms, parents, masks, opacity, effect settings, inner remaps) read (A/360)(k/N) 100/s frames later, s the holder's time stretch; drawings, in and out points, text, shapes and expressions hold at the whole frame. (5) The N pictures are summed in linear premultiplied light and divided by N; the result replaces what effects before it made, as CC Wide Time's does, and the holder's transform, its own motion blur, opacity and blend then apply as for any layer. (6) Native Motion Blur on keeps the compositions inside drawn with their own D-188 motion blur; off draws them with it switched off. The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing. FX-FMB-001 to 030 pin it.

CC Rainfall (D-582, 2026-10-12), after CycoreFX's CC Rainfall, whose rule CycoreFX does not give, so it is ours. `tools/cc_rainfall_reference.py` is the reference. The settings, every number keyable, the words exact: `drops` 0 to 50000, 5000 when added, its whole part counting; `size` 0 to 20 pixels, 3; `scene_depth` 0 to 20000 pixels, 5000; `speed` 0 to 20000 pixels a second, 4000; `wind` -5000 to 5000 pixels a second, 0; `wind_variation` 0 to 100 per cent, 0; `spread` 0 to 90 degrees, 15; `color` `#rrggbb`, "#b4bec8", saved in small letters; `opacity` 0 to 100, 25; `transfer_mode` "composite" or "lighten" (when added); `composite_with_original` "off" or "on" (when added); `ground_level` 0 to 100 per cent of the height from the top, 100; `random_seed` 0 to 100000, 0, its whole part counting. At composition frame n, at r frames a second, on a drawing w by h (its own size, before anything an earlier effect grew): (1) the drops are i = 0 .. floor(drops) - 1, each with five numbers u_k = (U(seed, i, 0, 0, k) + 1)/2, U Noise's hash (D-119). (2) Depth d = scene_depth u_0; with F = w, the scale s = F/(F + d). (3) The tilt theta = spread (u_1 - 1/2) degrees, its own wind wind (1 + wind_variation/100 (2 u_2 - 1)); on the picture it moves (vx, vy) = s (own wind + speed tan theta, speed) pixels a second. (4) Its streak L = (vx, vy)/(2r); its width ws = size s, its strength opacity/100 s. (5) The ground g = ground_level/100 h; the head falls from top = -(L_y + e), e = max(ws/2 + 1/2, 1), to the ground, R = g - top, and starts again: p = frac(u_3 + vy (n/r)/R), head y = top + p R; across, x0 = w (3/2 u_4 - 1/4), head x = x0 + (vx/vy)(p R - R/2), or x0 when vy is 0; the tail is the head less L; a drop with ws 0 or R at most 0 is not drawn. (6) At a pixel centre whose y is at most g, with delta its distance from the segment tail to head, the cover is clamp(ws/2 + 1/2 - delta, 0, 1) for ws at least 1, else ws clamp(1 - delta, 0, 1). (7) The drops are laid far to near (d largest first, equal depths by i), each at strength times cover, in `color` (linear), normal, onto clear: the rain. (8) Composite With Original off: the rain alone; on: the rain over the drawing, and with Lighten each colour channel is then the larger of that and the drawing's, so it never darkens. Buffer pixel (X, Y) reads x = X - ox, y = Y - oy. The layer never grows; a draft scales size, scene depth, speed and wind with the picture. FX-RAIN3D-001 to 037 pin it.

Wave World (D-630, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Wave World (Simulation), whose help gives no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/wave_world_reference.py` is the reference. The settings, every number keyable and held to its range, the words exact: `brightness` 0 to 1, 0.5 when added; `contrast` 0 to 10, 0.5; `gamma` 0.1 to 10, 1; `render_dry_areas` "level" (when added) or "transparent"; `transparency` 0 to 1, 0; `grid_resolution` 1 to 600, 60, rounded half away from zero; `grid_res_downsamples` "off" (when added) or "on"; `wave_speed` 0 to 1 layer widths a second, 0.25; `damping` 0 to 1, 0.05; `reflect_edges` "none" (when added) or "all"; `pre_roll` 0 to 10 seconds, 0; `ground_layer` D-189's layer setting, "" when added, fitted as `stretch`; `steepness` 0 to 1, 0.25; `height` 0 to 1, 0.5; `wave_strength` 0 to 10, 0; for k = 1 and 2, `producer_k_type` "ring" (when added) or "line"; `producer_k_position` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the layer, [50, 50] and [75, 25]; `producer_k_height` (a line's length) and `producer_k_width` 0 to 10 layer widths, 0.1; `producer_k_angle` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `producer_k_amplitude` 0 to 10, 0.5 and 0; `producer_k_frequency` 0 to 30 a second, 1; `producer_k_phase` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0. D-635's view: `view` "height_map" (when added) or "wireframe_preview"; `horizontal_rotation` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `vertical_rotation` -90 to 90 degrees, 30; `vertical_scale` 0 to 10, 1; these four read as when added where missing and written only when given. On a layer W by H at r frames a second, drawn at composition frame n, its in point n0, lengths in layer widths: (1) sample j is every number at frame n0 + j, j = 0 .. n - n0, read in a straight line between samples; grid resolution, downsamples, reflect edges and pre-roll come from sample 0, the ground picture from frame n0. (2) N = the grid resolution (times the draft's scale with downsamples on, rounded, at least 1) columns, M = max(1, round(N H / W)) rows, cell centres ((i + 1/2) dx, (j + 1/2) dy), dx = 1/N, dy = H/(M W). (3) g = the luma, clamped, of the ground picture read bilinearly at the cell centre (0 with none, a missing layer with D-189's warning, or one not begun at n0); the floor e = steepness g, the depth d = height - e; d <= 0 is dry, else c^2 = 2 d wave_speed^2. (4) h and u start at 0; the pre-roll is ceil(pre_roll r) frame lengths of q0 steps on sample 0; then frame interval j takes q_j steps of dt = 1/(r q_j), q = max(4, ceil(1.6 cmax/(r min(dx, dy)))), cmax the largest wave_speed sqrt(2 height) the interval reads. (5) A step from settings a to b: for every wet cell at once, a dry neighbour or (with "all") the edge gives the cell's own h, the edge with "none" gives h - s u/c (s = dx or dy); u += dt c^2 (the five-point Laplacian); u *= exp(-10 damping dt); h += dt u; then each producer adds w (P(b) - P(a)) and the ground adds wave_strength_b (e_b - e_a); dry cells hold 0. (6) P = amplitude (sin(T + phase) - sin(phase)), T the running sum of 2 pi frequency over time (linear between samples, quadratic within an interval); the weight about the producer's centre, the offset turned back by the angle to (a', b'): a ring w = (1 - rr)^2 where rr = (2a'/width)^2 + (2b'/height)^2 < 1, a line w = (1 - (2b'/width)^2)^2 where |a'| <= height/2 and |2b'| < width; 0 when width or height is 0. (7) At pixel (x, y) of the layer, h and d (d from this frame's height and steepness) are read bilinearly at gx = (x + 1/2) N/W - 1/2, gy = (y + 1/2) M/H - 1/2 held inside the grid; dry pixels are h = 0 ("level") or clear ("transparent"); v = clamp(brightness + contrast h, 0, 1), grey = v^(1/gamma), a = clamp(1 - transparency (1 - max(d, 0)/height), 0, 1) (1 - transparency where height is 0), and the pixel is (srgb_to_linear(grey) a, ..., a). (8) With `view` "wireframe_preview", in place of step 7 (D-635): each cell's point, in pixels from the layer's middle, a = (i + 1/2) W / N - W/2 and b = (j + 1/2) H / M - H/2, raised by z = h vertical_scale W / 10 (the current frame's h and vertical scale), is turned by the horizontal rotation p about the upright, a' = a cos p - b sin p, b' = a sin p + b cos p, and seen from the vertical rotation t above the water, square-on, at (sx, sy) = (W/2 - 1/2 + a', H/2 - 1/2 + b' sin t - z cos t). A segment joins each wet cell to its right and its lower neighbour where that is wet too (wet by the current frame's d). Buffer pixel (x, y) is opaque white (1, 1, 1, 1) where its centre (x + 1/2, y + 1/2) lies less than 1/2 from a segment (from its one point where both ends meet), else clear. Then each producer whose amplitude at the frame is not 0, its centre (px W / 100 - W/2, py H / 100 - H/2) at z = 0 seen the same way, marks the pixel (fl sx, fl sy) and the two beside it on each side, across and down: producer 1 red (1, 0, 0, 1), then producer 2 blue (0, 0, 1, 1) over it. The layer's own picture is not used and the layer never grows; no state is carried between frames (D-610's rule). FX-WW-001 to 062 pin it.

The motion estimate (D-650, ADR-022, 2026-10-12), ours: After Effects publishes no formula for its vectors. `tools/flow_reference.py` is the reference. Given pictures A and B of the same size, linear and premultiplied, it finds for each pixel p a vector u(p), in pixels, with A(p) close to B(p + u(p)); "held" means an index outside the picture moved to its nearest edge. (1) Two channels: c0 = 0.3 R + 0.6 G + 0.1 B of the premultiplied colour, and c1 = alpha. (2) A pyramid: level 0 the picture, level l + 1 ceil(w / 2) by ceil(h / 2), each pixel the mean of level l's four at (2x, 2y) to (2x + 1, 2y + 1), held; the top level L the largest whole number up to 6 with min(w, h) / 2^L >= 8 (0 when smaller). (3) Vector Detail VD picks the finest level lf = floor(log2(100 / VD) + 0.5) held inside 0 to L, and L for VD 0. (4) r = max(1, floor(6 / 2)) = 3 (Block Size 6); box_r(f) at p sums f over the (2r + 1)^2 pixels around p, held, across then down; lambda = (2r + 1)^2 (0.0001 + 0.01 x 0.5) (Global Smoothness 0.5). (5) From level L down to lf: u starts at 0 on level L, on a lower level it is the level above's u read bilinearly, held, at ((x + 0.5) / 2 - 0.5, (y + 0.5) / 2 - 0.5), times 2; A's gradients by central differences, held, Ax = (A(x + 1, y) - A(x - 1, y)) / 2 and Ay likewise; G = box_r of the sums over both channels of Ax Ax, Ax Ay, Ay Ay; 4 times: e = B read bilinearly, held, at (x + ux, y + uy) minus A(x, y), each channel, b = box_r of the sums of Ax e and Ay e, and with a = Gxx + lambda, d = Gyy + lambda, c = Gxy, det = a d - c^2: ux -= (d bx - c by) / det, uy -= (a by - c bx) / det; then u += 0.5 (box_r(u) / (2r + 1)^2 - u) (Local Smoothness 0.5). (6) The field at full size: level lf's u read bilinearly, held, at ((x + 0.5) / 2^lf - 0.5, (y + 0.5) / 2^lf - 0.5), times 2^lf. Everything in double precision; no search and no choice on the data, so the same pictures give the same field.

Pixel Motion Blur (D-650, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Pixel Motion Blur, whose page names the four controls and says Automatic takes the composition's motion blur settings but gives no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/pixel_motion_blur_reference.py` is the reference. `core.pixel_motion_blur`: `shutter_control` "automatic" (when added) or "manual", exact; `shutter_angle` 0 to 720 degrees, 180 when added; `shutter_samples` 2 to 64, 16; `vector_detail` 0 to 100, 20; the three numbers keyable and read at frame n. At the holder's composition frame n, its input O, W by H: (1) on an adjustment layer the output is O and the frame says TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED; (2) "automatic" takes the angle and the sample count from the composition's motion blur settings (D-188; 180 and 16 when it has none), whether its switch is on or not, "manual" takes `shutter_angle` and `shutter_samples`; a = angle / 360, N = floor(samples); with a = 0 the output is O; (3) P_- and P_+ are the holder at composition frames n - 1 and n + 1 with the effects before Pixel Motion Blur, by the layer's own timing, laid on O corner to corner and cut to W by H, clear outside; a frame outside the layer's in and out points is O itself; (4) F = the estimate from O to P_+ and B = the estimate from O to P_-, with `vector_detail`; (5) s_k = a ((k + 0.5) / N - 0.5), k = 0 to N - 1, V_k = B where s_k < 0, else F; (6) the output at (x, y) is (1 / N) times the sum over k of O read bilinearly (this document's sample, pixel centres at halves, clear outside) at (x + 0.5 - |s_k| V_k,x, y + 0.5 - |s_k| V_k,y), all four numbers. The bounds never grow. Draft draws O, P_- and P_+ at its own size; no setting is a distance, so nothing else changes. Processor only. FX-PMB-001 to 024 pin it.

Caustics (D-631, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Caustics (Simulation), whose help gives no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/caustics_reference.py` is the reference. The settings: Bottom: `bottom_layer` D-189's layer word, "" when added (the effect's own drawing); `bottom_scaling` 0.1 to 10, 1; `bottom_repeat_mode` "once", "tiled" (when added) or "reflected"; `bottom_fit` "center" or "stretch" (when added), After Effects' If Layer Size Differs; `bottom_blur` 0 to 50, 0. Water: `water_surface` a layer word, ""; `wave_height` 0 to 1, 0.2; `smoothing` 0 to 50, 5; `water_depth` 0 to 1, 0.1; `refractive_index` 1 to 2, 1.2; `surface_color` "#rrggbb", "#2a7fd4"; `surface_opacity` 0 to 1, 0.3; `caustics_strength` 0 to 1, 0. Sky: `sky_layer` a layer word, ""; `sky_scaling` 0.1 to 10, 1; `sky_repeat_mode` as the bottom's, "tiled"; `sky_fit` as the bottom's, "stretch"; `sky_intensity` 0 to 1, 0.3; `sky_convergence` 0 to 1, 0.2. Light and material, Card Wipe's (D-580): `light_type` "distant" (when added) or "point"; `light_intensity` 0 to 10, 1; `light_color` "#rrggbb", "#ffffff"; `light_position` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the layer, [50, 50]; `light_height` 0 to 10 layer widths, 1; `ambient_light` 0.25, `diffuse_reflection` 0.75, `specular_reflection` 0, each 0 to 2; `highlight_sharpness` 1 to 100, 5. Every number is keyable and held to its range; the words are exact. On a layer W by H pixels, at its pixel (x, y), its centre p = (x + 1/2, y + 1/2): (1) The pictures. The water surface is the named layer's picture stretched to the layer (D-189); the bottom and the sky are their layers' whole pictures, Q, w by h. A layer not begun is a clear picture. With no water surface named, or one not in the composition (D-189's warning), the water is flat. With no bottom named, the bottom is the layer's own drawing; one named but not in the composition is a clear bottom (and the warning). With no sky named, or one not there (and the warning), there is no sky. (2) Placing the bottom and the sky. With s the scaling, a = (x + 1/2 - W/2) / s + W/2 and b = (y + 1/2 - H/2) / s + H/2, the placed picture B at (x, y) is Q read bilinearly (pixel centres at halves) at (a - floor((W - w) / 2), b - floor((H - h) / 2)) for "center", or at (a w / W, b h / H) for "stretch"; its pixel indices i outside 0..w - 1 (or 0..h - 1) taken by the repeat mode: "once" clear, "tiled" i mod w, "reflected" m = i mod 2w, m or else 2w - 1 - m. An empty Q places clear. (3) The blurs. G(sigma) is the Gaussian of taps exp(-i^2 / (2 sigma^2)), |i| <= ceil(3 sigma), summed to 1, run across and then down with the outer pixels held; sigma 0 leaves a picture as it is. The bottom's placed picture is blurred by G(bottom_blur W / 1000), each channel of its premultiplied light. (4) The water. L = document 21's luma (clamped to 0..1) of the surface picture at each pixel (0.5 everywhere for flat water), blurred by G(smoothing W / 1000); the surface's height toward the viewer is z = wave_height (L - 1/2) W / 4. Its slopes gx = (z(x + 1, y) - z(x - 1, y)) / 2, gy likewise down, the neighbours held inside the layer. In the layer's space (x right, y down, depth away from the viewer) the surface's normal is N = (-gx, -gy, -1) / sqrt(gx^2 + gy^2 + 1). (5) Bending. The view looks straight down the depth, I = (0, 0, 1). With e = 1 / refractive_index, c = -N_z, k = 1 - e^2 (1 - c^2), the bent ray T = e I + (e c - sqrt(k)) N, and the bottom, D = water_depth W below, is read at p + o, o = D (T_x, T_y) / T_z. (6) The caustics. With o's differences taken as z's are, det = (1 + dox/dx)(1 + doy/dy) - (dox/dy)(doy/dx), C = 1 / max(|det|, 0.1), and the light on the bottom M = 1 + caustics_strength (C - 1). (7) The bottom seen: b = the blurred placed bottom read bilinearly at p + o, the place held inside the layer's pixel centres; its colour times M, its alpha as read. (8) The sky. The view's mirror R = I - 2 (I.N) N; the placed sky (not blurred) read as b is at p + sky_convergence (W / 2) (R_x, R_y), (r, a) premultiplied, a clear (0, 0) with no sky. The surface's colour s = Sc (1 - sky_intensity a) + sky_intensity r, Sc the surface colour's linear light. (9) The light, Card Wipe's: P = (lx W / 100 - W / 2, ly H / 100 - H / 2, -light_height W); Lv = unit(P) for "distant", unit(P - h) for "point", h = (p_x - W/2, p_y - H/2, 0) (a zero step stays zero). V = (0, 0, -1), Hv = Lv + V, the highlight sp = max(N.Hv / |Hv|, 0)^sharpness where N.Lv > 0 and |Hv| > 0, else 0. With i the intensity, Lc the light's linear colour and A, D, S the ambient, diffuse and specular numbers, the lit surface s' = s (A + D i Lc max(N.Lv, 0)) + S i sp Lc. (10) The pixel, with O the surface opacity: (b_rgb M (1 - O) + s' O, b_alpha (1 - O) + O). The layer never grows; buffer pixels outside it are clear; a draft draws the same picture on fewer pixels, every length a share of the layer; no state is carried between frames (D-610's rule). FX-CA-001 to 038 pin it.

Card Dance (D-632, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Card Dance (Simulation), whose help gives no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/card_dance_reference.py` is the reference. The settings: `rows_columns` "independent" (when added) or "columns_follow_rows"; `rows` and `columns` 1 to 1000, 8, each rounded to the nearest whole number (halves away from zero); `back_layer` D-189's layer word, "" when added (clear backs), fitted to the layer as `stretch`; `gradient_layer_1` and `gradient_layer_2` layer words, "" when added (the layer's own drawing), fitted as `stretch`; `rotation_order` "xyz" (when added), "xzy", "yxz", "yzx", "zxy" or "zyx", the turns in the order written; `transformation_order` "scale_rotate_position" (when added), "scale_position_rotate", "rotate_scale_position", "rotate_position_scale", "position_scale_rotate" or "position_rotate_scale". For each T of `x_position`, `y_position`, `z_position`, `x_rotation`, `y_rotation`, `z_rotation`, `x_scale` and `y_scale`: `T_source` "none", "red_1", "green_1", "blue_1", "intensity_1", "alpha_1", or the same ending "_2"; `T_multiplier` and `T_offset`, -100 to 100 card sizes for the positions (1 and 0 when added), -3600 to 3600 degrees for the rotations (90 and 0), -10 to 10 for the scales (0.5 and 1). When added every source is "none" but `z_position_source`, "intensity_1". The camera: `camera_system` "camera_position" (when added), "comp_camera" or "corner_pins" (D-634, with the four pins and `auto_focal_length` of the Corner Pins rule below); Card Wipe's `camera_x_rotation`, `camera_y_rotation`, `camera_z_rotation` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `camera_xy_position` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the layer, [50, 50]; `camera_z_position` 0.01 to 10, 1; `focal_length` 1 to 1000 mm on a 36 mm frame, 50. The light and material, Card Wipe's: `light_type` "distant" (when added) or "point"; `light_intensity` 0 to 10, 1; `light_color` #rrggbb, "#ffffff"; `light_position` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent, [50, 50]; `light_depth` 0 to 10 layer widths toward the viewer, 1; `ambient_light` 0.25, `diffuse_reflection` 0.75, `specular_reflection` 0, each 0 to 2; `highlight_sharpness` 1 to 100, 5. Every number is keyable; the words are exact. In the layer's own W by H space, y down, z away from the viewer, the origin at its middle C0 = (W/2, H/2, 0): (1) The cards. R = rows and C = columns, or C = R with "columns_follow_rows", each rounded. A card is cw = W/C by ch = H/R; card (a, b), column a and row b, has its home centre (cx, cy) = ((a + 1/2) cw, (b + 1/2) ch), q = (cx - W/2, cy - H/2, 0) from C0. (2) The sources. The gradient picture is the named layer's picture fitted to the layer, the layer's own drawing for "", a clear picture for a layer not in the composition (D-189's warning) or not yet begun. Its pixel (min(floor cx, W - 1), min(floor cy, H - 1)), premultiplied (r, g, b, a), gives v: red linear_to_srgb(clamp(r, 0, 1)), green and blue likewise, intensity document 21's luma (clamped, encoded), alpha a. A setting reads (2 v - 1) multiplier + offset, or the offset alone for "none". (3) The card's place. With the numbers px, py, pz, rx, ry, rz, sx, sy: the scale S = diag(sx, sy, 1); the turn R the product of Rx(rx), Ry(ry), Rz(rz) (each as in Card Wipe, Rx (x, y cos - z sin, y sin + z cos), Ry (x cos + z sin, y, -x sin + z cos), Rz (x cos - y sin, x sin + y cos, z)) with the first-written turn applied first (so "xyz" is Rz Ry Rx); the move P = (px cw, py ch, -pz ch) (a positive Z toward the viewer). Starting from A = I, B = 0, each step in the transformation order, applied first to last, is: scale A = S A, B = S B; rotate A = R A, B = R B; position B = B + P. The card's point (lx, ly), |lx| <= cw/2 and |ly| <= ch/2, is at A (lx, ly, 0) + B + q. (4) The camera. "camera_position": Card Wipe's: F = W focal/36, M = Rz Ry Rx of the camera's turns; the eye o = M^T ((xy_x W/100, xy_y H/100, -z F) - C0) and the ray through pixel (x, y) v = M^T (x + 1/2 - W/2, y + 1/2 - H/2, F). "comp_camera": the composition's camera, or D-58's default one where the file has none, given in the layer's own pixels as its place (ax, ay) and its distance A_l from the layer's plane (compose works them out, as for Particle Space): M = I, o = (ax - W/2, ay - H/2, -A_l), v = (x + 1/2 - ax, y + 1/2 - ay, A_l); nothing is drawn where A_l <= 0. The camera turns are not used. "corner_pins": the Corner Pins rule below, M = I. (5) The hits. For each card, with A0 and A1 A's first two columns, solve lx A0 + ly A1 - t v = o - B - q; the card is missed where that has no single answer (an edge-on card, a scale of 0). A hit needs t > 0, |lx| <= cw/2 and |ly| <= ch/2. The nearest hit (least t) wins; where two tie exactly, the first card in rows then columns. (6) The colour. With N = A0 x A1 and s the sign of sx sy, the ray meets the front where s (v . N) > 0: the drawing at (cx + lx, cy + ly) by document 21's bilinear, transparent outside; otherwise the back: the back picture at (cx - lx, cy + ly), or clear with no back picture (none named, not in the composition, with the warning, or not begun). (7) The light, Card Wipe's: Pl = M^T ((lp_x W/100, lp_y H/100, -depth W) - C0); the hit h = o + t v; L = unit(Pl) for "distant", unit(Pl - h) for "point" (a zero step stays zero). The front's normal n = -s N/|N|, the back's its opposite. V = -v/|v|, Hv = L + V; the highlight sp = max(n.Hv/|Hv|, 0)^sharpness where n.L > 0 and |Hv| > 0, else 0. With i the intensity, Lc the light's linear colour, A, D, S the ambient, diffuse and specular numbers, on the sampled premultiplied (c, alpha): rgb = c (A + D i Lc max(n.L, 0)) + S i sp Lc alpha, alpha unchanged. (8) The output at buffer pixel (X, Y), x = X - ox, y = Y - oy: the nearest hit's colour, or clear. The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing, every distance being a share of the layer; no state is carried between frames (D-610's rule). FX-CD-001 to 076 pin it.

Shatter (D-633, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Shatter (Simulation), whose help gives no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/shatter_reference.py` is the reference. The settings: `view` "rendered" (when added), "wireframe_front", "wireframe", "wireframe_front_forces" or "wireframe_forces" (D-635, step 9); `render` "all" (when added), "layer" (only pieces not yet freed) or "pieces" (only freed pieces); `pattern` "bricks" (when added), "squares", "hexagons", "triangles_1", "triangles_2", "rhombus", "planks", "herringbone_1", "octagons_squares", "glass", "custom", or (D-636) "herringbone_2", "squares_triangles", "stars_triangles", "overlapping_squares", "chevrons", "puzzle", "js", "eggs", "crescents" or "carpenters_wheel"; `custom_shatter_map` D-189's layer word, "" when added (the layer's own drawing), fitted as `stretch`; `white_tiles_fixed` "off" (when added) or "on"; `repetitions` 1 to 1000 tiles across the layer, 10; `direction` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 0; `origin` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the layer, [50, 50]; `extrusion_depth` 0 to 1 layer widths, 0.2. For each force F of 1 and 2: `force_F_position` x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent, [50, 50]; `force_F_depth` -10 to 10 layer widths toward the viewer, 0.1; `force_F_radius` 0 to 10 layer widths, 0.4 for force 1 and 0 for force 2; `force_F_strength` -100 to 100 layer widths a second, 5. `gradient_layer` a layer word, "" when added (no gradient: every piece reached is freed), fitted as `stretch`; `shatter_threshold` 0 to 100 per cent, 0; `invert_gradient` "off" (when added) or "on". `rotation_speed` 0 to 10 turns a second, 0.2; `tumble_axis` "free" (when added), "none", "x", "y", "z", "xy", "xz" or "yz"; `randomness` 0 to 1, 0.1; `viscosity` 0 to 10 a second, 0.1; `mass_variance` 0 to 100 per cent, 30; `gravity` 0 to 100 layer widths a second squared, 3; `gravity_direction` -3600 to 3600 degrees, 180 (down); `gravity_inclination` -90 to 90 degrees, 0. `color` #rrggbb, "#ffffff"; `opacity` 0 to 1, 1; `front_mode`, `side_mode` and `back_mode` each "color", "layer", "tinted_layer", "color_plus_opacity", "layer_plus_opacity" or "tinted_layer_plus_opacity", when added "layer", "tinted_layer" and "layer"; `front_layer`, `side_layer` and `back_layer` layer words, "" when added (the layer's own drawing), fitted as `stretch`. The camera and the light, Card Dance's exactly: `camera_system` "camera_position", "comp_camera" or "corner_pins" (D-634, with the four pins and `auto_focal_length` of the Corner Pins rule below); `camera_x_rotation`, `camera_y_rotation`, `camera_z_rotation`, `camera_xy_position`, `camera_z_position`, `focal_length`, `light_type`, `light_intensity`, `light_color`, `light_position`, `light_depth`, `ambient_light`, `diffuse_reflection`, `specular_reflection`, `highlight_sharpness`, with Card Dance's ranges and settings when added. Every number is keyable; the words are exact. In the layer's own W by H space, y down, z away from the viewer, the origin at its middle C0 = (W/2, H/2, 0): (1) The pattern. A pixel (x, y)'s centre p = (x + 1/2, y + 1/2) goes to pattern space: q = Rot(-direction) (p - (origin_x W/100, origin_y H/100)) repetitions / W, Rot turning (a, b) to (a cos - b sin, a sin + b cos). Its tile, with fl the floor and r3 = sqrt 3 / 2: squares (fl qx, fl qy); bricks, rows 1/2 high, every other row moved half a brick: r = fl(2 qy), (fl(qx + (r mod 2)/2), r); planks, rows 1/4 high, 2 long, each row moved 0.7: r = fl(4 qy), (fl((qx + 0.7 r)/2), r); triangles_1, each square cut corner to corner: (fl qx, fl qy, qx - fl qx > qy - fl qy); triangles_2, equal triangles: a = qy/r3, b = qx - a/2, (fl b, fl a, (b - fl b) + (a - fl a) > 1); rhombus (fl(qx + qy), fl(qx - qy)); hexagons, the nearest of the four points (i + di) e1 + (j + dj) e2, di and dj 0 or 1, i = fl b, j = fl a, e1 = (1, 0), e2 = (1/2, r3), nearest by squared distance, ties to the lower (dj, di); herringbone_1, columns 1/2 wide of planks slanting up and down by turns: c = fl(2 qx), s = 1 for even c and -1 for odd, (c, fl(2 qy + s (2 qx - c))); octagons_squares: (i, j) = (fl(qx + 1/2), fl(qy + 1/2)), the octagon (i, j) where |qx - i| + |qy - j| <= sqrt(2)/2, else the square (fl qx, fl qy); glass, the nearest seed of the cells (ci, cj) within two of (fl qx, fl qy), the seed (ci + g(ci, cj, 0), cj + g(ci, cj, 1)), ties to the lower (cj, ci), g(i, j, a) = unit(mix(mix(i, j), 100 + a)) with document 22's splitmix64 `mix` and `unit` on two's complement 64-bit integers. D-636's ten, with i = fl qx, j = fl qy, fx = qx - i, fy = qy - j, u = fx - 1/2, v = fy - 1/2, [..] 1 when true and 0 when not, and a corner piece (9, fl(qx + 1/2), fl(qy + 1/2)): herringbone_2, bricks 1 by 1/2 laid in a herringbone: cx = fl 2qx, cy = fl 2qy, the tile (0, cx, cy), (0, cx - 1, cy), (1, cx, cy - 1) or (1, cx, cy) as (cx - cy) mod 4 is 0, 1, 2 or 3; squares_triangles, a chequer of whole squares, (4, i, j) where i + j is even, and squares cut along both diagonals, (2 [fx > fy] + [fx + fy > 1], i, j); stars_triangles, triangles_2's triangles joined into six-pointed stars about the points 3m e1 + 3n e2: a triangle's centroid in (b, a) is (fl b + 2/3, fl a + 2/3) for an upper one and (fl b + 1/3, fl a + 1/3) for a lower, and it joins the star (2, si, sj) of the corner (si, sj) = (3 fl(cb/3) + 0 or 3, 3 fl(ca/3) + 0 or 3) whose distance (db + da/2)^2 + (da r3)^2 to it is under 1.3^2, else it is its own piece ([upper], fl b, fl a); overlapping_squares, squares of 1 and 1/2 in the Pythagorean tiling: with (x, y) = 2q, the square (0, m, n) is [2m - n, 2m - n + 2) by [m + 2n, m + 2n + 2) and the small one (1, m, n) is [2m - n + 2, 2m - n + 3) by [m + 2n, m + 2n + 1), m and n within two of fl((2x + y)/5) and fl((2y - x)/5); chevrons, columns 1 wide of rows 1/2 high bent into a V: (0, i, fl(2 qy - |2 fx - 1|)); puzzle, squares whose edges carry a round knob: the edge right of (i, j) belongs to (i, j) if g(i, j, 2) < 1/2 and to (i + 1, j) if not, the edge below (i, j) to (i, j) if g(i, j, 3) < 1/2 and to (i, j + 1) if not, and each edge's knob is the disc of radius 0.2 about its middle moved 0.1 off it, away from its owner; a point in a knob of one of its square's four edges (squared distance under 0.04) goes to that edge's owner, (0, owner), else it is (0, i, j); js, J-shaped pieces of four cells 1/2 square, two to a block four cells long and two high, every other band of blocks turned over and moved two cells: cx = fl 2qx, cy = fl 2qy, band = fl(cy/2), ry = cy - 2 band, for an odd band cx + 2 and 1 - ry in their place, k = fl(cx/4), rx = cx - 4k, the tile (2 (band mod 2) + [not (ry = 0 and rx <= 2 or ry = 1 and rx = 0)], k, band); eggs, an egg in each square, (1, i, j) where (u / (0.32 (1 + 0.35 v / 0.42)))^2 + (v / 0.42)^2 < 1, else the corner piece; crescents, ([u^2 + v^2 < 0.45^2 and (u - 0.2)^2 + v^2 >= 0.4^2], i, j); carpenters_wheel, a wheel in each square: the hub (8, i, j) where u^2 + v^2 < 0.12^2, else one of eight spokes (fl(8 (atan2(v, u) + pi) / (2 pi)) mod 8, i, j) where u^2 + v^2 < 0.45^2, else the corner piece. (2) The pieces. Pixels of one tile are one piece. "custom": the map picture (the layer's own drawing for "", clear for a layer not in the composition or not begun) read per pixel as Card Dance's red, green and blue (linear_to_srgb of the clamped premultiplied channel) and alpha: a pixel with alpha under 1/2 is in no piece; the others take one of eight colours, each channel at least 1/2 or not, and each 4-connected region of one colour is a piece. Pieces are numbered by their first pixel, rows then columns. A piece's balance point (bx, by) is the mean of its pixel centres, b = (bx - W/2, by - H/2, 0); its randoms r(i, a) = unit(mix(i, a)). (3) The settings history. Sample j is every number at composition frame n0 + j, j = 0 .. n - n0, n0 the layer's in point; step m (four a frame, dt = 1/(4 fps) seconds) reads the samples m div 4 and the next, in a straight line by (m mod 4)/4. (4) The release. With the gradient picture (the current frame's, none for ""), a piece's value v is document 21's luma of its pixel (min(fl bx, W - 1), min(fl by, H - 1)), 1 - v when inverted; it may go where there is no gradient or where v + threshold/100 >= 1. A piece is freed at the first step m = 0 .. 4 (n - n0) at which it may go and one of its pixel centres (px - W/2, py - H/2, 0) lies inside a force F with radius > 0: closer than radius W to (pos_x W/100 - W/2, pos_y H/100 - H/2, -depth W), force 1 first; the custom pieces of all three colours white never go with "on". Not freed by now: at rest. (5) The flight, from step m0 to step M = 4 (n - n0), with the numbers of the freeing force and of step m0: speed = strength W (1 + randomness (2 r(i,1) - 1)) / (1 + mass/100 (r(i,2) - 1/2)); v = speed unit(b - centre) (zero where b is the centre); the spin rate w = 2 pi rotation_speed (1 + randomness (2 r(i,3) - 1)) about the axis: free unit(2 r(i,4) - 1, 2 r(i,5) - 1, 2 r(i,6) - 1), xy, xz and yz the same with the third zeroed, x, y and z the unit axes, none no spin. Each step m0 .. M - 1, with its own gravity G, direction d, inclination c and viscosity k: g = G W (sin d cos c, -cos d cos c, -sin c); with e = exp(-k dt) and k > 1e-9, x += (v - g/k)(1 - e)/k + g dt/k, v = v e + g (1 - e)/k, angle += w (1 - e)/k, w = w e; else x += v dt + g dt^2/2, v += g dt, angle += w dt. The piece is then T(p) = R (p - b) + b + x, R the turn by angle about the axis. (6) The slab. Each of a piece's pixels (i, j) is the box [i, i+1] x [j, j+1] x [0, e] in the piece's own (u, v, z) space, e = extrusion_depth W, its front at z = 0 facing the viewer. The camera is Card Dance's step 4 (comp_camera drawing nothing where A_l <= 0). A ray (o, d) seen in the piece is o' = R^T (o - b - x) + (bx, by, 0), in the layer's pixels (u, v) and depth z, and d' = R^T d. For e > 0, the box is entered at the latest of the three slab entries, if before the earliest exit and with t > 0; the face is the axis of that entry: z, the front where d'z > 0 and the back otherwise; x or y a side, its normal against the ray on that axis. For e = 0 the plane z = 0 at t > 0, inside [i, i+1) x [j, j+1), the front where d'z > 0. A piece is hit at its nearest box; the nearest piece wins, ties to the lower number. "layer" draws only pieces not freed, "pieces" only freed ones. (7) The colour at the hit (u, v): the face's picture, the front and side layers at (u, v) and the back at (2 bx - u, v), each by document 21's bilinear (transparent outside), "" the layer's own drawing, clear for a layer not in the composition or not begun. Its mode: layer, the picture; tinted_layer, its colour times the colour; color, the colour, alpha 1; each "_plus_opacity" all four times the opacity. Lit by Card Dance's step 7 with the face's normal R n (front (0, 0, -1), back (0, 0, 1)). (8) The output at buffer pixel (X, Y): the winning hit's colour, or clear. (9) The wireframe views draw no faces, only outlines, every drawn piece's showing through the others. A piece's edge pixels are its pixels with a side neighbour not in the piece. For buffer pixel (X, Y), "wireframe_front" and "wireframe_front_forces" take the line o = (X + 1/2 - W/2, Y + 1/2 - H/2, 0), d = (0, 0, 1), every point of it counting (seen straight on, no camera); "wireframe" and "wireframe_forces" take step 6's camera ray, only t > 0 counting, and draw nothing where that camera draws nothing. The pixel is opaque white (1, 1, 1, 1) where the line, seen in a piece that Render draws (step 6's o' and d'), meets the plane z = 0 (d'z not 0) at (u, v) inside [i, i+1) x [j, j+1) of one of the piece's edge pixels (i, j); else clear. The "_forces" views then draw each force F with r = radius W > 0, its centre C as in step 4: where the line's least distance D from C (at t = (C - o).d / d.d, which must be > 0 for a camera ray) has |D - r| < 1/2, the sphere's outline, the pixel is force 1 red (1, 0, 0, 1), then force 2 blue (0, 0, 1, 1) over it. The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing, every distance being a share of the layer; each frame works the pieces again from the in point (D-610's rule), nothing carried between frames. FX-SH-001 to 104 pin it.

Corner Pins (D-634, 2026-10-12), the camera system of Card Wipe, Card Dance and Shatter, after After Effects' Corner Pins, whose help gives no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/card_dance_reference.py` (step 4) is the reference; Card Wipe and Shatter call it. The settings: `camera_system` "corner_pins"; `upper_left_corner`, `upper_right_corner`, `lower_left_corner`, `lower_right_corner`, x then y, -1000 to 1000 per cent of the layer, keyable, when added the layer's own corners [0, 0], [100, 0], [0, 100] and [100, 100]; `auto_focal_length` "off" (when added) or "on". A file from before D-634 lacks them: they read as when added and are written only when given, so old files load unchanged. The rule, `corner_camera`: the plane map Hs taking the unit square's corners (0, 0), (1, 0), (1, 1), (0, 1) to the four pins (p W/100 - W/2, q H/100 - H/2), upper left, upper right, lower right, lower left, in the closed form (with d1 = P1 - P2, d2 = P3 - P2, d3 = P0 - P1 + P2 - P3: g = det(d3, d2)/det(d1, d2), h = det(d1, d3)/det(d1, d2), its columns (P1 - P0 + g P1, g), (P3 - P0 + h P3, h) and (P0, 1)); nothing is drawn where det(d1, d2) = 0. The map from the layer's own centred point (u, v): h1 = Hs0/W, h2 = Hs1/H, h3 = (Hs0 + Hs1)/2 + Hs2. f: with `auto_focal_length` "on", sqrt(-(h1x h2x + h1y h2y)/(h1z h2z)) where h1z h2z is not 0 and that lies within Focal Length's range (W/36 to 1000 W/36, so a shape with next to no perspective is not thrown far off by rounding); otherwise F = W focal/36. a1, a2, t = (h1x/f, h1y/f, h1z), the same for h2 and h3, each times s = 2/(|a1| + |a2|), its sign that of t's z; nothing is drawn where t's z is 0. a3 = unit(a1 x a2) sqrt(|a1| |a2|), A = [a1 a2 a3]; nothing is drawn where A has no inverse. o = -A^-1 t, v = A^-1 (x + 1/2 - W/2, y + 1/2 - H/2, f), M = I (the light stays on the layer). The camera turns, place, Z position and, with Auto, the focal length are not used. The layer's own corners with Auto "off" are the camera as added, exactly. Card Wipe uses the same eye and ray in its step 3 and Shatter in its camera step, M the identity in both. FX-CW-057 to 069, FX-CD-052 and 065 to 076 and FX-SH-067 and 079 to 083 pin it.

Bubbles (D-671, 2026-10-12), after CycoreFX's CC Bubbles (Simulation); `tools/bubbles_reference.py` is the reference, and CycoreFX publishes no formula, so the rule is ours. On a drawing w by h, u frames since the in point at r a second: S and F are the trapezoid sums from the in point of `speed` and `wobble_frequency`, in seconds. Bubble i (0 to floor(`amount`) - 1) takes u_k = (U(seed, i, 0, 0, k) + 1) / 2, k = 0 to 5 (Noise's hash, D-119, seed floor(`random_seed`)); radius rho = `size` (1/2 + u_2) h / 40, pace p = 1/2 + u_3, rise R = h + 2 rho; centre y = h + rho - frac(u_1 + p S (h / 4) / R) R and x = u_0 (w + 2 rho) - rho + (`wobble_amplitude` / 10) rho sin(2 pi (u_4 + (3/4 + u_5 / 2) F)). At a pixel centre, cover = clamp(rho - d + 1/2, 0, 1); e = the offset over rho, cut to length 1 outside; t = |e|; the picture is read bilinearly at the centre minus (Inverse) or plus (World) 2 rho e / (1 + sqrt(1 - t^2)). With f = t^2: None keeps c; Lighten c (1 - f) + white f, opaque; Darken c (1 - f) + black f, opaque; Fade Inwards c f; Fade Outwards c (1 - f). Bubbles are laid in order of i onto clear. Every length is a share of the height, so a draft is the same picture smaller. On the card the `bubbles` pass does the same in double precision over the processor's tile lists. FX-BUBBLE-001 to 034 pin it.

Drizzle (D-672, 2026-10-12), after CycoreFX's CC Drizzle (Simulation); `tools/drizzle_reference.py` is the reference, and CycoreFX publishes no formula, so the rule is ours. On a drawing w by h, u frames since the in point at r a second: C(m) is the trapezoid sum of `drip_rate` from the in point divided by r, joined by straight lines between frames; drop j (1, 2, ...) is born at b_j, the first t with C(t) = j. L = `longevity` at the in point; drop j lives while C(u - L r) < j <= C(u), with s = (u - b_j) / (L r). It lands at (u_0 w, u_1 h), u_k = (U(0, j, 0, 0, k) + 1) / 2 (Noise's hash, D-119); radius R = s (`spreading` / 100) h / 4, height A = (`ripple_height` / 100) (h / 50) 4 s (1 - s), rings n = 1 + `rippling` / 360. Within R, q = d / R, the surface is z = A (1 - q^2)^2 cos(2 pi n q); the water's slope (Zx, Zy) is the sum of the live drops' slopes. The picture is read bilinearly at the pixel centre minus k (Zx, Zy), k = (`displacement` / 100) (h / 50), and its straight colour is lit by Blobbylize's rule (D-379) with the normal (-Zx, -Zy, 1), flat water lit too; the pixel keeps the read alpha. Every length is a share of the height but a point light's height, in pixels. On the card the `drizzle` pass does the same in double precision over the processor's tile lists. FX-DRIZZLE-001 to 036 pin it.

Hair (D-673, 2026-10-13), after CycoreFX's CC Hair (Simulation); `tools/hair_reference.py` is the reference, and CycoreFX publishes no formula, so the rule is ours. On a drawing w by h: N = floor((`density` / 100) 4000 w / h) strands; strand j takes u_k = (U(0, j, 0, 0, k) + 1) / 2 (Noise's hash, D-119), roots at (u_0 w, u_1 h) and grows only where u_2 < the layer's alpha there (bilinear, premultiplied). It leaves along d = (sin t cos p, sin t sin p, cos t), p = 2 pi u_3, t = (pi / 3) u_4; length L_j = L (0.6 + 0.4 u_5), L = (`length` / 100) h / 10; bend b_j = `weight` / 100, times L_j / L with `constant_mass` on; at s from 0 to 1 it is at root + L_j (d_x s, d_y s + b_j s^2, d_z s), drawn as the 8 straight pieces between s = 0, 1/8, .. 1, seen straight on. At a pixel centre each piece's nearest point gives a distance e and its s; the half-width hw = (T / 2)(1 - 0.75 s), T = `thickness` h / 1080; the strand's coverage c is the largest clamp(hw - e + 1/2, 0, 1) over its pieces. Its colour, `hair_color` mixed towards the picture's straight colour at its root by `color_inheritance` / 100, is lit: D the strand's direction at s, a = D.V_L with V_L = (u_x, u_y, 1) / |.| from `light_direction`, e = D_z, kd = sqrt(max(0, 1 - a^2)), ks = (kd sqrt(max(0, 1 - e^2)) - a e)^(1 / `roughness`) where positive; lit = col (ambient + diffuse I kd) + specular I (1 + (col - 1) metal) ks, I = `light_intensity` / 100, the shares over 100. The strands are laid over the layer in order of j at alpha c `opacity` / 100. Every length is a share of the height, so a draft is the same picture smaller. On the card the `hair` pass does the same in double precision over the processor's tile lists. FX-HAIR-001 to 032 pin it.

Timewarp (D-652, 2026-10-12), after After Effects' Timewarp, whose page names the controls and says what each is for but gives no formula, so the source time, the in-between rule and the error mix are ours. `tools/timewarp_reference.py` is the reference, with the motion estimate above. `core.timewarp`: `method` "whole_frames", "frame_mix" or "pixel_motion" (when added), exact; `adjust_time_by` "speed" (when added) or "source_frame"; `speed` -1000 to 1000 per cent, 50; `source_frame` -100000 to 100000 frames, 0; the Tuning settings `vector_detail` 0 to 100, 20; `global_smoothness` and `local_smoothness` 0 to 1, 0.5; `smoothing_iterations` 1 to 50, taken whole, 4; `build_from_one_image` and `correct_luminance_changes` "off" (when added) or "on"; `filtering` "normal" (when added) or "extreme"; `error_threshold` 0 to 10, 1; `block_size` 2 to 32, 6; `red_weight`, `green_weight` and `blue_weight` 0 to 1, 0.3, 0.6 and 0.1; the Motion Blur settings `enable_motion_blur` "off" (when added) or "on"; `shutter_control` "automatic" (when added) or "manual"; `shutter_angle` 0 to 720 degrees, 180; `shutter_samples` 2 to 64, 5. The numbers are keyable and read at frame n (Speed at every frame it sums), the words not. At the holder's composition frame n, its input O, with n0 its in point and m1 its last frame (out point - 1): (1) on an adjustment layer the output is O and the frame says TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED; (2) the source time t, in composition frames: with "speed", s(k) = Speed at frame k / 100, t = n0 - 0.5 plus s(k) for k = n0 to n - 1 in turn plus 0.5 s(n), and the rate is s(n) (a constant 50 gives n0 + 0.25, n0 + 0.75 and so on; 100 gives n); with "source_frame", t = n0 + Source Frame at n and the rate is (Source Frame at n + 1 - Source Frame at n - 1) / 2; then t is held inside n0 to m1; (3) P(m) is the holder at composition frame m with the effects before Timewarp, by the layer's own timing, laid on O corner to corner and cut to its size; A = floor(t), B = min(A + 1, m1), f = t - A; (4) the moments: with `enable_motion_blur` "off" or a shutter angle of 0, one, d = 0; otherwise the angle and the sample count are the composition's motion blur settings with "automatic" (whether its switch is on or not; 180 and 16 when it has none), the effect's own with "manual"; a = angle / 360, N = floor(samples), d_k = a ((k + 0.5) / N - 0.5) rate, k = 0 to N - 1; the output is the mean of the moments' pictures, in order; (5) one moment's picture: Whole Frames, with t' = t + d held inside n0 to m1, is P(floor(t')); Frame Mix, with A' = floor(t'), B' = min(A' + 1, m1) and g = t' - A', is (1 - g) P(A') + g P(B'), all four numbers; Pixel Motion, with g = f + d, not held, is P(A) when g = 0, and otherwise, with F01 the estimate from P(A) to P(B) and F10 from P(B) to P(A), both with the Tuning settings, F_a = -(1 - g) g F01 + g^2 F10 and F_b = (1 - g)^2 F01 - g (1 - g) F10, a = P(A) read at (x + 0.5 + F_a,x, y + 0.5 + F_a,y) and b = P(B) read at (x + 0.5 + F_b,x, y + 0.5 + F_b,y), by this document's bilinear sample with "normal" or Keys' cubic with a = -0.5 (Catmull-Rom, as Transform's bicubic, the covering held at 1) with "extreme", transparent outside; with h = g held inside 0 and 1, px = (1 - h) a + h b, or with Build From One Image "on" a when g < 0.5 and b otherwise; fm = (1 - h) P(A) + h P(B); e = |w . a - w . b| + |alpha(a) - alpha(b)|, w the Tuning's red, green and blue weights on the premultiplied colour; m = min(1, Error Threshold x e); the picture is (1 - m) px + m fm. Drawings held across frames (on twos) give A and B the same picture between them, and that drawing shows exactly. Timewarp never grows the drawing's bounds, and a draft preview leaves every setting as it is.

Mercury (D-674, 2026-10-13), after CycoreFX's CC Mr. Mercury (Simulation); `tools/mercury_reference.py` is the reference, and CycoreFX publishes no formula, so the rule is ours. On a drawing w by h, u frames since the in point at r a second: drops are counted as Drizzle's from `birth_rate`; L = `longevity` at the in point; drop j lives while C(u - L r) < j <= C(u), age a = (u - b_j) / r, s = a / L, every birth and path setting read at the whole frame its birth follows. u_k = (U(0, j, 0, 0, k) + 1) / 2 (Noise's hash, D-119). Born at p_0 = P + (R_x sqrt(u_0) cos 2 pi u_1, R_y sqrt(u_0) sin 2 pi u_1), P = (`producer_x` w, `producer_y` h) / 100, R_x = (`radius_x` / 100) h, likewise R_y; it lives only where u_2 < the layer's alpha at p_0 and takes the straight colour there. Speed v = S (1 - (`extra` / 100) u_3), S = (`velocity` / 100) h; k = `resistance` / 100, F_1 = a and F_2 = a^2 / 2 when k = 0, else F_1 = (1 - e^(-k a)) / k, F_2 = (a - F_1) / k; G = (`gravity` / 100) h downwards. Explosive: p = p_0 + v (cos, sin)(2 pi u_4) F_1 + (0, G F_2); Direction: the same along `direction` + (u_4 - 1/2) 90 (`extra` / 100) degrees; Twirl: along (-o_y, o_x) / |o|, o = p_0 - P; Vortex: at distance |o| + (v / 2) F_1 and angle atan2(o_y, o_x) + 2 pi (v / h) F_1, plus (0, G F_2); Fixed: p_0. Radius r_j = (h / 20)(B + (D - B) s) m(s), B and D `birth_size` and `death_size` over 100, m(s) = min(1, 4 s, 4 (1 - s)) for In and Out, min(1, 4 s) for In, min(1, 4 (1 - s)) for Out, 1 for Constant. Metaballs: A = `blob_influence` / 100, rho_j = r_j (1 + A), q = d / rho_j; each drop adds f = (1 - q^2)^2 to the field F where q < 1, its slope to (F_X, F_Y), f rho_j to a weight and f times its colour to a sum; the surface is F >= tau = (1 - 1 / (1 + A)^2)^2, coverage clamp((F - tau) / |(F_X, F_Y)| + 1/2, 0, 1); colour the sum over F; c the weight over F; normal (-c F_X, -c F_Y, 2 sqrt(max(F - tau, 0))) / |.|. Lit as Drizzle (D-672). The layer becomes the drops alone. Every length is a share of the height but a point light's height, in pixels. On the card the `mercury` pass does the same in double precision over the processor's tile lists. FX-MERCURY-001 to 038 pin it.

Polygon Shards (D-675, 2026-10-13), after CycoreFX's CC Pixel Polly (Simulation); `tools/polygon_shards_reference.py` is the reference, and CycoreFX publishes no formula, so the rule is ours. On a drawing w by h at frame f, every setting as it is at f: t = (f - in point) / r - `start_time` seconds; with t <= 0 the layer is drawn as it is. The grid s = `grid_spacing` pixels; a cell (s i, s j) is two triangles (Polygon kinds; the upper right a >= b, the lower left b >= a) or one square, centre c the centroid. Shard k's u_q = (U(0, k, 0, 0, q) + 1) / 2 (Noise's hash, D-119); F = (`force_center_x` w, `force_center_y` h) / 100, d = c - F; theta = atan2(d_y, d_x) + (u_1 - 1/2) pi D, phi = 45 + (u_2 - 1/2) 90 D degrees, D = `direction_randomness` / 100; e = (cos phi cos theta, cos phi sin theta, -sin phi), Z away from the eye; v = (`force` / 100)(h / 4)(h / (h + |d|))(1 - (`speed_randomness` / 100) u_3); P = (c, 0) + v e t + (0, G t^2 / 2, 0), G = `gravity` h. It turns about (sqrt(1 - z^2) cos psi, sqrt(1 - z^2) sin psi, z), z = 2 u_4 - 1, psi = 2 pi u_5, by `spinning` (1/2 + u_6) t degrees. The eye is the composition camera's place and distance A_l in the layer's pixels when the file has one, else (w / 2, h / 2) and A_l = (w / 2) / tan 19.8 degrees; a shard within a pixel of the eye's plane is not drawn. At a pixel centre the ray from the eye meets the shard's plane at Q; the drawing's point there, its distance e inside the shard's edges and the drawing's, coverage clamp(e A_l / (A_l + Q_z) + 1/2, 0, 1); colour the drawing there (bilinear, textured kinds) or the shard's own mean of the premultiplied pixels whose centres are inside it. Each pixel starts clear and takes the covering shards over it, far to near by P_z (then by number) with `depth_sort` on, by number with it off. Grid Spacing is in pixels, which a draft scales. On the card the `shards` pass does the same in double precision over the processor's tile lists of shards. FX-POLYGON-SHARDS-001 to 036 pin it.

Warp Stabilizer (D-653, 2026-10-13), after After Effects' Warp Stabilizer, whose pages name the controls and say what each is for but give no formula, so the fit, the smoothing window, the crop and the scale are ours. `tools/warp_stabilizer_reference.py` is the reference, with the motion estimate above. `core.warp_stabilizer`: `result` "smooth_motion" (when added) or "no_motion", exact; `smoothness` 0 to 1000 per cent, 50; `method` "position", "position_scale_rotation" (when added) or "perspective"; `preserve_scale` "off" (when added) or "on"; `framing` "stabilize_only", "stabilize_crop" or "stabilize_crop_auto_scale" (when added); `maximum_scale` 100 to 1000 per cent, 150; `action_safe_margin` 0 to 50 per cent, 0; `additional_scale` 1 to 1000 per cent, 100; `detailed_analysis` "off" (when added) or "on". The numbers are keyable and read at frame n, the words not. At the holder's composition frame n, its input O, W by H, with n0 its in point, m1 its last frame (out point - 1) and P(m) the holder at composition frame m with the effects before Warp Stabilizer, by the layer's own timing, laid on O corner to corner and cut to its size: (1) on an adjustment layer the output is O and the frame says TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED; (2) points are centred on the picture and divided by D = hypot(W, H) / 2, pixel (x, y)'s centre being ((x + 0.5 - W / 2) / D, (y + 0.5 - H / 2) / D); (3) the pair model M_k from frame k to frame k + 1: two frames that are the same give the unchanged map; otherwise F = the estimate from P(k) to P(k + 1), Vector Detail 50, or 100 with Detailed Analysis "on", its other settings as added; every pixel p of P(k) is a point with target p + F(p) and weight w0 = P(k)'s alpha there; three weighted least-squares fits, the first with w0, the second and third with w0 / (1 + r^2), r the point's distance in pixels from where the fit before sent it: "position" a move, the weighted mean of target - point; "position_scale_rotation" x' = a x - b y + tx, y' = b x + a y + ty, with Preserve Scale "on" (a, b) divided by its length after each fit; "perspective" x' = (a x + b y + c) / (g x + h y + 1) and y' likewise with d, e, f, from the linear form; the normal equations solved by Gaussian elimination with partial pivoting, a pivot below 1e-12 or weights summing below 4 giving the unchanged map; every model a 3 by 3 matrix whose corner is 1; (4) T_k, from frame n to frame k: T_n the unchanged map, T_(k+1) = M_k T_k above n and T_(k-1) = M_(k-1)^-1 T_k below, each product divided through by its corner; (5) the steadied map S: with "smooth_motion", R = floor(fps Smoothness / 200 + 0.5), fps the composition's frames a second, R_n = min(R, n - n0, m1 - n), S the unchanged map when R_n = 0 and otherwise the mean of T_k for k = n - R_n to n + R_n, number by number, weighted exp(-(k - n)^2 / (2 (R_n / 2)^2)); with "no_motion", S = T_n0; (6) framing: c = (W / 2, H / 2); s is the largest number from 0 to 1 for which the four corners of the rectangle about c, s W by s H, all land inside 0 to W and 0 to H through S^-1, found by 60 halvings, starting from 1 when 1 already fits; "stabilize_only" z = 1 and no rectangle; "stabilize_crop" z = 1 and the rectangle; "stabilize_crop_auto_scale" the rectangle and z = (1 - 2 m) / s held inside 1 and Maximum Scale / 100 (Maximum Scale / 100 when s = 0), m = Action-Safe Margin / 100; Z = z Additional Scale / 100; (7) with S the unchanged map and Z = 1 the output is P(n) exactly; otherwise at pixel (x, y), q = c + ((x + 0.5, y + 0.5) - c) / Z, clear when a rectangle is used and q lies outside it, otherwise P(n) read bilinearly by this document's sample (transparent outside) at S^-1(q), with points as in (2). Warp Stabilizer never grows the drawing's bounds, and a draft preview leaves every setting as it is (the estimate's Vector Detail is counted at each size).

Rolling Shutter Repair (D-654, 2026-10-13), after After Effects' Rolling Shutter Repair, whose pages name the controls and say what each is for but give no formula, so the scan's timing and the two methods' rule are ours. `tools/rolling_shutter_repair_reference.py` is the reference, with the motion estimate and Warp Stabilizer's fit above. `core.rolling_shutter_repair`: `rolling_shutter_rate` 0 to 100 per cent, 50 when added; `scan_direction` "top_to_bottom" (when added), "bottom_to_top", "left_to_right" or "right_to_left"; `method` "warp" (when added) or "pixel_motion"; `detailed_analysis` "off" (when added) or "on"; `pixel_motion_detail` 0 to 100, 20. The numbers are keyable and read at frame n, the words not. At the holder's composition frame n, W by H, with n0 its in point, m1 its last frame (out point - 1) and P(m) the holder at composition frame m with the effects before Rolling Shutter Repair, by the layer's own timing, laid on its input corner to corner and cut to its size, O = P(n): (1) on an adjustment layer the output is O and the frame says TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED; (2) r = Rolling Shutter Rate / 100, and with r = 0 the output is O; pixel (x, y) is scanned at u = (y + 0.5) / H with "top_to_bottom", 1 - (y + 0.5) / H with "bottom_to_top", (x + 0.5) / W with "left_to_right" and 1 - (x + 0.5) / W with "right_to_left", and was seen tau = r (u - 0.5) frames after the frame's middle moment; (3) the motion d+ toward frame n + 1 and d- toward frame n - 1, in pixels at each pixel: with "pixel_motion" the estimate from O to P(n + 1), and from O to P(n - 1), at Vector Detail = Pixel Motion Detail, its other settings as added; with "warp" M+ = Warp Stabilizer's step-3 pair model from O to P(n + 1) with Method "position_scale_rotation" and Preserve Scale "off" (Vector Detail 50, or 100 with Detailed Analysis "on"), M- likewise to P(n - 1), and d+ at pixel p = (M+(p) - p) D, d- likewise, with points as Warp Stabilizer's (centred and divided by D = hypot(W, H) / 2); a side is still when its frame is outside n0 to m1 or is the same picture as O, a still side takes the other side's motion turned round (d- = -d+, or d+ = -d-), and with both sides still the output is O; (4) the output at (x, y) is O read bilinearly by this document's sample (transparent outside) at (x + 0.5, y + 0.5) + tau d+ where tau >= 0 and (x + 0.5, y + 0.5) + (-tau) d- where tau < 0. Rolling Shutter Repair never grows the drawing's bounds, and a draft preview leaves every setting as it is.

Spherize (D-406, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Spherize, whose page gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/spherize_reference.py` is the reference. `core.spherize`: `radius`, pixels 0..2500 (100); `center`, two numbers, per cent of the width and height of the buffer as it reaches the effect, -1000..1000 (50, 50). Both keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; both written. With the buffer w by h, c = (center / 100 w, center / 100 h), R = radius. Each output pixel's centre p (buffer coordinates), v = p - c, d = |v|: when R = 0 or d >= R the pixel is the buffer's own; else rho = d / R, k = 0 at d = 0 and (2 asin(rho) / pi) / rho otherwise, and the pixel is document 21's bilinear sample of the buffer at c + k v (clear outside the buffer). The middle is enlarged pi / 2 times, the picture crowds toward the rim, and k reaches 1 at the rim, so there is no step at the edge. A draft scales the radius; the centre is a share. **Deviations:** Adobe's page says only that Spherize wraps a region onto a sphere and gives no formula, so the rule is ours: a half sphere seen from in front, read at c + v 2 asin(rho) / (pi rho), the same curve as Fisheye's at convergence 100 (D-387); the starting radius of 100 is ours (Adobe's was not found); the radius runs 0 to 2500, the limit a Creative COW answer gives for After Effects; only the outward swell, as After Effects; the centre is a share of the picture as it reaches the effect; a draft scales the radius and keeps the bilinear sample, where After Effects samples whole pixels in Draft. Drawn on the card in the warp pass, mode 24, Bulge's branch. FX-SPHERIZE-001 to 015 pin it.

Detail-preserving Upscale (D-407, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Detail-preserving Upscale, whose page gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/detail_upscale_reference.py` is the reference. `core.detail_upscale`: `scale`, per cent 100..1000 (100); `reduce_noise`, 0..100 (0); `detail`, 0..100 (20). All keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; all written. On the buffer as it reaches the effect, w by h, premultiplied, s = scale / 100: (1) a Gaussian of sigma reduce_noise / 50 pixels, taps -ceil(3 sigma)..ceil(3 sigma), weights exp(-t^2 / (2 sigma^2)) normalised, across then down, clear outside, kept w by h; (2) the layer grows by gx = ceil(w (scale - 100) / 200) each side across and gy likewise down, its middle staying put; output column x reads u = w / 2 + (x + 1/2 - W / 2) / s - 1/2 with the six Lanczos-3 taps floor(u) - 2..floor(u) + 3, weights sinc(t) sinc(t / 3) divided by their sum, a tap outside the buffer clear; across, then down; (3) out = up + (detail / 50)(up - G(up)), G the step 1 Gaussian of sigma s / 2 output pixels, clear outside the grown layer; (4) alpha held to 0..1 and each colour to 0..alpha. At scale 100 every u is whole and step 2 leaves the picture as it was. A grown side past 30000 pixels, or a buffer whose memory cannot be had, leaves the Upscale out with EFFECT_LAYER_TOO_LARGE (document 28). **Deviations:** Adobe's page names the controls and says what they do but gives no formula (the effect is related to Photoshop's Preserve Details resampling, which Adobe does not publish either), so the rule is ours: a Gaussian softening, Lanczos-3 resampling, an unsharp mask; it will not match After Effects pixel for pixel. Detail's starting value of 20 is ours (Adobe's page gives none); Scale starts at 100 and Reduce Noise at 0. After Effects' Alpha choice (the alpha channel enlarged by a cheaper filter, Bicubic by default, for speed) is left out: alpha goes through the same filter as colour. Fit to Comp Width / Height are one-time buttons, as the owner chose: they set Scale from the layer as it reaches the Upscale on the frame shown and are not remembered; on a hidden layer they are refused in words (the layer is not drawn, so there is no size to fit). A layer grown past 30000 pixels, After Effects' largest, or one whose memory cannot be had, is drawn without the Upscale and EFFECT_LAYER_TOO_LARGE (WARNING) says so; a memory failure inside the card's own fall-back to the processor is not reported separately, and an Upscale on an adjustment layer enlarges what is under it as any adjustment effect does. A draft scales Reduce Noise's sigma but not Detail's, which follows Scale (s / 2 pixels of the picture as drawn). Drawn on the card as warp mode 29 (the Lanczos weights worked out on the processor) and sharp mode 2 (Detail and the hold). FX-UPSCALE-001 to 020 pin it.

Audio Spectrum (D-420, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Audio Spectrum (Generate), whose manual pages (509 to 510) say what it shows but not how it measures the frequencies or how tall a level is drawn, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/audio_spectrum_reference.py` is the reference. `core.audio_spectrum`: `audio_layer`, a layer id or "" (silence); `start_point` and `end_point`, per cent of the drawing, -1000..1000 ((10, 50), (90, 50)); `path`, 0..1000 (0); `use_polar_path` (off); `start_frequency` and `end_frequency`, 1..20000 hertz (20, 2000); `frequency_bands`, 1..4096 (64); `maximum_height`, pixels 0..10000 (200); `audio_duration`, 1..30000 ms (90); `audio_offset`, -30000..30000 ms (0); `thickness`, pixels 0..10000 (3); `softness`, 0..100 (50); `inside_color` and `outside_color`, `#rrggbb` (white, #3c8cff), read in small letters; `blend_overlapping_colors` (off); `hue_interpolation`, -3600..3600 degrees (0); `dynamic_hue_phase` and `color_symmetry` (off); `display_options`, "digital", "analog_lines" or "analog_dots" (digital); `side_options`, "side_a", "side_b" or "side_a_b" (side_a_b); `duration_averaging` (off); `composite` (off).
