The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. **The sound.** At composition frame f the sound layer's own frame is n = f - in + source offset (silence outside the layer); its first sample s = floor(n rate den / num) (ADR-018), the window starts at s0 = s + floor(offset rate / 1000 + 1/2) and is N = max(1, floor(duration rate / 1000 + 1/2)) samples long. A sample is the mean of the file's channels times the layer's gain 10^(gain / 20), 0 outside the file. **The levels.** B = floor(bands); band b's frequency F_b = start + (end - start)(b + 1/2) / B; with the Hann window w_k = 1/2 - 1/2 cos(2 pi (k + 1/2) / N), the level a_b = 2 |sum_k x_k w_k e^(-2 pi i F_b k / rate)| / sum_k w_k, so a sine of amplitude A at F_b reads A; Duration Averaging on takes the mean over windows starting at s0 - floor(N / 2), s0 and s0 + floor(N / 2). Band b stands h_b = maximum_height min(a_b, 1). **Where the bands stand**, t_b = (b + 1/2) / B along the way, in the drawing's own pixels: on the line from Start Point P to End Point Q at P + t_b (Q - P), facing n = (dy, -dx) for its direction (dx, dy), up for a line drawn left to right ((0, -1) when the points meet); Use Polar Path, every band at Start Point facing (cos a_b, sin a_b), a_b = -pi/2 + 2 pi t_b, from straight up, clockwise; Path k, along mask k as document 19 flattens it (closed), at t_b of its length, facing the normal of its piece; a mask that is not there, is off or has fewer than two points draws nothing with EFFECT_PATH_MISSING. Side A's tip is p + h n, Side B's p - h n. **The marks**, straight pieces Thickness across: Digital, foot to tip (one side) or tip to tip (both); Analog Lines, one line through each side's tips, Side A's first, band b's colours from tip b to tip b + 1, closed round when the way is closed (polar or a mask); Analog Dots, a piece of no length at each tip. **Colours.** Hue Interpolation H turns band b's two colours round the hue (HSL, as written) by H u_b, u_b = t_b; Dynamic Hue Phase counts from the loudest band m, u_b = (((b - m) mod B) + 1/2) / B; Color Symmetry folds it, u_b = 1 - |2 u_b - 1|. **Drawing**, at a pixel centre: a mark's distance d is the least to its pieces, its colours the nearest piece's (the first of equals); then Beam's line (D-207): r = thickness / 2, sw = max(2 r softness / 100, 1), c = clamp((min(d + sw/2, r) - max(d - sw/2, -r)) / sw, 0, 1), L = (1 - q) inside + q outside in linear light, q = clamp(d / r, 0, 1) (1 when r = 0). Blend Overlapping Colors off: the marks laid one over the next, (L c, c) over what is there; on: covering 1 - prod(1 - c), colour the mean of L weighted by c. Composite on: that over the layer; off: that alone. An Audio Layer naming a layer that is gone says EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING, one with no sound EFFECT_SOUND_MISSING, the effect drawing nothing. The layer never grows. Maximum height and thickness are distances: a draft divides them by its scale. **Deviation:** Adobe says the effect uses the sound "without time-remapping, effects, stretch, or levels"; the layer's gain is applied here. Only WAV files are read. Drawn on the card in its own "marks" pass, the levels worked out on the processor. FX-ASPEC-001 to 053 pin it.

Audio Waveform (D-421, 2026-10-10), after After Effects' Audio Waveform (Generate), whose manual says what each display shows but not how a displayed sample is taken from the sound, how tall it stands or how the seed picks, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/audio_waveform_reference.py` is the reference. `core.audio_waveform`: `audio_layer`, a layer id or "" (silence); `start_point` and `end_point`, per cent of the drawing, -1000..1000 ((10, 50), (90, 50)); `path`, 0..1000 (0); `displayed_samples`, 1..4096 (32); `maximum_height`, pixels 0..10000 (300); `audio_duration`, 1..30000 ms (90); `audio_offset`, -30000..30000 ms (0); `thickness`, pixels 0..10000 (2); `softness`, 0..100 (50); `random_seed`, 0..100000, its whole part counted (1); `inside_color` and `outside_color`, `#rrggbb` (white, #3c8cff), read in small letters; `waveform_options`, "mono", "left" or "right" (mono); `display_options`, "digital", "analog_lines" or "analog_dots" (analog_lines); `composite` (off). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. **The sound** is Audio Spectrum's window: first sample s0, N samples, each times the layer's gain, 0 outside the file; Mono takes the channels' mean, Left channel 0, Right channel 1 (channel 0 of a one-channel file: "Nonstereo audio layers play as Mono"). **The displayed samples.** D = floor(displayed_samples); sample j (0 <= j < D) covers the source samples from s0 + floor(j N / D) up to, not including, s0 + floor((j + 1) N / D), or the first alone when that is none; lo_j and hi_j are their least and greatest, each held to -1..1 (both 0 at silence). **Where they stand**, t_j = (j + 1/2) / D along the way, as Audio Spectrum's: on the line from Start Point to End Point facing n = (dy, -dx), up for a line drawn left to right, or along mask k at t_j of its length facing its piece's normal (a missing, off or too short mask draws nothing with EFFECT_PATH_MISSING); a level v stands at p + maximum_height v n. **The marks**, straight pieces Thickness across: Digital, a piece from lo_j to hi_j for each sample, each its own mark; Analog Lines and Analog Dots take v_j = hi_j when the top bit of mix(mix(floor(random_seed)) xor j) is 1, else lo_j (mix SplitMix64's finaliser, D-119's `grade::mix`); Analog Lines, one mark from point j to point j + 1, closed round a mask, a dot when D is 1; Analog Dots, a piece of no length at each point. **Drawing** is Audio Spectrum's with one colour pair and Blend Overlapping Colors off: Beam's covering c and colour L (D-207), the marks laid one over the next. Composite on: added to the layer (Adobe's "Add blending mode"), colour P + L c, alpha min(P_a + c, 1); off: (L c, c) alone. An Audio Layer naming a layer that is gone says EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING, one with no sound EFFECT_SOUND_MISSING, the effect drawing nothing. The layer never grows. Maximum height and thickness are distances: a draft divides them by its scale. **Deviation:** the layer's gain is applied, as D-420. Only WAV files are read. Drawn on the card on Audio Spectrum's "marks" pass, the least and greatest worked out on the processor. FX-AWAVE-001 to 049 pin it.

The working sound (D-639, 2026-10-13, S-1 of D-638): every sound the core reads is turned, on reading, into 48,000 samples a second in two channels, 64-bit floating point, by this rule of ours; `tools/sound_read_reference.py` is the reference. **Channels first.** One channel plays in both. Two stay as they are. Of more: channel 0 goes left and 1 right; channel 2 (centre) is added to both at 0.7071 (the square root of one half); channel 3 (low frequencies) is left out; channels 4 on are added at 0.7071, even ones to the left and odd ones to the right. Nothing is scaled down afterwards, so a loud six-channel file can pass 1. **Then the rate.** At 48,000 nothing changes, sample for sample. Otherwise, for a file of n samples at rate r, there are ceil(n x 48000 / r) outputs; output j sits at t = j x r / 48000 input samples and is the sum over input samples k of x[k] fc sinc(fc (t - k)) w((t - k) / W), with fc = min(1, 48000 / r) (so a faster file is filtered below 24,000 a second before it is thinned), W = 32 / fc input samples, sinc(x) = sin(pi x) / (pi x), and the Blackman window w(u) = 0.42 + 0.5 cos(pi u) + 0.08 cos(2 pi u) for |u| < 1 and 0 outside; samples before the start or past the end are 0. The decoded samples themselves are each format's decoder's (WAV by hand, D-71; FLAC, MP3 and Vorbis by `symphonia`; AAC by Windows' decoder with the file's edit list applied), held to ffmpeg 8.1's decode within 2e-5.

Mixing a composition's sound (D-640, 2026-10-13, S-2 of D-638): the sound of frames a to a + k of a composition at num / den frames a second is samples first_sample(a) to first_sample(a + k) at 48,000 a second, two channels, with first_sample(f) = floor(f x 48000 x den / num) (ADR-018); `tools/sound_mix_reference.py` is the reference. Sample s belongs to frame f, the last frame whose first sample is not after s. An audio layer that is on, and whose span holds f (in_frame <= f < out_frame), is heard at f through its local frame n = f - in_frame + source_offset_frames: s reads sample first_sample(n) + (s - first_sample(f)) of the layer's working sound, and is silent where that is before its first sample or past its last. So every frame begins on its own first sample of the file, even at 29.97 a second, and a stretch mixed alone is exactly the whole mix there. Each layer is multiplied by 10^(gain_db / 20); the layers are added; nothing is clipped or limited, so a sum past 1 stays past 1 here (export decides what to do with it, S-3). A layer whose sound is not read is left out with its warning (`MEDIA_AUDIO_PREVIEW_ONLY`, `MEDIA_AUDIO_UNREADABLE`) and the others are heard. Composition layers, keyed level and pan come with S-7; stretch and time remap are not applied to sound.

Exporting sound (D-641, 2026-10-13, S-3 of D-638): an export of frames a to b holds samples first_sample(a) to first_sample(b + 1) of the composition's mix (above), counted from first_sample(a), so the sound of frame f starts at sample first_sample(f) - first_sample(a) of the file. An MP4 carries it as AAC at 48,000 a second, two channels; the encoder's start delay and end padding are hidden by an edit list in the file, so a reader that honours it hears exactly those samples (the start delay is measured as 0 for Windows' encoder). A WAV beside frames holds the same samples as 24-bit integers: each value kept between -1 and 1, times 8,388,607, rounded half away from nought; a value past full level is written at full level and counted (`EXPORT_SOUND_CLIPPED`). `tools/sound_export_reference.py` is the reference.

Noise Gate (D-642, 2026-10-13, S-4 of D-638; `core.noise_gate`, a sound effect): it works on an audio layer's sound before the layer's level, from the layer's first sample, first_sample(in_frame); sound effects run in the layer's order, and one switched off, or with a setting or key outside its range, lets the sound through unchanged. The gate keeps a gain g, 0 (shut) at the layer's first sample, and a hold count, 0. For each sample, with p the louder channel's size, max(|left|, |right|), the threshold T = 10^(threshold / 20), and the times in whole samples A = floor(attack x 48 + 0.5), H = floor(hold x 48 + 0.5), R = floor(release x 48 + 0.5) (milliseconds at 48,000 a second): if p >= T, the hold becomes H and g rises by 1/A (to 1 at once when A is 0); else, if the hold is above 0, it falls by 1 and g rises the same way; else g falls by 1/R. Then g at or above 1 - 1e-12 becomes 1, and g at or below 1e-12 becomes 0; the sample is multiplied by g. So with constant settings the gate starts to open on the first sample at or above the threshold, is full A - 1 samples later, starts closing H + 1 samples after the last sample at or above the threshold, and is shut R samples after that. A keyed setting is read at sample s of frame f as v(f) + (v(f + 1) - v(f)) x (s - first_sample(f)) / (first_sample(f + 1) - first_sample(f)), v being document 20's value at the frame kept inside the setting's range, so it moves in a straight line across a frame's samples rather than jumping at frame edges. Ranges: threshold -60 to 0 dB (-60), attack 0 to 1000 ms (10), hold 0 to 2500 ms (40), release 5 to 4000 ms (220). A sound effect has no Mix and takes keys but no expression. `tools/noise_gate_reference.py` is the reference.

Compressor (D-643, 2026-10-13, S-5 of D-638; `core.compressor`, a sound effect): it works where Noise Gate does (D-642), on an audio layer's sound before its level, from the layer's first sample, in the layer's order. It keeps a gain change g in dB, 0 at the layer's first sample. For each sample, with p the louder channel's size, max(|left|, |right|), its level x = 20 log10(max(p, 1e-10)), the threshold T, the ratio R and the knee width W in dB (the whole width, centred on T), and d = x - T, the wanted change c is 0 when 2d < -W; (1/R - 1) (d + W/2)^2 / (2W) when W > 0 and 2|d| <= W (the soft knee); and (1/R - 1) d otherwise. Then g moves toward c: when c < g with a time t = attack x 48 samples; otherwise t = release x 48, or a tenth of that while Auto Release is "on" and c - g > 3 dB; with a = exp(-1/t) (0 when t is 0), g becomes a g + (1 - a) c. Both channels are multiplied by 10^((g + makeup) / 20); then, if the louder channel is above L = 10^(output_limit / 20), both are multiplied by L over that channel's size. So a steady level x settles at a change of c(x) plus the makeup, a step reaches 63% of its change in about attack (or release) milliseconds, and the louder channel never leaves above the Output Limit. A Ratio below 1 makes c positive above the knee (expansion). Keyed numbers are read across a frame's samples as Noise Gate's. Ranges: threshold -60 to 0 dB (-16), ratio 0.4 to 30 (3), knee 0 to 30 dB (15), attack 0 to 400 ms (6), release 1 to 4000 ms (440), auto_release "on" or "off" ("on"), makeup -30 to 30 dB (0), output_limit -30 to 0 dB (0). `tools/compressor_reference.py` is the reference.

Distortion (D-644, 2026-10-13, S-6 of D-638; `core.distortion`, a sound effect): it works where Noise Gate does (D-642), on an audio layer's sound before its level, from the layer's first sample, in the layer's order, on each channel's samples. For a sample x, with k = 10^(drive / 50) and u = k x, the type's curve gives w: Soft Clip tanh(u); Hard Clip u held to -1..1; Saturation 1 u / (1 + |u|); Saturation 2 (2 / pi) atan(pi u / 2); Tube (tanh(u + 1/4) - tanh(1/4)) / (1 - tanh(1/4)^2), whose slope at 0 is 1 and which is lopsided, so it adds even harmonics; Fuzz sign(u) min(1, sqrt|u|). With b = floor(resolution), below 24 w becomes floor(w q + 1/2) / q, q = 2^(b - 1); at 24 it is left. With n = floor(downsample), a count that starts at 0 on the layer's first sample takes w (both channels) when it is 0 and then holds it for n samples, so 1 holds nothing. The output is volume / 11 x ((1 - m) x + m held x gain / 25), m = mix / 100 (saved as `wet`). Keyed numbers are read across a frame's samples as Noise Gate's. No oversampling. Ranges: type soft_clip, hard_clip, saturation_1, saturation_2, tube or fuzz (soft_clip); drive 0 to 100 (25); gain 0 to 300 (25); wet 0 to 100 (100); volume 0 to 100 (11); resolution 1 to 24 (24); downsample 1 to 32 (1). `tools/distortion_reference.py` is the reference.

Level, pan and composition layers (D-645, 2026-10-13, S-7 of D-638): an audio layer's level `gain_db` (-96 to +12 dB) is a number or keys, its pan `pan` (-100 to +100, absent is 0) the same; keys only. At composition frame f with first(f) = floor(f x 48000 x den / num), the values at f and f + 1 (each held to its range) are mixed a share t = (s - first(f)) / (first(f + 1) - first(f)) of the way for sample s: level L dB and pan p; the gain is 10^(L / 20), the left channel times min(1, 1 - p / 100) and the right times min(1, 1 + p / 100). This is applied after the layer's sound effects. A layer with neither keys nor a pan is multiplied by 10^(gain_db / 20) as before D-645. A composition layer switched on adds the inner composition's mix: for outer frame f inside the layer, g = floor(source time of f); if g is within the inner composition's start to end, outer sample first(f) + k (k below the frame's length) is inner sample first_inner(g) + k, silent past the inner composition's last sample; otherwise the frame is silent. A composition layer has no level or pan of its own. A missing or cyclic composition is silent. `tools/sound_layer_reference.py` is the reference.

Light Burst (D-422, 2026-10-10), our name for CycoreFX's CC Light Burst 2.5, whose manual names the controls but gives no formula, ranges or defaults, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/light_burst_reference.py` is the reference. `core.light_burst`: `center`, per cent of the drawing, -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `intensity`, 0..2000 (100); `ray_length`, 0..100 (50); `burst`, "straight", "fade" or "center" (straight); `set_color`, "off" or "on" (off); `color`, `#rrggbb` (white), read in small letters. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. In linear light, premultiplied, with O the layer's pixel: **the light** S is the layer, or (a, a, a, a) with Set Color on; **the rays** Ry are Spin & Zoom Blur's zoom (D-361) of S about `center`, amount `ray_length`, straight_zoom for straight, fading_zoom for fade, centered_zoom for center, a sample a pixel of path; **added on** with k = intensity / 100 and T white, or the colour in linear light with Set Color on: colour O + k T Ry (Ry's colour, or Ry.a with Set Color on), covering min(1, O.a + k Ry.a), as Light Rays' last step (D-226). Straight with Set Color off is Light Rays at threshold 0 and intensity 1. The layer never grows. Drawn on the card on Light Rays' rays pass after the card's zoom blur. FX-BURST-001 to 030 pin it.

Light Rays with CC's controls (D-423, 2026-10-10), CycoreFX's CC Light Rays, whose manual names the controls but gives no formula, ranges or defaults, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/light_rays_cc_reference.py` is the reference. `core.light_rays` in its new form: `intensity`, 0..2000 (100); `center`, per cent of the drawing, -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `radius`, pixels, 0..10000 (50); `warp_softness`, 0..1000 (50); `shape`, "round" or "square" (round); `direction`, degrees, -3600..3600 (0); `color_from_source`, "off" or "on" (on); `allow_brightening`, "off" or "on" (on); `color`, `#rrggbb` (white), read in small letters; `transfer_mode`, "none", "add", "lighten" or "screen" (none). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; the words are not keyable. In linear light, premultiplied, with O the layer's pixel, c the centre in pixels and d a pixel centre less c: **the share** m = clamp(radius + 0.5 - D, 0, 1), D = |d| for round, max(|u|, |v|) of d turned by -direction for square; **the light** Lt is m times the layer, or m times its covering in every channel with Color from Source off; **the rays** Ry are Radial Blur's zoom of Lt about c at amount 100, then its spin of warp_softness / 10 degrees; k = intensity / 100, min(k, 1) with Allow Brightening off; R = k T Ry (Ry.a for Ry's colour with Color from Source off), T white, or the colour in linear light with it off; a = min(1, k Ry.a); **laid on**: none R + O (1 - a), covering a + O.a (1 - a); add O + R, covering min(1, O.a + k Ry.a) (D-124's last step); screen O + R - O R, covering O.a + a - O.a a; lighten max(O, R), covering max(O.a, a). The layer never grows; a draft divides the radius by its scale. A Light Rays whose settings hold `length` or `threshold` is D-124's form and draws by D-124's rule. Drawn on the card by warp mode 33, the card's zoom and spin blurs and Light Rays' rays pass with a mode flag. FX-RAYSCC-001 to 039 pin it.

Glue Gun (D-424, 2026-10-10), CycoreFX's CC Glue Gun, whose manual names the controls but gives no formula, ranges or defaults, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/glue_gun_reference.py` is the reference. `core.glue_gun`: `brush_position`, per cent, -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `stroke_width`, pixels, 0..500 (20); `density`, blobs a frame, 0..100 (5); `time_span`, seconds, 0..100 (1); `reflection`, 0..100 (50); `strength`, 0..100 (50); `paint_style`, "plain" or "wobbly" (plain); `wobble_width` and `wobble_height`, pixels, 0..1000 (10); `wobble_speed`, turns a second, 0..100 (1); and Blobbylize's `light_intensity`, `light_color`, `light_type`, `light_height`, `light_position`, `light_direction`, `ambient`, `diffuse`, `specular`, `roughness`, `metal`, with its ranges and defaults. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; only `brush_position` is read at frames before, every other setting as it is now. At composition frame f, fps frames a second, in linear light, premultiplied, O the layer: **the history** n = f - the layer's in point with time_span 0, else floor(time_span fps + 1/2); P_j, j = 0..n, the brush at frame f - j in pixels. **The blobs** k = ceil((f - n) d) .. floor(f d), d = density; u = (f d - k) / d, j = floor(u), t = u - j; blob k at P_j + (P_j+1 - P_j) t; wobbly moves it by (wobble_width sin(2 pi (s T + frac(0.6180339887498949 k))), wobble_height sin(2 pi (s T + frac(0.7548776662466927 k)))), T = f / fps, s = wobble_speed. **The field** r = stroke_width / 2, R = r (1 + strength / 100), T0 = (1 - (r / R)^2)^2; q = |p - c|^2 / R^2; F = sum over q < 1 of (1 - q)^2, G = sum of -4 (1 - q) (p - c) / R^2; s = (F - T0) / |G|; covering a = clamp(s + 1/2, 0, 1) (with |G| = 0, a = 1 if F > T0, else 0). **The tube** m = clamp(1 - s / r, 0, 1), N = (-m Gx / |G|, -m Gy / |G|, sqrt(1 - m^2)), (0, 0, 1) with |G| = 0. **The colour** C = O at p - (reflection / 100) 2 r (Nx, Ny), the bilinear sample (transparent outside), straight, black where clear. **The light** Blobbylize's (D-379, steps 6 and 7) on C and N. **Laid on** lit a + O (1 - a), covering a + O.a (1 - a). The layer never grows; a draft scales stroke_width, the wobble's width and height and a point light's height. Drawn on the card by warp mode 34 from the blobs the processor lays. FX-GLUE-001 to 027 pin it.

Threads (D-425, 2026-10-10), CycoreFX's CC Threads, whose manual names the controls but gives no formula, ranges or defaults, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/threads_reference.py` is the reference. `core.threads`: `width` and `height`, pixels, 1..1000 (50); `overlaps`, 1..10 (1), taken whole; `direction`, degrees, -3600..3600 (0); `center`, per cent, -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `coverage`, `shadowing`, `texture`, 0..100 (90, 50, 0). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. With O the layer (linear, premultiplied), W by H, p a pixel's centre: c = (center_x W, center_y H) / 100; d = p - c; t = direction in radians; u = dx cos t + dy sin t, v = -dx sin t + dy cos t. i = floor(u / width), e = u - (i + 1/2) width; j = floor(v / height), f = v - (j + 1/2) height; hw = coverage / 100 width / 2, hh = coverage / 100 height / 2. A thread at offset x, half width h: share m = max(0, min(x + 1/2, h) - max(x - 1/2, -h)); shade 1 - T min(1, (x / h)^2), T = texture / 100 (1 with h = 0). n = floor(overlaps); the warp (e, hw) is over the weft (f, hh) where (i + j) mod 2n < n, else the weft over. The thread beneath darkened by 1 - S (1 + g) / 2, S = shadowing / 100, g = clamp(1 - (|x_up| - h_up) / h_up, 0, 1) (0 with h_up = 0). Colour O (m_up shade_up + m_down shade_down dark (1 - m_up)), alpha O.a (m_up + m_down (1 - m_up)). The layer never grows; a draft scales width and height. Drawn on the card by warp mode 35. FX-THREADS-001 to 028 pin it.

Lens Flare (D-426, 2026-10-10), After Effects' Lens Flare built from the parts of Video Copilot's Optical Flares. No formula was available, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/lens_flare_reference.py` is the reference and holds each lens's parts (`layer_fx::FLARE_LENSES` holds the same). `core.lens_flare`: `flare_center`, per cent, -1000..1000 ((30, 30)); `flare_brightness`, 0..300 (100); `lens_type`, "zoom", "35mm" or "105mm" ("zoom"); `blend_with_original`, 0..100 (0). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. With O the layer (linear, premultiplied), W by H, p a pixel's centre: F = flare_center (W, H) / 100, M = (W, H) / 2, D = hypot(W, H). Each part (kind, t, r, w, n, colour) sits at P = F + t (M - F), R = r D; d = |p - P|. A glow gives exp(-(d / R)^2); a ring exp(-((d - R) / (w D))^2); a disc clamp((R - d) / (w D) + 1/2, 0, 1); a star exp(-d / R) ((1 + cos(n a)) / 2)^w, a the angle of p - P (0 at d = 0). L = flare_brightness / 100 times the sum of colour times part. The flared pixel is (O.rgb + L, O.a + (1 - O.a) min(1, max L)); the result O + (1 - blend_with_original / 100) (flared - O). The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing (the sizes are shares of D). Drawn on the card by warp mode 36, the parts placed on the processor. FX-FLARE-001 to 019 pin it.

Arbitrary Map (D-395, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' PS Arbitrary Map. `tools/arbitrary_map_reference.py` is the reference. `core.arbitrary_map`: `map`, the id of a `lut` asset of the project or "" for none ("" when added, which leaves the layer as it is); `phase`, -255 to 255 levels, keyable, 0 when added; `apply_to_alpha`, "off" or "on", "off" when added. The file is read by Adobe's Photoshop File Formats Specification: no header, one or more tables of 256 bytes, entry x the value x becomes. One table is the master for every colour channel; exactly three are red, green and blue; any other count is the master then the channels in turn (four: master, red, green, blue; five: those and alpha). A channel without a table is straight. Empty, a length not a whole number of tables, or more than five tables is refused with the reason in words. `phase` cycles each held table right: T'(u) = T(v), mixed linearly between entries floor(v) and floor(v) + 1 (past 255 is entry 0), at v = (u - phase) mod 256. The colour tables become one 256-entry table per colour, F_c[x] = M'(C_c'(x)), the channel's then the master's as Curves (D-111), applied by Color Lookup's 1D rule (D-182) to the encoded straight colour, u = 255 e. With `apply_to_alpha` "on" and an alpha table, the covering then goes through it as Curves' alpha curve (D-302): the straight colour kept, a pixel that did not show and now does black. A `map` naming no `lut` asset, a missing file and an unreadable file leave the layer as it is with EFFECT_PARAMETER_INVALID, MEDIA_MISSING and MEDIA_DECODE_FAILED. Nothing grows; a draft changes nothing. **Deviations:** Adobe does not publish Phase's range or unit, so -255 to 255 levels is ours; a file of more than five tables (CMYK or multichannel) is refused rather than guessed. Drawn on the card in the tone pass (mode 11, Color Lookup's), except with Apply Phase Map To Alpha on over a file with an alpha table, which stays on the processor as Curves' alpha curve does. FX-AMAP-001 to 022 pin it.

Selective Color (D-396, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Selective Color (Photoshop's adjustment). `tools/selective_color_reference.py` is the reference. `core.selective_color`: `method`, "relative" (when added, as Photoshop starts) or "absolute"; `reds`, `yellows`, `greens`, `cyans`, `blues`, `magentas`, `whites`, `neutrals`, `blacks`, each four per cents, cyan, magenta, yellow and black, -100 to 100, keyable, all 0 when added; all written. The rule is Clement Boesch's reverse-engineering of Photoshop's, published with FFmpeg's selectivecolor filter, worked in fractions. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1)) is its encoded straight colour, with largest, middle and smallest mx, md, mn. Each family's weight w: reds, greens and blues mx - md where red, green or blue is the largest (ties count for each); cyans, magentas and yellows md - mn where red, green or blue is the smallest; whites 2 mn - 1; neutrals 1 - (|mx - 0.5| + |mn - 0.5|); blacks 1 - 2 mx. For each family with w > 0, with s its cyan, magenta or yellow over 100 for channel red, green or blue and k its black over 100, the channel moves by w times t, t = (-1 - s) k - s, times 1 - e_c when relative, held inside -e_c to 1 - e_c; the moves add, and the result is held inside 0 to 1, back to linear and times a. Every weight is continuous in the colour. Every amount 0 is the input. Nothing grows; a draft changes nothing. **Deviations:** FFmpeg works on whole 8-bit levels and rounds each family's move to a level, and counts a pixel as white only when its smallest channel is above 128, where in fractions a smallest channel of exactly 128 is a white of weight 1/255; this program works in fractions. FFmpeg starts absolute; Photoshop and this program start relative. After Effects' Colors menu only chooses which family's sliders are shown, so it is not a setting. Drawn on the card in the tone pass (mode 60), one pixel, so it joins fused runs. FX-SELC-001 to 023 pin it.

Shadow/Highlight (D-400, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Shadow/Highlight. `tools/shadow_highlight_reference.py` is the reference. `core.shadow_highlight`: `shadow_amount` and `highlight_amount` 0 to 100 (50 and 0 when added), `shadow_tonal_width` and `highlight_tonal_width` 0 to 100 (50), `shadow_radius` and `highlight_radius` 0 to 500 pixels (30), `color_correction` 0 to 100 (20), each keyable, all written. The rule is darktable's shadows and highlights (src/iop/shadhi.c, Gaussian softening), not ported. At a pixel with covering a > 0, b = clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1) its straight linear colour; its CIE L*a*b* on the sRGB primaries' own white (X, Y, Z = M b, M the sRGB matrix of IEC 61966-2-1, the white M (1, 1, 1)), scaled l = L* / 100, p = a* / 128, q = b* / 128; a pixel with a = 0 has l = 0 for the blur and is left as it is. l over the layer is blurred by Gaussian Blur's kernel (sigma the radius, the edge pixels repeated): ls at the shadow radius, lh at the highlight radius, a radius 0 the pixel's own; ts = 1 - ls, th = 1 - lh. s = 2 shadow / 100, h = 2 highlight / 100, cs = min(1 - shadow width / 100, 0.99), ch likewise, k = colour correction / 100. Highlights first: n = h^2; while n > 0, with x = clamp(1 - th / (1 - ch), 0, 1), o = min(n, 1) x, lb = (th - 1/2) sign(1 - l) + 1/2, l' = l (1 - o) + (l > 1/2 ? 1 - (1 - 2 (l - 1/2)) (1 - lb) : 2 l lb) o, f = l' r(l) (1 - k) + (1 - l') r(1 - l) k, p and q times (1 - o + f o), l = l', n less 1. Then the shadows, n = s^2, x = clamp(ts / (1 - cs) - cs / (1 - cs), 0, 1), lb from ts, f = l' r(l) k + (1 - l') r(1 - l) (1 - k). r(v) is 1 / v, held at plus or minus 10^6 where |v| <= 10^-6; sign(0) is 1. Back to linear by the inverse, each channel held inside 0 to 1, times a; the covering never changes. Both amounts 0 is the input; black and white stay as they are. A draft scales both radii with the picture. **Deviations:** two radii and two tonal widths where darktable has one radius and one compress; darktable's white point adjustment left at 0; L*a*b* on sRGB's white, not D50; the result held inside 0 to 1; Gaussian, not bilateral. After Effects' Auto Amounts, Temporal Smoothing, Scene Detect, Midtone Contrast and Black and White Clip are not settings (D-400); D-563 adds Midtone Contrast and the clips, below. Drawn on the card as a lightness pass, the held Gaussian at each radius (the second copied into green when the radii differ) and the rule in double precision (the cube root from single precision with two Newton steps). FX-SHHI-001 to 021 pin it.

Shadow/Highlight's Midtone Contrast and Black and White Clip (D-563, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Shadow/Highlight. `tools/shadow_highlight_clip_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `midtone_contrast` in -100..100, `black_clip` and `white_clip` in 0..50 per cent, each keyable, held inside their ranges at each frame, starting at 0 (ours; a file without them reads 0), written only if moved, keyed or already in the file. The rule is ours. After D-400's highlights and shadows, at each pixel with covering, m = midtone contrast / 100 and l' = l + m l (1 - l) (2 l - 1), Color Grade's contrast curve (D-397). The chroma follows: where l' < l, p and q are times l' / l; where l' > l, times (1 - l') / (1 - l). When either clip is above 0, a count of 256 bins of l' over every pixel with a > 0, each adding a to bin floor(255 l' + 1/2) held inside 0..255 (D-351); kb, kw from `frame_stats::clip_points` at the two clips and lo, hi at 0 and 0. Where kw > kb, l'' = clamp(lo / 255 + (l' - kb / 255) (hi - lo) / (kw - kb), 0, 1), the chroma following as before; where kw <= kb, nothing. Back to linear as D-400, each channel held inside 0..1, times a. With both amounts, the midtone contrast and both clips 0 the layer is left exactly as it is; with both amounts 0 the blurs are not made. The count is of the whole layer at the quality drawn. On the card Midtone Contrast is drawn in the `shhi` pass; a clip above 0 is drawn on the processor. FX-SHCLIP-001 to 021 pin it.

Shadow/Highlight's Auto Amounts, Temporal Smoothing and Scene Detect (D-564, 2026-10-11), after After Effects' Shadow/Highlight. `tools/shadow_highlight_auto_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `auto_amounts` "off" or "on", `temporal_smoothing` in 0..10 seconds, keyable, `scene_detect` "off" or "on", starting off, 0 and off (ours; After Effects is recalled to start Auto Amounts on; a file without them reads those), written only if moved, keyed or already in the file. The rule is ours. With `auto_amounts` off nothing changes and the other two are not read. With it on, D-351's statistics of the layer's picture as the effects before this one left it: the brightness histogram h, 256 bins, each pixel adding its covering a to bin floor(255 y + 1/2), y = 0.2126 eR + 0.7152 eG + 0.0722 eB on its straight sRGB-encoded colour; T the sum of h, y_k = k / 255; d = sum h_k max(0, 1 - 2 y_k)^2 / T and b = sum h_k max(0, 2 y_k - 1)^2 / T; the shadow amount 100 min(1, 2 d) and the highlight amount 100 min(1, 2 b) replace the sliders, and D-400's and D-563's rule runs as before. A layer where nothing shows has both amounts 0. Temporal Smoothing and Scene Detect are D-351's: N = floor(seconds x frame rate + 1e-9) frames each side, each frame's statistics added over its own total, Scene Detect stopping a side past a difference above one half; on an adjustment layer each frame is judged by itself with TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED each frame. Auto Amounts on is drawn on the processor. FX-SHAUTO-001 to 014 pin it.

Radial Shadow (D-211, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/radial_shadow_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `color`, `#rrggbb`; `opacity` in 0..100; `light`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each in -1000..1000; `distance` in 0..1000; `softness` in 0..500 pixels; `render`, `regular` or `glass_edge`; `color_influence` in 0..100; `shadow_only`, `off` or `on`; all but `render` and `shadow_only` keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. With `L` the light in the drawing's own space as Bulge's centre (D-152) and `k = 1 + distance / 100`, the layer, its buffer spanning `left..right` across and `top..bottom` down, first grows across by `gx = min(ceil(distance / 100 * max(Lx - left, right - Lx)), its width)` and down by `gy`, the same with the height; each pixel whose centre is `P` there takes document 21's bilinear sample of the input, all four channels, at `L + (P - L) / k`, transparent outside it; that is blurred by document 21's Gaussian at sigma `softness / 3`, transparent edges, which grows it by the blur's reach. With `S` a pixel of that and `o = opacity / 100`, the shadow is `(C * S.a * o, S.a * o)` for `regular`, `C` the colour's linear value, or `(((1 - f) * C * S.a + f * S.rgb) * o, S.a * o)` for `glass_edge`, `f = color_influence / 100`; the output is the input `I` over it, `I + shadow * (1 - I.a)`, or with `shadow_only` `on` the shadow alone. A draft scales `softness`. FX-RSHADOW-001 to 025 pin it.

Extract (D-212, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/extract_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `channel`, `luminance`, `red`, `green`, `blue` or `alpha`; `black_point`, `white_point`, `black_softness` and `white_softness`, each in 0..255, keyable and held inside that range at each frame; `invert`, `off` or `on`. At a pixel with covering `a > 0`, with `e` its encoded straight colour as Threshold (D-138) takes it, the value `v` is `255 Y(e)` with Threshold's luma for `luminance`, 255 times that channel of `e` for `red`, `green` or `blue`, and `255 a` for `alpha`. With `b`, `w`, `s` and `t` the four numbers, `low = clamp((v - b) / s, 0, 1)` when `s > 0`, else 1 when `v + 1e-4 >= b` and 0 otherwise; `high = clamp((w - v) / t, 0, 1)` when `t > 0`, else 1 when `v - 1e-4 <= w` and 0 otherwise; `m = low * high`, or `1 - low * high` with `invert` `on`; the output is the pixel with all four channels times `m`. A pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is. The layer does not grow and a draft changes nothing. FX-EXTRACT-001 to 027 pin it.

Bevel Alpha and Bevel Edges (D-213, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/bevel_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `edge_thickness`, `0..200` pixels for `core.bevel_alpha` and `0..0.5` for `core.bevel_edges`; `light_angle`, `-3600..3600`; `light_intensity`, `0..1`; each keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `light_color`, `#rrggbb`. With `u = (sin θ, -cos θ)` for `θ = light_angle`, exact at whole quarter turns, each pixel has a slope `s` in `-1..1`. Bevel Alpha: `H` is the covering blurred by this document's Gaussian at sigma `T / 2`, `T` the thickness, transparent outside the layer and 0 beyond the blur's reach; `gx = (H(x+1, y) - H(x-1, y)) / 2`, `gy = (H(x, y+1) - H(x, y-1)) / 2`, and `s = clamp(-1.25 T (gx ux + gy uy), -1, 1)`. Bevel Edges: `T` is the thickness times the smaller of the layer's width and height as it reaches the effect; a pixel whose centre is nearer than `T` to the nearest side of the layer's rectangle, the first of left, top, right and bottom among equals, has `s = n . u` with `n` that side's outward normal, and every other pixel `s = 0`. At a pixel with covering `a > 0`, `L` the colour's linear value and `I` the intensity: `p + (L a - p) I s` when `s > 0`, `p (1 - I |s|)` when `s < 0`, the covering unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is. The layer does not grow. A draft scales Bevel Alpha's `T`. FX-BEVEL-001 to 026 pin it.

Block Dissolve (D-214, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/block_dissolve_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `completion`, `0..100`; `block_width` and `block_height`, `1..10000` pixels; `feather`, `0..10000` pixels; each keyable and held inside its range at each frame. At completion 0 the layer is unchanged; at 100 every channel of every pixel is 0. Otherwise `L = completion / 100`. With `(X, Y)` a pixel centre in the drawing's own coordinates, from its top left corner as the layer reaches the effect, the block `(i, j)` is `[i bw, (i+1) bw) x [j bh, (j+1) bh)` for every whole `i` and `j`, past the drawing's edges too, with the number `r = (u + 1) / 2`, `u` D-119's hash for seed 0 and the whole numbers `(i, j, 0, 0)`, and `K = 1` when `r >= L`, else 0. With feather `f = 0`, `k = K` of the block holding `(X, Y)`; with `f > 0`, `k` is the mean of `K` over the square `[X - f/2, X + f/2] x [Y - f/2, Y + f/2]`, held in `0..1`. The output is `p k` on all four premultiplied channels. The layer does not grow. A draft scales `bw` and `bh`, never below 1, and `f`. FX-BDISSOLVE-001 to 019 pin it.

## Evaluation bounds and ROI

Every effect declares input bounds expansion. Transform/mask/matte operations declare the region they can affect. Cache keys include all parameters that alter pixels or bounds. An optimization may skip pixels outside ROI only if output equals full-frame reference math within test tolerance.

An export may draw several frames at the same time. Each frame is drawn from the project alone, as it always was, so drawing frames together changes no pixel. Files are written, and notes added to the report, in frame order. D-231, B-160.

## Numeric tolerance

CPU reference scalar tests should use exact/near-exact float comparisons appropriate to the operation. GPU comparison tolerance begins at absolute channel error <= 1e-5 for simple arithmetic fixtures and requires per-effect declared tolerances for filters. A tolerance increase requires evidence, not convenience.

## Display pipeline

Viewer checkerboard, alpha-only display, overlays, selection outlines and draft-resolution indicators are presentation layers. They must not alter cached final pixels or export results.

D-311: a composition's `background_color` is presentation too. The frame, a PNG, an EXR, an animated PNG, a GIF and a composition layer drawing it are unchanged. The viewer shows it behind the frame with the checkerboard off. An MP4 has no alpha: each 8-bit straight pixel `(e, a)` of the frame becomes `round(e a + 255 linear_to_srgb(bg) (1 - a))` per channel with alpha 255, in encoded values, the same space the MP4 was laid over black in before, so a black background gives the MP4 as before. B-194 pins it.

## Deferred rendering questions

Intersecting transparent planes, motion blur (its own contract above, ADR-019, accepted on 2026-09-28), frame blending (its own contract above, ADR-020, accepted on 2026-09-29), depth of field, HDR display transforms and higher-order resampling are separate contracts. They must not be implied by this G1 specification. G2 camera projection was one of them until D-58, which the owner accepted on 2026-09-15 and B-13c built; it is specified under Camera and depth above, and it deliberately specifies none of the rest - in particular a camera that tilts or turns, which would need a perspective transform rather than the affine one written there.

Related documents: 08, 18, 20, 25 and 27.

---

## Tile contract (added in version 0.3, ADR-011)

The unit of render work is a tile: a fixed-size rectangular region of the output frame, composited independently of every other tile in that frame. A frame evaluation divides the output extent into tiles and distributes them across worker threads. Tiles within one frame evaluation never depend on one another.

All math specified above is defined per pixel and is therefore unchanged by tiling. Tiling changes only the order and grouping of evaluation, and must not change results: a tiled render and a hypothetical whole-frame render of the same request must be byte-identical, and B-05a proves this rather than assuming it.

Tile size is a tunable measured on the reference machine, not a constant chosen in advance. Too small wastes scheduling overhead; too large starves threads at the frame edges.

### Spatial support and margins

An operation whose output pixel depends only on the corresponding input pixel is tile-safe without qualification. This covers transforms sampled per output pixel, blending, opacity and color conversion, which is the whole of G1-core.

An operation whose output pixel depends on a neighborhood, such as blur, requires the tile to be evaluated with a margin of the operation's radius, then cropped. The margin is part of the operation's contract and must be declared by the operation rather than assumed by the renderer. An operation with unbounded support cannot be tiled without an explicit strategy and must not be added casually.

This is a direct reason R-05 sits in G1-rest: the blur in that requirement is the first operation that will exercise margin handling, and it deserves its own fixtures rather than arriving inside a milestone that has none.

### Relationship to a future GPU path

The tile decomposition is also the decomposition a GPU compute dispatch needs. Committing to it now is what makes ADR-006, which defers GPU work entirely, a deferral rather than a dead end: adding a GPU path later becomes a port of tile execution, not a rewrite of the renderer.

This is the one piece of forward design in the pack that is not justified by present need, and it is accepted deliberately, because retrofitting tiling onto a whole-frame renderer would be substantially more expensive than adopting it from the start.

### Determinism

Tile results must not depend on the number of worker threads, the order of completion, or scheduling. Two renders of the same request on the same build produce identical bytes, which SP-04 establishes and B-10 re-verifies against the exported sequence.
